Namo tassa bhagavato arahato sammāsambuddhassa

Homage to the Blessed One, the Arahant, the Perfectly Self-Enlightened One.

Saṃyuttanikāye

In the Connected Discourses

Mahāvaggaṭīkā

The Subcommentary on the Great Chapter

1. Maggasaṃyuttaṃ

1. The Connected Discourses on the Path

1. Avijjāvaggo

1. The Chapter on Ignorance

1-2. Avijjāsuttādivaṇṇanā

1–2. Commentary on the Discourse on Ignorance and Others

1-2. Pubbaṅgamāti [M 2.0393][V 2.0103] pubbecarā. Avijjā hi aññāṇalakkhaṇā sammuyhanākārena ārammaṇe pavattatīti sampayuttadhammānampi tadākārānuvidhānatāya paccayo hoti. Tathā hi te aniccāsubhadukkhānattasabhāvepi dhamme niccādito gaṇhanti, ayamassā tesaṃ sahajātavasena pubbaṅgamatā. Yaṃ pana mohena abhibhūto pāpakiriyāya ādīnavaṃ apassanto pāṇaṃ hanati, adinnaṃ ādiyati, kāmesu micchā carati, musā bhaṇati, aññampi vividhaṃ dussīlyaṃ ācarati, ayamassa sahajātavasena ca upanissayavasena ca pubbaṅgamatā. Samāpajjanāyāti tabbhāvāpajjanāya [V 2.0104] akusalappattiyā. Sabhāvapaṭilābhāyāti attalābhāya. Tenāha “uppattiyā”ti. Sā panesā vuttākārena akusalānaṃ pubbaṅgamabhūtā avijjā uppajjatīti sambandho. Yadetanti yaṃ etaṃ pāpājigucchanatāya pāpato alajjanākārasaṇṭhitaṃ ahirikaṃ, pāpānutrāsatāya pāpato abhāyanākārasaṇṭhitañca anottappaṃ, etaṃ [M 2.0394] dvayaṃ anudeva anvāgatameva. Anu-saddena cettha etanti upayogavacanaṃ. Anudevāti etassa attho saheva ekatoti. Ettha avijjāya vuttanayānusārena tappaṭipakkhato ca attho veditabbo. Ayaṃ pana viseso – tattha yathā akusalakammapathavasena pavattiyaṃ pubbaṅgamatā avijjāya, evaṃ kusalakammapathavasena puññakiriyavatthuvasena ca pavattiyaṃ vijjāya pubbaṅgamatā vattabbā. Vīmaṃsādhipativasena pavattiyaṃ ādhipaccākāravasena ca pubbaṅgamatā veditabbā. Dvīhevāti ca avadhāraṇaṃ ādhipaccākārassa sahajāteneva saṅgahetabbato.

‘Forerunner’ means going before. Ignorance, which has the characteristic of unknowing, proceeds by way of delusion in regard to the object; thus it becomes a condition for its associated states by making them conform to its own mode. For when they grasp as permanent, etc., things that are in their own nature impermanent, foul, suffering, and non-self, this is its forerunning by way of co-nascence. Further, when one, overcome by delusion, not seeing the danger in evil actions, destroys life, takes what is not given, engages in sexual misconduct, speaks falsehood, and practices various other kinds of wrong conduct, this is its forerunning by way of co-nascence and by way of decisive support. ‘For attainment’ means for entering into that state, for reaching the unwholesome. ‘For acquiring a nature’ means for acquiring an individual existence. Hence it is said, ‘for arising.’ The connection is that this ignorance, which has become the forerunner of unwholesome states in the way described, arises. ‘That which’ refers to this shamelessness (ahirika), which has the nature of not being ashamed of evil, and this fearlessness of wrongdoing (anottappa), which has the nature of not dreading evil. These two follow right along. Here, with the word anu-, etaṃ is a word of application. The meaning of anudeva is ‘together with, as one.’ Here the meaning should be understood in accordance with the method stated for ignorance and, conversely, for its opposite. But this is the distinction: just as ignorance is the forerunner in the occurrence of unwholesome courses of action, so true knowledge (vijjā) should be spoken of as the forerunner in the occurrence of wholesome courses of action and the grounds for meritorious deeds. In an occurrence under the dominance of investigation, its forerunning should be understood as being in the manner of supremacy. The word ‘two’ is for emphasis, since the mode of supremacy is to be included together with co-nascence.

Lajjanākārasaṇṭhitāti pāpato jigucchanākārasaṇṭhitā. Bhāyanākārasaṇṭhitanti uttasanākārasaṇṭhitaṃ. Etthāti hiriottappe. Vidati, vindatīti vā vijjā. Viddasūti ca sappaññapariyāyoti āha “viddasunoti viduno”ti. Yāthāvadiṭṭhīti aviparītā diṭṭhi, saṃkilesato niyyānikadiṭṭhi. Sammādiṭṭhi pahotīti ettha sā vijjā sammādiṭṭhi veditabbā. Na ekato sabbāni labbhanti sammāvācākammantājīvānaṃ pubbābhisaṅkhārassa anekarūpattā. Lokuttaramaggakkhaṇe ekato labbhanti kiccato bhinnānampi tāsaṃ tattha sarūpato abhinnattā. Ekā eva hi virati maggakkhaṇe tissannampi viratīnaṃ kiccaṃ sādhentī pavattati, yathā ekā eva sammādiṭṭhi parijānanādivasena catubbidhakiccaṃ sādhentī pavattati. Tāni ca kho sabbāni aṭṭhapi paṭhamajjhānike magge labbhantīti yojanā. Paṭhamajjhāniketi paṭhamajhānavante.

‘Having the nature of shame’ means having the nature of revulsion toward evil. ‘Having the nature of fear’ means having the nature of dread. ‘Here’ means in the case of moral shame and moral dread. ‘One knows’ (vidati) or ‘one finds’ (vindati), thus it is ‘true knowledge’ (vijjā). ‘A wise person’ (viddasū) is a synonym for one with wisdom; thus it is said: ‘a wise person means one who knows.’ ‘View in accordance with actuality’ means unperverted view, a view that leads out from defilement. ‘Right view is powerful’: here that true knowledge should be understood as right view. Not all are obtained at once—right speech, right action, and right livelihood—because of the manifold nature of the preliminary volition. At the moment of the supramundane path they are obtained at once because, though they are distinct in function, they are not different in essence. For at the moment of the path a single abstinence proceeds accomplishing the function of all three abstinences, just as a single right view proceeds accomplishing the fourfold function by way of full understanding, etc. And indeed, all eight are obtained in the path pertaining to the first jhāna: this is the connection. ‘Pertaining to the first jhāna’ means possessing the first jhāna.

Tathābhūtassāti ariyamaggasamaṅgino. Yasmā mahāsaḷāyatanasutte vuttaṃ “sammādiṭṭhiādīnaṃ pañcannaṃ eva aṅgānaṃ vasenā”ti, tasmā pañcaṅgiko lokuttaramaggo hoti. “Pubbeva kho panā”ti hi vacanaṃ tadā maggakkhaṇe viratīnaṃ abhāvaṃ ñāpeti, tasmā kāmāvacaracittesu viya lokuttaracittesu virati aniyatāti adhippāyo. Parisuddhabhāvadassananti parisuddhasīlabhāvadassanatthaṃ. Ayamattho dīpito, na ariyamagge viratīnaṃ abhāvo.

‘Of one who has become thus’ means of one endowed with the noble path. Because in the Great Discourse on the Sixfold Base it is said, ‘by way of just the five factors, right view and so on,’ therefore the supramundane path is fivefold. The statement ‘But just before’ indeed indicates the absence of the abstinences at the moment of the path; therefore, the intention is that abstinence is unfixed in supramundane consciousnesses, as it is in sense-sphere consciousnesses. ‘Seeing the state of purity’ is for the purpose of seeing the state of pure virtue. This meaning is clarified, not the absence of the abstinences in the noble path.

Yadi evaṃ kasmā abhidhamme maggavibhaṅge pañcaṅgikavāro āgatoti āha “yampi abhidhamme”tiādi. Tanti “pañcaṅgiko maggo hotī”ti vacanaṃ. “Ekaṃ [M 2.0395] kiccantaraṃ dassetuṃ vutta”nti vatvā taṃ dassetuṃ “yasmiñhi kāle”tiādi vuttaṃ. Yasmiñhi kāleti lokiyakāle[V 2.0105]. Tena “ekaṃ kiccantara”nti vuttaṃ aṭṭhaṅgikakiccaṃ dasseti. Viratiuppādanena micchāvācādīni puggalena maggasamaye pajahāpentīti sammādiṭṭhiādīni “pañca kārakaṅgānī”ti vuttāni. Sammāvācādikiriyā hi virati, tañca etāni kārāpentīti. Virativasenāti viramaṇakiriyāvasena kārāpakabhāvena, kattubhāvena vāti attho. “Viratittayavasenā”ti vā pāṭho.

If so, why in the Abhidhamma, in the Analysis of the Path, does the section on the fivefold path occur? He says, ‘And that in the Abhidhamma,’ etc. This refers to the statement, ‘the path is fivefold.’ Having said, ‘It is stated to show another function,’ in order to show this he said, ‘For at which time,’ etc. ‘For at which time’ means at the mundane time. Thereby, what is stated as ‘another function’ shows the function of the eightfold path. Because they cause the person to abandon wrong speech, etc., at the time of the path by producing the abstinences, right view and the others are called ‘the five causal factors.’ For the action of right speech, etc., is abstinence, and these five factors cause that to be done. ‘By way of abstinence’ means by way of the action of refraining, in the capacity of a causative agent or in the capacity of an agent; this is the meaning. Or the reading is ‘by way of the triad of abstinences.’

Sammākammanto pūratīti imehi sammādiṭṭhiādīhi sammākammantakiccaṃ pūrati nāma tehi vīriyādikehi tadatthasiddhito. Tampi sandhāya “ekaṃ kiccantaraṃ dassetu”nti vuttaṃ. Imaṃ kiccantaraṃ dassetunti lokuttaramaggakkhaṇepi imāneva pañca sammāvācādiviratittayassa ekakkhaṇe kārāpakaṅgānīti dassetuṃ. Evaṃ vuttanti “tasmiṃ samaye pañcaṅgiko maggo hotī”ti (vibha. 494) evaṃ vuttaṃ. Lokiyamaggakkhaṇe pañceva honti, virati pana aniyatā, tasmā “chaaṅgiko”ti avatvā “pañcaṅgiko”icceva vuttaṃ. Tayidaṃ abhidhamme pañcaṅgikavāradesanāya kāraṇakittanamaggo, ariyamaggo pana aṭṭhaṅgikovāti dassetuṃ, “yā ca, bhikkhave”tiādimāha, taṃ suviññeyyameva. Micchādiṭṭhiādikā dasa, tappaccayā akusalā ca dasāti vīsati akusalapakkhiyā, sammādiṭṭhiādikā dasa, tappaccayā kusalā ca dasāti vīsati kusalapakkhiyā mahācattārīsakasutte vuttā. Mahācattārīsakanti tassetaṃ nāmaṃ. Missakova kathito lokuttarassapi idha labbhamānattā.

‘Right action is fulfilled’: by these—right view and so on—the function of right action is fulfilled because its purpose is accomplished by them—by energy, etc. With reference to this too it was said, ‘to show another function.’ To show this other function means to show that even at the moment of the supramundane path, these same five are the causal factors for the triad of abstinences—right speech, etc.—in a single moment. Thus it is said: ‘At that time the path is fivefold’ (Vibh 494). At the moment of the mundane path there are only five factors, while the abstinences are unfixed. Therefore, without saying ‘sixfold,’ it was said just ‘fivefold.’ This is the way of stating the reason for the teaching of the fivefold section in the Abhidhamma, namely, to show that the noble path is eightfold. He said, ‘And what, bhikkhus,’ etc. That is easily understood. Wrong view and so on are ten; the unwholesome states conditioned by them are also ten; thus there are twenty on the side of the unwholesome. Right view and so on are ten; the wholesome states conditioned by them are also ten; thus there are twenty on the side of the wholesome. These are stated in the Great Forty Sutta. ‘Great Forty’ is its name. It is said to be mixed because the supramundane is also obtained here.

Yasmā kosalasaṃyuttepi idha ca therena “upaḍḍhamidaṃ, bhante, brahmacariyassā”tiādinā vuttaṃ “mā hevaṃ ānandā”tiādinā paṭikkhipitvā “sakalamevidaṃ ānandā”tiādinā bhagavatā desitaṃ suttaṃ āgataṃ. Tassattho kosalasaṃyuttavaṇṇanāyaṃ vutto, tasmā vuttaṃ “kosalasaṃyutte vuttatthamevā”ti.

Because both in the Kosala Saṃyutta and here the sutta has come down in which the Elder said, ‘Venerable sir, this is half of the holy life,’ etc., which the Blessed One rejected, saying, ‘Not so, Ānanda,’ etc., and then taught, ‘Ānanda, this is the entire holy life,’ etc. Its meaning has been stated in the commentary on the Kosala Saṃyutta. Therefore it is said: ‘The meaning is just as stated in the Kosala Saṃyutta.’

Avijjāsuttādivaṇṇanā niṭṭhitā.

Here ends the commentary on the Discourse on Ignorance and others.

3. Sāriputtasuttavaṇṇanā

3. Commentary on the Sāriputta Sutta

3. Sāvakabodhi [M 2.0396] sāvakapāramiyo, tappariyāpannaṃ ñāṇaṃ sāvakapāramiñāṇaṃ, taṃ pana dvinnaṃ aggasāvakānaṃ tatthapi dhammasenāpatino eva savisesaṃ matthakaṃ pattaṃ, na itaresanti āha – “sāvaka…pe… appattatāyā”ti. Tasmā tassa matthakappattiyā maggabrahmacariye ijjhante tassa ekadeso idha ijjhati, na sakalanti. Na hi addhabrahmacariyaṃ nāma atthi, tasmā vuttaṃ, “sakalampi [V 2.0106] …pe… labbhatī”ti, taṃ pana bhaṇḍāgāriko nāññāsi ñāṇassa sāvakavisayepi sappadesikattā, dhammasenāpati pana ñāṇassa tattha nippadesikattā aññāsīti. Tenāha – “ānandatthero…pe… aññāsī”ti. Evamāhāti “sakalamidaṃ, bhante”ti evaṃ avoca.

3. A disciple’s enlightenment consists of the perfections of a disciple; the knowledge pertaining to it is the knowledge of the perfections of a disciple. But among the two chief disciples, it was only for the General of the Dhamma that this knowledge reached its special summit, not for the other. Hence it is said: 'a disciple... due to not having attained.' Therefore, since the holy life of the path was accomplished by him in its culmination, only a part of it is accomplished here, not the whole. For there is no such thing as a half-holy life. Thus it is said: 'The whole... can be obtained.' But the Treasurer did not know this, as his knowledge in the disciple's domain was partial, whereas the General of the Dhamma knew it, as his knowledge there was complete. Therefore it is said: 'The Venerable Ānanda... knew.' Thus he said—that is, he spoke thus: 'All this, venerable sir.'

Sāriputtasuttavaṇṇanā niṭṭhitā.

The Explanation of the Sāriputta Sutta is finished.

4. Jāṇussoṇibrāhmaṇasuttavaṇṇanā

4. Explanation of the Sutta to the Brahmin Jāṇussoṇi

4. Vaḷavābhi-saddo vaḷavāpariyāyoti āha “catūhi vaḷavāhi yuttarathenā”ti. Yodharathoti yodhehi yujjhanatthaṃ ārohitabbaratho. Alaṅkāraratho maṅgaladivasesu alaṅkatapaṭiyattehi ārohitabbaratho. Ghanadukulena parivāritoti rajatapaṭṭavaṇṇena setadukulena paṭicchādito. Paṭicchādanattho hi idha parivārasaddo. Rajatapanāḷisuparikkhittā setabhāvakaraṇatthaṃ.

4. The word vaḷavābhi is a synonym for vaḷavā (mare), hence it is said: 'with a chariot yoked to four mares.' A war chariot is a chariot to be mounted by warriors for the purpose of fighting. A decorated chariot is a chariot to be mounted on festival days, having been adorned and prepared. 'Enveloped by thick cloth' means covered with white cloth the color of a silver sheet. Here the word 'enveloped' (parivāra) has the meaning of 'covering.' 'Encircled by silver pipes' is for the purpose of making it white.

Channaṃ channaṃ māsānanti niddhāraṇe sāmivacanaṃ. Ekavāraṃ nagaraṃ padakkhiṇaṃ karotīti idaṃ tasmiṃ ṭhānantare ṭhitena kātabbaṃ cārittaṃ. Nagarato na pakkantāti nagarato bahi na gatā. Maṅgalavacane niyuttā maṅgalikā, suvatthivacane niyuttā sovatthikā. Ādi-saddena thutimāgadhavandikācariyake saṅgaṇhāti. Sukapattasadisāni vaṇṇato.

'Every six months' is the genitive case of specification. 'He circumambulates the city once': this is the custom to be performed by one who holds that office. 'Not having departed from the city' means not having gone outside the city. Those engaged in auspicious speech are maṅgalikā; those engaged in words of well-being are sovatthikā. By the word 'etc.' (ādi), it includes eulogists, Magadhan bards, and panegyrists. Similar to a parrot's wing in color.

Vaṇṇagītanti thutigītaṃ. Brahmabhūtaṃ seṭṭhabhūtaṃ yānaṃ, brahmabhūtānaṃ seṭṭhabhūtānaṃ yānanti vā brahmayānaṃ. Vijitattā visesena jinanato. Rāgaṃ vinayamānā pariyosāpetīti sabbampi rāgaṃ samucchedavinayavasena vineti, attano kiccaṃ pariyosāpeti. Kiccapariyosāpaneneva hi sayampi [M 2.0397] pariyosānaṃ nipphattiṃ upagacchati. Tenāha “pariyosānaṃ gacchati nipphajjatī”ti.

'A song of description' (vaṇṇagīta) is a song of praise. Brahmayāna means the divine vehicle, the best vehicle; or the vehicle of divine beings, of the best beings. 'Because of having conquered' (vijitattā) means because of conquering in a special way. 'Subduing lust, it brings it to an end' means it subdues all lust by way of subduing through eradication and brings its own task to an end. For it is by bringing the task to an end that it itself reaches its end, its fulfillment. Therefore it is said: 'it reaches an end, it is accomplished.'

Dhuranti bhummatthe upayogavacananti āha “tatramajjhattatāyuge yuttā”ti. Īsāti yugasandhārikā dāruyugaḷā. Yathā vā bāhiraṃ yugaṃ dhāreti, tassā ṭhitāya eva kiccasiddhi, evaṃ kiriyāvasena laddhabalena tatramajjhattatāyuge thiraṃ dhāreti, teheva ariyamaggarathassa pavattanaṃ. Hiriggahaṇena cettha taṃsahacaraṇato ottappampi gahitaṃyeva hoti. Tenāha “attanā saddhi”ntiādi. Nāḷiyā minamāno puriso viya ārammaṇaṃ minātīti mano. Kataraṃ pana taṃ mano, kathañcassa yottasadisatāti āha “vipassanācitta”ntiādi. Tena yottaṃ viyāti yottanti dasseti. Lokiyavipassanācittaṃ atirekapaññāsa kusaladhamme ekābaddhe ekasaṅgahite karotīti sambandho. Te pana “phasso hoti…pe… avikkhepo hotī”ti cittaṅgavasena dhammasaṅgahe (dha. sa. 1) āgatanayeneva [V 2.0107] veditabbā. Lokuttaravipassanācittanti maggacittaṃ āha. Atirekasaṭṭhīti te eva sammākammantājīvehi anaññātaññassāmītindriyādīhi ca saddhiṃ atirekasaṭṭhi kusaladhamme. Ekābaddheti ekasmiṃ eva ārammaṇe ābaddhe. Ekasaṅgaheti tatheva vipassanākiccavasena ekasaṅgahe karoti. Pubbaṅgamabhāvena ārakkhaṃ sāretīti ārakkhasārathī. “Yathā hi rathassa…pe… sārathī”ti vatvā taṃ dassetuṃ “yoggiyo”ti vuttaṃ. Dhuraṃ vāheti yogge. Yojeti yogge samagatiyañca. Akkhaṃ abbhañjati sukhappavattanatthaṃ. Rathaṃ peseti yoggacodanena. Nibbisevane karoti gamanavīthiyaṃ paṭipādanena sanniyojeti. Ārakkhapaccupaṭṭhānāti ārakkhaṃ paccupaṭṭhapeti asammosasabhāvattā. Gatiyoti pavattiyo, nipphattiyo vā. Samanvesatīti gavesati.

'Pole' (dhura) is a term of application in the locative sense; thus it says: 'yoked to the yoke of equanimity.' 'Shaft' (īsā) is the pair of wooden poles that connect the yoke. Just as an external yoke bears the load, and the task is accomplished only when it is stable, so too, by the power obtained through action, it firmly bears the yoke of equanimity, and by that very means the noble path-chariot proceeds. And here, by the mention of moral shame (hiri), moral dread (ottappa) is also included, since it accompanies it. Therefore it is said: 'together with oneself,' etc. 'Mind' (mano) is so called because it measures the object, like a person measuring with a tube measure. But what is that mind, and how is it like a yoke-strap (yotta)? It is said: 'the insight-consciousness,' etc. Thereby he shows that it is a yoke-strap because it is like a yoke-strap. The connection is that the mundane insight-consciousness makes over fifty wholesome states bound together, collected together. These are to be understood according to the method stated in the Dhammasaṅgaṇī (Dhs §1) by way of mental factors, as 'contact occurs... non-distraction occurs.' 'Supramundane insight-consciousness' refers to the path-consciousness. 'Over sixty' means those same wholesome states along with right action, right livelihood, the faculty of 'I shall come to know the unknown,' etc., making over sixty wholesome states. 'Bound together' means bound to a single object. 'Collected together' means it makes them collected together in the same way by the function of insight. Because it is the forerunner, it directs the guard; hence it is the 'guarding charioteer.' Having said, 'For just as of a chariot... a charioteer,' to illustrate this, it is said 'fit for yoking' (yoggī). He drives the yoked animals at the pole. He yokes the yoked animals for even movement. He anoints the axle for smooth running. He sends the chariot forth by urging the yoked animals. He makes it unentangled; he directs it by setting it on the path of travel. 'Protection is its manifestation' means it establishes protection because its nature is non-bewilderment. 'Courses' (gatiyo) means progressions or accomplishments. 'He investigates' means he seeks.

Ariyapuggalassa nibbānaṃ paṭimukhaṃ sampāpane ratho viyāti ratho. Parikaroti vibhūsayatīti parikkhāro, vibhūsanaṃ, sīlañca ariyamaggassa vibhūsanaṭṭhāniyaṃ. Tena vuttaṃ “catupārisuddhisīlālaṅkāro”ti, sīlabhūsanoti attho. Vipassanāsampayuttānanti lokiyāya lokuttarāya ca vipassanāya sampayuttānaṃ. Vidhinā īretabbato pavattetabbato vīriyaṃ, [M 2.0398] sammāvāyāmo. Samaṃ sammā ca dhiyatīti samādhi, dhurañca taṃ samādhi cāti dhurasamādhi, upekkhā dhurasamādhi etassāti upekkhādhurasamādhi, ariyamaggo upekkhāsaṅkhātadhurasamādhīti attho. Aṭṭhakathāyaṃ pana byañjanaṃ anādiyitvā dhurasamādhisaddānaṃ bhinnādhikaraṇatā vuttā. Payogamajjhatteti vīriyasamatāya. Anicchāti icchāpaṭipakkhā. Tenāha “alobhasaṅkhātā”ti. Parivāraṇanti parivāro, paricchadoti attho.

A chariot (ratha) is so called because it is like a chariot for a noble person in conveying him face to face with Nibbāna. An accessory (parikkhāra) is so called because it furnishes and adorns; it is an adornment. And virtue is in the position of an adornment for the noble path. Therefore it is said: 'the ornament of the fourfold purification virtue,' which means 'the adornment of virtue.' 'Of those conjoined with insight' means of those conjoined with both mundane and supramundane insight. Energy (vīriya) is so called because it should be stirred up and set in motion according to rule; it is right effort. Concentration (samādhi) is so called because it is placed evenly and rightly. It is a pole (dhura) and it is concentration, thus dhurasamādhi. Equanimity is the pole-concentration of this, thus upekkhādhurasamādhi. The meaning is that the noble path has the pole-concentration called equanimity. In the commentary, however, without regard for the letter, the words dhura and samādhi are said to have different referents. 'Neutrality in application' means by the balance of energy. 'Desirelessness' (anicchā) is the opposite of desire. Therefore it is said: 'which is called non-greed.' 'Encirclement' (parivāraṇa) means an entourage or equipment.

Mettāti mettācetovimutti. Tathā karuṇā. Pubbabhāgoti ubhinnampi upacāro. Dvepi kāyacittavivekā viya pubbabhāgadhammavasena vuttā. Ariyamaggaratheti parisuddhamaggasaṅkhāte rathe. Ariyamaggaratho ca maggaratho cāti ariyamaggaratho, evaṃ ekasesanayena vā attho veditabbo. Tenāha “imasmiṃ lokiyalokuttaramaggarathe ṭhito”ti. Sannaddhacammoti yogāvacarassa paṭimukkacammaṃ. Na naṃ te vijjhantīti vacanapathā na naṃ vijjhanti. Dhammabhedanavasena na bhañjati, tassa ariyamaggassa rathassa sammā yojitassa antarā bhaṅgo natthīti attho.

'Loving-kindness' (mettā) means the liberation of mind by loving-kindness. Similarly with compassion. 'Preliminary part' (pubbabhāga) is the preliminary concentration for both. Both are mentioned by way of preliminary-part states, like bodily and mental seclusion. 'In the noble path-chariot' means in the chariot called the purified path. Or the meaning of ariyamaggaratha should be understood by the method of ellipsis as 'the noble path and the path-chariot.' Therefore it is said: 'standing in this mundane and supramundane path-chariot.' 'With armor and leather bound on' means the leather armor put on by the meditator. 'They do not pierce him' means paths of speech do not pierce him. It does not break by way of the breaking of the teaching; the meaning is that there is no breaking midway for that well-yoked chariot of the noble path.

Attano purisakāraṃ nissāya laddhattā attano santāneti adhippāyo. Anuttaranti uttararahitaṃ. Tato eva seṭṭhayānaṃ, nassa kenaci sadisanti asadisaṃ. Dhitisampannatāya dhīrā paṇḍitapurisā [V 2.0108] lokamhā niyyanti gacchanti. “Jayaṃ jaya”nti gāthāyaṃ vacanavipallāsena vuttanti āha “jinantā jinantā”ti.

The intention is 'one's own' because it is obtained by relying on one's own human effort. 'Unsurpassed' (anuttara) means without a superior. For that very reason it is the best vehicle. 'It has no equal' means it is incomparable. Because they are endowed with fortitude, the wise, the sagacious persons, go forth, that is, they depart, from the world. In the verse, 'conquering, conquering' (jayaṃ jayaṃ) is said with a reversal of speech; thus it says 'conquering, conquering' (jinantā jinantā).

Jāṇussoṇibrāhmaṇasuttavaṇṇanā niṭṭhitā.

The Explanation of the Sutta to the Brahmin Jāṇussoṇi is finished.

5-6. Kimatthiyasuttādivaṇṇanā

5-6. Commentary on the Kimatthiya Sutta and so on.

5-6. Niyamatthoti avadhāraṇattho. Tena niyamena avadhāraṇena – aññaṃ maggaṃ paṭikkhipati ito aññassa niyyānikamaggassa abhāvato. “Dukkhassa pariññattha”nti vuttattā vaṭṭadukkhaṃ kathitaṃ. Ariyamagge gahite tassa pubbabhāgamaggo vipassanāya gahito evāti “missakamaggo kathito”ti vuttaṃ. Uttānameva apubbassa abhāvā. Ayaṃ pana viseso “rāgakkhayo”tiādīhi [M 2.0399] yadipi nibbānaṃ vuttaṃ. Tathāpi arahattaṃ viya brahmacariyampi. Tena nibbānaṃ eva vuccati “idaṃ brahmacariyapariyosāna”nti.

5-6. The meaning of 'determination' is the meaning of specification. By that determination, that specification, it rejects other paths, since there is no other liberating path apart from this one. Because it is said, 'for the full understanding of suffering,' the suffering of the round is mentioned. When the noble path is taken up, its preliminary-stage path is taken up by insight; thus it is said, 'the mixed path is spoken of.' This is plain, as there is nothing unprecedented. This, however, is the distinction: although Nibbāna is spoken of by such terms as 'the destruction of lust,' so too is the holy life, like arahantship. Therefore, Nibbāna itself is called: 'this is the culmination of the holy life.'

Kimatthiyasuttādivaṇṇanā niṭṭhitā.

The commentary on the Kimatthiya Sutta and so on is finished.

7. Dutiyaaññatarabhikkhusuttavaṇṇanā

7. Commentary on the Second Sutta on a Certain Monk

7. Rāgavinayādipadehi nibbānaṃ vāpi vucceyya arahattaṃ vāpi. Yasmā so bhikkhu ubhayatthapi niviṭṭhabuddhi, tasmā bhagavā tassa ajjhāsayavasena “nibbānadhātuyā kho eta”ntiādinā nibbānadhātuṃ vissajjetvā puna “āsavānaṃ khayo tena vuccatī”ti āha. Yasmā ariyamaggo rāgādike samucchedavasena vineti, āsavañca sabbaso khepeti, tena ca vuttaṃ nibbānaṃ arahattañca, tasmā tadubhayaṃ “rāgavinayotiādi nāmamevā”ti vuttaṃ. Anusandhikusalatāya pucchanto etaṃ avocāti iminā “pucchānusandhi idha labbhatī”ti dīpitaṃ, ajjhāsayānusandhipi ettha labbhatevāti daṭṭhabbaṃ.

7. By terms such as 'the removal of lust,' either Nibbāna or arahantship might be spoken of. Since that bhikkhu's understanding was settled on both, the Blessed One, in accordance with his disposition, first answered concerning the Nibbāna-element with the words beginning, 'This, indeed, is the Nibbāna-element,' and then said, 'By that is meant the destruction of the taints.' Because the noble path removes lust and so on by way of eradication and completely destroys the taints, and by it both Nibbāna and arahantship are expressed, therefore it is said that both are just a name for 'the removal of lust, etc.' By this, 'He asked this, being skilled in making connections,' it is shown that 'a connection by question is found here.' It should be seen that a connection by disposition is also found here.

Dutiyaaññatarabhikkhusuttavaṇṇanā niṭṭhitā.

The commentary on the Second Sutta on a Certain Monk is finished.

8. Vibhaṅgasuttavaṇṇanā

8. Commentary on the Sutta on Analysis

8. Ekena pariyāyena aṭṭhaṅgikamaggaṃ vibhajitvāti “sammādiṭṭhī”tiādinā ekena pariyāyena ariyaṃ aṭṭhaṅgikaṃ maggaṃ vibhāgena dassetvā “katamā ca, bhikkhave, sammādiṭṭhī”tiādinā [V 2.0109] puna aparena pariyāyena vibhajitukāmo. Uggahadhāraṇaparicayañāṇānipi savanañāṇe eva avarodhaṃ gacchantīti “savanasammasanapaṭivedhapaccavekkhaṇavasenā”ti vuttaṃ.

8. 'Having analyzed the Noble Eightfold Path in one way': having shown the Noble Eightfold Path in detail in one way with the words beginning 'right view,' he wishes to analyze it again in another way with the words beginning 'And what, bhikkhus, is right view?' The knowledges of learning, retaining, and becoming familiar are included in the knowledge of hearing; thus it is said, 'by way of hearing, exploring, penetrating, and reviewing.'

Kammaṭṭhānābhinivesoti kammaṭṭhānapaṭipatti. Purimāni dve saccāni uggaṇhitvāti sambandho. Iṭṭhaṃ kantaṃ manāpanti nirodhamaggesu ninnabhāvaṃ dasseti, na abhinandanaṃ, tanninnabhāvo eva ca tattha kammakaraṇaṃ daṭṭhabbaṃ. Ekenevākārena saccānaṃ paṭivedhanimittatā, so eva abhimukhabhāvo tesaṃ samāgamoti ekābhisamayo.

'Devotion to the meditation subject' means the practice of the meditation subject. The connection is with 'having learned the first two truths.' 'Pleasant, desirable, agreeable' shows the inclination towards cessation and the path, not delight; and that very inclination should be seen as the doing of the work there. The sign for the penetration of the truths is of a single aspect; that very facing them is their convergence, thus 'a single breakthrough.'

Assāti [M 2.0400] ñāṇassa, yogino vā. Ettha ca keci “lokiyañāṇampi paṭivedho sabbassa yāthāvabodhabhāvato”ti vadanti. Nanu uggahādipaṭivedho ca paṭivedhova, na ca so lokuttaroti? Taṃ na, kevalena paṭivedha-saddena uggahādipaṭivedhānaṃ avacanīyattā, paṭivedhanimittattā vā uggahādivasena pavattaṃ dukkhādīsu pubbabhāge ñāṇaṃ “paṭivedho”ti vuccati, na paṭivedhattā, paṭivedhabhūtameva pana ñāṇaṃ ujukaṃ paṭivedhoti vattabbataṃ arahati. Kiccatoti pariññādikiccato. Ārammaṇapaṭivedhoti sacchikiriyāpaṭivedhamāha. Kiccatoti asammohapaṭivedhaṃ. Uggahādīhi saccassa pariggaṇhanaṃ pariggaho.

'Of it': of the knowledge, or of the yogi. Here some say: 'Mundane knowledge too is penetration, because it understands everything as it really is.' But is not the penetration by learning, etc., also penetration, though it is not supramundane? That is not so. For the penetration by learning, etc., is not expressed by the mere word 'penetration.' Or, because it is a sign for penetration, the knowledge concerning suffering, etc., that proceeds in the preliminary stage by way of learning, etc., is called 'penetration'—not because it is penetration itself, but the knowledge that is actual penetration alone deserves to be called direct penetration. 'By function': by the function of full understanding, etc. 'Penetration of the object': this refers to penetration as realization. 'By function': this refers to penetration as non-delusion. The grasping of the truth by way of learning, etc., is 'grasping.'

Duddasattāti anadhigatañāṇena yāthāvasarasalakkhaṇato daṭṭhuṃ asakkuṇeyyattā uppattito pākaṭānipi. Tenāha “dukkhasaccaṃ hī”tiādi. Ubhayanti purimaṃ saccadvayaṃ. Payogoti kiriyā, vāyāmo vā. Tassa mahantatarassa icchitabbataṃ dukkarataratañca upamāhi dasseti “bhavaggaggahaṇattha”ntiādinā. Yathā purimaṃ saccadvayaṃ viya kenaci pariyāyena apākaṭatāya paramagambhīrattā uggahādivasena pubbabhāge pavattibhedaṃ gahetvā “dukkhe ñāṇa”ntiādinā catubbidhaṃ katvā vuttaṃ. Ekameva taṃ ñāṇaṃ hoti ekābhisamayavaseneva pavattanato.

'Because it is hard to see': because it is impossible for one who has not acquired the knowledge to see them according to their own particular characteristics, even though they are manifest from their arising. Hence he said: 'For the truth of suffering,' etc. 'Both': the first pair of truths. 'Effort': action, or exertion. He shows that it is more desirable and more difficult to do with similes beginning, 'for the sake of grasping the peak of existence.' Just as the first two truths, being in a way unmanifest and extremely profound, were spoken of as fourfold—'knowledge in suffering,' etc.—by taking the distinction of its occurrence in the preliminary stage by way of learning, etc., so that knowledge is just one, since it proceeds by way of a single breakthrough.

Kāmapaccanīkaṭṭhenāti kāmānaṃ ujupaccanīkabhāvena. Kāmato nissaṭabhāvenāti kāmehi visaṃyuttabhāvena. Kāmaṃ sammasantassāti duvidhampi kāmaṃ aniccādito sammasantassa. Pajjati pavattati etenāti padaṃ, kāmassa padanti kāmapadaṃ, kāmassa uppattikāraṇassa ghāto samugghāto, taṃ kāmapadaghātaṃ. Tenāha “kāmavūpasama”nti. Kāmehi vivittaṃ kāmavivittaṃ. So eva ca nesaṃ anto samucchedaviveketi katvā tasmiṃ sādhetabbe uppannoti vuttaṃ “kāmavivittante uppanno”ti. Kāmato nikkhamatīti nikkhamo, so eva nekkhammasaṅkappo. Imasmiñca [V 2.0110] nekkhammasaṅkappassa saddatthavibhāvena yathāvutto kāmapaccanīkaṭṭhādiko atthaniddhāraṇaviseso antogadho.

'By being opposed to sensual pleasures': by being directly contrary to sensual pleasures. 'By being issued from sensual pleasures': by being disjoined from sensual pleasures. 'Of one who explores sensual pleasure': of one who explores both kinds of sensual pleasure as impermanent, etc. 'Path' (`pada`) is that by which one goes (`pajjati`) or proceeds (`pavati`). The path of sensual pleasure is `kāmapada`. The destruction, the uprooting, of the cause for the arising of sensual pleasure is the destruction of the path of sensual pleasure. Hence he said, 'the calming of sensual pleasures.' 'Secluded from sensual pleasures' (`kāmavivittaṃ`). And that itself is their end, as it is seclusion by cutting off; therefore, because it arises in what is to be accomplished, it is said, 'arisen at the end of seclusion from sensual pleasures.' 'Renunciation' (`nekkhamo`) is departing from sensual pleasures (`kāmato nikkhamati`); that itself is the thought of renunciation. And in this thought of renunciation, the specific determination of meaning as stated—being opposed to sensual pleasures, etc.—is included by way of the analysis of the word's meaning.

Eseva nayoti iminā byāpādapaccanīkaṭṭhena vihiṃsāya paccanīkaṭṭhenātiādikaṃ abyāpādāvihiṃsāsaṅkappānaṃ atthuddhāraṇavidhiṃ atidisati. Nekkhammasaṅkappādayoti ādi-saddena abyāpādaavihiṃsāsaṅkappe eva saṅgaṇhāti. Kāma…pe… saññānanti kāmavitakkādiviratisampayuttānaṃ nekkhammādisaññānaṃ. Nānattāti nānākhaṇikattā. Tīsu ṭhānesūti [M 2.0401] tippakāresu kāraṇesu. Uppannassāti uppajjanārahassa. Bhūmiladdhauppannaṃ idhādhippetaṃ. Esa nayo ito paresupi. Padacchedatoti kāraṇupacchedato. Padanti hi uppattikāraṇanti vuttovāyamattho. Anuppattisādhanavasenāti yathā saṅkappo āyatiṃ nuppajjati, evaṃ anuppattisādhanavasena. Sammādiṭṭhi viya ekova kusalasaṅkappo uppajjati.

'This is the method': by this he extends the method of extracting the meaning of the thoughts of non-ill-will and non-harming, namely, 'by being opposed to ill will, by being opposed to harming,' etc. 'Thoughts of renunciation, etc.': by the word 'etc.' it includes only the thoughts of non-ill-will and non-harming. 'Perceptions of sensual pleasure...': of the perceptions of renunciation, etc., associated with abstention from sensual thoughts, etc. 'Variety': because of being of various moments. 'In three cases': in three kinds of reasons. 'Of what has arisen': of what is fit to arise. What has arisen on gaining the plane is intended here. This is the method also in the subsequent cases. 'By cutting off the root': by cutting off the cause. For `pada` means the cause of arising; this is the meaning here. 'By way of effecting non-arising': by way of effecting that the thought does not arise in the future. Like right view, only one wholesome thought arises.

Catūsu ṭhānesūti visaṃvādanādīsu catūsu vītikkamaṭṭhānesu. Pabbajitānaṃ micchājīvo nāma āhāranimittakoti āha “khādanīyabhojanīyādīnaṃ atthāyā”ti. Sabbaso anesanāya pahānaṃ sammāājīvoti āha “buddhappasatthena ājīvenā”ti. Kammapathapattānaṃ vasena “sattasu ṭhānesū”ti vuttaṃ. Akammapathapattāya hi anesanāya so padaghātaṃ karotiyeva.

'In four cases': in the four cases of transgression, such as lying, etc. For the renunciants, wrong livelihood is scheming for food; thus he said, 'for the sake of hard and soft foods, etc.' The abandoning of all improper seeking is right livelihood; thus he said, 'with a livelihood praised by the Buddha.' It is said, 'in seven cases,' with reference to those who have entered the courses of action. For by improper seeking that has not entered the courses of action, one indeed creates an obstacle.

Tathārūpe vā ārammaṇeti yasmiṃ ārammaṇe imassa pubbe kilesā na uppannā, tasmiṃ eva. Anuppannānanti anuppādassapi patthanāvasena anuppannānaṃ. Vīriyacchandanti vīriyassa nibbattetukāmatāchandaṃ. “Chandasampayuttavīriyañcā”ti vadanti. Vīriyameva pana anuppannākusalānuppādane labbhamānachandatāya dhurasampaggahatāya chandapariyāyena vuttaṃ. Tathā hi vīriyaṃ – “anikkhittachandatā anikkhittadhuratā”ti (dha. sa. 26) niddiṭṭhaṃ. Kosajjapakkhe patituṃ adatvā cittaṃ paggahitaṃ karoti. Padhānanti padhānabhūtavīriyaṃ.

'Or in such an object': in that very object in which defilements have not arisen for him before. 'Of the unarisen': of those that have not arisen, by way of the aspiration for their non-arising. 'Zeal for energy': the desire to generate energy. Some say: 'and energy associated with zeal.' But energy itself is spoken of by the synonym 'zeal' because in preventing the arising of unarisen unwholesome states it is accompanied by attainable zeal and by taking up the yoke. For energy is explained thus: 'the state of not casting off zeal, the state of not casting off the yoke' (Dhs §26). Not allowing the mind to fall into the party of laziness, one exerts the mind. 'Striving': energy that has become the chief thing.

Uppattipabandhavasenāti nirantaruppādanavasena. Catūsu ṭhānesu kiccasādhanavasenāti yathāvuttesu catūsu ṭhānesu padhānakiccassa nipphādanavasena anuppādanādivasena. Kiccasādhanavasenāti kāyavedanācittadhammesu subhasukhaniccaattagāhavidhamanavasena asubhadukkhāniccānattasādhanavasena.

'By way of a series of arisings': by way of uninterrupted arising. 'By way of accomplishing the function in four cases': by way of accomplishing the principal function in the four cases as stated, that is, by way of non-arising, etc. 'By way of accomplishing the function': by way of dispelling the grasp of the beautiful, pleasant, permanent, and self in regard to body, feelings, mind, and phenomena, and by way of accomplishing the foul, suffering, impermanent, and non-self.

Ayanti yathāvutto sadisāsadisatāviseso. Assāti maggassa. Ettha kathanti yadi rūpāvacaracatutthajjhānato [V 2.0111] paṭṭhāya yāva sabbabhavaggā jhānaṅgamaggaṅgabojjhaṅgānaṃ sadisatā, evaṃ sante “āruppe catukkapañcakajjhānaṃ uppajjati, tañca lokuttara”nti ettha kathaṃ attho gahetabboti āha “etthāpī”tiādi. Taṃjhānikāvāti paṭhamajjhānādīsu yaṃ jhānaṃ maggapaṭilābhassa pādakabhūtaṃ, taṃjhānikāva assa ariyassa uparipi tayo maggā[M 2.0402]. Evanti vuttākārena. Pādakajjhānameva niyameti āruppe catukkapañcakajjhānuppattiyaṃ. Vipassanāya ārammaṇabhūtā khandhāti sammasitakhandhe vadanti. Puggalajjhāsayo niyameti pādakasammasitajjhānānaṃ bhede. Yasmā saṅkhārupekkhāñāṇameva ariyamaggassa bojjhaṅgādivisesaṃ niyameti, tato dutiyādipādakajjhānato uppannassa saṅkhārupekkhāñāṇassa pādakajjhānātikkantānaṃ aṅgānaṃ asamāpajjitukāmatāvirāgabhāvato itarassa ca atabbhāvato tīsupi vādesu vipassanāva niyametīti veditabbo, tasmā vipassanāniyameneva hi paṭhamavādepi apādakajjhānādipādakāpi maggā paṭhamajjhānikā honti. Itarehi ca pādakajjhānehi vipassanāniyamehi taṃtaṃjhānikāva. Evaṃ sesavādesu vipassanāniyamo yathāsambhavaṃ yojetabbo. Dutiyavāde taṃtaṃjhānikatā sammasitasaṅkhāravipassanāniyamehi hoti. Tatra hi vipassanā taṃtaṃvirāgabhāvanā bhāvetabbā, na somanassasahagatā upekkhāsahagatā hutvā jhānaṅgādiniyamaṃ maggassa karotīti evaṃ vipassanāniyamo vuttanayeneva veditabbo. Imasmiñca vāde pādakasammasitajjhānupanissayasabbhāve ajjhāsayo ekantena hotīti “puggalajjhāsayo niyametīti vadantī”ti vuttaṃ, aṭṭhakathāyaṃ pana visuddhimaggassa etissā aṭṭhakathāya ekasaṅgahitattā “tesaṃ vādavinicchayo…pe… veditabbo”ti vuttaṃ. Pubbabhāgeti vipassanākkhaṇe.

This distinction of similarity and dissimilarity is as stated. 'Of this' (assa) means of the path. Here the question is: if, starting from the fourth jhāna of the form realm up to the peak of all existence, there is a similarity of jhāna factors, path factors, and enlightenment factors, then how should the meaning of the statement, 'in the formless realm the fourth and fifth jhānas arise, and they are supramundane,' be understood? To this it is said, 'Here too,' etc. Of that jhāna-type: among the first jhāna and so on, whichever jhāna is the basis for the attainment of the path, the three higher paths of that noble one are of that very jhāna-type. Thus: in the manner stated. The foundational jhāna itself determines the arising of the fourth and fifth jhānas in the formless realm. The aggregates that are the object of insight are called the 'contemplated aggregates.' The individual’s disposition determines the difference between the foundational and the contemplated jhānas. Since the knowledge of equanimity towards formations alone determines the special qualities of the noble path, such as the enlightenment factors, and since for the knowledge of equanimity towards formations that has arisen from the second and subsequent foundational jhānas there is a dispassion consisting in the unwillingness to attain the factors that have transcended the foundational jhāna, and an absence of the opposite, it should be understood that in all three theories, insight alone is the determining factor. Therefore, by the determination of insight alone, even in the first theory, paths based on other jhānas or lacking a jhāna-base become of the first-jhāna type. And by means of other foundational jhānas and the determination by insight, they are of their respective jhāna-types. Thus, in the remaining theories, the determination by insight should be applied as is appropriate. In the second theory, their being of a respective jhāna-type comes about by the determination of insight into the contemplated formations. For there, insight is to be developed as the development of dispassion for this or that; it is not as something accompanied by joy or accompanied by equanimity that it determines the jhāna factors, etc., of the path. Thus the determination by insight should be understood in the way stated. And in this theory, when there is the supporting condition of the foundational and contemplated jhānas, the disposition is definitive; hence it is said: 'The individual’s disposition determines, they say.' However, in the commentary, since it is compiled together with the Visuddhimagga, it is said, 'The resolution of their theories... should be understood.' 'In the preliminary phase' (pubbabhāge) means at the moment of insight.

Vibhaṅgasuttavaṇṇanā niṭṭhitā.

The Commentary on the Vibhaṅga Sutta is completed.

9. Sūkasuttavaṇṇanā

9. Commentary on the Sūka Sutta

9. Sūkanti sāliyavādīnaṃ vālamāha. So hi nikantakasadiso paṭimukhagataṃ hatthaṃ vā pādaṃ vā bhindati, tasmā bhedaṃ icchantena uddhaggaṃ katvā ṭhapitaṃ sammāpaṇihitaṃ nāma, tathā aṭṭhapitaṃ micchāpaṇihitaṃ nāmāti vuttaṃ. Micchāpaṇihitāyāti kammassakatapaññāya micchāṭhapanaṃ nāma – “ime sattā kammavasena sukhadukkhaṃ paccanubhavanti, taṃ pana kammaṃ issarassa icchāvasena brahmā nimminātī”tiādinā micchāpakappanaṃ. Keci pana “natthi dinnantiādinā nayena pavatti, tassa vā ñāṇassa appavattī”ti vadanti. Maggabhāvanāyāti [M 2.0403] etthāpi micchāmaggassa pavattanaṃ, ariyamaggassa vā appavattanaṃ micchāṭhapanaṃ. Tenāha “appavattitattā”ti. Avijjaṃ bhindissatīti avijjaṃ samucchindissati. Magganissitaṃ katvā magge [V 2.0112] eva pakkhipitvā. Tañhi ñāṇaṃ maggassa mūlakāraṇaṃ magge siddhe tassa kiccassa matthakappattito. “Sammāpaṇihitāya diṭṭhiyā sammāpaṇihitāya maggabhāvanāyā”ti vuttattā missakamaggo kathito. Chavibhedasadiso cettha avijjābhedo, lohituppādasadiso lokuttaramaggabhāvo daṭṭhabbo.

9. 'Awn' (sūka) refers to the bristle of rice, barley, etc. For it, being like a thorn, pierces a hand or foot that comes against it. Therefore, when held upright by one who wishes to pierce, it is called 'rightly directed'; it is said that when not so placed, it is called 'wrongly directed.' 'Wrongly directed' means the wrong application of the wisdom of ownership of kamma—a wrong conception such as: 'These beings experience pleasure and pain through the power of kamma, but that kamma is created by Brahmā according to the will of Issara.' Some, however, say it is the occurrence of the view 'There is nothing given,' etc., or the non-occurrence of that knowledge. 'By the development of the path': here too, the wrong application is the promotion of the wrong path or the non-development of the noble path. Hence it is said, 'because of non-occurrence.' 'He will pierce ignorance' means he will eradicate ignorance. Having made it dependent on the path, having placed it right in the path. For that knowledge is the root cause of the path, since when the path is accomplished, its function has reached its consummation. Because it is said, 'with a rightly directed view, with a rightly directed development of the path,' the mixed path is spoken of. Here, the piercing of ignorance should be seen as similar to the piercing of the skin, and the arising of the supramundane path as similar to the arising of blood.

Sūkasuttavaṇṇanā niṭṭhitā.

The Commentary on the Sūka Sutta is completed.

10. Nandiyasuttavaṇṇanā

10. Commentary on the Nandiya Sutta

10. Channaparibbājako vatthacchādiyā channaṅgaparibbājako, na naggaparibbājako.

10. The wanderer Channa is a wanderer who covers his body with a cloth; he is not a naked wanderer.

Avijjāvaggavaṇṇanā niṭṭhitā.

The Commentary on the Chapter on Ignorance is completed.

2. Vihāravaggo

2. The Chapter on Dwellings

1. Paṭhamavihārasuttavaṇṇanā

1. Commentary on the First Sutta on Dwelling

11. Aḍḍhamāsanti accantasaṃyoge upayogavacanaṃ. Paṭisallīyitunti yathāvuttaṃ kālaṃ paṭi divase divase samāpattiyaṃ dhammacintāyaṃ cittaṃ nilīyituṃ. Vinetabboti samucchedavinayena vinetabbo ariyamaggādhigantabbo. Tanti diṭṭhānugatiāpajjanaṃ. Assāti janatāya. Apagacchatīti satthu santikato apeti. Sūti nipātamattaṃ.

11. 'For half a month' (aḍḍhamāsaṃ) is a usage in the sense of continuous connection. 'To go into seclusion' (paṭisallīyituṃ) means for the mind to be immersed day after day for the stated period in meditative attainment and in reflection on the Dhamma. 'To be trained' (vinetabbo) means to be trained by the training of eradication; the noble path is to be attained. 'Lineage' (tanti) is falling into the pursuit of a view. 'Of this one' (assa) means of the populace. 'He goes away' (apagacchati) means he departs from the Teacher’s presence. 'Sū' is just a particle.

Padesenāti ekadesena. Saha padesenāti sapadeso. Svāyaṃ sapadeso yasmā vedanāvaseneva pāḷiyaṃ āgato, tasmā paramatthadhammakoṭṭhāse vedanā anavasesato labbhati, te gaṇhanto “khandhapadeso”tiādimāha. Taṃ sabbanti khandhapadesādikaṃ sabbampi. “Sammasanto”ti padassa atthadassanavasena “paccavekkhanto”ti āha. Paccavekkhaṇā idha sammasanaṃ nāma, na vipassanā. Vipassanāsammasanaṃ pana bhagavato [M 2.0404] visākhapuṇṇamāyaṃ eva nipphannaṃ, tasmā bhagavato aññabhūmikāpi vedanā aññabhūmikānaṃ sattānaṃ viruddhā uppajjatevāti vuttaṃ “yāva bhavaggā pavattā sukhā vedanā”ti. Sabbākārenāti sarūpato samudayato atthaṅgamato assādāditoti sabbākārena. Pariggaṇhanto upaparikkhanto.

'Partially' (padesena) means by a part. 'With a part' (saha padesena) means partial (sapadeso). Since this 'partial' occurs in the Pāli canon only in connection with feeling, therefore in the category of ultimate realities, feeling is obtained without remainder. Taking these, he says, 'a part of the aggregates,' etc. 'All that' (taṃ sabbaṃ) means all of it, the part of the aggregates, etc. To show the meaning of the word 'comprehending' (sammasanto), he says 'reviewing' (paccavekkhanto). Here, reviewing (paccavekkhaṇā) is called comprehension (sammasana), not insight (vipassanā). But the comprehension that is insight was accomplished by the Blessed One only on the full-moon day of Visākha. Therefore it is said that for the Blessed One feelings of a different plane, which are contrary to those of beings on other planes, do arise, as stated: 'pleasant feeling that extends up to the peak of existence.' 'In all aspects' (sabbākārena) means in all aspects: as to characteristic, origin, cessation, gratification, etc. 'Grasping' (pariggaṇhanto) means investigating.

Nippadesāneva [V 2.0113] anavasesāneva. Indriyasatipaṭṭhānapadeso suviññeyyoti anuddhato. Assāti bhagavato. Ṭhāneti tasmiṃ tasmiṃ paccavekkhitabbasaṅkhāte okāse. Sā sā ca vihārasamāpattīti khandhavasena āyatanādivasena ca pavattitvā tesaṃ ekadesabhūtaṃ vedanaṃyeva pariggahetvā taṃ sammasitvā anukkamena samāpannā jhānasamāpatti phalasamāpatti ca. Phalasamāpatti hi tathā sammasitvā punappunaṃ samāpajjanavasena atthato abhinnāpi adhiṭṭhānabhūtadhammabhedena bhinnā viya vuccati, yato catuvīsatikoṭisatasahassabhedā devasikaṃ vaḷañjanasamāpattiyo aṭṭhakathāyaṃ vuttā. Kāmaṃ aññadhammavasenapi jātā eva, vedanāvasena panettha abhiniveso kato vedanānubhāvena jātā. Kasmā evaṃ jātāti? Buddhānaṃ ñāṇapadassa antaravibhāgattā. Tathā hi bhagavā sakalampi aḍḍhamāsaṃ vedanāvaseneva sammasanaṃ pavatteti, tadanusārena ca tā vihārasamāpattiyo samāpajji. Tayidaṃ acchariyaṃ anaññasādhāraṇaṃ bhikkhū pavedento satthā – “yena svāha”ntiādimavoca.

'Without a part' means without remainder. The section on the faculty as a foundation of mindfulness is easily understood because it is not abstruse. 'Of this one' (assa) means of the Blessed One. 'In the place' (ṭhāne) means in this or that opportunity designated for reviewing. Those various dwelling-attainments: having proceeded by way of the aggregates, sense bases, etc., and having grasped only feeling which is a part of them, and having comprehended it, he sequentially attained the jhāna-attainment and the fruition-attainment. For the fruition attainment, though in essence undivided, is said to be as if divided because of the difference in the phenomena that are its basis of resolve, since in the commentaries twenty-four trillion different daily 'attainments for use' are mentioned. Indeed, they are born also by way of other phenomena, but here the focus is on feeling; they are born through the power of feeling. Why are they born thus? Because of the internal division of the domain of the Buddhas’ knowledge. For the Blessed One conducted his comprehension for a full half-month by way of feeling alone, and in accordance with that he attained those dwelling-attainments. The Teacher, explaining this wonderful and unique quality to the bhikkhus, said, 'That by which I...,' etc.

Akusalāvāti pāṇātipāta-adinnādāna-kāmesumicchācāra-musāvāda-pisuṇavācāsamphappalāpa-abhijjhā-byāpādavasena taṃtaṃmicchādassanavasena ca akusalā vedanā eva hoti. Brahmalokādīsu uppajjitvā tattha niccā dhuvā bhavissāmāti evaṃ diṭṭhiṃ upanissāyāti yojetabbaṃ. Devakulādīsu devapūjatthaṃ, sabbajanaparibhogatthaṃ vā mālāvacchaṃ ropenti. Vadhabandhanādīnīti ādi-saddena adinnādāna-micchācāra-musāvāda-pisuṇavācā-samphappalāpādīnaṃ saṅgaho daṭṭhabbo. Diṭṭhadhammavipākassa apacurattā apākaṭattā ca “bhavantaragatāna”nti vuttaṃ.

'Unwholesome': by way of killing living beings, taking what is not given, sexual misconduct, false speech, divisive speech, idle chatter, covetousness, and ill will, and by way of the respective wrong views, it is just unwholesome feeling that occurs. It should be connected thus: 'relying on the view that, "Having been reborn in the Brahma-worlds and so on, we will be permanent and stable there."' In places such as shrines to deities, they plant flower gardens for the purpose of worshipping the deities or for the enjoyment of all people. 'Killing, imprisonment, etc.': by the word 'etc.' the inclusion of taking what is not given, sexual misconduct, false speech, divisive speech, idle chatter, etc., should be seen. Because the result in this very life is not abundant and not manifest, it is said, 'for those who have gone to another existence.'

Iti nesanti ettha iti-saddo ādiattho, pakārattho vā. Tena yathā pharusavācāvasena, evaṃ tadaññesampi akusalakammānaṃ vasena sammādiṭṭhipaccayā [M 2.0405] akusalavedanāppavatti yathārahaṃ nīharitvā vattabbā. Eseva nayoti iminā yathā micchādiṭṭhipaccayā sammādiṭṭhipaccayā ca kusalākusalavipākavedanā sahajātakoṭiyā upanissayakoṭiyā ca vasena yathārahaṃ yojetvā dassitā, evaṃ micchāsaṅkappapaccayādīsupi yathārahaṃ yojetvā dassetabbāti imamatthaṃ atidisati. Chandapaccayāti ettha taṇhāchandasahito kattukāmatāchando adhippetoti āha “chandapaccayātiādīsu pana chandapaccayā aṭṭhalobhasahagatacittasampayuttā vedanā veditabbā”ti. Vitakkapaccayāti ettha appanāppattova vitakko adhippetoti vuttaṃ “vitakkapaccayā paṭhamajjhānavedanāvā”ti. Vitakkapaccayā paṭhamajjhānavedanāya gahitattā “ṭhapetvā paṭhamajjhāna”nti. Upari tisso rūpāvacarā, heṭṭhā tisso arūpāvacarā evaṃ sesā cha saññāsamāpattivedanā.

Herein, in iti nesanti, the word iti has the sense of 'beginning' or 'kind.' Therefore, just as in the case of harsh speech, so too in the case of other unwholesome kammas, the occurrence of unwholesome feeling conditioned by right view should be explained by deducing it as appropriate. By 'This is the method,' he extends this meaning: just as wholesome and unwholesome resultant feelings conditioned by wrong view and right view have been shown by connecting them as appropriate by way of the conascence category and the decisive support category, so too they should be shown by connecting them as appropriate in the case of wrong intention, etc., as conditions. Regarding 'conditioned by desire,' he says that here the desire to act accompanied by the desire of craving is intended: 'In such cases as "conditioned by desire," the feeling associated with the eight types of consciousness accompanied by greed should be understood.' Regarding 'conditioned by thought,' it is said that here thought that has reached absorption is intended: 'Conditioned by thought is the feeling of the first jhāna.' Since the feeling of the first jhāna is taken as 'conditioned by thought,' it is said, 'excluding the first jhāna.' The three higher ones are of the form sphere, the three lower ones are of the formless sphere; thus the remaining six are feelings of the attainments of perception.

Tiṇṇanti [V 2.0114] chandavitakkasaññānaṃ. Avūpasameti paṭipakkhena avūpasamite. Tiṇṇañhi tesaṃ sahabhāvena paccayatā aṭṭhalobhasahagatacittesu eva. Tattha yaṃ vattabbaṃ taṃ vuttameva. Chandamattassāti tesu tīsu chandamattassa. Vūpasame paṭhamajjhānavedanāva appanāppattassa adhippetattā. Chandavitakkānaṃ vūpasame dutiyajjhānādivedanā adhippetā saññāya avūpasantattā. Dutiyajjhānādivedanāgahaṇena hi sabbā saññāsamāpattiyo ca gahitāva honti. Tiṇṇampi vūpasameti chandavitakkasaññānaṃ vūpasame nevasaññānāsaññāyatanavedanā adhippetā. Bhavaggappattasaññā hi vūpasamanti chandasaṅkappānaṃ accantasukhumabhāvappattiyā. Heṭṭhā “sammādiṭṭhipaccayā”ti ettha sammādiṭṭhiggahaṇena heṭṭhimamaggasammādiṭṭhipi gahitāva hotīti āha – “appattassa pattiyāti arahattaphalassa pattatthāyā”ti. Atha vā heṭṭhimamaggādhigamena vinā aggamaggo natthīti heṭṭhimamaggādhigamaṃ atthāpannaṃ katvā “arahattaphalassa pattatthāyā”ti vuttaṃ. Āyameti phalena missito hoti etenāti āyāmo, sammāvāyāmoti āha “atthi āyāmanti atthi vīriya”nti. Tassa vīriyārambhassāti aññādhigamakāraṇassa sammāvāyāmassa vasena. Pāḷiyaṃ ṭhāna-saddo kāraṇapariyāyoti āha – “arahattaphalassa kāraṇe”ti. Tappaccayāti ettha taṃ-saddena “ṭhāne”ti vuttakāraṇameva paccāmaṭṭhanti āha – “arahattassa ṭhānapaccayā”ti. Catumaggasahajātāti etena “arahattaphalassa pattatthāyā”ti [M 2.0406] ettha heṭṭhimamaggānaṃ atthāpattivasena gahitabhāvameva joteti. Keci pana “catumaggasahajātāti vatvā nibbattitalokuttaravedanāti bhūtakathanaṃ visesanaṃ. Nibbattitalokuttaravedanāti paṭhamaṃ apekkhitabbaṃ, pacchā catumaggasahajātā”ti vadanti.

Of the three: desire, thought, and perception. Not subsided means not subsided by its antidote. For their conditionality by way of co-nascence is only in the eight types of consciousness accompanied by greed. What should be said there has already been said. Of desire alone means of desire alone among those three. In the case of subsidence, the feeling of the first jhāna itself is intended, because it has reached absorption. In the case of the subsidence of desire and thought, the feelings from the second jhāna onwards are intended, because perception has not subsided. For by the inclusion of the feelings from the second jhāna onwards, all the attainments of perception are also included. The subsidence of all three means that with the subsidence of desire, thought, and perception, the feeling of the base of neither-perception-nor-non-perception is intended. For perception that has reached the peak of existence subsides because desire and intention have attained an extremely subtle state. Below, in 'conditioned by right view,' he says that by the term 'right view' the right view of the lower paths is also included: '“For the attainment of the unattained” means for the purpose of attaining the fruit of arahantship.' Or, since the highest path does not exist without the realization of the lower paths, the realization of the lower paths is implied, and thus it is said, 'for the purpose of attaining the fruit of arahantship.' Āyāma means that by which one is endowed with the fruit; it is right effort. He says: '“There is exertion” means there is energy.' Of that undertaking of energy means by way of the right effort that is the cause for the realization of final knowledge. He says that in the Pāli the word ṭhāna is a synonym for cause: 'as the cause for the fruit of arahantship.' He says that here, in tappaccayā, the word taṃ refers back to the cause spoken of as ṭhāna: 'conditioned by the basis for arahantship.' By conascent with the four paths, he illuminates the fact that in the phrase 'for the purpose of attaining the fruit of arahantship,' the lower paths are included by way of implication. Some, however, say: 'Having said “conascent with the four paths,” the phrase “the produced supramundane feeling” is a factual statement and a qualifier. “The produced supramundane feeling” should be considered first, and then “conascent with the four paths.”'

Paṭhamavihārasuttavaṇṇanā niṭṭhitā.

The commentary on the First Sutta on Dwellings is finished.

2. Dutiyavihārasuttavaṇṇanā

2. Commentary on the Second Sutta on Dwellings

12. Micchādiṭṭhi vūpasamati sabbaso pahīyati etenāti micchādiṭṭhivūpasamo. “Micchādiṭṭhivūpasamo nāma sammādiṭṭhi. Bhavantare uppajjanto atidūreti maññamāno vipākavedanaṃ na gaṇhātī”ti aṭṭhakathāyaṃ vuttaṃ. “Iminā nayenā”ti atidisitvāpi tamatthaṃ pākaṭataraṃ kātuṃ “yassa yassā”tiādiṃ vatvā eva sāmaññavasena vuttamatthaṃ pacchimesu tīsu padesu sarūpatova dassetuṃ “chandavūpasamapaccayā”tiādimāha, taṃ suviññeyyameva. Vuttatthāneva anantarasutte.

12. Wrong view subsides, is entirely abandoned by this; thus it is the subsidence of wrong view. It is said in the commentary: 'The subsidence of wrong view is right view. Thinking, “What arises in another existence is too far away,” one does not grasp the resultant feeling.' Although he has extended the meaning by 'by this method,' in order to make that meaning clearer, having said 'for whomever,' etc., and in order to show the meaning stated in general terms in its own form in the last three sections, he said 'conditioned by the subsidence of desire,' etc. That is easily understood. The meaning is the same in the immediately following sutta.

Dutiyavihārasuttavaṇṇanā niṭṭhitā.

The commentary on the Second Sutta on Dwellings is finished.

3-7. Sekkhasuttādivaṇṇanā

3-7. Commentary on the Discourses Beginning with the Trainee

13-17. Tissannampi [V 2.0115] sikkhānaṃ sikkhanaṃ sīlaṃ etassāti sikkhanasīlo. Sikkhatītipi vā sekkho. Vuttañhetaṃ “sikkhatīti kho, bhikkhave, tasmā sekkhoti vuccati. Kiñca sikkhati? Adhisīlampi sikkhatī”tiādi (a. ni. 3.86). Tīhi phalehi heṭṭhā. Sāpi catutthamaggena saddhiṃ uppannasikkhāpi. Maggakkhaṇe hi sikkhākiccaṃ na niṭṭhitaṃ vippakatabhāvato, phalakkhaṇe pana niṭṭhitaṃ nāma. Uttānatthāneva heṭṭhā vuttanayattā.

13-17. Training in the three trainings is his virtue, thus he is one whose virtue is training. Or, because he trains, he is a trainee. For it has been said: 'Because he trains, monks, therefore he is called a trainee. And what does he train in? He trains in higher virtue,' and so on (Aṅguttara Nikāya 3.86). This refers to those of the lower three fruits. That training also arises together with the fourth path. For at the moment of the path the task of training is not finished because it is incomplete, but at the moment of fruition it is indeed finished. The meaning is clear because of the method stated above.

Sekkhasuttādivaṇṇanā niṭṭhitā.

The Commentary on the Discourses Beginning with the Trainee is finished.

Vihāravaggavaṇṇanā niṭṭhitā.

The Commentary on the Chapter on Dwellings is finished.

3. Micchattavaggavaṇṇanā

3. Commentary on the Chapter on Wrongness

21-30. Micchāsabhāvanti [M 2.0407] ayāthāvasabhāvaṃ aniyyānikasabhāvaṃ. Sammāsabhāvanti yāthāvasabhāvaṃ niyyānikasabhāvaṃ. Micchāpaṭipattādhikaraṇahetūti ettha adhi-saddo anatthakoti āha – “micchāpaṭipattikaraṇahetū”ti. Ñāyati paṭividdhavasena nibbānaṃ gacchatīti ñāyo. So eva taṃsamaṅgīnaṃ vaṭṭadukkhapātato dhāraṇaṭṭhena dhammoti āha – “ñāyaṃ dhammanti ariyamaggadhamma”nti. Ñāṇassa micchāsabhāvo nāma natthīti viññāṇamevettha paccavekkhaṇavasena pavattaṃ ñāṇa-saddena vuccatīti āha “micchāviññāṇo”ti. Micchāpaccavekkhaṇoti kiñci pāpaṃ katvā “aho mayā kataṃ sukata”nti evaṃ pavatto micchāpaccavekkhaṇo. Gosīlagovatādipūraṇaṃ muttīti evaṃ gaṇhato micchāvimutti nāma. Micchāpaṭipadādīhi vivaṭṭanti evaṃ vaṭṭavivaṭṭaṃ kathitaṃ. Puggalo pucchitoti nigamito ca “ayaṃ vuccati, bhikkhave, asappuriso”tiādinā. Kiñcāpi “micchādiṭṭhiko hotī”tiādinā puggalova niddiṭṭho, tathāpi puggalasīsenāyaṃ dhammadesanāti āha “dhammo vibhatto”ti. Tenevāha “dhammena puggalo dassito”ti. Dhammenāti micchādiṭṭhiādikena dhammena. Kalyāṇaputhujjanato paṭṭhāya sabbaso sappurisā nāma, khīṇāsavo sappurisataro. Suppavattaniyoti sukhena pavattetuṃ sakkuṇeyyo. Dhāvatīti gacchati. Paccayuppannena upecca nissitabbato upanisā, paccayo, ekassa sa-kārassa lopaṃ katvā vāti āha – “saupanisaṃ sapaccaya”nti. Parikaraṇato parikkhāro, parivāroti [V 2.0116] āha – “saparikkhāraṃ saparivāra”nti. Sahajātavasena upanissayavasena ca sapaccayatā kiccasādhane nipphādane sahāyabhāvūpagamane ca saparivāratā daṭṭhabbā.

21-30. ‘Wrong nature’ means an unreal nature, a nature that does not lead out. ‘Right nature’ means a real nature, a nature that leads out. In `micchāpaṭipattādhikaraṇahetu`, the prefix `adhi-` is without meaning, hence it is said: `micchāpaṭipattikaraṇahetu`. ‘Method’ (ñāya) is so called because one goes to Nibbāna by way of penetration. That very thing, because it sustains those endowed with it from falling into the suffering of the round, is called Dhamma; therefore he said: ‘the method, the Dhamma’ means ‘the Dhamma of the noble path.’ There is no such thing as a wrong nature of knowledge; here, it is just consciousness that arises by way of reviewing that is called by the word ‘knowledge,’ hence he said ‘wrong consciousness.’ ‘Wrong reviewing’ is when, after doing some evil deed, one thinks, ‘Ah, what I have done is well done’—such is wrong reviewing. ‘Wrong liberation’ is so called for one who holds that liberation is the fulfillment of such things as the cow-practice and the cow-vow. ‘They turn away from wrong practice,’ and so on—thus the round and the turning away from the round have been spoken of. The person was asked about and concluded with: ‘This, monks, is called an untrue person,’ and so on. Although it is a person who is indicated by ‘he is one of wrong view,’ etc., still, since this Dhamma teaching has a person as its heading, it is said: ‘the Dhamma is analyzed.’ Therefore he said: ‘the person is shown by means of the Dhamma.’ ‘By means of the Dhamma’ means by the Dhamma beginning with wrong view. Beginning with the virtuous worldling, all are called true persons; the one whose taints are destroyed is a truer person. `Suppavattaniyo` means ‘it can be easily set in motion.’ `Dhāvati` means ‘goes.’ Because it is produced by a condition and is to be approached and relied upon, it is a supporting condition (upanisā), a condition (paccayo); or, after eliding one letter ‘sa,’ he said: ‘with supporting condition, with condition.’ Because it is an accessory, it is a requisite, a retinue; therefore he said: ‘with requisites, with retinue.’ The state of being with condition should be seen in terms of co-nascence and decisive support; the state of being with retinue should be seen in terms of assisting in the accomplishment of a task, in its completion, and in acting as a companion.

Micchattavaggavaṇṇanā niṭṭhitā.

The Commentary on the Chapter on Wrongness is finished.

4. Paṭipattivaggavaṇṇanā

4. Commentary on the Chapter on Practice

31-40. Ayāthāvapaṭipatti, na yathāpaṭipatti, hetumhipi phalepi ayāthāvavatthusādhanato. Ekaṃ suttaṃ dhammavasena kathitaṃ paṭipattivasena. Ekaṃ suttaṃ puggalavasena kathitaṃ paṭipannakavasena. Saṃsāramahoghassa paratīrabhāvato yo naṃ adhigacchati, taṃ pāreti gametīti pāraṃ, nibbānaṃ, tabbidhuratāya [M 2.0408] natthi ettha pāranti apāraṃ, saṃsāroti vuttaṃ – “apārāpāranti vaṭṭato nibbāna”nti. Pāraṅgatāti asekkhe sandhāya. Yepi gacchantīti sekkhe. Yepi gamissantīti kalyāṇaputhujjane. Pāragāminoti ettha kita-saddo tikālavācīti evaṃ vuttaṃ.

31-40. Improper practice, not proper practice, because of accomplishing an unreal object in regard to both cause and effect. One discourse was spoken in terms of the Dhamma, in terms of practice. One discourse was spoken in terms of individuals, in terms of those who have entered the practice. Because the great flood of saṃsāra has a further shore, one who attains it, it makes him cross over, makes him go; thus it is called the far shore (pāraṃ), Nibbāna. Because of the absence of that, there is no far shore here, thus the near shore (apāraṃ), saṃsāra, is spoken of—‘from the near shore to the far shore’ means ‘from the round to Nibbāna.’ ‘Gone to the far shore’ refers to the non-trainees. ‘Those who are going’ refers to the trainees. ‘Those who will go’ refers to virtuous worldlings. In ‘one going to the far shore’ (pāragāmī), here the kṛt-suffix indicates the three times; thus it is said.

Tīranti orimatīramāha. Tena vuttaṃ “vaṭṭameva anudhāvatī”ti. Ekantakāḷakattā cittassa apabhassarabhāvakaraṇato kaṇhābhijātihetuto ca vuttaṃ “kaṇhanti akusaladhamma”nti. Vodānabhāvato cittassa pabhassarabhāvakaraṇato sukkābhijātihetuto ca vuttaṃ – “sukkanti kusaladhamma”nti. Kilesamāra-abhisaṅkhāramāra-maccumārānaṃ pavattiṭṭhānatāya okaṃ vuccati vaṭṭaṃ, tabbidhuratāya anokanti nibbānanti āha – “okā anokanti vaṭṭato nibbāna”nti.

‘Shore’ refers to the near shore. Therefore it is said: ‘He runs along just the round.’ Because it is entirely black, because it makes the mind non-luminous, and because it is a cause for a black rebirth, it is said: ‘black’ means ‘unwholesome states.’ Because it is pure, because it makes the mind luminous, and because it is a cause for a bright rebirth, it is said: ‘bright’ means ‘wholesome states.’ Because it is the ground for the occurrence of the Māra of defilements, the Māra of formations, and the Māra of death, the round is called a ‘resort’ (okaṃ). Because of the absence of that, Nibbāna is the ‘non-resort’ (anokaṃ). Thus it is said: ‘from resort to non-resort’ means ‘from the round to Nibbāna.’

Paramatthato samaṇā vuccanti ariyā, samaṇānaṃ bhāvo sāmaññaṃ, ariyamaggo, tena araṇīyato upagantabbato sāmaññattho nibbānanti āha – “sāmaññatthanti nibbānaṃ, taṃ hī”tiādi. Brahmaññatthanti etthāpi iminā nayena attho veditabbo. Brahmaññena ariyamaggena. Rāgakkhayoti ettha iti-saddo ādisaddattho. Tena “dosakkhayo mohakkhayo”ti padadvayaṃ saṅgaṇhāti. Vaṭṭatiyevāti vadanti “rāgakkhayo”ti. Pariyāyena hi arahattassa vattabbattāti.

In the ultimate sense, the noble ones are called recluses; the state of a recluse is recluseship, the noble path. Because it is to be approached as being without conflict (araṇa), the goal of recluseship is Nibbāna—thus it is said, ‘the goal of recluseship is Nibbāna, for that…,’ and so on. In ‘the goal of the holy life,’ here too the meaning should be understood in this way: by the holy life is meant the noble path. In ‘the destruction of lust,’ here the word `iti` has the sense of ‘and so on.’ Thus, it includes the two phrases ‘the destruction of hatred’ and ‘the destruction of delusion.’ They say just ‘the destruction of lust’ because it is a way of speaking about arahantship.

Paṭipattivaggavaṇṇanā niṭṭhitā.

The Commentary on the Chapter on Practice is finished.

5. Aññatitthiyapeyyālavaggavaṇṇanā

5. Commentary on the Chapter of Repetition Series on Other Sects

41-48. Aparāparaṃ [V 2.0117] parivattamānena vattasampannena saṃsāraddhānapariññāvaseneva nibbānassa pattabbattā vuttaṃ – “nibbānaṃ patvā pariññātaṃ nāma hotī”ti. Nibbānaṃ patvāti nibbānappattihetu. Hetuattho hi ayaṃ tvā-saddo yathā – “ghataṃ pivitvā balaṃ hoti, sīhaṃ disvā bhayaṃ hotī”ti. Tasmāti yasmā apariññeyyaparijānanakiccena nibbānassa pattiyā addhānapariññāsiddhi ñāyati, tasmā upacāravasena nibbānaṃ “addhānapariññā”ti vuccati yathā “himasanti sūriyaṃ uggametī”ti. Vijjāvimuttiphalasacchikiriyatthanti ettha vijjāti aggamaggavijjā. Vimuttīti aggamaggasamādhi adhippeto[M 2.0409]. Tesaṃ phalaṃ aññāti āha – “vijjāvimuttiphalena arahattaṃ kathita”nti. Yāthāvato jānanato paccakkhato dassanato ca ñāṇadassananti idha phalanibbānapaccavekkhaṇā adhippetāti āha – “ñāṇadassanena paccavekkhaṇā kathitā”ti. Sesehīti rāga-virāga-saṃyojanappahāna-anusayasamugghāta-addhānapariññā- āsavakkhaya-vijjā-vimutti-phalasacchikiriyā-ñāṇadassana-anupādāparinibbānapadehi.

41-48. Because Nibbāna is to be attained by one who, revolving again and again, is endowed with the practice, and by means of the full understanding of the journey of saṃsāra, it is said: 'Having attained Nibbāna, it is called fully understood.' Here, 'having attained Nibbāna' indicates the cause for the attainment of Nibbāna. For this suffix '-tvā' has the meaning of cause, as in: 'Having drunk ghee, one becomes strong; having seen a lion, fear arises.' Therefore, since the accomplishment of the full understanding of the journey is known through the attainment of Nibbāna by the task of fully understanding what is to be fully understood, Nibbāna is figuratively called 'the full understanding of the journey,' just as it is said: 'The rising sun dispels the frost.' Regarding the phrase 'for the purpose of realizing the fruit of knowledge and liberation': here 'knowledge' is the knowledge of the supreme path, and 'liberation' is intended as the concentration of the supreme path. Their fruit is something else, so it is said: 'Arahantship is spoken of as the fruit of knowledge and liberation.' Regarding 'knowledge and vision': since it is knowing things as they really are and seeing them directly, here the reviewing of the fruit and Nibbāna is intended; thus it is said: 'Reviewing is spoken of as knowledge and vision.' By the remaining terms: dispassion, abandoning of fetters, uprooting of latent tendencies, full understanding of the journey, destruction of the taints, knowledge, liberation, realization of the fruit, knowledge and vision, and final Nibbāna without clinging.

Aññatitthiyapeyyālavaggavaṇṇanā niṭṭhitā.

The Commentary on the Chapter of Repetition Series on Other Sects is finished.

6. Sūriyapeyyālavaggavaṇṇanā

6. Commentary on the Chapter of the Sun Repetition Series

49-62. Yathā aruṇuggaṃ sūriyuggamanassa ekantikaṃ pubbanimittaṃ, evaṃ kalyāṇamittatā ariyamaggapātubhāvassāti sadisūpamā aruṇuggaṃ kalyāṇamittatāya. Kalyāṇamittoti cettha ariyo, ariyamaggo vā daṭṭhabbo sūriyapātubhāvo viya tena vidhūpanīyandhakāravidhamanato. Kusalakattukamyatāchando chandasampadā itarachandato sampannattā. Kārāpakaappamādassāti saccapaṭivedhassa kārāpakassa. Evaṃ sabbattheva sampadāsaddā visesādhigamahetutāya veditabbā. Aññenapi ākārenāti “vivekanissita”ntiādiākārato aññena “rāgavinayapariyosāna”ntiādinā ākārena.

49-62. Just as the rising of the dawn is the certain forerunner of the rising of the sun, so noble friendship is the certain forerunner of the arising of the noble path. Thus the simile of the dawn is appropriate for noble friendship. Here, a 'noble friend' should be understood as a noble person, or as the noble path itself, like the appearance of the sun, because it dispels the darkness that is to be dispelled. The desire consisting in the wish to do what is skillful is the 'excellence of will' because it is more accomplished than other kinds of will. As to 'heedfulness that causes': this refers to one who causes the penetration of the truths. Thus in all cases the word 'excellence' should be understood as a cause for the attainment of a special quality. The phrase 'by another mode' means by a mode different from the one beginning 'dependent on seclusion,' that is, by the mode beginning 'culminating in the removal of lust.'

Sūriyapeyyālavaggavaṇṇanā niṭṭhitā.

The Commentary on the Chapter of the Sun Repetition Series is finished.

7. Ekadhammapeyyālavaggādivaṇṇanā

7. Commentary on the Chapter of the Single-Dhamma Repetition Series, etc.

63-138. Tathā [V 2.0118] tathā vutte bujjhanakānaṃ ajjhāsayavasena kathito, tasmā “vutto eva attho, kasmā puna vutto”ti na codetabbaṃ.

63-138. When it is spoken in this way and that, it is taught in accordance with the disposition of those who are to be awakened. Therefore, one should not raise the objection: 'The meaning has already been stated; why is it stated again?'

Ekadhammapeyyālavaggādivaṇṇanā niṭṭhitā.

The Commentary on the Chapter of the Single-Dhamma Repetition Series, etc., is finished.

8. Appamādapeyyālavaggo

8. The Chapter of the Heedfulness Repetition Series

1. Tathāgatasuttavaṇṇanā

1. Commentary on the Discourse on the Tathāgata

139. Kārāpakaappamādo [M 2.0410] nāma “ime akusalā dhammā pahātabbā, ime kusalā dhammā uppādetabbā”ti vuttavajjetabbavajjanasampādetabbasampādanavasena pavatto appamādo. Esāti appamādo. Lokiyova. Na lokuttaro. Ayanti esāti ca appamādameva vadati. Tesanti catubhūmakadhammānaṃ. Paṭilābhakaṭṭhenāti paṭilābhāpanaṭṭhena.

139. Heedfulness that causes (kārāpaka-appamāda) is the name for the heedfulness that proceeds by way of avoiding what should be avoided and accomplishing what should be accomplished, as stated: 'These unwholesome states are to be abandoned; these wholesome states are to be aroused.' This is heedfulness. It is only mundane, not supramundane. Both 'this' and 'that' refer to heedfulness itself. 'Of them' means of the phenomena of the four planes. 'In the sense of acquisition' means in the sense of causing to acquire.

Tathāgatasuttavaṇṇanā niṭṭhitā.

The commentary on the Discourse on the Tathāgata is finished.

2. Padasuttavaṇṇanā

2. Commentary on the Discourse on the Footprint

140. Jaṅgalānanti jaṅgalavāsīnaṃ. Jaṅgala-saddo cettha thaddhabhāvasāmaññena pathavīpariyāyo, na anupaṭṭhānavidūradesavācī. Tenāha – “pathavītalavāsīna”nti. Padānaṃ vuccamānattā “sapādakapāṇāna”nti visesetvā vuttaṃ. Samodhānanti antogadhabhāvaṃ. Tenāha – “odhānaṃ upakkhepa”nti, upanetvā pakkhipitabbanti attho.

140. 'Of jungle creatures' means of jungle dwellers. Here the word 'jungle' is a synonym for the earth because of the common characteristic of solidity; it is not a term for a remote and inaccessible place. Therefore it is said: 'of dwellers on the earth's surface.' Because footprints are being spoken of, it is specified as 'of creatures that have feet.' 'Inclusion' means the state of being contained within. Therefore it is said: 'inclusion is a placing into,' the meaning of which is 'placing into after bringing near.'

Padasuttavaṇṇanā niṭṭhitā.

The commentary on the Discourse on the Footprint is finished.

3-10. Kūṭasuttādivaṇṇanā

3-10. Commentary on the Discourse on the Peak, etc.

141-148. Vassikāya pupphaṃ vassikaṃ yathā “āmalakiyā phalaṃ āmalaka”nti. Mahātalasminti [V 2.0119] uparipāsāde. “Yāni kānicī”ti padehi itarāni samānādhikaraṇāni bhavituṃ yuttānīti “paccatte sāmivacana”nti vatvā tathā vibhattivipariṇāmo kato. “Tantāvutāna”nti padaṃ niddhāraṇe sāmivacananti tattha “vatthānī”ti vacanasesena atthaṃ dassetuṃ “atha vā”tiādi vuttaṃ.

141-148. 'Vassika' is the flower of the vassikā plant, just as 'āmalaka' is the fruit of the āmalakī tree. 'On the great floor' means on the upper story of the palace. Because it is proper for the other words to be in apposition with the words 'whatever soever,' having said, 'the genitive is used in the sense of the subject itself,' the case ending has been changed accordingly. The word 'of those woven with thread' is a genitive of specification; to show the meaning there by supplying the word 'cloths,' the passage beginning 'or else' has been stated.

Kūṭasuttādivaṇṇanā niṭṭhitā.

The commentary on the Discourse on the Peak, etc., is finished.

Appamādavaggavaṇṇanā niṭṭhitā.

The commentary on the Chapter on Heedfulness is finished.

9. Balakaraṇīyavaggo

9. The Chapter on Deeds Requiring Strength

1. Balasuttavaṇṇanā

1. Commentary on the Sutta on Strength

149. Kammāniyeva [M 2.0411] kammantā yathā suttantā. Ariyaṃ aṭṭhaṅgikaṃ magganti ettha nānantariyakatāya vipassanāpi gahitā eva hotīti vuttaṃ “sahavipassana”nti.

149. ‘Deeds’ (`kammantā`) are simply actions (`kammāni`), just as `suttantā` are suttas. In the phrase ‘the noble eightfold path,’ insight is also included here on account of its inseparability; thus it is said to be ‘with insight.’

2. Bījasuttavaṇṇanā

2. Commentary on the Sutta on Seeds

150. Pañcavidhampi samūhaṭṭhena bījagāmo nāma. Tadevāti mūlabījādi eva. Sampannanti sahajātamūlavantaṃ. Nīlabhāvato paṭṭhāyāti nīlabhāvāpattito paṭṭhāya.

150. The five kinds, in the sense of a collection, are called ‘plant life.’ ‘That very thing’ refers to root-seeds and so on. ‘Complete’ means possessing its natural root. ‘From becoming blue’ means from the time it has become blue.

3. Nāgasuttavaṇṇanā

3. Commentary on the Nāga Sutta

151. Balaṃ gāhentīti attano sarīrabalaṃ gāhenti. Taṃ pana nāgānaṃ balappatti evāti āha – “balaṃ gaṇhantī”ti. Sambhejjamukhadvāranti mahāsamuddena sambhedagatamahānadīnaṃ mukhadvāraṃ. Nāgā kāyaṃ vaḍḍhentītiādi yasmā ca bhagavatā upamāvasena ābhataṃ, tasmā evameva khoti etthātiādinā upamaṃ saṃsandati. Āgatesūtiādīsu tīsu padesu bhāvenabhāvalakkhaṇe.

151. ‘They take on strength’ means they take on their own bodily strength. But since this is the attainment of strength by the nāgas (elephants), he says, ‘they acquire strength.’ ‘The mouth of confluence’ is the mouth of the great rivers that have reached confluence with the great ocean. Since the passage beginning ‘The nāgas make their bodies grow’ was introduced by the Blessed One as a simile, he connects the simile by saying, ‘so too here,’ etc. In the three phrases beginning with ‘when they have come,’ the locative absolute is used.

5. Kumbhasuttavaṇṇanā

5. Commentary on the Sutta on the Pot

153. Na [V 2.0120] patiāvamatīti ca nikujjitabhāvena udakavamano ghaṭo, na taṃ puna mukhena gaṇhāti. Tenāha “na anto pavesetī”ti.

153. ‘It does not re-ingest’: Just as a pot that has disgorged water because it was overturned does not take it in again through its mouth, so he says, ‘it does not let it enter inside.’

7. Ākāsasuttavaṇṇanā

7. Commentary on the Sutta on Space

155. Tenetaṃ vuttanti tena ariyamaggassa ijjhanena etesaṃ sabbesaṃ bodhipakkhiyadhammānaṃ ijjhanaṃ vuttaṃ.

155. ‘Thus this was said’ means: through the success of the noble path, the success of all these aids to enlightenment is declared.

8-9-10. Paṭhamameghasuttādivaṇṇanā

8-9-10. Commentary on the First Sutta on Clouds, etc.

156-158. Paṃsurajojallanti bhūmireṇusahajātamalaṃ. Vāṇijakopameti vāṇijakopamapaṭhamasutte cāpi.

156-158. ‘Dust, dirt, and grime’: this is the stain that naturally accompanies dust from the earth. ‘Like a merchant’: this is also in the first Sutta with the Simile of the Merchant.

11-12. Āgantukasuttādivaṇṇanā

11-12. Commentary on the Sutta on the Newcomer, etc.

159-160. Sahavipassanassa [M 2.0412] ariyamaggassa bhāvanāya ijjhanena etaṃ abhiññāpariññeyyādidhammānaṃ abhiññāparijānanādīnaṃ ijjhanaṃ vuttaṃ khattiyādīnaṃ visayaādikaṃ karontassa kathāya sajjitattā.

159-160. Through the successful development of the noble path accompanied by insight, the success in such things as directly knowing and fully understanding the phenomena to be directly known and fully understood is declared, this being stated in a discourse concerning the domain, etc., of khattiyas and others.

Balakaraṇīyavaggavaṇṇanā niṭṭhitā.

The commentary on the Chapter on Deeds Requiring Strength is finished.

10. Esanāvaggo

10. The Chapter on Searching

1. Esanāsuttavaṇṇanā

1. Commentary on the Sutta on Searching
