161. Kāmānanti vatthukāmakilesakāmānaṃ. Kilesakāmopi hi kāmitanti parikappitena vidhinā ca adhikarāgehi esanīyo. Bhavānanti tiṇṇaṃ gatīnaṃ. Diṭṭhigatikaparikappitassa [V 2.0121] brahmacariyassa nimittabhāvato micchādiṭṭhi “brahmacariya”nti adhippetā.

161. ‘Of sensual pleasures’: of sensual pleasures as objects and sensual pleasures as defilements. For desire as a defilement is to be sought for by way of what is imagined as desirable and through excessive greed. ‘Of existences’: of the three destinies. Because it is the characteristic of a holy life that is imagined to be a destiny based on a view, wrong view is intended by ‘the holy life.’

2-11. Vidhāsuttādivaṇṇanā

2-11. Commentary on the Sutta on Conceits, etc.

162-171. Seyyohamasmītiādinā taṃtaṃvibhāgena dhīyanti vidhīyantīti vidhā, mānakoṭṭhāsā, mānaṭṭhapanā vā. Nīhanantīti vibādhenti.

162-171. Because by such distinctions as ‘I am better,’ they are assessed and arranged, they are called `vidhā` (conceits); that is, portions of conceit or establishments of conceit. ‘They remove’ means they obstruct.

Esanāvaggavaṇṇanā niṭṭhitā.

The commentary on the Chapter on Searching is finished.

11. Oghavaggo

11. The Chapter on Floods

1-2. Oghasuttādivaṇṇanā

1-2. Commentary on the Sutta on Floods, etc.

172-173. Vaṭṭe ohananti osīdāpentīti oghā. Rūpārūpabhaveti rūpabhave ca arūpabhave ca rūpārūpataṇhopanissayā rūpārūpāvacarakammanibbattā khandhā. Yojanaṭṭhena yogo.

172-173. They are ‘floods’ because they drag down and cause beings to sink in the round of existence. ‘In form and formless existence’: in both the form existence and the formless existence, this refers to the aggregates that are produced by kamma pertaining to the form and formless spheres and are supported by craving for form and formless existence. A ‘yoke’ is so called in the sense of yoking.

3-4. Upādānasuttādivaṇṇanā

3-4. Commentary on the Sutta on Clinging, etc.

174-175. Kāmanavasena upādiyanato kāmupādānaṃ. Tenāha “kāmaggahaṇa”nti. Nāmakāyassāti vedanādīnaṃ catunnaṃ arūpakkhandhānaṃ. Ghaṭanapabandhanakilesoti [M 2.0413] hetunā phalassa kammavaṭṭassa vipākavaṭṭena dukkhappabandhasaññitassa ghaṭanassa sambajjhanassa nibbattakakileso. Antaggāhikadiṭṭhi sassatucchedagāho.

174-175. It is ‘clinging to sensual pleasures’ because one clings by way of sensual pleasure. Therefore he says, ‘the grasping of sensual pleasure.’ ‘Of the mentality-group’: of the four formless aggregates beginning with feeling. ‘The defilement that connects and binds’: the defilement that, as cause, produces the connection and binding of the round of kamma to the fruit, the round of results, which is known as the continuity of suffering. ‘A view that grasps at extremes’ is the grasping of eternalism and annihilationism.

Upādānasuttādivaṇṇanā niṭṭhitā.

The commentary on the Sutta on Clinging, etc., is finished.

5-10. Anusayasuttādivaṇṇanā

5-10. Commentary on the Sutta on Underlying Tendencies, etc.

176-181. Thāmagataṭṭhenāti sattasantāne thirabhāvūpagamanabhāvena. Thāmagatanti ca aññehi asādhāraṇo kāmarāgādīnaṃyeva āveṇiko sabhāvo daṭṭhabbo. Kāmarāgovāti kāmarāgo [V 2.0122] eva appahīno. So sati paccayalābhe uppajjanārahatāya santāne anusetīti anusayo. Sesesupīti paṭighānusayādīsu. Orambhāgo vuccati kāmadhātu rūpārūpabhāvato heṭṭhābhūtattā. Tattha pavattiyā paccayabhāvato orambhāgiyāni yathā “pacchiyo goduhako”ti. Saṃyojentīti saṃyojanāni, heṭṭhā viya attho vattabbo. Uddhambhāgo mahaggatabhāgo, tassa hitānīti sabbaṃ heṭṭhā vuttanayattā na vuttanti adhippāyo.

176-181. ‘In the sense of having become strong’ means by way of having reached a firm state in a being’s mental continuum. ‘Having become strong’ should be seen as the unique, intrinsic nature proper only to sensual desire and the like, not shared by others. ‘Sensual desire’ means sensual desire itself, when unabandoned. It lies latent in the mental continuum, being capable of arising when it obtains a condition; therefore it is called an ‘underlying tendency.’ ‘In the remaining cases too’ applies to the underlying tendency of aversion, etc. The ‘lower part’ is said to be the sense-sphere realm, because it is lower than the form and formless realms. Things that are conditions for occurrence there are called ‘pertaining to the lower part,’ just as one says ‘the hind milker.’ ‘They fetter,’ thus they are ‘fetters’; the meaning should be understood as explained below. The ‘upper part’ is the sublime part; the intention is that the phrase ‘pertaining to it’ is not stated because everything is in accordance with the method stated below.

Anusayasuttādivaṇṇanā niṭṭhitā.

The commentary on the Sutta on Underlying Tendencies, etc., is finished.

Oghavaggavaṇṇanā niṭṭhitā.

The commentary on the Chapter on Floods is finished.

Maggasaṃyuttavaṇṇanā niṭṭhitā.

The Commentary on the Connected Discourses on the Path is finished.

2. Bojjhaṅgasaṃyuttaṃ

2. Connected Discourses on the Factors of Enlightenment

1. Pabbatavaggo

1. The Chapter on the Mountain

1.Himavantasuttavaṇṇanā

1. Commentary on the Himavanta Sutta

182. Bujjhati [V 2.0123][M 2.0414] catusaccaṃ ariyasāvako etāyāti bodhaṃ, dhammasāmaggī, ariyasāvako pana catusaccaṃ bujjhatīti bodhi. Aṅgāti kāraṇā. Yāya dhammasāmaggiyāti sambandho. Taṇhāvasena patiṭṭhānaṃ, diṭṭhivasena āyūhanā. Sassatadiṭṭhiyā patiṭṭhānaṃ, ucchedadiṭṭhiyā āyūhanā. Līnavasena patiṭṭhānaṃ, uddhaccavasena āyūhanā. Kāmasukhānuyogavasena patiṭṭhānaṃ, attakilamathānuyogavasena āyūhanā. Oghataraṇasuttavaṇṇanāyaṃ (saṃ. ni. 1.1) –

182. By this the noble disciple awakens to the Four Truths, thus it is awakening, that is, the harmony of principles; or, because the noble disciple awakens to the Four Truths, it is enlightenment. 'Factors' means causes. The connection is with 'by which harmony of principles.' By way of craving there is establishment; by way of views there is accumulation. The eternalist view is the establishment; the annihilationist view is the accumulation. By way of sluggishness there is establishment; by way of restlessness there is accumulation. By way of indulgence in sensual pleasures there is establishment; by way of the practice of self-mortification there is accumulation. In the Commentary on the Oghataraṇa Sutta (SN 1.1):

“Kilesavasena patiṭṭhānaṃ, abhisaṅkhāravasena āyūhanā. Taṇhādiṭṭhīhi patiṭṭhānaṃ, avasesakilesābhisaṅkhārehi āyūhanā, sabbākusalābhisaṅkhāravasena patiṭṭhānaṃ, sabbalokiyakusalābhisaṅkhāravasena āyūhanā”ti –

“By way of defilements there is establishment; by way of volitional formations there is accumulation. By craving and views there is establishment; by the remaining defilements and volitional formations there is accumulation. By way of all unwholesome volitional formations there is establishment; by way of all worldly wholesome volitional formations there is accumulation.”

Vuttesu pakāresu idha avuttānaṃ vasena veditabbo. Kilesasantānaniddāya uṭṭhahatīti etena sikhāpattavipassanāsahagatānampi satiādīnaṃ bojjhaṅgabhāvaṃ dasseti. Cattārītiādinā maggaphalena sahagatānaṃ. Sattahi bojjhaṅgehi bhāvitehi saccapaṭivedho hotīti kathamidaṃ jānitabbanti codanaṃ sandhāyāha “yathāhā”tiādi. Jhānaṅgamaggaṅgādayo viyāti etena bodhibojjhaṅgasaddānaṃ samudāyāvayavavisayataṃ dasseti. Senaṅgarathaṅgādayo viyāti etena puggalapaññattiyā avijjamānapaññattibhāvaṃ dasseti.

What is unstated here should be understood by way of the modes that have been stated. 'It awakens from the sleep of the continuity of defilements'—by this he shows that even mindfulness and so on, when associated with insight that has reached its peak, can become enlightenment factors. By 'the four,' etc., he shows that they are associated with the path and its fruit. In reference to the challenge, 'How is it to be known that the penetration of the truths occurs when the seven enlightenment factors have been developed?' he said, 'as it is said,' etc. 'Like the factors of jhāna, the factors of the path, etc.'—by this he shows that the words 'bodhi' and 'bojjhaṅga' refer to the whole and the part. 'Like the constituents of an army, the parts of a chariot, etc.'—by this he shows that the designation of a person is a designation of what does not exist in reality.

Bodhāya saṃvattantīti bojjhaṅgāti vuttaṃ “kāraṇattho aṅgasaddo”ti. Bujjhatīti bodhi, bodhiyā eva aṅgāti bojjhaṅgāti vuttaṃ “bujjhantīti bojjhaṅgā”ti. Vipassanādīnaṃ kāraṇānaṃ bujjhitabbānaṃ saccānaṃ anurūpaṃ paccakkhabhāvena paṭimukhaṃ aviparītaṃ sammā bujjhantīti evaṃ vatthuvisesadīpakehi [V 2.0124] uparimaggehi anubujjhantītiādinā vuttabodhisaddehi nippadesena vuttaṃ [M 2.0415] “bujjhanatāsāmaññena saṅgaṇhātī”ti. Ettha ca līnapatiṭṭhāna-kāmasukhallikānuyoga-ucchedābhinivesānaṃ dhammavicaya-vīriyapītipadhāna-dhammasāmaggī paṭipakkho. Uddhaccāyūhanaattakilamathānuyoga-sassatābhinivesānaṃ passaddhisamādhi-upekkhāpadhāna-dhammasāmaggī paṭipakkho. Sati pana ubhayatthāpi icchitabbā. Tathā hi sā sabbatthikā vuttā.

'They conduce to enlightenment, thus they are enlightenment factors'—this is said with the meaning that the word 'factor' means 'cause.' 'One awakens, thus it is enlightenment; they are factors of enlightenment itself, thus they are enlightenment factors'—this is said with the meaning 'they are enlightenment factors because they awaken.' 'Because of causes such as insight, they awaken to the truths to be awakened to in a suitable, direct, face-to-face, undistorted, and right way; thus by the higher paths, which illuminate the specific object, they subsequently awaken'—by such terms for awakening stated without remainder, it is said, 'it includes them by the common characteristic of awakening.' Here, the harmony of principles predominated by investigation-of-dharmas, energy, and rapture is the opponent of establishment in sluggishness, indulgence in sensual pleasure, and adherence to annihilationism. The harmony of principles predominated by tranquility, concentration, and equanimity is the opponent of the accumulation of restlessness, indulgence in self-mortification, and adherence to eternalism. But mindfulness is desirable in both cases. For it has been called 'useful in all situations.'

Saṃ-saddo pasaṃsāyaṃ. Punadeva sundaro ca atthopīti āha “pasattho sundaro ca bojjhaṅgo”ti. Abhinibbattetīti abhivisiṭṭhabhāvena nibbatteti savisesabhāvaṃ vadati. “Eke vaṇṇayantī”ti vatvā tattha yathāvuttavivekattayato aññaṃ vivekadvayaṃ uddharitvā dassetuṃ “te hī”tiādi vuttaṃ. Tattha jhānakkhaṇe tāva kiccato vikkhambhanavivekanissitaṃ, vipassanākkhaṇe ajjhāsayato paṭippassaddhivivekanissitaṃ bhāvetīti. Tenāha – “anuttaraṃ vimokkhaṃ upasampajja viharissāmī”ti. Tattha tattha nicchayatāya kasiṇajjhānaggahaṇena anuppādānampi gahaṇaṃ daṭṭhabbaṃ.

The prefix saṃ- is in the sense of praise. Again, it is also beautiful and meaningful, thus he says, 'a praised and beautiful enlightenment factor.' 'It produces' means it produces in a pre-eminent way; it expresses a special quality. Having said, 'Some explain,' in order to extract and show another pair of seclusions apart from the threefold seclusion already mentioned, it is said, 'For they…' etc. There, at the moment of jhāna, one develops what is based on the seclusion of suppression by way of function; at the moment of insight, one develops what is based on the seclusion of calming by way of disposition. Therefore he said: 'Having attained the unsurpassed liberation, I will dwell.' Here and there, because of its certainty, by the taking up of the kasiṇa absorption the inclusion of non-arisings should also be understood.

Himavantasuttavaṇṇanā niṭṭhitā.

The Commentary on the Himavanta Sutta is concluded.

2. Kāyasuttavaṇṇanā

2. Commentary on the Kāya Sutta

183. Tiṭṭhanti etenāti ṭhiti, kāraṇaṃ. Kammautucittāhārasaññito catubbidho paccayo ṭhiti etassāti paccayaṭṭhitiko. Āhārapaccayasaddā hi ekatthā. Subhampīti kāmacchando paccayo, asubhe subhākārena pavattanato subhanti vuccati. Tena kāraṇena pavattanakassa aññassa kāmacchandassa nimittattā subhanimittanti. Subhassāti yathāvuttassa subhassa. Ārammaṇampīti subhākārena, iṭṭhākārena vā gayhamānaṃ rūpādiārammaṇampi subhanimittaṃ vuttākārena. Anupāyamanasikāroti ākaṅkhitassa hitasukhassa anupāyabhūto manasikāro, tato eva uppathamanasikāroti ayonisomanasikāro. Tasminti yathāniddhārite kāmacchandabhūte tadārammaṇabhūte ca duvidhepi subhanimitte. Aṭṭhakathāyaṃ pana “subhārammaṇe”icceva vuttaṃ. Atthi, bhikkhave, subhanimittantiādīti ādi-saddena kāmacchandanīvaraṇassa āhāradassanapāḷi uttānāti katvā vuttaṃ – “evaṃ sabbanīvaraṇesu yojanā veditabbā”ti.

183. 'They stand by this,' thus it is a basis, a cause. The fourfold condition designated as kamma, temperature, mind, and nutriment is its basis, thus it is 'condition-based.' For the words 'nutriment' and 'condition' are synonymous. 'The beautiful': sensual desire is a condition; because it proceeds in the manner of the beautiful even in what is not beautiful, it is called 'beautiful.' For that reason, because it is a sign for another instance of sensual desire that causes it to arise, it is called 'the sign of the beautiful.' 'Of the beautiful' means of the beautiful as stated. 'The object': an object such as a form, etc., being grasped in the manner of the beautiful or in a pleasing manner, is also 'the sign of the beautiful' in the way stated. 'Unmethodical attention' is attention that is not a means to the welfare and happiness one aspires for; for that very reason it is 'wrong-path attention,' that is, 'unwise attention.' 'In that' refers to that twofold sign of the beautiful as specified, which is both sensual desire itself and its object. In the commentary, however, it is just said 'in a beautiful object.' 'There is, bhikkhus, the sign of the beautiful,' etc.—by the word 'etc.' it is said that the Pali passage showing the nutriment for the hindrance of sensual desire is plain, thus it is stated: 'In this way the application should be understood in regard to all the hindrances.'

Paṭighopi [M 2.0416] paṭighanimittaṃ purimuppannassa pacchā uppajjanakassa nimittabhāvato. Paṭighārammaṇaṃ nāma ekūnavīsati āghātavatthubhūtā sattasaṅkhārā. Aratīti pantasenāsanādīsu aramaṇaṃ[V 2.0125]. Ukkaṇṭhitāti ukkaṇṭhabhāvo. Pantesūti dūresu, vivittesu vā. Adhikusalesūti samathavipassanādhammesu. Arati ratipaṭipakkho. Aratitāti aramaṇākāro. Anabhiratīti anabhiratabhāvo. Anabhiramaṇāti anabhiramaṇākāro. Ukkaṇṭhitāti ukkaṇṭhanākāro. Paritassitāti ukkaṇṭhanavaseneva paritassanā.

Ill will is also the sign of ill will, because it is a sign for what arises later based on what arose before. The object of ill will refers to the nineteen grounds for vexation, which are beings and formations. 'Discontent' (arati) is non-delight in remote lodgings and so on. 'Restlessness' (ukkaṇṭhitā) is the state of being restless. 'In remote places' means in distant or secluded ones. 'In higher wholesome states' refers to the states of serenity and insight. Discontent is the opposite of delight. Aratitā is the mode of non-delight. Anabhirati is the state of non-delight. Anabhiramaṇā is the mode of non-delight. Ukkaṇṭhitā is the mode of restlessness. Paritassitā is agitation by way of restlessness.

Āgantukaṃ, na sabhāvasiddhaṃ. Kāyālasiyanti nāmakāye alasabhāvo. Sammohavinodaniyaṃ pana “tandīti jātiālasiya”nti vuttaṃ. Vadatīti etena atisītādipaccayā saṅkocāpattiṃ dasseti. Yaṃ sandhāya vuttaṃ kilesavatthuvibhaṅge (vibha. aṭṭha. 857). Tandīti jātiālasiyaṃ. Tandiyanāti tandiyanākāro. Tandimanakatāti tandiyā abhibhūtacittatā. Alasassa bhāvo ālasyaṃ. Ālasyāyanākāro ālasyāyanā. Ālasyāyitassa bhāvo ālasyāyitattaṃ. Iti sabbehipi imehi padehi kilesavasena kāyālasiyaṃ kathitaṃ.

Adventitious, not established by nature. 'Bodily laziness' (kāyālasiya) means a state of laziness in the mental body. But in the Sammohavinodanī it is said: 'Languor (tandī) is inborn laziness.' 'He says'—by this he shows the occurrence of contraction due to causes such as excessive cold. In reference to which it was said in the Kilesavatthuvibhaṅga (Vibh-a 857): 'Languor is inborn laziness. Tandiyanā is the mode of being languid. Tandimanakatā is the state of the mind being overcome by languor. Ālasya is the state of being lazy. Ālasyāyanā is the mode of being lazy. Ālasyāyitatta is the state of one who is lazy.' Thus by all these terms bodily laziness is described by way of defilements.

Kilesavasenāti sammohavasena. Kāyavinamanāti kāyassa virūpato namanā. Jambhanāti phandanā. Punappunaṃ jambhanā vijambhanā. Ānamanāti purato namanā. Vinamanāti pacchato namanā. Sannamanāti samantato namanā. Paṇamanāti yathā hi tantato uṭṭhitapesakāro kiñcideva upariṭṭhitaṃ gahetvā ujuṃ kāyaṃ ussāpeti, evaṃ kāyassa uddhaṃ ṭhapanā. Byādhiyakanti uppannabyādhitā. Iti sabbehipi imehi padehi kilesavasena kāyaphandanameva kathitaṃ (vibha. aṭṭha. 858).

'By way of defilements' means by way of delusion. 'Bending of the body' means bending the body unnaturally. 'Yawning' is trembling. Repeated yawning is stretching. 'Bending forward' is bending to the front. 'Bending back' is bending to the back. 'Bending around' is bending on all sides. 'Stretching up' is like when a weaver, having risen from his loom, takes something placed above and raises his body straight up; thus it is the placing of the body upright. 'Sickness' means an arisen illness. Thus by all these terms, just the trembling of the body by way of defilements is described (Vibh-a 858).

Bhattapariḷāhoti bhattavasena pariḷāhuppatti. Bhuttāvissāti bhuttavato. Bhattamucchāti bhattagelaññaṃ. Atibhuttapaccayā hi mucchāpatto viya hoti. Bhattakilamathoti bhuttapaccayā kilantabhāvo. Bhattapariḷāhoti bhattadaratho. Kucchipūraṃ bhuttavato hi pariḷāhuppattiyā upahatindriyo viya hoti, kāyo khijjati. Kāyaduṭṭhullanti bhuttabhattaṃ nissāya kāyassa akammaññatā.

'Feverishness from food' is the arising of feverishness on account of food. 'Of one who has eaten' means of one who has eaten. 'Fainting from food' is sickness from food. For on account of overeating, one is as if overcome by a faint. 'Weariness from food' is the state of being weary on account of eating. 'Feverishness from food' is discomfort from food. For one who has eaten his belly full, his faculties are impaired by the arising of feverishness, as it were, and the body is afflicted. 'Bodily grossness' is the unwieldiness of the body depending on the food eaten.

Cittassa [M 2.0417] līyanākāroti ārammaṇe cittassa saṅkocappatti. Cittassa akalyatāti cittassa gilānabhāvo. Gilānoti akallako vuccati. Tathā cāha “nāhaṃ, bhante, akallako”ti. Akammaññatāti cittagelaññasaṅkhāto akammaññanākāro. Olīyanāti olīyanākāro. Iriyāpathikampi cittaṃ yassa vasena iriyāpathaṃ sandhāretuṃ asakkonto olīyati, tassa taṃ ākāraṃ sandhāya vuttaṃ “olīyanā”ti. Dutiyapadaṃ upasaggena vaḍḍhitaṃ. Līnanti avipphārikatāya [V 2.0126] saṅkocappattaṃ. Itare dve ākāraniddesā. Thinanti avipphārikatāya anussāhanā asaṅgahanasaṅgahanaṃ. Thiyanākāro thiyanā. Thiyitattanti thiyitassa ākāro, avipphārikatāti attho.

'The mode of the mind's sluggishness' is the mind's attainment of contraction in regard to its object. 'The mind's unhealthiness' is the state of the mind's sickness. A sick person is called 'unhealthy.' And thus he said: 'I am not unhealthy, venerable sir.' 'Unwieldiness' is the mode of unwieldiness designated as mental sickness. 'Sluggishness' (olīyanā) is the mode of being sluggish. In reference to that mode of a person who, on account of a mind related to posture, is unable to maintain the posture and becomes sluggish, it is said 'sluggishness.' The second term is augmented by a prefix. 'Sluggish' (līna) means having attained contraction through lack of expansion. The other two are specifications of its mode. 'Sloth' (thīna) is non-exertion and non-grasping due to lack of expansion. Thiyanā is the mode of being slothful. Thiyitatta is the mode of one who is slothful, meaning lack of expansion.

Cetaso avūpasamoti cittassa avūpasantatā asannisinnabhāvo. Tenāha – “avūpasantākāro”ti. Atthato panetanti svāyaṃ avūpasantākāro vikkhepasabhāvattā vikkhepahetutāya ca atthato etaṃ uddhaccakukkuccameva.

'Non-tranquility of mind' is the unpacified state of the mind, the state of not being settled. Therefore he said: 'the mode of being unpacified.' But in meaning, this mode of being unpacified, because it has the nature of distraction and is a cause of distraction, is in meaning just restlessness and remorse.

Vicikicchāya ārammaṇadhammā nāma “buddhe kaṅkhatī”tiādinā āgataaṭṭhakaṅkhāvatthubhūtā dhammā. Yasmā vicikicchā byāpādādayo viya anu anu uggahaṇapaccayā uppajjati, tasmā kaṅkhāṭṭhānīyaṃ ārammaṇameva dassitaṃ “vicikicchāya ārammaṇadhammā”ti. Yasmā purimuppannā vicikicchā pacchā vicikicchāya paccayo hoti, tasmā vicikicchāpi vicikicchāṭṭhānīyadhammā veditabbā. Tatrāyaṃ vacanattho – tiṭṭhanti pavattanti etthāti ṭhānīyā, vicikicchā eva ṭhānīyā vicikicchāṭṭhānīyā. Aṭṭhakathāyaṃ pana ārammaṇassapi tattha visesapaccayataṃ upādāya “kāmacchando vicikicchāti ime dve dhammā ārammaṇena kathitā”ti vuttaṃ. Subhanimittassa hi paccayabhāvamattaṃ sandhāyetaṃ vuttaṃ, tathāpi yathā “paṭighampi paṭighanimitta”nti katvā “byāpādo upanissayena kathito”ti vuttaṃ, evaṃ subhampi subhanimittanti katvā kāmacchando upanissayena kathitoti sakkā viññātuṃ. Sesā thinamiddhauddhaccakukkuccāni. Tattha thinamiddhaṃ aññamaññaṃ sahajātādivasena paccayo, tathā uddhaccakukkuccanti. Ubhayesampi upanissayakoṭiyā paccayabhāve vattabbameva natthīti āha “sahajātena ca upanissayena cā”ti.

The objects of doubt are the states that are the grounds for the eight kinds of doubt, which are mentioned with the words 'one doubts about the Buddha,' and so on. Because doubt, like ill will, arises from the condition of repeated grasping, the object itself is shown as the basis for doubt; hence it is said, 'the objects of doubt.' Since a previously arisen doubt is a condition for a subsequent doubt, doubt itself should be understood as a state that is a basis for doubt. Here, this is the meaning of the word: 'they stand, they occur in this,' thus they are bases. Doubt itself is a basis, hence 'a basis for doubt.' In the commentary, however, on account of the object's specific conditionality in that case, it is said: 'Sensual desire and doubt—these two states are spoken of in terms of their object.' This is said with reference merely to the conditionality of the sign of the beautiful. Still, just as it is said, 'ill will is also the sign of ill will,' and thus, 'ill will is spoken of in terms of strong dependence condition,' so too, since the beautiful is the sign of the beautiful, it can be understood that sensual desire is spoken of in terms of strong dependence condition. The remaining are sloth and torpor, and restlessness and worry. Therein, sloth and torpor are conditions for each other by way of co-nascence and so on, and similarly for restlessness and worry. It goes without saying that for both pairs there is conditionality in the category of strong dependence; thus it says: 'by co-nascence and by strong dependence.'

Yasmā [M 2.0418] sati nāma “cattāro satipaṭṭhānā”tiādinā tesaṃ tesaṃ dhammānaṃ anussaraṇavasena vattati, tasmā te dhammā satisambojjhaṅgaṭṭhānīyā nāma. Lokuttaradhamme ca anussavādivasena gahetvā tathā pavattateva. Tena vuttaṃ “satiyā”tiādi.

Because mindfulness operates by way of repeatedly remembering those various states, as in the passage beginning 'the four establishments of mindfulness,' those states are called the grounds for the enlightenment factor of mindfulness. And with regard to supramundane states, having grasped them by way of oral tradition and so on, it operates in just that way. Therefore it is said: 'of mindfulness,' and so on.

Kosallaṃ vuccati paññā, tato uppannā kosallasambhūtā. Anavajjasukhavipākāti anavajjā hutvā sukhavipākā vipaccanakā. Padadvayena paccayato sabhāvato kiccato phalato kusaladhammaṃ dasseti. Akusalaniddesepi eseva nayo. Sāvajjāti gārayhā. Anavajjāti agārayhā. Hīnā lāmakā. Paṇītā seṭṭhā. Kaṇhā kāḷakā asuddhā. Sukkā odātā suddhā[V 2.0127]. Paṭibhāga-saddo paṭhame vikappe sadisakoṭṭhāsattho, dutiye paṭipakkhakoṭṭhāsattho, tatiye niggahetabbapaṭipakkhakoṭṭhāsattho daṭṭhabbo.

Skill is called wisdom; what has arisen from that is 'born of skill.' 'Blameless and resulting in happiness' means that being blameless, they are states that ripen with a happy result. By means of these two terms, the wholesome state is shown in terms of its condition, nature, function, and fruit. This same method applies in the exposition of the unwholesome. 'Blameworthy' means censurable. 'Blameless' means uncensurable. 'Inferior' means base. 'Superior' means excellent. 'Dark' means black, impure. 'Bright' means white, pure. The word 'counterpart' should be seen as having the meaning of a similar portion in the first alternative, the meaning of an opposing portion in the second, and the meaning of an opposing portion to be subdued in the third.

Kusalakiriyāya ādikammabhāvena pavattavīriyaṃ dhitisabhāvatāya dhātūti vuttanti āha “ārambhadhātūti paṭhamārambhavīriya”nti. Laddhāsevanaṃ vīriyaṃ balappattaṃ hutvā paṭipakkhaṃ vidhamatīti āha – “nikkamadhātūti kosajjato nikkhantattā tato balavatara”nti. Adhimattādhimattatarānaṃ paṭipakkhadhammānaṃ vidhamanasamatthaṃ paṭupaṭutarādibhāvappattaṃ hotīti āha – “parakkamadhātūti paraṃ paraṃ ṭhānaṃ akkamanatāya tatopi balavatara”nti.

He says that the energy that proceeds as the initial action in a wholesome deed is called an 'element' because its nature is persistence; thus he says: 'The element of initiative is the initial, beginning energy.' He says that the energy that has been cultivated, having become strong, shatters the opposing states; thus he says: 'The element of exertion is stronger than that because it has departed from laziness.' He says that it becomes capable of shattering opposing states that are progressively more intense, and reaches a state of being ever more vigorous; thus he says: 'The element of striving is even stronger than that because it advances to a higher and higher stage.'

Tiṭṭhati pavattati etthāti ṭhānīyā. Ārammaṇadhammā, pītisambojjhaṅgassa ṭhānīyāti pītisambojjhaṅgaṭṭhānīyāti “pītiyā ārammaṇadhammā”ti vuttaṃ. Yasmā aparāparuppattiyā pītipi tathā vattabbataṃ labhatīti vuttaṃ visuddhimagge “pītiyā eva taṃ nāma”nti. Darathapassaddhīti daratho kilesapariḷāho, so passambhati etāyāti darathapassaddhi, kāyapassaddhiyā vedanādikhandhattayassa viya rūpakāyassapi passambhanaṃ hoti, cittapassaddhiyā cittasseva passambhanaṃ, tato evettha bhagavatā lahutādīnaṃ viya duvidhatā vuttā. Tathā samāhitākāraṃ sallakkhetvā gayhamāno samathova samathanimittaṃ, tassa ārammaṇabhūtaṃ paṭibhāganimittampi. Vividhaṃ aggaṃ etassāti byaggo, vikkhepo. Tathā hi so anavaṭṭhānaraso bhantatāpaccupaṭṭhāno vutto. Ekaggabhāvato byaggapaṭipakkhoti abyaggo, samādhi, so eva nimittanti pubbe viya vattabbaṃ. Tenāha “tasseva vevacana”nti.

'It stands, it occurs, in this,' thus it is a ground. The objects are the ground for the enlightenment factor of rapture; thus 'the ground for the enlightenment factor of rapture' is said to mean 'the objects of rapture.' Because, with its successive arising, rapture itself can be spoken of in this way, it is said in the Visuddhimagga: 'That is indeed the name for rapture.' 'Tranquillity from agitation': agitation is the fever of the defilements; it is tranquillized by this, thus it is 'tranquillity from agitation.' By means of bodily tranquillity there is the tranquillizing of the physical body as well as of the three aggregates of feeling, etc. By means of mental tranquillity there is the tranquillizing of the mind itself. Therefore the Blessed One has spoken of it here as twofold, like lightness and so on. Similarly, serenity itself, when grasped after discerning the composed state, is the sign of serenity; its object, the counterpart sign, is also included. 'It has various points,' thus it is 'distracted' (byagga), which means distraction. For it is said to have the characteristic of instability and the manifestation of bewilderment. Being the opposite of distraction due to its state of one-pointedness, 'non-distraction' (abyagga) is concentration. That itself is the sign, as should be stated as before. Therefore he says: 'It is a synonym for that.'

Yo [M 2.0419] ārammaṇe iṭṭhāniṭṭhākāraṃ anādiyitvā gahetabbo majjhattākāro, yo ca pubbe upekkhāsambojjhaṅgassa bhāvanāvasena uppanno majjhattākāro, duvidhopi so upekkhāya ārammaṇadhammoti adhippetoti āha – “atthato pana majjhattākāro upekkhāṭṭhānīyā dhammāti veditabbo”ti. Ārammaṇena kathitā ārammaṇasseva tesaṃ visesapaccayabhāvato. Sesāti vīriyādayo cattāro dhammā. Tesañhi upanissayova sātisayo icchitabboti.

He says that both the neutral state that is to be grasped in an object without attending to its desirable or undesirable aspects, and the neutral state that has previously arisen by way of developing the enlightenment factor of equanimity, are intended as the objective domain of equanimity. Thus he says: 'But in meaning, the neutral state is to be understood as the states that are the ground for equanimity.' They are spoken of in terms of the object because the object itself is a specific condition for them. 'The remaining' refers to the four states beginning with energy. For in their case, it is the pre-eminent strong dependence condition that is to be desired.

Kāyasuttavaṇṇanā niṭṭhitā.

The commentary on the Kāyasutta is concluded.

3. Sīlasuttavaṇṇanā

3. Commentary on the Sutta on Virtue

184. Khīṇāsavassa lokuttaraṃ sīlaṃ nāma maggaphalapariyāpannā sammāvācākammantājīvā sīlalakkhaṇappattā [V 2.0128] tadaññe cetanādayo. Lokiyaṃ pana kiriyābyākatacittapariyāpannaṃ cārittasīlaṃ, vārittasīlassa pana sambhavo eva natthi viramaṇavasena pavattiyā abhāvato. “Pubbabhāgasīlaṃ lokiyasīla”nti keci.

184. For one whose taints are destroyed, supramundane virtue consists of the right speech, right action, and right livelihood included in the path and its fruition, which have attained the characteristic of virtue, along with the other associated factors such as volition. Mundane virtue, however, is the virtue of conduct included in functional indeterminate consciousness; but for such a one, the virtue of restraint does not arise at all, since there is no occurrence by way of abstention. Some say: "The virtue of the preliminary part is mundane virtue."

Cakkhudassananti cakkhūhi dassanaṃ. Lakkhaṇassa dassananti sabhāvadhammānaṃ saṅkhatānaṃ paccattalakkhaṇassa ñātapariññāya, aniccādisāmaññalakkhaṇassa tīraṇapariññāya dassanaṃ. Pajahantopi hi te pahātabbākārato passati nāma. Nibbānassa tathalakkhaṇaṃ maggaphalehi dassanaṃ, taṃ pana paṭivijjhanaṃ. Jhānena pathavīkasiṇādīnaṃ, abhiññāhi rūpānaṃ dassanampi ñāṇadassanameva. Cakkhudassanaṃ adhippetaṃ savanapayirupāsanānaṃ parato gahitattā. Pañhapayirupāsananti pañhapucchanavasena payirupāsanaṃ aññakammatthāya upasaṅkamanassa kevalaṃ upasaṅkamaneneva jotitattā.

"Vision of the eye" means seeing with the eyes. "Seeing the characteristics" means seeing the individual characteristics of conditioned phenomena by means of full understanding as the known, and seeing their general characteristics such as impermanence by means of full understanding as investigation. For even while abandoning them, one indeed sees them under the aspect of what is to be abandoned. The seeing of Nibbāna’s true characteristic by means of the path and fruition is penetration. The seeing of the earth kasiṇa and so on by means of jhāna, and the seeing of forms by means of the direct knowledges, is also just knowledge and vision. "Vision of the eye" is intended here, since hearing and attending are taken up later. "Attending by questioning" means attending by way of asking questions, since an approach for some other purpose is indicated simply by the act of approaching.

Ariyānaṃ anussati nāma guṇavasena, tatthāpi laddhaovādāvajjanamukhena yathābhūtasīlādiguṇānussaraṇanti dassetuṃ “jhānavipassanā”tiādi vuttaṃ. Aññesaṃyeva santiketi ariyehi aññesaṃ sāsanikānaṃyeva santike. Tenāha – “anupabbajjā nāmā”ti. Aññesūti sāsanikehi aññesu tāpasaparibbājakādīsu. Tattha hi pabbajjā ariyānaṃ anupabbajjā nāma na hotīti vuttaṃ.

Recollection of the noble ones is by way of their qualities. And to show that even there it is a recollection of such qualities as virtue as it really is, by way of the advice and attention one has received, the words "jhāna and insight," etc., are stated. "In the presence of others" means in the presence of those followers of the teaching who are other than the noble ones. Therefore he says: "It is called going forth in imitation." "Among others" means among those other than the followers of the teaching, such as ascetics and wanderers. For it is said that going forth among them is not what is called 'going forth in imitation' of the noble ones.

Satasahassamattā [M 2.0420] ahesuṃ samantapāsādikattā mahātherassa. Laṅkādīpeti nissayasīsena nissitasallakkhaṇaṃ. Na hi pabbajjā dīpapaṭiladdhā, atha kho dīpanivāsiācariyapaṭiladdhā. Mahinda…pe… pabbajanti nāma tassa parivāratāya pabbajjāyāti.

There were about a hundred thousand, owing to the great elder's being inspiring in every way. "On the island of Laṅkā" indicates the characteristic of dependence, with dependence as the main point. For the going forth is not obtained from the island, but rather is obtained from the teachers dwelling on the island. "Mahinda... they go forth" means the going forth for the purpose of becoming his retinue.

Saratīti taṃ ovādānusāsanidhammaṃ cinteti citte karoti. Vitakkāhataṃ karotīti punappunaṃ parivitakkanena tadatthaṃ vitakkanipphāditaṃ karoti. Āraddho hotīti sampādito hoti. Taṃ pana sampādanaṃ pāripūri evāti āha “paripuṇṇo hotī”ti. Tatthāti yathāvutte dhamme. Ñāṇacāravasenāti ñāṇassa pavattanavasena. Tesaṃ tesaṃ dhammānanti tasmiṃ tasmiṃ ovādadhamme āgatānaṃ rūpārūpadhammānaṃ. Lakkhaṇanti visesalakkhaṇaṃ sāmaññalakkhaṇañca. Pavicinatīti “idaṃ rūpaṃ ettakaṃ rūpa”ntiādinā vicayaṃ āpajjati. Ñāṇañca ropetīti “aniccaṃ calaṃ palokaṃ pabhaṅgū”tiādinā ñāṇaṃ pavatteti. Vīmaṃsanaṃ…pe… āpajjatīti rūpasattakārūpasattakakkamena [V 2.0129] vipassanaṃ paccakkhato viya aniccatādīnaṃ dassanaṃ sammasanaṃ āpajjati.

"He recollects" means he contemplates that teaching and instruction, he brings it to mind. "He makes it struck by thought" means that by repeated reflection he brings its meaning to completion by thought. "He is committed" means he has accomplished it. And that accomplishment is a fulfillment, so he says, "He is fulfilled." "Therein" means in the aforesaid teaching. "By way of the course of knowledge" means by way of the proceeding of knowledge. "Of those various phenomena" means of the material and immaterial phenomena that occur in each particular teaching. "Characteristic" means the specific characteristic and the general characteristic. "He investigates" means he undertakes an investigation, thinking: "This is material form; this much is material form," and so on. "And he implants knowledge" means he makes knowledge proceed with such thoughts as: "It is impermanent, transient, disintegrating, and fragile." "He undertakes investigation..." means he undertakes the comprehension which is the seeing of impermanence and so on as if directly, by means of insight meditation following the procedure of the sevenfold material and sevenfold immaterial.

Ubhayampetanti phalānisaṃsāti vuttadvayaṃ. Atthato ekaṃ pariyāyasaddattā. Paṭikaccāti pageva. Maraṇakāleti maraṇakālasamīpe. Samīpatthe hi idaṃ bhummanti āha “maraṇassa āsannakāle”ti.

"Both of these" refers to the two things stated as the benefits of the fruit. They are one in meaning, being synonymous terms. "In advance" means beforehand. "At the time of death" means near the time of death. For this locative case is used in the sense of proximity; thus he says, "at a time close to death."

So tividho hoti ñāṇassa tikkhamajjhamudubhāvena. Tenāha “kappasahassāyukesū”tiādi. Upahaccaparinibbāyī nāma āyuvemajjhaṃ atikkamitvā parinibbāyanato. Yattha katthacīti avihādīsu yattha katthaci. Sappayogenāti vipassanāñāṇasaṅkhārasaṅkhātena payogena, saha vipassanāpayogenāti attho. Suddhāvāsabhūmiyaṃ uddhaṃyeva maggasoto etassāti uddhaṃsoto. Paṭisandhivasena akaniṭṭhabhavaṃ gacchatīti akaniṭṭhagāmī.

He is of three kinds according to the sharpness, middlingness, or dullness of his knowledge. Thus it is said: "For those with a lifespan of a thousand eons," and so on. One is called a "one who attains Nibbāna by reducing the lifespan" because he attains final Nibbāna after passing the midpoint of the lifespan. "In whatever" means in whatever of the Avihā realms and so on. "With exertion" means with the exertion designated as the formation of insight-knowledge; the meaning is "together with the exertion of insight." His stream of the path goes only upward in the plane of the Pure Abodes, thus he is an "upward-streamer." He goes to the Akaniṭṭha existence by way of rebirth-linking, thus he is an "Akaniṭṭha-goer."

Avihādīsu vattamānopi ekaṃsato uddhaṃgamanāraho puggalo akaniṭṭhagāmī eva nāmāti vuttaṃ “eko uddhaṃsoto akaniṭṭhagāmīti pañca hontī”ti. Tesanti niddhāraṇe sāmivacanaṃ. Uddhaṃsotabhāvato yadipi heṭṭhimādīsupi ariyabhūmi nibbattateva, tathāpi tattha bhūmīsu āyuṃ [M 2.0421] aggahetvā akaniṭṭhabhave āyuvaseneva soḷasakappasahassāyukatā daṭṭhabbā. “Satta phalā sattānisaṃsā pāṭikaṅkhā”ti vuttattā “arahattamaggassa pubbabhāgavipassanā bojjhaṅgā kathitā”ti vuttaṃ. Sattannampi sahabhāvo labbhatīti “apubbaṃ acarimaṃ ekacittakkhaṇikā”ti vuttaṃ. Tayidaṃ pāḷiyaṃ tattha tattha “tasmiṃ samaye”ti āgatavacanena viññāyati, bojjhaṅgānaṃ pana nānāsabhāvattā “nānālakkhaṇā”ti vuttaṃ.

A person dwelling in the Avihā realms and so on, who is fit to go exclusively upward, is indeed called an "Akaniṭṭha-goer." Thus it is said: "The upward-streamer is one, the Akaniṭṭha-goer another; thus there are five." "Of them" is a genitive of specification. Although, due to his being an upward-streamer, a noble plane arises even in the lower planes, still, without his taking up the lifespan in those planes, his having a lifespan of sixteen thousand eons should be understood solely by way of his lifespan in the Akaniṭṭha existence. Because it is said, "Seven fruits and seven benefits are to be expected," it is therefore stated: "The enlightenment factors are spoken of as the insight of the preliminary part of the path to arahantship." That all seven can exist together is stated thus: "Not one before another, not one after another, they occur in a single mind-moment." This is known from the phrase "at that time," which occurs here and there in the Pāli Canon. But because the enlightenment factors have different intrinsic natures, they are said to be "of diverse characteristics."

Sīlasuttavaṇṇanā niṭṭhitā.

The Commentary on the Sutta on Virtue is concluded.

4. Vatthasuttavaṇṇanā

4. The Commentary on the Vattha Sutta

185. “Satisambojjhaṅgo”ti evaṃ ce mayhaṃ hotīti satisambojjhaṅgo nāma seṭṭho uttamo pavaro, tasmāhaṃ satisambojjhaṅgasīsena phalasamāpattiṃ appetvā viharissāmīti evaṃ ce mayhaṃ pubbabhāge hotīti attho. “Appamāṇo”ti evaṃ mayhaṃ hotīti svāyaṃ satisambojjhaṅgo, sabbaso pamāṇakarakilesābhāvato appamāṇadhammārammaṇato ca appamāṇoti evaṃ mayhaṃ antosamāpattiyaṃ asammohavasena hoti. Suparipuṇṇoti bhāvanāpāripūriyā suṭṭhu paripuṇṇoti evaṃ [V 2.0130] mayhaṃ antosamāpattiyaṃ asammohavasena hotīti. Tiṭṭhatīti yathākālaparicchedasamāpattiyā avaṭṭhānena tappariyāpannatāya satisambojjhaṅgo tiṭṭhati paṭibandhavasena. Uppādaṃ anāvajjitattāti uppādassa anāvajjanena asamannāhārena. Uppādasīsena cettha uppādavantova saṅkhārā gahitā. Anuppādanti nibbānaṃ uppādābhāvato uppādavantehi ca vinissaṭattā. Pavattanti vipākappavattaṃ. Appavattanti nibbānaṃ tappaṭikkhepato. Nimittanti sabbasaṅkhāranimittaṃ. Animittanti nibbānaṃ. Saṅkhāreti uppādādianāmasanena kevalameva saṅkhāragahaṇaṃ. Visaṅkhāranti nibbānaṃ. Āvajjitattā āvajjitakālato paṭṭhāya ārabbha pavattiyā satisambojjhaṅgo tiṭṭhati. Aṭṭhahākārehīti aṭṭhahi kāraṇehi. Jānātīti samāpattito vuṭṭhitakāle pajānāti. Aṭṭhahākārehīti uppādāvajjanādīhi ceva anuppādāvajjanādīhi ca vuttākāraviparītehi aṭṭhahi ākārehi cavantaṃ samāpattivasena anavaṭṭhānatopi gacchantaṃ cavatīti thero pajānātīti.

185. ‘The enlightenment factor of mindfulness’: if it occurs to me thus: ‘The enlightenment factor of mindfulness is the best, the highest, the most excellent; therefore, having made the enlightenment factor of mindfulness the head, I will enter upon and dwell in the attainment of fruition’—if this occurs to me in the preliminary stage, this is the meaning. ‘Immeasurable’: if it occurs to me thus: ‘This very enlightenment factor of mindfulness is immeasurable because of the complete absence of defilements that create limits and because its object is the immeasurable Dhamma’—thus it occurs to me within the attainment by way of non-delusion. ‘Perfectly fulfilled’: because the development is fully perfected, it is well perfected—thus it occurs to me within the attainment by way of non-delusion. ‘It remains’: the enlightenment factor of mindfulness remains by being established in the attainment that is defined by a period of time, by being included in it, by way of being connected to it. ‘Because of not attending to arising’: by not attending to arising, by not bringing it to mind. Here, by ‘arising’ as the heading, formations possessing arising are taken. ‘Non-arising’ is Nibbāna, because of the absence of arising and because it is released from things that have arising. ‘Occurring’ is the occurrence of resultants. ‘Non-occurring’ is Nibbāna, because of its rejection. ‘The sign’ is the sign of all formations. ‘The signless’ is Nibbāna. ‘Formations’: the mere grasping of formations without adverting to arising and so on. ‘Non-formation’ is Nibbāna. ‘Because of having attended’: from the time of having attended onwards, the enlightenment factor of mindfulness remains by occurring with an object. ‘In eight ways’: by eight causes. ‘He knows’: at the time of emerging from the attainment, he understands. ‘In eight ways’: by the eight modes that are the opposite of the modes mentioned—attending to arising, etc., and not attending to arising, etc.—the elder understands that it ceases, that it passes away because of the instability of the attainment.

Phalabojjhaṅgāti [M 2.0422] phalasamāpattipariyāpannā bojjhaṅgā. Kiṃ pana te visuṃ visuṃ pavattantīti āha “yadā hī”tiādi. Sīsaṃ katvāti padhānaṃ seṭṭhaṃ katvā. Tadanvayāti tadanugatā satisambojjhaṅgaṃ anugacchanakā. Tañca kho tathā katvā dhammaṃ paccavekkhaṇavasena. Keci pana “taṃ paccavekkhaṇādikaṃ katvā”ti vadanti.

‘The enlightenment factors of fruition’ means the enlightenment factors included in the attainment of fruition. But do they occur separately? He says, ‘For when,’ etc. ‘Having made it the head’ means having made it primary, the best. ‘Following it’ means those that follow it, that accompany the enlightenment factor of mindfulness. And having done so, by way of reviewing the Dhamma. Some, however, say, ‘having done that reviewing, etc.’

Vatthasuttavaṇṇanā niṭṭhitā.

The Commentary on the Vattha Sutta is concluded.

5. Bhikkhusuttavaṇṇanā

5. The Commentary on the Bhikkhu Sutta

186. Bodhāyāti ettha bodho nāma bujjhanaṃ, taṃ pana kissa kenāti pucchanto “kiṃ bujjhanatthāyā”ti vatvā taṃ dassento “maggenā”tiādimāha. Maggena nibbānaṃ bujjhanatthāya saṃvattanti, paccavekkhaṇāya katakiccataṃ bujjhanatthāya saṃvattanti, paṭhamavikappe sacchikiriyābhisamayo eva dassitoti tena atuṭṭhe “maggena vā”ti dutiyavikappamāha. Vivekanissitaṃ virāganissitanti padehi sabbaṃ maggakiccaṃ tassa phalañca dassitaṃ. Nirodhanissitanti iminā nibbānasacchikiriyā. Kāmāsavāpi cittaṃ vimuccatītiādinā kilesappahānaṃ. Vimuttasmiṃ vimuttamiti ñāṇaṃ hotītiādinā paccavekkhaṇā dassitā.

186. Herein, in the word ‘for awakening,’ `bodho` means understanding. Questioning, ‘But understanding of what, and by what means?’ he says, ‘For the sake of understanding what?’ and showing this, he says, ‘by the path,’ etc. They conduce to understanding Nibbāna by the path; they conduce to understanding that the task is done by reviewing. In the first alternative, realization and direct knowledge are shown, so being dissatisfied with that, he stated the second alternative, ‘or by the path.’ With the phrases ‘based on seclusion, based on dispassion,’ the entire function of the path and its fruit are shown. By this, ‘based on cessation,’ the realization of Nibbāna is shown. By ‘the mind is liberated from the taint of sensual desire,’ etc., the abandonment of the defilements is shown. By ‘in the liberated one the knowledge arises, “It is liberated,”’ etc., the reviewing is shown.

Bhikkhusuttavaṇṇanā niṭṭhitā.

The Commentary on the Bhikkhu Sutta is concluded.

6-7. Kuṇḍaliyasuttādivaṇṇanā

6-7. The Commentary on the Kuṇḍaliya Sutta and Others

187-188. Nibaddhavāsavasena [V 2.0131] ārāme nisīdanasīloti ārāmanisādī. Parisaṃ ogāḷho hutvā caratīti parisāvacaroti āha – “yo panā”tiādi. Evanti iminākārena. Gahaṇanti niggahaṇaṃ. Tena pucchāpadassa atthaṃ vivarati. Nibbeṭhananti niggahanibbeṭhanaṃ. Tena vissajjanapadassa atthaṃ vivarati. Iminā nayenāti etena “itivādo”ti ettha iti-saddassa atthaṃ dasseti. Upārambhādhippāyo vadati etenāti vādo, doso. Itivādo hotīti evaṃ imassa upari vādāropanaṃ hoti. Itivādappamokkhoti evaṃ tato pamokkho hoti[M 2.0423]. Evaṃ vādappamokkhānisaṃsaṃ parehi āropitadosassa nibbeṭhanavasena dassetvā idāni dosapavedanavasena dassetuṃ “ayaṃ pucchāya doso”tiādi vuttaṃ.

187-188. ‘One who has the habit of sitting in a monastery because of a fixed residence’ is a monastery-sitter. ‘One who moves about having entered an assembly’ is an assembly-goer. He says, ‘But who,’ etc. ‘Thus’ means in this manner. ‘Grasping’ is refutation. With this he explains the meaning of the word of the question. ‘Unraveling’ is refutation and unraveling. With this he explains the meaning of the word of the answer. ‘By this method’: by this he shows the meaning of the word `iti` here in `itivādo` (such talk). ‘He speaks with an intention of reproach’: this is `vādo` (talk), a fault. ‘Such talk arises’ means thus an imputation of talk is made upon this person. ‘Release from such talk’ means thus there is release from that. Having thus shown the benefit of release from talk by way of unraveling the fault imputed by others, now in order to show it by way of declaring the fault, it is said, ‘this is the fault of the question,’ etc.

Ettakaṃ ṭhānantiādito paṭṭhāya yāva “tīṇi sucaritānī”ti ettakaṃ ṭhānaṃ. Imaṃ desananti “indriyasaṃvaro kho”tiādinayappavattaṃ imaṃ desanaṃ. Nābhijjhāyatīti na abhijjhāyati. Nābhihaṃsatīti na abhitussati. Gocarajjhatte ṭhitaṃ hotīti kammaṭṭhānārammaṇe samādhānavasena ṭhitaṃ hoti avaṭṭhitaṃ. Tenāha “susaṇṭhita”nti. Susaṇṭhitanti sammā avikkhepavasena ṭhitaṃ. Kammaṭṭhānavimuttiyāti kammaṭṭhānānuyuñjanavasena paṭipakkhato nīvaraṇato vimuttiyā. Suṭṭhu vimuttanti suvimuttaṃ. Tasmiṃ amanāparūpadassane na maṅku vilakkho na hoti. Kilesavasena dosavasena. Aṭṭhitacitto athaddhacitto. Kovesena hi cittaṃ thaddhaṃ hoti, na mudukaṃ. Adīnamānasoti domanassavasena yo dīnabhāvo, tadabhāvena niddosamānaso. Apūticittoti byāpajjābhāvena sītibhūtacitto.

‘This much is the passage’: from the beginning up to ‘three kinds of good conduct,’ this much is the passage. ‘This teaching’: this teaching that proceeds in the manner of ‘restraint of the faculties,’ etc. ‘He does not covet’ means he does not covet. ‘He is not elated’ means he is not pleased. ‘It is established within its own resort’ means it is established, settled, in the meditation subject by way of concentration. Therefore he said, ‘well-established.’ ‘Well-established’ means properly established by way of non-distraction. ‘By liberation through the meditation subject’: by liberation from the opposing hindrances through devotion to the meditation subject. ‘Thoroughly liberated’ is `suvimuttaṃ`. On seeing an disagreeable form on that occasion, he is not troubled, not embarrassed. By way of defilements, by way of aversion. A mind that is not fixed, a mind that is not rigid. For the mind becomes rigid through aversion, not pliant. ‘Of undaunted mind’: one whose mind is without fault because of the absence of that dejected state that is due to displeasure. ‘With a mind that is not putrid’: with a mind that has become cool because of the absence of ill will.

Imesu chasu dvāresu aṭṭhārasa duccaritāni honti paccekaṃ kāyavacīmanoduccaritabhedena. Tāni vibhāgena dassetuṃ “katha”ntiādi vuttaṃ. Tattha iṭṭhārammaṇe āpāthagateti nayadānamattametaṃ. Tena “aniṭṭhārammaṇe āpāthagate dosaṃ uppādentassā”tiādinā tividhaduccaritaṃ nīharitvā vattabbaṃ, tathā “majjhattārammaṇe mohaṃ uppādentassā”tiādinā ca. Manoduccaritādisāmaññena pana tīṇiyeva duccaritāni hontīti veditabbaṃ.

At these six doors there are eighteen kinds of misconduct, distinguished individually as misconduct of body, speech, and mind. To show them in detail, ‘How?’ etc., is said. Herein, ‘when an agreeable object has come into range’ is just an indication of the method. Therefore, having drawn out the threefold misconduct with such phrases as ‘for one who generates aversion when a disagreeable object has come into range,’ etc., it should be stated; and similarly with such phrases as ‘for one who generates delusion when a neutral object has come into range,’ etc. But it should be understood that by way of the general category of mental misconduct, etc., there are just three kinds of misconduct.

Paññattivasenāti vatthuṃ anāmasitvā piṇḍagahaṇamukhena kevalaṃ paññattivaseneva. Bhāvanāpaṭisaṅkhāneti bhāvanāsiddhe paṭisaṅkhāne, bhāvanāya paṭisaṅkhāne vāti attho. Imānīti yathāvuttāni chadvārārammaṇāni. Duccaritānīti duccaritakāraṇāni. Appaṭisaṅkhāne ṭhitassa duccaritāni [V 2.0132] sucaritāni katvā. Pariṇāmetīti parivatteti duccaritāni tattha anuppādetvā sucaritāni [M 2.0424] uppādento. Evanti vuttappakārena. Indriyasaṃvaro…pe… veditabbo indriyasaṃvarasampādanavasena tiṇṇaṃ sucaritānaṃ sijjhanato. Tenāha “ettāvatā”tiādi. Ettāvatāti ādito paṭṭhāya yāva “tīṇi sucaritāni paripūrentī”ti padaṃ, ettāvatā. Sīlānurakkhakaṃ indriyasaṃvarasīlanti catupārisuddhisīlassa anurakkhakaṃ indriyasaṃvarasīlaṃ kathikaṃ. Kathaṃ pana tadeva tassa anurakkhakaṃ hotīti? Aparāparuppattiyā upanissayabhāvato.

‘By way of designation’: solely by way of designation, without touching the substance, by the method of taking almsfood. ‘He reflects on the development’: the meaning is, in the reflection accomplished by development, or in the reflection by means of development. ‘These’: the aforesaid objects of the six doors. ‘Misconducts’: the causes of misconduct. The misconducts of one who abides in non-reflection are made into good conducts. ‘He transforms’: he converts, generating good conducts by not generating misconducts there. ‘Thus’: in the way stated. ‘Restraint of the faculties…pe… should be understood’: because the three kinds of good conduct are accomplished by way of perfecting the restraint of the faculties. Therefore he said, ‘to this extent,’ etc. ‘To this extent’: from the beginning up to the phrase ‘they fulfill the three kinds of good conduct’—to this extent. ‘The virtue of sense-restraint that guards virtue’: the virtue of sense-restraint that guards the fourfold purification virtue is spoken of. But how does that very thing become its guardian? Because of being a supporting condition for subsequent arising.

Tīṇi sīlānīti indriyasaṃvara-ājīvapārisuddhi-paccayasannissita-sīlāni. Lokuttaramissakāti lokiyāpi lokuttarāpi hontīti attho. Sattannaṃ bojjhaṅgānanti lokuttarānaṃ sattannaṃ bojjhaṅgānaṃ. Mūlabhūtā satipaṭṭhānā pubbabhāgā, te sandhāya vuttaṃ “cattāro kho, kuṇḍaliya, satipaṭṭhānā bhāvitā bahulīkatā satta bojjhaṅge paripūrentī”ti. Tepīti yathāvuttasatipaṭṭhānā. Satipaṭṭhānamūlakā bojjhaṅgāti lokiyasatipaṭṭhānamūlakā bojjhaṅgāva. Pubbabhāgāvāti ettha keci “pubbabhāgā cā”ti pāṭhaṃ katvā “pubbeva lokuttarā pubbabhāgā cā”ti atthaṃ vadanti. Vijjāvimuttimūlakāti “satta kho, kuṇḍaliya, bojjhaṅgā bhāvitā bahulīkatā vijjāvimuttiṃ paripūrentī”ti evaṃ vuttā bojjhaṅgā lokuttarāva vijjāvimuttisahagatabhāvato.

‘The three kinds of virtue’ are the virtues of restraint of the faculties, purity of livelihood, and that dependent on the requisites. ‘Mixed with the supramundane’: the meaning is that they are both mundane and supramundane. ‘Of the seven enlightenment factors’: of the seven supramundane enlightenment factors. The foundations of mindfulness are fundamental, the preliminary part. With reference to them it is said: ‘Kuṇḍaliya, when the four foundations of mindfulness are developed and cultivated, they fulfill the seven enlightenment factors.’ ‘These too’: the aforesaid foundations of mindfulness. ‘The enlightenment factors rooted in the foundations of mindfulness’ are only the enlightenment factors rooted in the mundane foundations of mindfulness. ‘They are the preliminary part’: here some, adopting the reading `pubbabhāgā cā` (‘and the preliminary part’), state the meaning as, ‘even before the supramundane ones, they are the preliminary part.’ ‘Rooted in knowledge and liberation’: the enlightenment factors spoken of thus, ‘Kuṇḍaliya, when the seven enlightenment factors are developed and cultivated, they fulfill knowledge and liberation,’ are only supramundane because they are associated with knowledge and liberation.

Kuṇḍaliyasuttādivaṇṇanā niṭṭhitā.

The Commentary on the Kuṇḍaliya Sutta and Others is concluded.

8. Upavānasuttavaṇṇanā

8. Commentary on the Upavāṇa Sutta

189. Paccattanti karaṇaniddeso ayanti āha – “attanāvā”ti. Kurumānoyevāti uppādento eva. Kammaṭṭhānavimuttiyā suṭṭhu vimuttanti kammaṭṭhānamanasikārena nīvaraṇānaṃ dūrībhāvato tehi suṭṭhu vimuttaṃ. Atthaṃ karitvāti bhāvanāmanasikāraṃ uttamaṃ katvā. “Mahā vata me ayaṃ attho uppanno”ti atthiko hutvā.

189. Paccattaṃ: This is an instrumental designation, meaning “by oneself.” Kurumāno yeva means just producing. Well liberated by liberation through the meditation subject means well liberated from the hindrances, since they are kept at a distance through attention to the meditation subject. Having made it the goal means having made the attention to development supreme. It means having become one who seeks the goal, thinking: “Truly, this great benefit has arisen for me.”

9. Paṭhamauppannasuttavaṇṇanā

9. Commentary on the First Discourse on Arising

190. Tathāgatassa pātubhāvātiādinā buddhuppādakāle eva bojjhaṅgaratanapaṭilābhoti dasseti.

190. With the phrase “the arising of the Tathāgata,” it is shown that the attainment of the jewel of the factors of awakening occurs at the very time of a Buddha’s appearance.

Pabbatavaggavaṇṇanā niṭṭhitā.

The commentary on the Chapter on the Mountain is finished.

2. Gilānavaggo

2. The Chapter on the Sick

1-3. Pāṇasuttādivaṇṇanā

1-3. Commentary on the Pāṇa Sutta and others

192-194. Yesanti [M 2.0425][V 2.0133] yesaṃ sattānaṃ. Cattāro iriyāpathā atthi labbhanti tadupagasarīrāvayavalābhena. Etanti “cattāro iriyāpathe kappentī”ti etaṃ vacanaṃ. “Vivekanissita”ntiādivacanato “sahavipassanake maggabojjhaṅge”icceva vuttaṃ. Dutiyatatiyāni uttānatthāneva heṭṭhā vuttanayattā.

192-194. Yesaṃ means: of which beings. The four postures exist and are obtainable through the acquisition of bodily parts suitable for them. This refers to the statement: “they adopt the four postures.” Because of the words beginning with “resorting to seclusion,” it is said thus: “the path factors of awakening accompanied by insight.” The second and third suttas are of clear meaning because of the method stated previously.

4-10. Paṭhamagilānasuttādivaṇṇanā

4-10. Commentary on the First Discourse on the Sick and others.

195-201. Visuddhaṃ ahosi visabhāgadhātukkhobhaṃ vūpasamentaṃ. Tenāha – “pokkharapatte …pe… vinivattitvā gato”ti. Eseva nayo pāḷito atthato ca catutthena pañcamachaṭṭhānaṃ samānattā. Visarukkhavātasamphassenāti visarukkhasannissitavātasamphassena. Mandasītajaroti muduko sītajaro. Sesanti vuttāvasesaṃ. Sabbatthāti sattamādīsu catūsu.

195-201. It became pure, calming the disturbance of the dissimilar elements. Therefore he said: “Like water on a lotus leaf … having turned back, it departed.” This is the same method, both in the text and in meaning, because the fifth and sixth suttas are similar to the fourth. By contact with wind from a poison tree means by contact with wind associated with a poison tree. A mild cool fever means a soft, cool fever. The rest means what remains of what was said. In all means in the four suttas beginning with the seventh.

Gilānavaggavaṇṇanā niṭṭhitā.

The commentary on the Chapter on the Sick is finished.

3. Udāyivaggo

3. The Udāyi Chapter

1-2. Bodhāyasuttādivaṇṇanā

1-2. Commentary on the Bodhāya Sutta and Others

202-203. Kittakena nu kho kāraṇena bujjhanakaaṅgā nāma vuccanti bujjhanakassa puggalassa aṅgāti vattabbataṃ labhanti. Missakabojjhaṅgā kathitā sahavipassanā maggabojjhaṅgā kathitāti katvā. Dhammaparicchedo kathito gaṇanāmattena paricchinditvā vuttattā na bhūmantaraparicchedo, vipassanādiparicchedo vā.

202-203. For what reason are they called ‘factors for awakening’? They are fit to be called the ‘factors of a person who is awakening.’ The mixed factors of awakening are taught on the grounds that the path factors of awakening together with insight are taught. The classification of phenomena is taught, having been defined by mere enumeration, not as a classification of other planes or a classification of insight and so on.

3-5. Ṭhāniyasuttādivaṇṇanā

3-5. Commentary on the Ṭhāniya Sutta, etc.

204-206. Kāmarāgena [V 2.0134] gadhitabbaṭṭhānabhūtā kāmarāgaṭṭhāniyāti āha “ārammaṇadhammāna”nti. “Manasikārabahulīkārā”ti vuttattā “ārammaṇeneva kathita”nti [M 2.0426] vuttaṃ. Vuttaparicchedoti etena na kevalaṃ ārammaṇavaseneva, atha kho upanissayavasenapettha attho labbhatīti dasseti. Paṭhamavaggassa hi dutiye sutte upanissayavaseneva attho dassito. Missakabojjhaṅgā kathitā avibhāgeneva kathitattā. Aparihāniyeti tīhi sikkhāhi aparihānāvahe.

204-206. Because they are a basis for being seized by sensual lust, they are called “conducive to sensual lust”; thus he says, “of the phenomena that are objects.” Because it is said, “from making attention frequent,” it is said, “it is taught only by way of the object.” The stated definition: by this it shows that the meaning here is obtained not only by way of the object but also by way of decisive support condition. For in the second sutta of the first chapter, the meaning is shown only by way of decisive support condition. The mixed factors of awakening are taught because they are taught without distinction. For non-decline means bringing about non-decline from the three trainings.

6-7. Taṇhakkhayasuttādivaṇṇanā

6-7. Commentary on the Taṇhakkhaya Sutta, etc.

207-208. “So maṃ pucchissatī”ti adhippāyena bhagavatā osāpitadesanaṃ. Patthaṭattā bhāvanāpāripūriyā vitthāritaṃ gatattā. Mahantabhāvanti bhāvanāvaseneva mahattaṃ gatattā. Tato eva vaḍḍhippamāṇā. Nīvaraṇavigame sambhavato paccayato byāpādo vigato hotīti āha – “nīvaraṇānaṃ dūrībhāvena byāpādavirahitattā”ti. Taṇhāmūlakanti taṇhāpaccayaṃ. Yañhi taṇhāsahagataṃ asahagatampi taṇhaṃ upanissāya nipphannaṃ, sabbaṃ taṃ taṇhāmūlakaṃ. Pahīyati anuppādappahānena. Taṇhādīnaṃyeva khayā, na tesaṃ saṅkhārānaṃ khayā. Etehi taṇhakkhayādipadehi.

207-208. The teaching was concluded by the Blessed One with the intention, “He will ask me.” Because it is widespread, it is explained in detail on account of the fulfillment of development. Greatness means having attained greatness by way of development. From that alone comes the measure of its growth. He says that ill will departs with the removal of the hindrances, its supporting condition, thus: “because of being without ill will due to the removal of the hindrances.” Rooted in craving means conditioned by craving. For whatever—whether associated with craving or not—is produced in dependence on craving, all that is rooted in craving. It is abandoned by the abandonment of non-arising. By the destruction of craving and so on alone, not by the destruction of those formations. By these terms: “destruction of craving,” etc.

8. Nibbedhabhāgiyasuttavaṇṇanā

8. Commentary on the Sutta Conducive to Penetration

209. Nibbijjhantīti nibbedhā, nibbijjhanadhammā dhammavinayādayo, tappariyāpannatāya nibbedhabhāge gato nibbedhabhāgiyo, taṃ nibbedhabhāgiyaṃ. Tenāha “nibbijjhanakoṭṭhāsiya”nti. Bhāvetvā ṭhitena cittena. Vipassanāmaggampi gahetvā “maggabojjhaṅgā missakā”ti vuttā. Tehīti bojjhaṅgehi bhāvitaṃ cittaṃ. Te vā bojjhaṅge bhāvetvā ṭhitaṃ cittaṃ nāma phalacittaṃ, tasmā nibbattitalokuttarameva. Tampīti phalacittampi maggānantaratāya magganissitaṃ katvā missakameva kathetuṃ vaṭṭati “bodhāya saṃvattantī”ti vuttattā.

209. Nibbedhā means they penetrate. The things that penetrate are the Dhamma and Discipline, etc. Because it is included therein, it belongs to the share of penetration; hence it is nibbedhabhāgiya, conducive to penetration. Therefore he says, “belonging to the portion of penetration.” With a mind established through development. Including also insight and path, it is said, “the path factors of awakening are mixed.” By them means the mind developed by these factors of awakening. Or, the mind that is established having developed those factors of awakening is the fruition-mind; therefore it is purely supramundane, having been produced as such. Even that—that is, the fruition-mind—since it is immediately after the path and is based on the path, it is proper to speak of it as mixed, because it is said, “they conduce to awakening.”

9. Ekadhammasuttavaṇṇanā

9. Commentary on the Ekadhamma Sutta

210. Saṃyojanasaṅkhātā vinibandhāti kāmarāgādisaṃyojanasaññitā bandhanā. Pariniṭṭhapetvā gahaṇāti gilitvā viya pariniṭṭhapetvā gahaṇākārā.

210. ‘Bonds known as fetters’ means the ties designated as fetters, such as sensual lust. ‘Grasping after having completely finished’ means a mode of grasping that brings to completion, as if by swallowing.

10. Udāyisuttavaṇṇanā

10. Commentary on the Udāyi Sutta

211. Bahukataṃ [M 2.0427][V 2.0135] vuccati bahukāro bahumāno, natthi etassa bahukatanti abahukato, akatabahumāno. Dhammo uppajjamāno ukkujjanto viya nirujjhamāno avakujjanto viya hotīti vuttaṃ “ukkujjaṃ vuccati udayo, avakujjaṃ vayo”ti. Parivattentoti aniccātipi dukkhātipi anattātipi. “Eso hi te udāyi maggo paṭiladdho, yo te…pe… tathattāya upanessatī”ti pariyosāne bhagavato vacanañcettha sādhakaṃ daṭṭhabbaṃ. Tena tenākārena viharantanti yena sammasanākārena vipassanāvihārena viharantaṃ. Tathābhāvāyāti khīṇāsavabhāvapaccavekkhaṇāya. Tenāha – “khīṇā jātīti…pe… taṃ dassento evamāhā”ti.

211. ‘Made much of’ is said of one for whom much is done, who is highly esteemed. ‘Not made much of’ means one for whom much is not done, who is not held in high esteem. The Dhamma, when arising, is like setting something upright; when ceasing, it is like overturning something. Thus it is said: ‘Setting upright is called arising; overturning, passing away.’ ‘Turning it over’ means as impermanent, as suffering, and as nonself. The Blessed One’s statement at the conclusion, ‘This, Udāyi, is the path you have obtained, which … will lead to the state of thusness,’ should be seen here as corroboration. ‘Dwelling in this or that way’ means dwelling with that mode of comprehension, with the dwelling in insight. ‘For the state of thusness’ means for reviewing the state of one whose taints are destroyed. Therefore he said: ‘“Destroyed is birth…,” showing this, he spoke thus.’

Udāyivaggavaṇṇanā niṭṭhitā.

The Commentary on the Udāyi Vagga is concluded.

4. Nīvaraṇavaggo

4. The Chapter on the Hindrances

3-4. Upakkilesasuttādivaṇṇanā

3-4. Commentary on the Upakkilesa Sutta and Others

214-215. Na ca pabhāvantanti na ca pabhāsampannaṃ. Pabhijjanasabhāvanti tāpetvā tālane pabhaṅgutaṃ. Avasesaṃ lohanti vuttāvasesaṃ jātilohaṃ, vijātilohaṃ, kittimalohanti pabhedaṃ sabbampi lohaṃ. Uppajjituṃ appadānenāti ettha nanu lokiyakusalacittassapi suvisuddhassa uppajjituṃ appadānena upakkilesatāti? Saccametaṃ, yasmiṃ pana santāne nīvaraṇāni laddhapatiṭṭhāni, tattha mahaggatakusalassapi asambhavo, pageva lokuttarakusalassa, parittakusalaṃ pana yathāpaccayaṃ uppajjati. Nīvaraṇe hi vūpasante santāne uppattiyā aparisuddhaṃ hoti, upakkiliṭṭhaṃ nāma hoti, aparisuddhadīpakapallikavaṭṭhitelādisannissayo dīpo viya, apica nippariyāyato uppajjituṃ appadāneneva tesaṃ upakkilesatāti dassento “yadaggena hī”tiādimāha. Ārammaṇe vikkhittappattivasena cuṇṇavicuṇṇatā veditabbā. Na āvarantīti kusaladhamme uppajjituṃ appadānavasena na āvaranti, atha kho tesaṃ uppattiyā honti. Na paṭicchādentīti na [M 2.0428] vinandhanti. Catubhūmakacittassāti catutthabhūmakakusalacittassa anupakkilesā, tehi akilissanato.

214-215. ‘Not radiant’ means not endowed with radiance. ‘Of a breaking nature’ means being fragile when heated and struck. ‘The remaining metals’ means all the remaining kinds of metal that have been mentioned: native metal, alloyed metal, and artificial metal. Regarding ‘by being difficult to arise,’ the question is raised: Is it not the case that even a very pure mundane wholesome mind is a defilement because of its difficulty in arising? This is true. However, in a continuum where the hindrances have gained a footing, the arising of sublime wholesome states is impossible, still more so supramundane wholesome states; but limited wholesome states arise according to conditions. For when the hindrances are not pacified in the continuum, any arising is impure; it is called defiled, like a lamp that depends on an impure wick, cloth, oil, and so on. Moreover, to show that they are defilements directly because they make arising difficult, he said, ‘By which…’ and so on. Utter fragmentation should be understood as the state of being scattered in regard to the object. ‘They do not obstruct’ means they do not obstruct wholesome states by preventing their arising; rather, they are conducive to their arising. ‘They do not cover’ means they do not destroy. ‘Of a mind of the four planes’ means the wholesome mind of the fourth plane is undefiled, because it is not defiled by them.

8. Āvaraṇanīvaraṇasuttavaṇṇanā

8. Commentary on the Sutta on Obstructions and Hindrances

219. Paññā [V 2.0136] dubbalā hoti, na balavatī paṭipakkhena upakkiliṭṭhabhāvato. Tenāha “mandā avisadā”ti.

219. Wisdom is weak, not strong, because it is defiled by opposing factors. Therefore he said: ‘Dull and unclear.’

Pañca nīvaraṇā dūre honti āvaraṇābhāvato. Tameva pītinti sappāyadhammasavane uppannaṃ pītiṃ. Tassā tadā uppannākārasallakkhaṇena avijahanto punappunaṃ tassā nibbattanena. Tenāha “pañca nīvaraṇe vikkhambhetvā”ti. Idaṃ sandhāyāti ettake divasepi na vinassanti, sā dhammapīti laddhapaccayā hutvā visesāvahāti imamatthaṃ sandhāya etaṃ “imassa pañca nīvaraṇā tasmiṃ samaye na hontī”tiādi vuttaṃ. Pītipāmojjapakkhiyāti pītipāmojjapaccayā. Nassantīti nirodhapaccayavasena pavattanato nassanti. Sabhāgapaccayavasena puna uppajjantāpi…pe… vuccati kiccasādhanavasena pavattanato.

The five hindrances are far away because of the absence of obstruction. ‘That very joy’ means the joy that arose on hearing the suitable Dhamma. By not abandoning the mark of its characteristic of having arisen then, and by repeatedly producing it. Therefore he said, ‘having suppressed the five hindrances.’ With reference to this meaning—that this joy in the Dhamma does not perish even after so many days and, having obtained its conditions, brings distinction—it was said: ‘For him, these five hindrances are not present at that time,’ and so on. ‘On the side of joy and gladness’ means because joy and gladness are its condition. ‘They cease’ means they cease because they proceed by way of the condition of cessation. Though arising again by way of a condition of a similar nature … it is said … because it proceeds by way of accomplishing its function.

9. Rukkhasuttavaṇṇanā

9. Commentary on the Tree Sutta

220. Abhiruhanakāti samīparukkhe abhibhavitvā ruhanakā. Aṭṭhikacchakoti aṭṭhibahulakacchako. Kapithanasadisaphalattā kapitthanoti laddhanāmo.

220. ‘A climber’ means one that climbs by overpowering a nearby tree. ‘A bone-thicket’ means a thicket with many bones. It gets the name ‘kapittha’ because its fruit is similar to the wood-apple.

10. Nīvaraṇasuttavaṇṇanā

10. Commentary on the Hindrances Sutta

221. Andhabhāvakaraṇā paññācakkhussa vibandhanato. Tathā hi te “acakkhukaraṇā paññānirodhikā”ti vuttā. Vihanati vibādhatīti vighāto, dukkhanti āha “vighātapakkhiyāti dukkhapakkhikā”ti. Nibbānatthāya na saṃvattantīti anibbānasaṃvattanikā. Missakabojjhaṅgāva kathitā pubbabhāgikānaṃ kathitattā.

221. Because they make for blindness, since they obstruct the eye of wisdom. For thus they are called ‘makers of non-vision, destroyers of wisdom.’ ‘It harasses, it oppresses,’ thus it is vexation, suffering; he said: ‘on the side of vexation’ means on the side of suffering. ‘They do not lead to Nibbāna,’ thus they are unconducive to Nibbāna. They are called ‘mixed factors of enlightenment’ because the preliminary factors have been spoken of.

Nīvaraṇavaggavaṇṇanā niṭṭhitā.

The Commentary on the Chapter on the Hindrances is concluded.

5. Cakkavattivaggo

5. The Chapter on the Wheel-Turning Monarch

1. Vidhāsuttavaṇṇanā

1. Commentary on the Discourse on Kinds

222. Vidhīyantīti [M 2.0429][V 2.0137] vidhā, mānādibhāgā koṭṭhāsāti āha “tayo mānakoṭṭhāsā”ti. Tathā tathā vidahanatoti “seyyohamasmī”tiādinā tena tenākārena vidahanato ṭhapanato, ṭhapetabbato vā.

222. They are arranged (vidhīyanti), thus they are 'kinds' (vidhā); that is, portions, parts such as conceit. Thus it is said: 'three portions of conceit.' And because of crushing (vidahanato) in this or that way—that is, because of establishing in this or that manner, such as 'I am better,' or because it is to be established.

2. Cakkavattisuttavaṇṇanā

2. Commentary on the Discourse on the Wheel-Turning Monarch

223. Sirisampattiyā rājati dippati sobhatīti rājā, dānapiyavacanaatthacariyāsamānattatāsaṅkhātehi catūhi saṅgahavatthūhi. Rañjetīti rameti. Abbhuggatāyāti udīritā nibbattito tattha tattha gacchanato. Cakkaṃ vattetīti cakkaratanaṃ pavatteti. Devaṭṭhānanti pūjanīyadevaṭṭhānaṃ. Cittīkataṭṭhenāti pūjanīyabhāvena. Aggho natthi cirakālasambhavapuññānubhāvasiddharatanasabbhāvato. Aññehi cakkavattino pariggahabhūtaratanehi. Loketi manussaloke. Tena tadaññalokaṃ nivatteti. Vijjamānaggahaṇena atītānāgataṃ nivatteti. Buddhā ca kadāci karahaci uppajjanti cakkavattinopi yebhuyyena tasmiṃyeva uppajjanatoti adhippāyo. Anomassāti alāmakassa ukkaṭṭhassa. Sesāni ratanāni.

223. Because he shines (rājati), gleams, and is resplendent with glorious fortune, he is a king (rājā). He delights (rañjeti) them with the four bases of beneficence: giving, endearing speech, beneficial conduct, and impartiality. To delight (rañjeti) means to please (rameti). Arisen (abbhuggatāya) means spoken of, or having come into being, it goes here and there. He turns the wheel (cakkaṃ vatteti) means he sets the wheel-treasure in motion. A divine state (devaṭṭhānaṃ) means a state worthy of reverence like that of a deity. By being esteemed (cittīkataṭṭhena) means by being worthy of reverence. It is priceless (aggho natthi) because of the nature of a treasure accomplished through the power of merit accumulated over a long time, in comparison with the other treasures that are the possessions of the wheel-turning monarch. In the world (loke) means in the human world; thereby he excludes the other worlds. By taking up ‘what is present,’ he excludes the past and the future. The meaning is that Buddhas arise only now and then, and wheel-turning monarchs for the most part arise only then. Not inferior (anomassa) means of one who is not low, of one who is supreme. The remaining treasures.

Tatrāti vākyopaññāsane nipāto, tasmiṃ pātubhāvavacane. “Ayutta”nti vatvā tattha adhippāyaṃ vivaranto “uppannaṃ hī”tiādimāha. Tehi ratanehi cakkavattananiyamāpekkhatāya cakkavattivacanassa. Niyamenāti ekantena. Vattabbataṃ āpajjati bhāvini bhūte viya upacāroti yathā – “agamā rājagahaṃ buddho”ti (su. ni. 410). Laddhanāmassāti cakkavattīti loke laddhasamaññassa patthanīyassa purisavisesassa. Mūluppattivacanatopīti “cakkavattissa pātubhāvā”ti etassa paṭhamuppattiyā vacanatopi. Idāni tamatthaṃ vivaranto “yo hī”tiādimāha. Yo hi cakkavattirājā, tassa uppattiyā cakkaratanassa uppajjanato cakkavattīti evaṃ nāmaṃ uppajjati. “Cakkaṃ vattessatī”ti idaṃ pana niyāmaṃ anapekkhitvā tassa uppajjatīti ratanānuppattiṃ [M 2.0430] gahetvā vuttanayato saññā uppajjati “cakkavattī”ti. Ekamevāti cakkaratanameva paṭhamaṃ pātubhavati. Yasmiṃ bhūte rañño cakkavattisamaññā, atha pacchā [V 2.0138] ratanāni pātubhavantīti bahūnaṃ pātubhāvaṃ upādāya bahulavacanatopi etaṃ “cakkavattissa pātubhāvā ratanānaṃ pātubhāvo”ti vuttaṃ. Ayaṃ hetukattusaññito atthabhedo. Pātubhāvāti pātubhāvato. Puññasambhāro bhinnasantānatāya ratanānampi pariyāyena upanissayahetūti vuttaṃ. Yuttamevetaṃ yathāvuttayuttiyuttattā.

The word tatra ('therein') is a particle for introducing a sentence, used here in the statement about the appearance. Having said 'unsuitable' (ayuttaṃ), explaining the meaning there he says, 'for it has arisen' (uppannaṃ hi), etc. This is because the term 'wheel-turning monarch' is dependent on the fixed law concerning those treasures. By rule (niyamena) means definitely. What is to be said is arrived at by treating the future as if it were the past, a figurative usage, as in: 'The Buddha went to Rājagaha' (Sn 410). Of one who has obtained the name (laddhanāmassa) means of a special person who is to be wished for, who has obtained the designation 'wheel-turning monarch' in the world. Also because of the statement of the original arising: that is, also because of this statement of the first arising, 'from the appearance of the wheel-turning monarch.' Now, explaining that meaning, he says, 'For whoever...' (yo hi), etc. For when a wheel-turning king arises, with his arising the wheel-treasure arises, and from this the name 'wheel-turning monarch' arises. But the idea 'he will turn the wheel' arises without regard to the fixed law; rather, the designation 'wheel-turning monarch' arises based on the arising of the treasure, according to the method stated. Only one means the wheel-treasure alone appears first. When it has appeared, the king receives the designation 'wheel-turning monarch,' and then afterwards the other treasures appear. Thus, on account of the appearance of many treasures, the plural is used when it is said: 'With the appearance of the wheel-turning monarch comes the appearance of the treasures.' This is a designated difference of meaning. Appearance (pātubhāvā) means because of the appearance. It is said that the store of merit, because of the different continuities of existence, is in turn a decisive support condition for the treasures too. This is indeed proper, because it is endowed with the reason already stated.

Vattabbabhūto adhippāyo etassa atthīti adhippāyo, atthaniddeso, saṅkhepato adhippāyo saṅkhepādhippāyo. Cakkaratanānubhāvena cakkavattissariyassa sijjhanato “dātuṃ samatthassā”ti vuttaṃ. Yojanappamāṇe padese pavattattā yojanappamāṇaṃ andhakāraṃ. Atidīghātirassatādiṃ chabbidhaṃ dosaṃ vivajjetvā ṭhitassāti vacanaseso.

An intention (adhippāyo) is a meaning that is to be stated; it is an explanation of meaning. A brief intention (saṅkhepādhippāyo) is an intention in brief. Because the sovereignty of the wheel-turning monarch is accomplished through the power of the wheel-treasure, it is said, 'of one able to give.' Because it extends over a region a yojana in measure, the darkness is a yojana in measure. The rest of the sentence is: 'of one who stands having avoided the six kinds of faults, such as being too long or too short.'

Sabbesaṃ catubhūmakadhammānaṃ purecaraṃ kusalānaṃ dhammānaṃ gatiyo samanvesanavasena pavattanato. Buddhādīhipi appahānīyatāya mahantadhammasabhāvattā dhammakāye ca jeṭṭhakaṭṭhena dhammakāyūpapannaṃ. Paññāpāsādatāya cassa uparigataṭṭhena accuggataṃ. Vitthataṭṭhena vipulaṃ. Mahantatāya mahantaṃ. Anādikālabhāvitassa kilesasantānassa khaṇeneva viddhaṃsanato sīghaṃ lahu javanti pariyāyā. Bojjhaṅgadhammapariyāpannattā hi vuttaṃ “ekanta-kusalattā”ti. Sampayuttavasena pītiyā ālokaviddhaṃsanabhāvavasenāti vuttaṃ “sahajātapaccayādī”tiādi. Sabbasaṅgāhikadhammaparicchedoti catubhūmakattā sabbasaṅgāhako bojjhaṅgadhammaparicchedo kathito.

It is a forerunner of all wholesome states of the four planes because it proceeds by way of investigating their destinations. Because it cannot be abandoned even by Buddhas and others, because it has the nature of a great Dhamma, and because it is senior in the Dhamma-body, it partakes of the Dhamma-body. Because it is a palace of wisdom, and because it is situated above, it is 'exalted.' Because it is extensive, it is 'vast.' Because it is great, it is 'great.' Because it instantly demolishes the continuum of defilements that has been developed since beginningless time, 'swift,' 'quick,' and 'fast' are synonyms. Because it is included among the dhammas that are enlightenment factors, it is said to be 'exclusively wholesome.' Because of its association with rapture and because of its nature of dispelling darkness with light, it is said, 'conascence condition, etc.' The section on all-embracing dhammas: because it pertains to the four planes, the section on the enlightenment factors is said to be all-embracing.

4-10. Duppaññasuttādivaṇṇanā

4-10. Commentary on the Duppañña Sutta, etc.

225-231. Eḷaṃ vuccati doso, eḷena mūgo viyāti eḷamūgoti imamatthaṃ dassento “mukhena vāca”ntiādimāha.

225-231. 'Eḷaṃ' is said to be a defect. One who is like a mute on account of a defect is called an 'eḷamūga.' To show this meaning, he said, 'by mouth, speech,' and so on.

Cakkavattivaggavaṇṇanā niṭṭhitā.

The commentary on the Cakkavatti Vagga is concluded.

6. Sākacchavaggo

6. The Chapter on Discussion

1. Āhārasuttavaṇṇanā

1. Explanation of the Discourse on Nourishment

232. Purimanayatoti [V 2.0139][M 2.0431] “satisambojjhaṅgaṭṭhānīyānaṃ dhammāna”ntiādinā āgatanayato. Evanti idāni vuccamānākārena. Sati ca sampajaññañca satisampajaññaṃ. Satipadhānaṃ vā abhikkantādīsu satthakabhāvapariggaṇhakañāṇaṃ satisampajaññaṃ. Taṃ sabbattha satokārībhāvāvahattā satisambojjhaṅgassa uppādāya saṃvattati. Yathā ca paccanīkadhammappahānaṃ anurūpadhammadesanā ca anuppannānaṃ kusalānaṃ dhammānaṃ uppādāya hoti, evaṃ satirahitapuggalavajjanā satokārīpuggalasevanā ca tattha ca yuttapayuttatā satisambojjhaṅgassa uppādāya hotīti imamatthaṃ dasseti “satisampajañña”ntiādinā. Arahattamaggena bhāvanāpāripūrī hoti. Tathā hi arahāva “sativepullappatto”ti vuccati.

232. "By the previous method" refers to the way it was stated earlier, "among the phenomena that are the basis for the enlightenment factor of mindfulness," etc. "Thus" means in the manner now being explained. Mindfulness and clear comprehension together are called "mindfulness-and-clear-comprehension." Alternatively, mindfulness as a foundation, or the knowledge that grasps the beneficial nature in acts like "going forward," etc., is called "mindfulness-and-clear-comprehension." Because it brings about being mindful in all actions, it conduces to the arising of the enlightenment factor of mindfulness. Just as abandoning opposing phenomena and teaching suitable phenomena lead to the arising of unarisen wholesome states, so too avoiding persons devoid of mindfulness, associating with mindful persons, and diligence therein, lead to the arising of the enlightenment factor of mindfulness. This meaning is shown by the phrase "mindfulness-and-clear-comprehension," etc. The perfection of development is achieved through the path of arahantship. For indeed, an arahant is called "one who has attained fullness of mindfulness."

Dhammānaṃ, dhammesu vā vicayo, so eva heṭṭhā vuttanayena sambojjhaṅgo, tassa dhammavicayasambojjhaṅgassa. Paripucchakatāti ācariyaṃ payirupāsitvā pañcapi nikāye sahaṭṭhakathāya pariyogāhetvā yaṃ yaṃ tattha gaṇṭhiṭṭhānaṃ, tassa tassa “idaṃ, bhante, kathaṃ imassa ko attho”ti evaṃ khandhādīsu atthapucchakabhāvo.
