Tenāha “khandha…pe… bahulatā”ti.

The investigation of phenomena, or into phenomena, that itself, as explained previously, is an enlightenment factor; of the enlightenment factor of investigation of phenomena. Being given to questioning means approaching a teacher, thoroughly mastering the five Nikāyas with their commentaries, and wherever there is a difficult passage, asking, "Venerable sir, how is this? What is the meaning of this?"—thus being one who questions about the meaning in regard to the aggregates and so on. Hence it is said: "aggregates... and so on... abundance."

Vatthuvisadakiriyāti cittacetasikānaṃ pavattiṭṭhānabhāvato sarīraṃ tappaṭibaddhāni ca cīvarādīni idha “vatthūnī”ti adhippetāni, tāni yathā cittassa sukhāvahāni honti, tathā karaṇaṃ tesaṃ visadabhāvakaraṇaṃ. Tena vuttaṃ “ajjhattikabāhirāna”ntiādi. Ussannadosanti vātādiussannadosaṃ. Sedamalamakkhitanti sedena ceva jallikāsaṅkhātena sarīramalena ca makkhitaṃ. Ca-saddena aññampi sarīrassa ca cittassa ca pīḷāvahaṃ saṅgaṇhāti. Senāsanaṃ vāti vā-saddena pattādīnaṃ saṅgaho daṭṭhabbo. Avisade sati, visayabhūte vā. Kathaṃ bhāvanamanuyuttassa tāni visayoti? Antarantarā pavattanakacittuppādavasena evaṃ vuttaṃ. Te hi cittuppādā cittekaggatāya ijjhantiyāpi aparisuddhabhāvāya saṃvattanti. Cittacetasikesu nissayādipaccayabhūtesu. Ñāṇampīti pi-saddo sampiṇḍane. Tena na kevalaṃ taṃ vatthuyeva[M 2.0432], atha kho tasmiṃ aparisuddhe ñāṇampi aparisuddhaṃ hotīti nissayāparisuddhiyā nissitāparisuddhi viya visayassa aparisuddhatāya visayīnaṃ aparisuddhiṃ dasseti anvayato byatirekato ca.

The purification of the bases refers to the body as the basis for the arising of mind and mental factors, along with the robe and other requisites connected to it—here called "bases." Their preparation in a way that brings comfort to the mind constitutes their purification. Hence, it is said, "internal and external," etc. "Excessive affliction" refers to an excess of bodily humors like wind, etc. "Smeared with sweat and dirt" means stained by sweat and the bodily filth known as grime. The word "ca" (and) also includes other things that afflict both body and mind. "Or the dwelling"—the word "vā" (or) should be understood to include the bowl, etc. When these are impure or become objects of thought, how can they be objects for one devoted to meditation? It is explained through the intermittent arising of thought-moments. Even when those thought-moments succeed in one-pointedness of mind, they still lead to impurity. This is because mind and mental factors depend on supporting conditions, etc. "Even knowledge"—the word "pi" (even) serves to combine. Thus, not only is that base itself impure, but when it is impure, knowledge dependent on it also becomes impure. Just as the impurity of the support affects the supported, so too the impurity of the object affects the subject. This is shown both by direct correlation and by contrast.

Samabhāvakaraṇaṃ [V 2.0140] kiccato anūnādhikabhāvakaraṇaṃ. Yathāpaccayaṃ saddheyyavatthusmiṃ adhimokkhakiccassa paṭutarabhāvena paññāya avisadatāya vīriyādīnañca anubalappadānasithilatādinā saddhindriyaṃ balavaṃ hoti. Tenāha “itarāni mandānī”ti. Tatoti tasmā saddhindriyassa balavabhāvato itaresañca mandattā. Kosajjapakkhe patituṃ adatvā sampayuttadhammānaṃ paggaṇhanaṃ anubalappadānaṃ paggaho, paggahakiccaṃ kātuṃ na sakkotīti sambandhitabbaṃ. Ārammaṇaṃ upagantvā ṭhānaṃ, anissajjanaṃ vā upaṭṭhānaṃ, vikkhepapaṭipakkho. Yena vā sampayuttā avikkhittā honti, so avikkhepo. Rūpagataṃ viya cakkhunā yena yāthāvato visayasabhāvaṃ passati, taṃ dassanakiccaṃ kātuṃ na sakkoti balavatā saddhindriyena abhibhūtattā. Sahajātadhammesu hi indaṭṭhaṃ karontānaṃ saha pavattamānānaṃ dhammānaṃ ekarasatāvaseneva atthasiddhi, na aññathā. Tasmāti vuttamevatthaṃ kāraṇabhāvena paccāmasati. Tanti saddhindriyaṃ.

The function of equalizing is to make things neither deficient nor excessive in function. Because of the strength of the function of resolution regarding a trustworthy object, due to the lack of sharpness in wisdom and the slackness in providing underlying support to energy and other factors, the faculty of faith becomes strong. Hence it is said, "The others are weak." That means, because of the strength of the faculty of faith and the weakness of the others. Exertion is unable to perform its function of uplifting—which consists in preventing a fall into the side of laziness and in supporting the associated states—and this is how it should be connected. Having approached the object, presence is the state of not abandoning it, the opponent of distraction. Or, that by which associated states remain undistracted is non-distraction. Just as with the eye regarding a form, wisdom is unable to perform its function of seeing the true nature of the object as it is, being overpowered by the strong faculty of faith. For when states arise together, performing the role of mastery, the fulfillment of the purpose is achieved only through the unity of their essence, not otherwise. The word 'Therefore' refers back to the very meaning stated, taking it as the reason. 'That' refers to the faculty of faith.

Dhammasabhāvapaccavekkhaṇenāti yassa saddheyyavatthuno uḷāratādiguṇe adhimuccanassa sātisayappavattiyā saddhindriyaṃ balavaṃ jātaṃ, tassa paccayapaccayuppannatādivibhāgato yāthāvato vīmaṃsanena. Evañhi evaṃdhammatānayena sabhāvasarasato pariggayhamāne savipphāro adhimokkho na hoti – “ayaṃ imesaṃ dhammānaṃ sabhāvo”ti paññābyāpārassa sātisayattā. Dhuriyadhammesu hi yathā saddhāya balavabhāve paññāya mandabhāvo hoti, evaṃ paññāya balavabhāve saddhāya mandabhāvo hoti. Tena vuttaṃ – “taṃ dhammasabhāvapaccavekkhaṇena…pe… hāpetabba”nti. Tathā amanasikaraṇenāti yenākārena bhāvanamanuyuñjantassa saddhindriyaṃ balavaṃ jātaṃ, tenākārena bhāvanaṃ nānuyuñjanenāti vuttaṃ hoti. Idha duvidhena saddhindriyassa balavabhāvo attano vā paccayavisesena kiccuttariyato vīriyādīnaṃ vā mandakiccatāya. Tattha paṭhamavikappe hāpanavidhi dassito, dutiyavikappe pana yathā manasikaroto vīriyādīnaṃ mandakiccatāya saddhindriyaṃ balavaṃ jātaṃ, tathā amanasikārena vīriyādīnaṃ paṭutarabhāvāvahena manasikārena saddhindriyaṃ tehi [M 2.0433] samataṃ karontena hāpetabbaṃ. Iminā nayena sesindriyesupi hāpanavidhi veditabbo.

"By reviewing the nature of phenomena" means reviewing according to the actual distinctions of conditions and their results for one whose faith faculty has become strong due to an excessive inclination, with heightened application, toward the noble qualities of an object worthy of faith, such as its excellence. For when the nature is grasped thoroughly in this way through the method of such conditionality, an expansive resolution does not arise—"This is the nature of these phenomena"—due to the pre-eminent function of wisdom. For in the case of the leading faculties, just as when faith is strong, wisdom is weak, so too when wisdom is strong, faith is weak. Therefore, it is said: "That should be diminished by reviewing the nature of phenomena..." "By non-attention" means that one should not engage in meditation in the way that causes the faith faculty to become strong—this is what is meant. Here, the strength of the faith faculty arises in two ways: either through its own special condition with a superior function, or through the diminished function of energy and the other faculties. In the first alternative, the method of diminishing has been shown. In the second alternative, however, just as the faith faculty becomes strong due to the diminished function of energy and the other faculties when one attends in a certain way, so too it should be diminished by making it equal with them through non-attention to that way and through attention that brings about greater effectiveness in energy and the other faculties. In this way, the method of diminishing should be understood for the remaining faculties as well.

Vakkalittheravatthūti so hi āyasmā saddhādhimutto tattha ca katādhikāro satthu rūpakāyadassane pasuto eva hutvā viharanto satthārā – “kiṃ te, vakkali, iminā pūtikāyena diṭṭhena, yo kho, vakkali, dhammaṃ passati, so maṃ passatī”tiādinā (saṃ. ni. 3.87) ovadiyamāno kammaṭṭhāne niyojitopi taṃ ananuyuñjanto paṇāmito attānaṃ [V 2.0141] vinipātetuṃ papātaṭṭhānaṃ abhiruhi. Atha naṃ satthā yathānisinnova obhāsavissajjanena attānaṃ dassetvā –

The story of the Elder Vakkali: That venerable one, being resolved by faith and having made his aspiration in that regard, remained constantly intent on seeing the Teacher’s physical body. While he was living thus, the Teacher admonished him, saying, 'What is the use to you, Vakkali, of seeing this putrid body? Whoever sees the Dhamma, Vakkali, sees me,' and so forth (SN 22.87). Although he was assigned a meditation subject, he did not apply himself to it and, being dejected, he climbed a precipice to throw himself down. Then the Teacher, while remaining seated just as he was, showed himself by emitting a radiance and...

“Pāmojjabahulo bhikkhu, pasanno buddhasāsane;

Adhigacche padaṃ santaṃ, saṅkhārūpasamaṃ sukha”nti. (dha. pa. 381) –

"Full of joy, the monk, confident in the Buddha's teaching, may attain the peaceful state, the bliss of the stilling of formations." (Dhp 381)

Gāthaṃ vatvā “ehi, vakkalī”ti āha. So tena vacanena amateneva abhisitto haṭṭhatuṭṭho hutvā vipassanaṃ paṭṭhapesi, saddhāya bahulabhāvato vipassanāvīthiṃ nārohati. Taṃ ñatvā bhagavā indriyasamattapaṭipādanāya kammaṭṭhānaṃ sodhetvā adāsi. So satthārā dinnanayena vipassanaṃ ussukkāpetvā maggapaṭipāṭiyā arahattaṃ pāpuṇi. Tena vuttaṃ – “vakkalittheravatthu cettha nidassana”nti. Etthāti saddhindriyassa adhimattabhāve sesindriyānaṃ sakiccākaraṇe.

Having recited the verse, he said, "Come, Vakkali." By that utterance, he was as if anointed with the deathless and, filled with joy and delight, began to establish insight. Because of the abundance of faith, he did not ascend the path of insight. Knowing this, the Blessed One purified the meditation subject to bring about a balance of the faculties and gave it to him. He, having striven at insight meditation in accordance with the method given by the Teacher, attained arahantship in due order of the path. Therefore, it was said: "Here, the story of the Elder Vakkali is an illustration." Here, it refers to the case when the faculty of faith is excessive and the other faculties fail to fulfill their functions.

Itarakiccabhedanti upaṭṭhānādikiccavisesaṃ. Passaddhādīti ādi-saddena samādhiupekkhāsambojjhaṅgānaṃ saṅgaho daṭṭhabbo. Hāpetabbanti yathā saddhindriyassa balavabhāvo dhammasabhāvapaccavekkhaṇena hāyati, evaṃ vīriyindriyassa adhimattatā passaddhiyādibhāvanāya hāyati samādhipakkhikattā tassā. Tathā hi samādhindriyassa adhimattataṃ kosajjapātato rakkhantī vīriyādibhāvanā viya vīriyindriyassa adhimattataṃ uddhaccapātato rakkhantī ekaṃsato hāpeti. Tena vuttaṃ “passaddhādibhāvanāya hāpetabba”nti. Soṇattherassa vatthūti sukumārasoṇattherassa vatthu. So hi āyasmāpi satthu santikā kammaṭṭhānaṃ gahetvā sītavane viharanto – “mama sarīraṃ sukhumālaṃ, na ca sakkā sukheneva sukhaṃ adhigantuṃ, kāyaṃ kilametvāpi samaṇadhammo kātabbo”ti ṭhānacaṅkamanameva adhiṭṭhāya [M 2.0434] padhānamanuyuñjanto pādatalesu phoṭesu uṭṭhitesupi vedanaṃ ajjhupekkhitvā daḷhavīriyaṃ karonto accāraddhavīriyatāya visesaṃ pavattetuṃ nāsakkhi. Satthā tattha gantvā vīṇopamovādena ovaditvā vīriyasamatāyojanavidhiṃ dassento kammaṭṭhānaṃ sodhetvā gijjhakūṭaṃ gato. Theropi satthārā dinnanayena vīriyasamataṃ yājetvā bhāvento vipassanaṃ ussukkāpetvā arahatteva patiṭṭhāsi. Tena vuttaṃ “soṇattherassa vatthu dassetabba”nti. Sesesupīti satisamādhipaññindriyesupi.

The differentiation of functions refers to the specific duties such as attending, etc. Regarding `Passaddhādīti` (tranquility, etc.): by the word `ādi` (etc.), the inclusion of the enlightenment factors of concentration and equanimity should be understood. Regarding `Hāpetabba` (it should be diminished): just as the strength of the faculty of faith is diminished through reflection on the nature of phenomena, so too the excessiveness of the faculty of energy is diminished through the development of tranquility and the other factors, because tranquility belongs to the side of concentration. For just as the development of energy and other factors protects the faculty of concentration from falling into laziness, so the development of tranquility and other factors protects the faculty of energy from falling into restlessness and decisively diminishes it. Hence, it is said, 'It should be diminished through the development of tranquility, etc.' Regarding `Soṇattherassa vatthu` (the story of the Elder Soṇa): this is the story of the Elder Soṇa the Delicate. For he, the venerable one, after receiving a meditation subject from the Teacher, dwelt in the Cool Wood, thinking, 'My body is delicate, and it is not possible to attain happiness with ease. The ascetic's duty must be performed even by exhausting the body.' Resolving only on standing and walking meditation and applying himself to striving, even when blisters arose on the soles of his feet, he disregarded the pain and made a strong effort, but could not achieve any distinction due to his excessively exerted energy. The Teacher went there and, advising him with the simile of the lute, showed him the method of balancing energy. After purifying the meditation subject, he went to the Vulture's Peak. The elder, following the method given by the Teacher, balanced his energy, and by developing it, made insight surge and became established in arahantship. Hence, it is said, 'The story of the Elder Soṇa should be shown.' Regarding `Sesesupi` (in the remaining cases too): likewise, in the case of the faculties of mindfulness, concentration, and wisdom.

Samatanti saddhāpaññānaṃ aññamaññaṃ anūnādhikabhāvaṃ, tathā samādhivīriyānañca. Yathā hi saddhāpaññānaṃ visuṃ visuṃ dhuriyadhammabhūtānaṃ kiccato aññamaññanātivattanaṃ visesato icchitabbaṃ. Yato tesaṃ samadhuratāya appanā sampajjati, evaṃ samādhivīriyānaṃ kosajjuddhaccapakkhikānaṃ [V 2.0142] samatāya sati aññamaññupatthambhanato sampayuttadhammānaṃ antadvayapātābhāvena sammadeva appanā ijjhatīti. Balavasaddhotiādi vuttasseva atthassa byatirekamukhena samatthanaṃ. Tassattho – yo balavatiyā saddhāya samannāgato avisadañāṇo, so mudhappasanno hoti, na aveccappasanno. Tathā hi so avatthusmiṃ pasīdati, seyyathāpi titthiyasāvakā. Kerāṭikapakkhanti sāṭheyyapakkhaṃ bhajati. Saddhāhīnāya paññāya atidhāvanto “deyyavatthupariccāgena vinā cittuppādamattenapi dānamayaṃ puññaṃ hotī”tiādīni parikappeti hetupatirūpakehi vañcito, evaṃbhūto ca lūkhatakkaviluttacitto paṇḍitānaṃ vacanaṃ nādiyati, saññattiṃ na gacchati. Tenāha “bhesajjasamuṭṭhito viya rogo atekiccho hotī”ti. Yathā cettha saddhāpaññānaṃ aññamaññaṃ samabhāvo atthāvaho, visamabhāvo anatthāvaho, evaṃ samādhivīriyānaṃ aññamaññaṃ samabhāvo atthāvaho, itaro anatthāvaho, tathā samabhāvo avikkhepāvaho, itaro vikkhepāvaho. Kosajjaṃ abhibhavati, tena appanaṃ na pāpuṇātīti adhippāyo. Esa nayo uddhaccaṃ abhibhavatīti etthāpi. Tadubhayanti saddhāpaññādvayaṃ samādhivīriyadvayañca. Samaṃ kātabbanti samataṃ kātabbaṃ.

Balance (`samatā`) means the state of faith and wisdom not being deficient or excessive in relation to each other; likewise for concentration and energy. For just as it is especially desirable that faith and wisdom, which are individually leading states, should not functionally surpass one another—since absorption is accomplished by their balanced yoking—so too, when concentration and energy, which belong to the sides of laziness and restlessness respectively, are in balance, absorption is properly achieved because they support one another and their associated states do not fall into either extreme. 'One with strong faith,' etc., is a confirmation of what has been said by way of contrast. The meaning is this: one who is endowed with strong faith but has unclear knowledge becomes blindly confident, not confidently convinced. For such a person takes confidence in what is not a proper basis, like the disciples of the sectarians. He resorts to the side of deceit (`sāṭheyya`), the Kerāṭika faction. One who rushes ahead with wisdom deficient in faith, deceived by specious reasons, speculates such things as, 'Merit consisting in giving can be made by a mere thought-moment, without the relinquishing of any gift.' Such a person, with a mind corrupted by coarse logic, does not heed the word of the wise and does not come to a conviction. Therefore it is said: 'Like a disease arisen from the medicine itself, it becomes incurable.' Just as in this case the balance between faith and wisdom is beneficial and their imbalance is harmful, so too the balance between concentration and energy is beneficial and the alternative is harmful. Likewise, balance leads to non-distraction, while the alternative leads to distraction. Laziness overpowers, and for that reason one does not attain absorption—this is the intention. This same method applies where restlessness overpowers. By 'both' (`tadubhayaṃ`) is meant the pair of faith and wisdom and the pair of concentration and energy. 'Should be made equal' (`samaṃ kātabbaṃ`) means that balance should be established.

Samādhikammikassāti samathakammaṭṭhānikassa. Evanti evaṃ sante, saddhāya thokaṃ balavabhāve satīti attho. Saddahantoti “pathavī pathavīti manasikāramattena [M 2.0435] kathaṃ jhānuppattī”ti acintetvā “addhā sambuddhena vuttavidhi ijjhatī”ti saddahanto saddhaṃ janento. Okappentoti ārammaṇaṃ anupavisitvā viya adhimuccanavasena avakappento pakkhandanto. Ekaggatā balavatī vaṭṭati samādhipadhānattā jhānassa. Ubhinnanti samādhipaññānaṃ. Samādhikammikassa samādhino adhimattatāya paññāya adhimattatāpi icchitabbāti āha “samatāyapī”ti, samabhāvenāpīti attho. Appanāti lokiyaappanā. Tathā hi “hotiyevā”ti sāsaṅkaṃ vadati, lokuttarappanā pana tesaṃ samabhāveneva icchitā. Yathāha “samathavipassanaṃ yuganaddhaṃ bhāvetī”ti (a. ni. 4.170). Yadi visesato saddhāpaññānaṃ samādhivīriyānañca samānataṃ icchati, kathaṃ satīti āha – “sati pana sabbattha balavatī vaṭṭatī”ti. Sabbatthāti līnuddhaccapakkhikesu pañcindriyesu. Uddhaccapakkhikekadese gaṇhanto “saddhāvīriyapaññāna”nti āha. Aññathā pīti ca gahetabbā siyā. Tathā hi “kosajjapakkhikena samādhinā”icceva vuttaṃ, na ca “passaddhisamādhiupekkhāhī”ti. Sāti sati. Sabbesu rājakammesu niyuttoti sabbakammiko. Tenāti yena kāraṇena sabbattha icchitabbā, tena āha aṭṭhakathāyaṃ. Sabbattha niyuttā sabbatthikā[V 2.0143], sabbena vā līnuddhaccapakkhikena bojjhaṅgena atthetabbā sabbatthiyā, sabbatthiyāva sabbatthikā. Cittanti kusalacittaṃ. Tassa hi satipaṭisaraṇaṃ parāyaṇaṃ appattassa pattiyā anadhigatassa adhigamāya. Tenāha – “ārakkhapaccupaṭṭhānā”tiādi.

Of one engaged in the work of concentration (`samādhikammikassa`) means of one practicing a serenity meditation subject. `Thus` (`evaṃ`) means: this being so, the meaning is that faith is slightly strong. `Believing` (`saddahanto`) means: without thinking, 'How can jhāna arise by merely attending to "earth, earth"?', one generates faith, believing, 'Surely the method taught by the Fully Enlightened One succeeds.' `Trusting` (`okappento`) means: resolving upon the object as if entering into it, one plunges into it. One-pointedness becomes strong and is suitable because jhāna has concentration as its chief. `Of both` (`ubhinnaṃ`) means of concentration and wisdom. For one engaged in the work of concentration, since concentration is predominant, it is desirable that wisdom also be predominant. Thus he says, `even in balance` (`samatāyapi`), meaning, even in a state of balance. `Absorption` (`appanā`) means mundane absorption. For he says with some doubt, 'It may be.' But supramundane absorption is desired for them only when they are in balance. As it is said: 'He develops serenity and insight yoked together' (AN 4.170). If one especially desires equality between faith and wisdom, and between concentration and energy, what about mindfulness? He says: 'But mindfulness is strong and is suitable everywhere.' `Everywhere` means among the five faculties belonging to the sides of laxity and agitation. Taking one part of the side of agitation, he says, 'faith, energy, and wisdom.' Otherwise, joy (`pīti`) should also be taken. For it is said only 'with concentration belonging to the side of laziness,' and not 'with tranquility, concentration, and equanimity.' `That` (`sā`) is mindfulness (`sati`). Engaged in all the king's affairs (`sabbesu rājakammesu niyutto`) means an all-tasks minister (`sabbakammiko`). `Therefore` (`tena`): for the reason that it is desirable everywhere, the commentary explains thus. Engaged everywhere (`sabbattha niyuttā`) is `sabbatthikā`. Or, to be sought for (`atthetabbā`) with every enlightenment factor, whether on the side of laxity or of agitation, is `sabbatthiyā`; `sabbatthiyā` is `sabbatthikā` (universally desirable). `Mind` (`cittaṃ`) means wholesome mind. For that mind, mindfulness is the resort, the final destination, for attaining the unattained, for realizing the unrealized. Hence he says: 'Its manifestation is guarding,' etc.

Khandhādibhede anogāḷhapaññānanti pariyattibāhusaccavasenapi khandhāyatanādīsu appatiṭṭhitabuddhīnaṃ. Bahussutasevanā hi sutamayañāṇāvahā. Taruṇavipassanāsamaṅgīpi bhāvanāmayañāṇe ṭhitattā ekaṃsato paññavā eva nāma hotīti āha – “samapaññāsa…pe… puggalasevanā”ti. Ñeyyadhammassa gambhīrabhāvavasena tapparicchedakañāṇassa gambhīrabhāvagahaṇanti āha – “gambhīresu khandhādīsu pavattāya gambhīrapaññāyā”ti. Tañhi ñeyyaṃ tādisāya paññāya caritabbato gambhīrañāṇacariyaṃ, tassā vā paññāya tattha pabhedato pavatti gambhīrañāṇacariyā, tassā paccavekkhaṇāti āha “gambhīrapaññāya pabhedapaccavekkhaṇā”ti. Yathā sativepullappatto nāma arahā eva, evaṃ so eva paññāvepullappattopīti āha [M 2.0436] “arahattamaggena bhāvanāpāripūrī hotī”ti. Vīriyādīsupi eseva nayoti.

Of those whose wisdom is not immersed in the analysis of the aggregates, etc. (`Khandhādibhede anogāḷhapaññānaṃ`): this means of those whose intelligence is not established in the aggregates, bases, etc., even by way of scriptural learning and great learning. For associating with the very learned brings knowledge based on hearing. Even one endowed with tender insight, because of being established in knowledge born of development, is definitely called wise. Thus it is said: 'association with a person... equal in wisdom.' Because of the profundity of the knowable phenomenon, the knowledge that defines it is taken as profound. Thus it is said: 'with profound wisdom that proceeds upon the profound aggregates, etc.' For that knowable thing, since it is to be practiced with such wisdom, is the practice of profound knowledge. Or the operation of that wisdom there by way of its analysis is the practice of profound knowledge; its reviewing. Thus it is said: 'the reviewing of the analysis by profound wisdom.' Just as one who has reached the fullness of mindfulness is an arahant, so too is one who has reached the fullness of wisdom. Thus it is said: 'by the path of arahantship the development reaches fulfillment.' This same method applies to energy and the other factors too.

“Tattaṃ ayokhilaṃ hatthe gamentī”tiādinā vuttapañcavidhabandhanakammakāraṇā niraye nibbattasattassa sabbapaṭhamaṃ karontīti devadūtasuttādīsu (ma. ni. 3.250), tassā ādito vuttattā ca āha – “pañcavidhabandhanakammakāraṇato paṭṭhāyā”ti. Sakaṭavahanādikāleti ādi-saddena tadaññamanussehi tiracchānehi ca vibādhanīyakālaṃ saṅgaṇhāti. Ekaṃ buddhantaranti idaṃ aparāparaṃ petesu eva uppajjanakasattavasena vuttaṃ, ekaccānaṃ vā petānaṃ, ekaccatiracchānānaṃ viya tathā dīghāyukatāpi siyāti tathā vuttaṃ. Tathā hi kālo nāgarājā catunnaṃ buddhānaṃ rūpadassāvī.

The action of the fivefold binding, spoken of by way of the phrase 'They make him grasp a heated iron stake in his hand,' etc., is what they do first of all to a being reborn in hell, as stated in the Devadūta Sutta and others (MN 130). And because this is stated from the beginning, he says: 'starting from the action of the fivefold binding.' At the time of pulling carts, etc.: by the word 'etc.' he includes the time of being harassed by other humans and by animals. One Buddha-interval: this is said with reference to a being who is reborn again and again only among the petas. Or it is said thus because some petas, like some animals, might have such a long lifespan. Thus the nāga king Kāla was a seer of the forms of four Buddhas.

Evaṃ ānisaṃsadassāvinoti “vīriyāyatto eva sakalalokiyalokuttaravisesādhigamo”ti evaṃ ānisaṃsadassanasīlassa. Gamanavīthinti sapubbabhāgaṃ nibbānagāminiṃ paṭipadaṃ. Saha vipassanāya ariyamaggapaṭipāṭi, sattavisuddhiparamparā vā. Sā hi vaṭṭato niyyānāya gantabbā paṭipadāti katvā gamanavīthi nāma.

Of one who sees the benefit thus (`Evaṃ ānisaṃsadassāvino`): of one who is by nature one who sees the benefit thus: 'The attainment of all worldly and supramundane distinction is dependent solely on energy.' The path of progress (`gamanavīthi`): the path leading to Nibbāna together with its preliminary part. It is the order of the noble path together with insight, or the succession of the seven purifications. For since it is the path to be travelled for escape from the round of existence, it is called the 'path of progress'.

Kāyadaḷhībahuloti kāyassa posanapasuto. Piṇḍanti raṭṭhapiṇḍaṃ. Paccayadāyakānaṃ attani kārassa attano sammāpaṭipattiyā mahapphalabhāvassa karaṇena piṇḍāya bhikkhāya paṭipūjanā piṇḍāpacāyanā. Nīharantoti pattatthavikato nīharanto. Taṃ saddaṃ sutvāti taṃ upāsikāya vacanaṃ paṇṇasāladvāre ṭhitova pañcābhiññatāya dibbasotena sutvāti vadanti. Manussasampatti, dibbasampatti[V 2.0144], ante nibbānasampattīti tisso sampattiyo. Sitaṃ karontovāti “akiccheneva mayā vaṭṭadukkhaṃ samatikkanta”nti paccavekkhaṇāvasāne sañjātapāmojjavasena sitaṃ karonto eva.

Being devoted to strengthening the body means being intent on nourishing the body. Alms means the alms from the country. Honoring the almsfood is the veneration paid to the alms received by making one's own right practice greatly fruitful for the service rendered by the donors of requisites. Taking it out means taking it out from the bowl-stand. Hearing that sound refers to hearing the laywoman's words with the divine ear, one of the five higher knowledges, while standing at the door of the leaf hut; so they say. Human attainment, divine attainment, and the ultimate attainment of Nibbāna—these are the three attainments. Making a smile means smiling with joy that arose at the conclusion of reflecting: 'Without difficulty, I have transcended the suffering of the round.'

Alasānaṃ bhāvanāya nāmamattampi ajānantānaṃ kāyassa posanabahulānaṃ yāvadatthaṃ paribhuñjitvā seyyasukhādiṃ anuyuñjantānaṃ tiracchānakathikānaṃ dūratova vajjanaṃ kusītapuggalaparivajjanā. “Divasaṃ caṅkamena nisajjāyā”tiādinā bhāvanārambhavasena āraddhavīriyānaṃ daḷhaparakkamānaṃ kālenakālaṃ [M 2.0437] upasaṅkamanā āraddhavīriyapuggalasevanā. Tenāha “kucchiṃ pūretvā”tiādi.

The avoidance of lazy persons is the avoidance from afar of those who engage in animal talk—those who are lazy, who do not know even the name of meditation, who are devoted to nourishing the body, who, having eaten their fill, pursue the pleasure of lying down and so on. Associating with energetic persons is approaching from time to time those who have aroused energy and are of steadfast exertion, who have undertaken meditation as stated in the passage beginning, 'By day with walking and sitting.' Therefore, it was said, 'having filled the belly,' etc.

Visuddhimagge pana “jātimahattapaccavekkhaṇā, sabrahmacārimahattapaccavekkhaṇā”ti idaṃ dvayaṃ na gahitaṃ, “thinamiddhavinodanatā, sammappadhānapaccavekkhaṇā”ti idaṃ dvayaṃ gahitaṃ. Tattha ānisaṃsadassāvitāya eva sammappadhānapaccavekkhaṇā gahitā lokiyalokuttaravisesādhigamassa vīriyāyattatādassanabhāvato. Thinamiddhavinodanaṃ tadadhimuttatāya gahitaṃ, vīriyuppādane yuttapayuttassa thinamiddhavinodanaṃ atthato siddhameva. Tattha thinamiddhavinodanaṃ kusītapuggalaparivajjana-āraddhavīriyapuggala-sevana- tadadhimuttatāpaṭipakkhavidhamana-paccayūpasaṃhāravasena, apāyabhayapaccavekkhaṇādayo samuttejanavasena vīriyasambojjhaṅgassa uppādakāti daṭṭhabbā.

In the Visuddhimagga, however, these two—'reflection on the greatness of birth' and 'reflection on the greatness of one's companions in the holy life'—are not included; but these two—'dispelling sloth and torpor' and 'reflection on the right efforts'—are included. There, 'reflection on the right efforts' is included because it reveals the benefits, for it shows that the attainment of worldly and supramundane distinctions is dependent on energy. 'Dispelling sloth and torpor' is included because of the inclination towards that, since for one who is properly and repeatedly engaged in arousing energy, the dispelling of sloth and torpor is in effect accomplished. Herein, it should be understood that dispelling sloth and torpor—by way of avoiding lazy persons, associating with energetic persons, being inclined towards that, dispelling opposing factors, and attending to the conditions—and reflections such as that on the fear of the planes of misery—by way of rousing—are producers of the enlightenment factor of energy.

Buddhānussatiyā upacārasamādhiniṭṭhattā vuttaṃ “yāva upacārā”ti. Sakalasarīraṃ pharamānoti pītisamuṭṭhānehi paṇītarūpehi sakalasarīraṃ pharamāno. Dhammasaṅghaguṇe anussarantassapi yāva upacārā sakalasarīraṃ pharamāno pītisambojjhaṅgo uppajjatīti yojanā. Evaṃ sesaanussatīsu pasādanīyasuttantapaccavekkhaṇāya ca yojetabbaṃ tassāpi vimuttāyatanabhāvena taggatikattā. Evarūpe kāleti dubbhikkhabhayādīsūti vuttakāle. Samāpattiyā…pe… na samudācarantīti idaṃ upasamānussatiyā vasena vuttaṃ. Saṅkhārānañhi sappadesavūpasamepi nippadesavūpasame viya tattha sapaññāya pavattanato bhāvanāmanasikāro kilesavikkhambhanasamattho hutvā upacārasamādhiṃ āvahanto tathārūpapītisomanassasamannāgato pītisambojjhaṅgassa uppādāya hotīti. Pasādanīyesu ṭhānesu pasādasinehābhāvena saṃsūcitahadayatā lūkhatā. Sā ca tattha ādaragāravākaraṇena viññāyatīti āha “asakkaccakiriyāya saṃsūcitalūkhabhāve”ti.

Because access concentration is established through the recollection of the Buddha, it is said, 'up to access.' Permeating the entire body means permeating the entire body with the refined forms that are the source of joy. The construction is: for one recollecting the qualities of the Dhamma and the Sangha too, the enlightenment factor of joy arises, permeating the entire body up to access concentration. This should be applied similarly to the remaining recollections and to the reflection on inspiring suttas, for that too is a basis for liberation because it has that as its destination. At such a time means: at the aforementioned time of famine, fear, and so on. 'One does not... engage in attainment'—this is said with reference to the recollection of peace. For, because of proceeding with wisdom therein, even in the partial calming of formations—just as in the complete calming—the attention of development, having become capable of suppressing the defilements, brings about access concentration and, being endowed with such joy and gladness, becomes a condition for the arising of the enlightenment factor of joy. In inspiring situations, coarseness is a dried-up heart due to the absence of faith and affection. And since this is known by the failure to act with care and reverence, it is said: 'in a state of coarseness indicated by careless action.'

Kāyacittadarathavūpasamalakkhaṇā passaddhi eva yathāvuttabodhiaṅgabhūto passaddhisambojjhaṅgo, tassa [V 2.0145] passaddhisambojjhaṅgassa. Paṇītabhojanasevanatāti paṇītasappāyabhojanasevanatā. Utuiriyāpathasukhaggahaṇehi sappāyautuiriyāpathaṃ gahitanti daṭṭhabbaṃ. Tañhi tividhampi sappāyaṃ seviyamānaṃ kāyassa kallatāpādanavasena cittassa kallataṃ [M 2.0438] āvahantaṃ duvidhāyapi passaddhiyā kāraṇaṃ hoti. Sattesu labbhamānaṃ sukhadukkhaṃ ahetukanti ayameko anto, issarādivisamahetukanti ayaṃ dutiyo, ete ubho ante anupagamma yathāsakaṃ kammunā hotīti ayaṃ majjhimā paṭipatti. Majjhatto payogo yassa so majjhattapayogo, tassa bhāvo majjhattapayogatā. Ayañhi pahānasāraddhakāyatā-saṅkhātapassaddhakāyatāya kāraṇaṃ hontī passaddhidvayaṃ āvahati. Eteneva sāraddhakāyapuggalaparivajjana-passaddhakāyapuggalasevanānaṃ tadāvahanatā saṃvaṇṇitāti daṭṭhabbaṃ.

Tranquility, which has the characteristic of calming bodily and mental distress, is itself the enlightenment factor of tranquility, a factor of awakening as stated. Of that tranquility enlightenment factor... Consuming refined food means consuming refined and suitable food. By adopting a suitable climate and posture: it should be understood that a suitable climate and posture are adopted. For when these three kinds of suitable things are used, by producing fitness of body they bring about fitness of mind, thus becoming a cause for both kinds of tranquility. That the pleasure and pain found in beings are without a cause is one extreme; that they are due to an unequal cause such as a creator god is the second extreme. Without approaching either of these two extremes, the middle practice is that they occur according to one's own kamma. One whose application is neutral has balanced application; the state of this is the quality of balanced application. For this balanced application, being a cause for the tranquil state of the body—which is known as the getting rid of bodily agitation—brings about the twofold tranquility. It should be understood that by this, the bringing about of tranquility by avoiding persons with agitated bodies and associating with persons with tranquil bodies is explained.

Vatthuvisadakiriyā indriyasamattapaṭipādanā ca “paññāvahā”ti vuttā. Samathāvahāpi tā honti samathāvahabhāveneva paññāvahattāti vuttaṃ “vatthuvisada…pe… veditabbā”ti.

Making the basis clear and balancing the faculties are said to be 'conducive to wisdom.' They are also conducive to serenity, and they are said to be conducive to wisdom precisely because of their being conducive to serenity, as stated: 'Making the basis clear... should be understood.'

Karaṇakosallabhāvanākosallānaṃ nānantariyabhāvato rakkhaṇakosallassa ca taṃmūlakattā “nimittakusalatā nāma kasiṇanimittassa uggahaṇakusalatā”icceva vuttaṃ. Atisithilavīriyatādīhīti ādi-saddena paññāpayogamandataṃ appamādavekallañca saṅgaṇhāti. Tassa paggaṇhananti tassa līnassa cittassa dhammavicayasambojjhaṅgādisamuṭṭhāpanena layāpattito samuṭṭhāpanaṃ. Vuttañhetaṃ bhagavatā –

Because skill in doing and skill in developing are not separate, and because skill in protecting is rooted in them, it is simply said: 'Skill in the sign means skill in grasping the kasiṇa sign.' Due to excessively slack energy, etc.: by the word 'etc.' it includes slowness in the application of wisdom and a lack of diligence. Lifting it up means rousing that sluggish mind from its state of sluggishness by arousing the enlightenment factor of investigation of phenomena and others. For this was said by the Blessed One:

“Yasmiñca kho, bhikkhave, samaye līnaṃ cittaṃ hoti, kālo tasmiṃ samaye dhammavicayasambojjhaṅgassa bhāvanāya, kālo vīriyasambojjhaṅgassa bhāvanāya, kālo pītisambojjhaṅgassa bhāvanāya. Taṃ kissa hetu? Līnaṃ, bhikkhave, cittaṃ, taṃ etehi dhammehi susamuṭṭhāpayaṃ hoti. Seyyathāpi, bhikkhave, puriso parittaṃ aggiṃ ujjāletukāmo assa, so tattha sukkhāni ceva tiṇāni pakkhipeyya, sukkhāni gomayāni pakkhipeyya, sukkhāni kaṭṭhāni pakkhipeyya, mukhavātañca dadeyya, na ca paṃsukena okireyya, bhabbo nu kho so puriso parittaṃ aggiṃ ujjāletunti. Evaṃ, bhante”ti (saṃ. ni. 5.234).

'Bhikkhus, on an occasion when the mind is sluggish, that is the proper time to develop the enlightenment factor of investigation of phenomena, the proper time to develop the enlightenment factor of energy, the proper time to develop the enlightenment factor of joy. For what reason? A sluggish mind, bhikkhus, is easy to arouse by means of these states. Suppose, bhikkhus, a man wanted to make a small fire blaze up. If he were to put on it dry grass, dry cow-dung, and dry sticks, and to give it a puff of air with his mouth, and not to sprinkle it with dust, would that man be able to make that small fire blaze up?' 'Yes, venerable sir.'

Ettha ca yathāsakaṃ āhāravasena dhammavicayasambojjhaṅgādīnaṃ bhāvanā samuṭṭhāpanāti veditabbā, sā anantaraṃ vibhāvitā eva.

Here it should be understood that the development of the enlightenment factor of investigation of phenomena and the others, by way of their respective nutriments, is the arousing of the mind. This was explained in detail immediately before.

Accāraddhavīriyatādīhīti [M 2.0439][V 2.0146] ādi-saddena paññāpayogabalavataṃ pamoduppilāvanañca saṅgaṇhāti. Tassa niggaṇhananti tassa uddhatassa cittassa samādhisambojjhaṅgādisamuṭṭhāpanena uddhatāpattito nisedhanaṃ. Vuttampi cetaṃ bhagavatā –

Due to excessively aroused energy, etc.: by the word 'etc.' it includes powerfulness in the application of wisdom and excessive elation. Restraining it means checking that agitated mind from the fault of agitation by arousing the enlightenment factor of concentration and others. This too has been said by the Blessed One:

“Yasmiñca kho, bhikkhave, samaye uddhataṃ cittaṃ hoti, kālo tasmiṃ samaye passaddhisambojjhaṅgassa bhāvanāya, kālo samādhisambojjhaṅgassa bhāvanāya, kālo upekkhāsambojjhaṅgassa bhāvanāya. Taṃ kissa hetu? Uddhataṃ, bhikkhave, cittaṃ, taṃ etehi dhammehi suvūpasamayaṃ hoti. Seyyathāpi, bhikkhave, puriso mahantaṃ aggikkhandhaṃ nibbāpetukāmo assa, so tattha allāni ceva tiṇāni….pe… paṃsukena ca okireyya, bhabbo nu kho so puriso mahantaṃ aggikkhandhaṃ nibbāpetunti. Evaṃ, bhante”ti (saṃ. ni. 5.234).

'Bhikkhus, on an occasion when the mind is restless, that is the proper time to develop the enlightenment factor of tranquility, the proper time to develop the enlightenment factor of concentration, the proper time to develop the enlightenment factor of equanimity. For what reason? A restless mind, bhikkhus, is easy to calm by means of these states. Suppose, bhikkhus, a man wanted to extinguish a great mass of fire. If he were to put on it wet grass... and to sprinkle it with dust, would that man be able to extinguish that great mass of fire?' 'Yes, venerable sir.'

Etthāpi yathāsakaṃ āhāravasena passaddhisambojjhaṅgādīnaṃ bhāvanā samuṭṭhāpanāti veditabbā. Tattha passaddhisambojjhaṅgassa bhāvanā vuttā eva, samādhisambojjhaṅgassa vuccamānā, itarassa anantaraṃ vakkhati. Paññāpayogamandatāyāti paññābyāpārassa appabhāvena. Yathā hi dānaṃ alobhappadhānaṃ, sīlaṃ adosappadhānaṃ, evaṃ bhāvanā amohappadhānā. Tattha yadā paññā na balavatī hoti, tadā bhāvanā pubbenāparaṃ visesāvahā na hoti, anabhisaṅkhato viya āhāro purisassa yogino cittassa abhiruciṃ na uppādeti, tena taṃ nirassādaṃ hoti. Tathā bhāvanāya sammadeva vīthipaṭipattiyā abhāvena upasamasukhaṃ na vindati, tenapi cittaṃ nirassādaṃ hoti. Tena vuttaṃ “paññāpayoga…pe… nirassādaṃ hotī”ti.

Here too, the development of the enlightenment factors such as tranquility, by way of their respective nutriments, should be understood as their arousing. Therein, the development of the enlightenment factor of tranquility has been stated; that of the enlightenment factor of concentration is being stated; that of the other will be stated next. Due to slowness in the application of wisdom means: due to the weakness of the activity of wisdom. For just as giving is predominated by non-greed and virtue by non-hatred, so development is predominated by non-delusion. Therein, when wisdom is not strong, the development does not bring about successive distinction; like unprepared food, it does not produce a liking in the yogi's mind, and thus it becomes tasteless. Similarly, due to the absence of a truly right path of practice in the development, one does not find the happiness of peace, and for that reason too the mind becomes tasteless. Therefore it was said: 'Due to the slowness of wisdom's application... it becomes tasteless.'

Tassa saṃveguppādanañca pasāduppādanañca tikicchananti taṃ dassento “aṭṭha saṃvegavatthūnī”tiādimāha. Tattha jātijarābyādhimaraṇāni yathārahaṃ sugatiyaṃ duggatiyañca hontīti tadaññameva pañcavidhabandhanādikhuppipāsādiaññamaññavibādhanādihetukaṃ apāyadukkhaṃ daṭṭhabbaṃ. Tayidaṃ sabbaṃ tesaṃ tesaṃ sattānaṃ paccuppannabhavanissitaṃ gahitanti atīte anāgate ca kāle [M 2.0440] vaṭṭamūlakadukkhāni visuṃ gahitāniyeva. Ye pana sattā āhārūpajīvino tattha ca uṭṭhānaphalūpajīvino, tesaṃ aññehi asādhāraṇaṃ jīvikadukkhaṃ aṭṭhamaṃ saṃvegavatthu gahitanti daṭṭhabbaṃ. Ayaṃ vuccati samaye sampahaṃsanāti ayaṃ sampahaṃsitabbasamaye vuttanayena saṃvegajananavasena ceva pasāduppādanavasena ca sammadeva pahaṃsanā, saṃvegajananapubbakapasāduppādanena bhāvanācittassa tosanātiattho.

To show that this is a remedy for generating a sense of urgency and confidence, he states, beginning with, “There are eight grounds for urgency.” Among these, birth, aging, sickness, and death occur appropriately in both happy and unhappy destinies. And distinct from these, the suffering of the lower realms should be seen as arising from various causes, such as the fivefold bondage, hunger, thirst, and mutual conflict. All this is understood as being based on the present existence of those beings, while the sufferings rooted in the cycle of existence in past and future times are taken separately. Furthermore, for beings who subsist on food and also live by the fruits of their effort, the suffering of livelihood, unique to them, is to be seen as the eighth ground for urgency. This is called “gladdening at the right time.” This means properly gladdening at the appropriate moment, according to the manner stated, by generating urgency and also by inspiring confidence; the meaning is the gladdening of the mind of development by generating urgency first and then confidence.

Sammāpaṭipattiṃ [V 2.0147] āgammāti līnuddhaccavirahena samathavīthipaṭipattiyā ca sammadeva bhāvanāpaṭipattiṃ āgamma.

By 'resorting to right practice' is meant resorting to the practice of development in the right way, by means of the practice of the path of serenity, which is devoid of sluggishness and restlessness.

Alīnantiādīsu kosajjapakkhikānaṃ dhammānaṃ anadhimattatāya alīnaṃ, uddhaccapakkhikānaṃ anadhimattatāya anuddhataṃ, paññāpayogasampattiyā upasamasukhādhigamena ca anirassādaṃ, tato eva ārammaṇe samappavattaṃ samathavīthipaṭipannañca. Tattha alīnatāya paggahe, anuddhatāya ca niggahe, anirassādatāya sampahaṃsane na byāpāraṃ āpajjati. Alīnānuddhaccatāhi ārammaṇe samappavattaṃ, anirassādatāya samathavīthipaṭipannaṃ, samappavattiyā vā alīnaṃ anuddhataṃ, samathavīthipaṭipattiyā anirassādanti daṭṭhabbaṃ. Ayaṃ vuccati samaye ajjhupekkhanatāti ayaṃ ajjhupekkhitabbasamaye cittassa paggahaniggahasampahaṃsanesu byāvaṭatāsaṅkhātaṃ paṭipakkhaṃ abhibhuyya upekkhanā vuccati. Esāti samādhibojjhaṅgo anuppanno uppajjati. Arahattamaggena bhāvanāpāripūrī hotīti etena nippariyāyato samādhivepullappattopi arahā evāti dasseti.

In the terms 'not sluggish' and so forth: it is 'not sluggish' due to the non-excessiveness of qualities associated with laziness; it is 'not restless' due to the non-excessiveness of qualities associated with restlessness; and it is 'not without relish' due to the accomplishment of the application of wisdom and the attainment of the bliss of tranquility. Therefore, it proceeds evenly in its object and has entered the path of serenity. Herein, because it is not sluggish, it does not become occupied with exertion; because it is not restless, it does not become occupied with restraint; and because it is not without relish, it does not become occupied with gladdening. By not being sluggish or restless, it proceeds evenly in its object; by not being without relish, it has entered the path of serenity. Or, it should be understood that by proceeding evenly, it is 'not sluggish' and 'not restless'; and by having entered the path of serenity, it is 'not without relish.' This is called 'equanimity at the proper time.' This is called equanimity, which, at the time when one should be equanimous, overcomes the opposing state, defined as the mind's being occupied with exertion, restraint, and gladdening. By 'this' is meant that the enlightenment factor of concentration, unarisen, arises. By the statement, 'Through the path of Arahantship, development reaches fulfillment,' it is shown that, in the ultimate sense, even one who has attained the fullness of concentration is indeed an Arahant.

Anurodhavirodhapahānavasena majjhattabhāvo upekkhāsambojjhaṅgassa kāraṇaṃ tasmiṃ sati sijjhanato, asati ca asijjhanato, so ca majjhattabhāvo visayavasena duvidhoti āha “sattamajjhattatā saṅkhāramajjhattatā”ti. Tadubhayavasena cassa virujjhanaṃ passaddhisambojjhaṅgassa bhāvanāya eva dūrīkatanti anurujjhanasseva pahānavidhiṃ dassento “sattamajjhattatā”tiādimāha. Tathā hissa sattasaṅkhārakelāyanapuggalaparivajjanaṃ “uppattiyā kāraṇa”nti vuccati. Upekkhāya hi visesato rāgo paṭipakkho, tato rāgabahulassa puggalassa upekkhā “visuddhimaggo”ti vuccati. Dvīhākārehīti kammassakatāpaccavekkhaṇaṃ attasuññatāpaccavekkhaṇanti imehi dvīhi kāraṇehi. Dvīhevāti avadhāraṇaṃ saṅkhārasahitāya saṅkhyāsamānatāya dassanatthaṃ. Saṅkhyā eva hettha samānaṃ, na [M 2.0441] saṅkhyeyyaṃ sabbathā samānanti. Assāmikabhāvo anattaniyatā. Sati hi attani tassa kiñcanabhāvena cīvaraṃ aññaṃ vā kiñci attaniyaṃ nāma siyā, so pana koci natthevāti adhippāyo. Anaddhaniyanti, na addhānakkhamaṃ, na ciraṭṭhāyi ittaraṃ aniccanti attho. Tāvakālikanti tasseva vevacanaṃ.

The state of neutrality, by abandoning both attraction and aversion, is the cause for the enlightenment factor of equanimity, for it is accomplished when this is present and not accomplished when it is absent. That state of neutrality is twofold in terms of its objects, as stated: “equanimity toward beings and equanimity toward formations.” And by means of both these, its opposition is removed precisely by the development of the enlightenment factor of tranquility. Thus, showing the method of abandoning precisely that which is clung to, he states, beginning with, “equanimity toward beings.” For this reason, avoiding a person who trifles with beings and formations is called “the cause for its arising.” Indeed, lust is specifically the opponent of equanimity; thus, for a person with much lust, equanimity is called “the path to purification.” By 'in two ways' is meant by these two reasons: reflecting on ownership of deeds and reflecting on emptiness of self. The emphasis 'just these two' is for the purpose of showing the numerical equivalence, which includes formations. Here, only the number is equal, but what is numbered is not equal in every respect. Non-ownership is the state of not belonging to a self. For if there were a self, then through its being something, a robe or anything else could be called 'belonging to a self'; but there is no such thing at all—this is the meaning. By 'not enduring' is meant not capable of lasting, not persisting for long—it is transient, impermanent. 'Temporary' is a synonym for the same.

Mamāyatīti mamattaṃ karoti. Mamāti taṇhāya pariggayha tiṭṭhati. Dhanāyantāti dhanaṃ dabbaṃ karontā. Assāti upekkhāsambojjhaṅgassa arahattamaggena bhāvanāpāripūrī hoti. Tathā hi arahato eva chaḷaṅgupekkhānipphatti.

By 'to consider as mine' is meant to create 'mineness.' By 'mine' is meant to stand grasping through craving. By 'desiring wealth' is meant making wealth into a substance. By 'its' is meant that the development of the enlightenment factor of equanimity reaches fulfillment by the path of Arahantship. Indeed, it is only for the Arahant that the six-factored equanimity is accomplished.

Asubhārammaṇā [V 2.0148] dhammāti asubhappakārā asubhajhānassa ārammaṇabhūtā dhammā. Kāmaṃ indriyabaddhāpi kesādayo asubhappakārā eva, visesato pana jigucchitabbe jigucchāvahe gaṇhanto “dasā”ti āha. Yathā manasikaroto sabhāvasarasato tattha asubhasaññā santiṭṭhati, tathā pavatto manasikāro upāyamanasikāro. Asubhe asubhapaṭikkūlākārassa uggaṇhanaṃ, yathā vā tattha uggahanimittaṃ uppajjati, tathā manasikāro asubhanimittassa uggaho. Upacārappanāvahāya asubhabhāvanāya anuyuñjanā asubhabhāvanānuyogo.

By 'phenomena as objects of foulness' are meant phenomena that are kinds of foulness and serve as the object for the foulness absorption. Although connected with the faculties, even hair and other bodily parts are indeed kinds of foulness; but especially when one grasps what is to be loathed and is loathsome, he says, “the ten.” The attention that proceeds in such a way that the perception of foulness becomes established in one who attends according to its own nature and essence is skillful attention. The grasping of the foul and repulsive aspect in the foul, or the attention by which the learning sign arises therein, is the taking up of the foulness sign. Devotion to the cultivation of foulness, which leads to access and absorption, is the practice of the cultivation of foulness.

Manacchaṭṭhānaṃ indriyānaṃ suṭṭhu susaṃvaraṇe sati avasaraṃ alabhanto kāmacchando pahīyateva, tathā bhojane mattaññuno mitāhārassa thinamiddhābhibhavābhāvā otāraṃ alabhamāno kāmacchando pahīyati. Yo pana āhāre paṭikkūlasaññaṃ tabbipariṇāmassa tadādhārassa tassa ca udariyabhūtassa ativiya jegucchataṃ, kāyassa ca āhāratiṭṭhakataṃ sammadeva jānāti, so sabbaso bhojane pamāṇassa jānanena visesato bhojane mattaññū nāma. Tassa kāmacchando pahīyateva, aṭṭhakathāyaṃ pana appāhārataṃyeva dassetuṃ “catunna”ntiādi vuttaṃ. Asubhakammikatissatthero dantaṭṭhidassāvī. Pahīnassāti vikkhambhanavasena pahīnassa. Abhidhammapariyāyena sabbopi lobho kāmacchandanīvaraṇanti “arahattamaggena āyatiṃ anuppādo”ti vuttaṃ.

When the six faculties with mind as the sixth are well and thoroughly restrained, sensual desire, finding no opportunity, is indeed abandoned. Similarly, for one who knows the measure in eating and has a measured diet, sensual desire, finding no opening due to the absence of being overcome by sloth and torpor, is abandoned. One who rightly knows the perception of repulsiveness in food, the extreme loathsomeness of its transformation, its receptacle, and of what is in the stomach, and that the body is sustained by food—such a one, by knowing the measure in eating in every way, is specifically called 'one who knows the measure in eating.' For such a one, sensual desire is indeed abandoned. In the commentary, however, to show just the smallness of food, it is said, 'of the four,' etc. The elder Tissa, a practitioner of foulness meditation, was one who saw the tooth-bone. By 'of the abandoned' is meant of that which is abandoned by way of suppression. According to the Abhidhamma exposition, all greed is the hindrance of sensual desire; thus it is said, 'non-arising in the future through the path of Arahantship.'

Mejjati hitapharaṇavasena siniyhatīti mitto, hitesī puggalo, tasmiṃ mitte bhavā, mittassa vā esāti mettā, hitesitā. Sā eva paṭipakkhato [M 2.0442] cetaso vimuttīti mettācetovimutti. Tattha mettāyanassa sattesu hitapharaṇassa uppādanaṃ pavattanaṃ mettānimittassa uggaho. Tenāha “odissakā”tiādi.

A friend is one who cherishes and is affectionate by way of pervading with what is beneficial; a person who desires welfare. What exists in that friend, or what belongs to a friend, is loving-kindness, the state of desiring welfare. That loving-kindness itself, as the liberation of the mind from its opponent, is the mind-liberation of loving-kindness. Therein, the arising and continuation of pervading beings with what is beneficial is the grasping of the sign of loving-kindness. Hence, he said, 'referring to particular individuals,' and so forth.

Tattha attapiyasahāyamajjhattaverivasena odissakatā. Sīmāsambhede kate anodissakatā. Ekādidisāpharaṇavasena disāpharaṇatā mettāya uggaṇhane veditabbā. Uggaho yāva upacārā daṭṭhabbo. Uggahitāya āsevanā bhāvanā, sabbā itthiyo purisā ariyā anariyā devā manussā vinipātikāti sattodhikaraṇavasena pavattā sattavidhā, aṭṭhavīsatividhā vā, dasahi disāhi disodhikaraṇavasena pavattā dasavidhā, ekekāya disāya sattādiitthādiaverādibhedena asītādhikacatusatappabhedā ca odhisopharaṇamettā. Sabbe sattā, pāṇā, bhūtā, puggalā, attabhāvapariyāpannāti etesaṃ vasena pañcavidhā. Ekekasmiṃ averā hontu, abyāpajjā, anīghā, sukhī attānaṃ pariharantūti catudhā pavattiyā vīsatividhā anodhisopharaṇamettā, taṃ sandhāyāha – “odhiso…pe… bhāventassapī”ti. Tvaṃ etassātiādinā kammassakatāpaccavekkhaṇaṃ dasseti[V 2.0149]. Paṭisaṅkhāne ṭhitassāti kodhe yathāvuttassa ādīnavassa tappaṭipakkhato akodhe mettāya ānisaṃsassa ca paṭisaṅkhāne sammadeva jānane. Sevantassāti bhajantassa byāpādo pahīyati.

Here, specification is by way of oneself, a dear friend, a neutral person, and an enemy. When the boundaries are broken, it becomes non-specification. The directional pervasion should be understood in the learning of loving-kindness by pervading one direction and so forth. The learning should be seen as lasting up to access concentration. For one who has learned it, repeated practice is development. This loving-kindness with specified pervasion proceeds in seven ways by reference to beings—all women, men, noble ones, ignoble ones, deities, humans, and those in states of deprivation—or in twenty-eight ways; and it proceeds in ten ways by reference to the directions. In each direction, it is subdivided by beings, women, and so on, and by being free from hostility and so forth, resulting in four hundred and eighty types. The fivefold classification is based on: all beings, all living creatures, all that have come to be, all individuals, and all those included in a personal existence. The loving-kindness with unspecified pervasion is of twenty types, proceeding in four ways for each of these: 'May they be free from enmity, free from ill will, free from distress, and may they maintain themselves in happiness.' With reference to this, it is said: 'specified… and so on… for one who develops it.' The reflection on ownership of kamma is shown by the phrase 'You are the owner of your kamma...' and so on. By 'one who stands in reflection' is meant rightly knowing through reflection the danger of anger as previously described and, in contrast, the benefit of loving-kindness in non-anger. By 'for one who cultivates' is meant that for one who practices it, ill will is abandoned.

Atibhojane nimittaggāhoti āhārassa adhikabhojane thinamiddhassa nimittaggāho, “ettake bhutte thinamiddhaṃ uppajjati, ettake no”ti thinamiddhassa kāraṇākāraṇaggāhoti attho. Divā sūriyālokanti divā gahitanimittaṃ sūriyālokaṃ, rattiyaṃ manasikarontassapīti evamettha attho veditabbo. Dhutaṅgānaṃ vīriyanissitattā vuttaṃ “dhutaṅganissitasappāyakathāyapī”ti.

By 'grasping the sign in overeating' is meant grasping the sign of sloth and torpor in excessive eating of food. The meaning is grasping the cause and non-cause of sloth and torpor, that is: 'When this much is eaten, sloth and torpor arise; when this much is eaten, they do not.' By 'sunlight by day' is meant the sign of sunlight grasped during the day, even for one who attends to it at night; the meaning here should be understood in this way. Because the ascetic practices are dependent on energy, it is said, 'Even in suitable talk connected with the ascetic practices.'

Kukkuccampi katākatānusocanavasena pavattamānaṃ cetaso avūpasamāvahatāya uddhaccena samānalakkhaṇamevāti tadubhayassa pahānakāraṇaṃ dassento bhagavā – “atthi, bhikkhave, cetaso vūpasamo”tiādimāha. Tasmā bāhusaccādi tassa pahānakāraṇanti dassetuṃ “apica cha dhammā”tiādimāha. Tattha bahussutassa ganthato, atthato dhammaṃ vicārentassa attavedādipaṭilābhasambhavato vikkhepo na hoti. Yathāvihitapaṭipattiyā [M 2.0443] yathādhammapaṭikārappattiyā ca vippaṭisāro anavasarovāti “bāhusaccenapi…pe… uddhaccakukkuccaṃ pahīyatī”ti vuttaṃ. Yadaggena bahussutassa paṭisaṅkhānavato uddhaccakukkuccaṃ pahīyati, tadaggena paripucchakatāvinayapakataññutāhipi taṃ pahīyatīti daṭṭhabbaṃ. Vuddhasevitā ca vuddhasīlitaṃ āvahatīti cetaso vūpasamakarattā uddhaccakukkuccassa pahānakārī vuttā, vuddhabhāvaṃ pana anapekkhitvā vinayadharā kukkuccavinodakā kalyāṇamittāti daṭṭhabbā. Vikkhepo ca bhikkhūnaṃ yebhuyyena kukkuccahetuko hotīti “kappiyākappiyaparipucchābahulassā”tiādinā vinayanayeneva paripucchakatādayo niddiṭṭhā. Pahīne uddhaccakukkucceti niddhāraṇe bhummaṃ. Kukkuccassa domanassasahagatattā anāgāmimaggena āyatiṃ anuppādo vutto.

Remorse, too, which proceeds by way of regret over what has been done and not done, has the same characteristic as restlessness in that it brings about the non-calming of the mind. Thus, to show the means of abandoning both, the Blessed One said, 'There is, monks, the calming of the mind,' and so on. Therefore, to indicate that extensive learning and so on are the means of abandoning them, he said, 'Moreover, there are six things...' Here, for one who is learned and investigates the Dhamma according to the text and the meaning, there is no distraction, due to the possibility of attaining self-knowledge and so on. For one who practices as prescribed and attains rectification according to the Dhamma, there is no occasion for regret. Thus, it is said, 'Through extensive learning... restlessness and remorse are abandoned.' It should be understood that in the same way that restlessness and remorse are abandoned by the learned through reflection, so too are they abandoned by being inclined to question and by familiarity with the Vinaya. Associating with elders brings the practice of elders; because this calms the mind, it is said to be a cause for abandoning restlessness and remorse. However, without regard to their elderliness, those who uphold the Vinaya and dispel remorse are to be seen as good friends. And since distraction for monks is for the most part caused by remorse, questioning and so on are explained through the method of the Vinaya, such as 'one who frequently asks what is allowable and what is not.' The locative case in 'when restlessness and remorse are abandoned' is one of specification. Since remorse is accompanied by displeasure, its non-arising in the future is said to be through the path of the non-returner.

Kusalākusalā dhammātiādīsu yaṃ vattabbaṃ, taṃ heṭṭhā vuttameva. Kāmaṃ bāhusaccaparipucchakatāhi aṭṭhavatthukāpi vicikicchā pahīyati, tathāpi ratanattayavicikicchāmūlikā sesavicikicchāti katvā vuttaṃ “tīṇi ratanāni ārabbhā”tiādi. Vinaye pakataññutāya ca sati sikkhāya kaṅkhāya asambhavo eva, tathā ratanattayaguṇāvabodhe sati pubbantādīsu saṃsayassāti āha – “vinaye”tiādi. Okappanīyasaddhā saddheyyavatthuṃ anupavisitvā viya adhimuccanaṃ, tañca tathā adhimokkhuppādanameva. Saddhāya ninnapoṇapabbhāratā adhimutti. Arahattena kūṭaṃ gaṇhi sattapi bojjhaṅge vitthāretvā desanāya osāpitattā.

Regarding wholesome and unwholesome states and so on, what needs to be said has already been stated below. Although doubt concerning the eight topics is also abandoned through extensive learning and questioning, still, considering that the remaining doubts are rooted in doubt about the Triple Gem, it is said, 'concerning the three jewels,' and so on. When there is familiarity with the Vinaya, there is indeed no possibility of doubt regarding the training. Similarly, when there is understanding of the qualities of the Triple Gem, there is no doubt about the past, and so forth. Hence, it is said, 'in the Vinaya,' and so on. Confident faith is a resolution, as if having entered into the object of faith, and that is indeed the arising of such a resolution. Resolution is the leaning, inclining, and sloping of faith. The discourse was brought to a conclusion by explaining the seven factors of enlightenment in detail, thereby reaching the peak with arahantship.

Āhārasuttavaṇṇanā niṭṭhitā.

The Commentary on the Āhāra Sutta is finished.

2. Pariyāyasuttavaṇṇanā

2. Commentary on the Pariyāya Sutta

233. Sambahulāti [V 2.0150] vuccanti saṅghasamaññāya abhāvato. Tato paranti tiṇṇaṃ janānaṃ upari saṅgho catuvaggakaraṇīyādisaṅghakammavasena kammappattattā. Pavisiṃsūti bhāvini pavisane bhūte viya katvā upacārena vuttanti āha – “paviṭṭhā”ti. Bhāvini hi bhūte viya upacāro. Tenāha – “te panā”tiādi, te pana bhikkhūti attho. Puna te panāti titthiyā titthiyasāvakā ca.

233. 'Many' is said because there is no designation of a Saṅgha. 'Beyond that' means more than three people, because the Saṅgha has become eligible for an act, by way of Saṅgha acts such as those requiring a quorum of four. 'They entered' (pavisiṃsu) is said metaphorically, treating a future entering as if it were past, as it is said, 'entered' (paviṭṭhā). For there is a metaphorical usage of the future as if it were past. Therefore, it is said: 'But those...' and so on, meaning 'but those monks.' Again, 'but those' refers to the sectarians and their disciples.

Imasmiṃ [M 2.0444] paññāpaneti “pañca nīvaraṇe pahāyā”tiādinayappavatte imasmiṃ pakāre atthapaññāpane. Visissati aññamaññato pabhijjatīti viseso, bhedo. Svāyaṃ idha antogadhādhikabhāvo adhippetoti āha – “ko visesoti kiṃ adhika”nti. Adhikaṃ payasanaṃ payuñjananti adhippayāso, adhikappayogo. Nānā karīyati etenāti nānākaraṇaṃ, bhedoti āha – “kiṃ nānatta”nti. Dutiyapadeti “anusāsaniyā vā anusāsani”nti etasmiṃ pade. Eseva nayoti yathā paṭhamasmiṃ pade atthayojanā, evaṃ dutiyapadepi yojetabbā.

'In this explanation' means: in this explanation of meaning, in this manner, which proceeds according to the method beginning with 'having abandoned the five hindrances.' 'Distinction' (viseso) is so called because it is distinguished and differentiated from another; it is a division. Here, the intended meaning is the state of having something additional included within; thus it is said, 'What is the distinction? What is additional?' 'Superior exertion' (adhippayāso) means applying greater effort, a heightened application. 'Diversification' (nānākaraṇaṃ) is that by which something is made diverse, a difference; thus it is said, 'What is the diversity?' 'In the second phrase' refers to the phrase 'or instruction.' This is the method: just as the meaning is to be construed in the first phrase, so too should it be construed in the second phrase.

Tīṇi ṭhānānīti devamārabrahmaṭṭhānāni tīṇi “sadevake loke”tiādinā loke pakkhipitvā. “Sassamaṇabrāhmaṇiyā pajāya sadevamanussāyā”ti dve ṭhānāni. “Pajāyā”ti ettha pakkhipitvā iti pañcahi padehi. Uddesanti uddisitabbataṃ. Gaṇananti eko kāmacchandoti gahetabbataṃ gacchati. Attano hatthapādādīsūti nidassanamattaṃ daṭṭhabbaṃ samudāyaṃ ārabbha paṭighassa uppajjanato. Attano khandhesu vimati “ahaṃ nu khosmī”tiādinā.

'The three positions' means the three positions of devas, Māra, and Brahmā, which are included in 'the world' by the phrase 'in the world with its devas,' and so on. The two positions are 'with the populace including ascetics and brahmins' and 'with its devas and humans.' These are included here under 'populace,' thus making five terms in total. 'Listing' (uddesa) means that which is to be listed. 'Counting' (gaṇana) means it comes to be taken as 'sensual desire is one.' 'Regarding one's own hands, feet, etc.' should be seen as a mere illustration, because aversion arises in relation to the aggregate. Doubt regarding one's own aggregates is expressed as: 'Am I?' and so on.

Kiñcāpi sabbaṃ vīriyaṃ cetasikameva, yaṃ pana kāyakammādhiṭṭhānādivasena manasi kāyikapayogasamuṭṭhāpanaṃ vīriyaṃ kāyikanti laddhapariyāyanti tato visesetvā cetasikanti yathādhippetadutiyatādassanatthaṃ. Yadipi yathākappitairiyāpathasandhāraṇavasena pavattamānaṃ vīriyaṃ kāyassa tathāpavattassa paccayabhūtanti na sakkā vattuṃ, tathāpi na tādiso kāyapayogo, tadā pavatto natthīti vuttaṃ “kāyapayogaṃ vinā uppannavīriya”nti. Satisambojjhaṅgasadiso vāti iminā ajjhattike saṅkhāre ajjhupekkhanavasena pavattā ajjhattadhammesu upekkhātiādinayaṃ atidisati.

Although all energy is mental, that energy which, in the mind, initiates bodily exertion based on bodily action and so on, is conventionally termed 'bodily energy.' To distinguish it from that, 'mental' is used to show the second type as intended. Although it cannot be said that the energy which proceeds by way of maintaining a contrived posture is a condition for the body so proceeding, still, there is no such bodily exertion occurring at that time. Thus it is said, 'energy arisen without bodily exertion.' Or, by 'similar to the mindfulness enlightenment factor,' it extends the method of 'equanimity towards internal phenomena,' and so on, which proceeds by way of observing internal formations with equanimity.

“Missakabojjhaṅgā kathitā”ti vatvā tamatthaṃ vibhāvetuṃ “etesū”tiādi vuttaṃ. Caṅkamantenapi [V 2.0151] ariyamaggaṃ adhigantuṃ sakkāti katvā vuttaṃ “maggaṃ apattaṃ kāyikavīriya”nti. Bojjhaṅgā na labbhantīti nippariyāyabojjhaṅgā na labbhantī. Bojjhaṅge uddharanti pariyāyatoti adhippāyo. Sesāti bahiddhādhammesu sati-dhammavicaya-upekkhā-cetasika-vīriya-savitakka-savicāra-pīti-samādhī dve passaddhiyo ca. Ayaṃ pana nayo pacurappavattivasena aṭṭhakathānayeneva [M 2.0445] vutto. Theravādavasena pana avitakkaavicāramattā pītisamādhisambojjhaṅgā rūpāvacarāpi atthīti tepi gahetvā dasa missakāva hontīti vattabbaṃ siyāti.

Having said, 'Mixed factors of enlightenment have been spoken of,' to clarify that meaning, 'among these,' and so on was said. Because it is possible to attain the noble path even while walking, it was said, 'bodily effort that has not yet attained the path.' 'The enlightenment factors are not obtained' means the enlightenment factors in the absolute sense are not obtained. The meaning is that they single out enlightenment factors in a relative sense. The rest are mindfulness, investigation of phenomena, equanimity, mental effort, initial application, sustained application, joy, concentration, and the two kinds of tranquility, in external phenomena. This method, however, is stated according to the way of the commentary, based on its frequent occurrence. But according to the Theravāda tradition, since the joy and concentration factors of enlightenment, which are merely without initial and sustained application, also exist pertaining to the form realm, including those, it should be said that there are indeed ten mixed factors.

Pariyāyasuttavaṇṇanā niṭṭhitā.

The Commentary on the Pariyāya Sutta is finished.

3. Aggisuttavaṇṇanā

3. The Commentary on the Aggisutta

234. Loṇadhūpananti loṇañca dhūpanañca loṇadhūpanaṃ. Yodhakammanti yodhapuggalena kattabbaṃ kammaṃ. Mantakammanti rājakiccamantanaṃ. Paṭihārakammanti rañño santikaṃ āgatānaṃ vacanaṃ rañño nivedetvā tato nesaṃ paṭiharaṇakammaṃ. Tasmāti sabbatthikattā satiyā. Evaṃ “satiñca khvāha”ntiādikaṃ avoca. Pubbabhāgavipassanā bojjhaṅgāva kathitā paggahaniggahavinoditattā.

234. ‘Loṇadhūpana’ means salt and fumigation, hence ‘loṇadhūpana.’ ‘Yodhakamma’ means the action to be performed by a warrior. ‘Mantakamma’ means deliberation regarding royal affairs. ‘Paṭihārakamma’ means the act of conveying the words of those who have come into the king’s presence to the king, and then the act of responding to them. ‘Tasmā’ is because of the existence of mindfulness in all instances. Thus, he spoke, ‘mindfulness, indeed I...,’ and so on. The preliminary insight is spoken of as the factors of enlightenment themselves, because of being characterized by exertion, restraint, and dispelling.

Aggisuttavaṇṇanā niṭṭhitā.

The Commentary on the Aggisutta is concluded.

4. Mettāsahagatasuttavaṇṇanā

4. The Commentary on the Discourse Accompanied by Loving-Kindness

235. Kīdisā gati nibbatti etissāti kiṃgatikā, kiṃniṭṭhāti vuttaṃ hoti. Kīdisī paramā uttamā koṭi etissāti kiṃparamā. Kīdisaṃ phalaṃ ānisaṃsaṃ udayo etissāti kiṃphalā. Saṃsaṭṭhaṃ sampayuttanti idaṃ sahagata-saddassa atthadassanamattaṃ, idha pana mettājhānaṃ pādakaṃ katvā vipassanāpubbabhāgabojjhaṅgā ca “mettāsahagataṃ satisambojjhaṅga”ntiādinā vuttāti veditabbaṃ. Sabbatthāti sabbesu bojjhaṅgesu sabbesu ca brahmavihāresu.

235. ‘What kind of destination or rebirth has this?’ is ‘kiṃgatikā’; this means it is said to be ‘what is its culmination?’ ‘What supreme, ultimate limit has this?’ is ‘kiṃparamā.’ ‘What fruit, benefit, or arising has this?’ is ‘kiṃphalā.’ ‘Saṃsaṭṭhaṃ’ and ‘sampayuttaṃ’—this is merely a presentation of the meaning of the word ‘sahagata.’ Here, however, it should be understood that having made the loving-kindness jhāna the basis, the factors of enlightenment that are the preliminary part of insight are also spoken of by such phrases as ‘the enlightenment factor of mindfulness accompanied by loving-kindness.’ ‘Sabbattha’ means in all the factors of enlightenment and in all the divine abodes.

Paṭikūleti virajjatīti paṭikūlaṃ, aniṭṭhaṃ. Na paṭikūlaṃ appaṭikūlaṃ, iṭṭhaṃ. Tenāha “iṭṭhe vatthusmi”nti. Etthāti appaṭikūlavatthusmiṃ. Evanti paṭikūlasaññī. Satte appaṭikūle asubhapharaṇaṃ, saṅkhāre appaṭikūle aniccanti manasikāraṃ karonto. Asubhāyāti asubhasaññāya. Aniccato vā upasaṃharatīti aniccanti manasikāraṃ pavatteti. “Eseva nayo”ti saṅkhepato vuttamatthaṃ vivarituṃ “appaṭikūlapaṭikūlesū”tiādi vuttaṃ. Chaḷaṅgupekkhanti chasu ārammaṇesu pahīnānurodhassa uppattiyā chaḷaṅgavantaṃ upekkhaṃ.

‘Paṭikūleti’ means ‘one is dispassionate towards it’; thus it is ‘paṭikūlaṃ’ (repulsive), undesirable. Not repulsive is ‘appaṭikūlaṃ’ (non-repulsive), desirable. Therefore, he said, ‘in a desirable object.’ ‘Ettha’ means in a non-repulsive object. ‘Evaṃ’ means one who perceives it as repulsive. One who, when beings are non-repulsive, pervades them with foulness, and when formations are non-repulsive, attends to them as impermanent. ‘Asubhāya’ means by the perception of foulness. Or, ‘aniccato vā upasaṃharati’ means one makes attention proceed as ‘impermanent.’ To explain the meaning stated briefly as ‘This is the method,’ it was said, ‘in the non-repulsive and the repulsive,’ and so on. ‘Chaḷaṅgupekkhā’ means equanimity endowed with six factors, due to its arising for one whose attachment to the six sense-objects has been abandoned.

Mettāyāti [M 2.0446][V 2.0152] mettābhāvanāya. Paṭikūlādīsu vatthūsu icchitavihārena viharituṃ samatthatā ariyānaṃ eva, tattha ca arahato eva ijjhanato ariyiddhi nāma. Tassā ariyiddhiyā ca dassitattā desanā vinivaṭṭetabbā pariyosānetabbā siyā. Arahattaṃ pāpuṇituṃ na sakkoti indriyānaṃ aparipakkattā nikantiyā ca duppariyādānato. Ayaṃ desanāti “mettāsahagataṃ bojjhaṅgaṃ bhāvetī”tiādinā ayaṃ desanā āraddhā. Yo hi mettājhānaṃ pādakaṃ katvā sammasanaṃ ārabhitvā arahattaṃ pāpuṇituṃ asakkonto parisuddhesu vaṇṇakasiṇesu vimokkhasaṅkhātaṃ rūpāvacarajjhānaṃ nibbatteti, taṃ sandhāyāha bhagavā – “subhaṃ kho pana vimokkhaṃ upasampajja viharatī”ti.

‘Mettāya’ means by the development of loving-kindness. The ability to dwell at will with regard to repulsive objects and so on belongs only to the noble ones, and among them, because it is accomplished only by an arahant, it is called noble power. And because that noble power has been shown, the teaching should be turned back, it should be brought to a conclusion. One cannot attain arahantship due to the immaturity of the faculties and because longing is difficult to completely abandon. ‘Ayaṃ desanā’ means this teaching was begun with ‘develops the enlightenment factor accompanied by loving-kindness,’ and so on. For one who, having made the loving-kindness jhāna the basis, begins contemplation but is unable to attain arahantship, generates the form-sphere jhāna called the ‘beautiful liberation’ in the purified color kasiṇas—referring to that one, the Blessed One said: ‘Having attained the beautiful liberation, one dwells.’

Subhaparamanti subhavimokkhaparamaṃ. Idha loke eva paññā assa. Tenāha “lokiyapaññassāti attho”ti. Arahattaparamāva mettā arahattamaggassa pādakattā. Karuṇādīsupi eseva nayoti karuṇādijhānaṃ pādakaṃ katvā saṅkhāre sammasanto arahattaṃ pattuṃ sakkoti, tassa arahatthaparamā karuṇā hoti, evaṃ muditāupekkhāsupi vattabbanti imamatthaṃ atidisati. Puna desanārambhapayojanaṃ pana “iminā nayenā”ti heṭṭhā atidiṭṭhameva.

‘Subhaparamā’ means the ultimate is the beautiful liberation. His wisdom is just here in this world. Therefore, he said, ‘the meaning is “of one with worldly wisdom.”’ Loving-kindness has arahantship as its ultimate, because it is the basis for the path to arahantship. ‘The same principle applies to compassion and the others’ extends this meaning: by making the jhānas of compassion and so on the basis, one who contemplates formations is able to attain arahantship; for that one, compassion has arahantship as its ultimate. Thus, it should be stated also for sympathetic joy and equanimity. Furthermore, the purpose for beginning the teaching again is just as was pointed out below with ‘by this method.’

Subhaparamāditāti mettākaruṇāmuditāupekkhānaṃ subhaparamatā ākāsānañcāyatanaparamatā, viññāṇañcāyatanaparamatā, ākiñcaññāyatanaparamatā. Tassa tassāti subhavimokkhassa heṭṭhā tiṇṇaṃ arūpajjhānānañca yathākkamaṃ upanissayattā. Appaṭikūlaparicayāti iṭṭhārammaṇe manasikārabahulīkārā. Appakasirenevāti sukheneva. Tatthāti visuddhatāya iṭṭhesu vaṇṇakasiṇesu. Cittanti bhāvanāmayacittaṃ pakkhandati appanāvasena. Tato paranti tato subhavimokkhato paraṃ vimokkhānaṃ upanissayo nāma na hoti, mettāsahagatabhāvo daṭṭhabbo.

‘Subhaparamādi’ means having the beautiful as ultimate for loving-kindness, compassion, sympathetic joy, and equanimity; having the sphere of infinite space as ultimate; having the sphere of infinite consciousness as ultimate; and having the sphere of nothingness as ultimate. ‘Tassa tassa’ means because of being the proximate cause, respectively, for the beautiful liberation and, below that, for the three formless jhānas. ‘Appaṭikūlaparicaya’ means the frequent practice of attention on a desirable object. ‘Appakasireneva’ means easily. ‘Tattha’ means in the desirable color kasiṇas, because of their purity. ‘Cittaṃ’ means the mind born of meditation plunges in by way of absorption. ‘Tato paraṃ’ means beyond that, beyond the beautiful liberation, there is no proximate cause for the liberations; the state accompanied by loving-kindness should be understood.

Sattadukkhaṃ samanupassantassāti daṇḍena abhihaṭappattarūpahetuṃ sattesu uppajjanakadukkhaṃ ñāṇena vīmaṃsantassa. Tayidaṃ rūpanimittakaṃ sattesu uppajjanakaṃ dukkhaṃ ñāṇena karuṇāvihārissa visesato pakkhandatīti katvā vuttaṃ “appakasireneva tattha cittaṃ pakkhandatī”ti, na pana sabbaso arūpe ānisaṃsadassanato.

‘For one who thoroughly sees the suffering of beings’ means for one who investigates with wisdom the suffering that arises in beings due to a cause such as a form that has been struck with a stick. Because this suffering, which has form as its sign and arises in beings, especially plunges in for one dwelling in compassion with wisdom, it was said, ‘With little difficulty the mind plunges in there,’ and not entirely because of seeing the benefit in the formless.

Viññāṇaṃ [M 2.0447] samanupassantassāti idaṃ pāmojjagahaṇamukhena tannissayaviññāṇassa gahaṇaṃ sambhavatīti katvā vuttaṃ. Viññāṇaggahaṇaparicitanti vuttanayena viññāṇaggahaṇe paricitaṃ.

‘For one who thoroughly sees consciousness’—this was said because the grasping of consciousness that is dependent on that joy is possible through the entrance of grasping joy. ‘Accustomed to grasping consciousness’ means accustomed to grasping consciousness in the way that was stated.

Upekkhāvihārissāti [V 2.0153] upekkhābrahmavihāraṃ viharato. Ābhogābhāvatoti sukhādivasena ābhujanābhāvato. Sukha…pe… sambhavatoti sukhadukkhāti paramatthakammaggahaṇe vimukhatāsambhavato. Avijjamānaggahaṇadukkhanti paramatthato avijjamānasattapaññattigahaṇaparicitaṃ tassa tassa abhāvamattakassa gahaṇampi dukkhaṃ kusalampi hoti. Sesaṃ suviññeyyameva.

‘For one dwelling in equanimity’ means for one dwelling in the divine abiding of equanimity. ‘From the absence of turning the mind towards’ means from the absence of turning the mind towards by way of pleasure and so on. ‘Because of the possibility of... happiness...’ means because of the possibility of turning away from grasping action in the ultimate sense, such as happiness and suffering. ‘The difficulty of grasping the non-existent’ means being accustomed to grasping the concept of a being, which is non-existent in an ultimate sense; the grasping of the mere absence of this and that is both difficult and wholesome. The rest is easy to understand.

Mettāsahagatasuttavaṇṇanā niṭṭhitā.

The Commentary on the Discourse Accompanied by Loving-Kindness is concluded.

5. Saṅgāravasuttavaṇṇanā

5. Commentary on the Saṅgārava Sutta

236. Paṭhamaññevāti puretaraṃyeva. Asajjhāyakatānaṃ mantānaṃ appaṭibhānaṃ pageva paṭhamaṃyeva siddhaṃ, tattha vattabbameva natthīti adhippāyo. Pariyuṭṭhānaṃ nāma abhibhavo gahaṇanti āha – “kāmarāgapariyuṭṭhitenāti kāmarāgagahitenā”ti. Vikkhambheti apanetīti vikkhambhanaṃ, paṭipakkhato nissarati etenāti nissaraṇaṃ, vikkhambhanañca taṃ nissaraṇañcāti vikkhambhananissaraṇaṃ. Tenāha – “tatthā”tiādi. Sesapadadvayepi eseva nayo. Attanā araṇiyo pattabbo attattho, tathā parattho veditabbo.

236. ‘Paṭhamaññeva’ means ‘even earlier.’ For those who do not recite, the non-recollection of the sacred texts is established from the very beginning; the meaning is that there is nothing to be said about that. ‘Pariyuṭṭhāna’ means overwhelming or seizing. Thus he said: ‘pariyuṭṭhitena by sensual desire’ means ‘seized by sensual desire.’ ‘Vikkhambheti’ means it removes, thus ‘vikkhambhana’ is suppression. ‘One escapes from the opposite by means of this,’ thus ‘nissaraṇa’ is escape. ‘Vikkhambhanañca taṃ nissaraṇañca’ is ‘vikkhambhananissaraṇa,’ suppression-and-escape. Therefore he said: ‘Therein,’ and so on. The same method applies to the remaining two terms. One's own welfare is to be attained by oneself; similarly, the welfare of others should be understood.

“Aniccato anupassanto niccasaññaṃ pajahatī”tiādīsu byāpādādīnaṃ anāgatattā abyāpādavāre tadaṅganissaraṇaṃ na gahitaṃ. Kiñcāpi na gahitaṃ, paṭisaṅkhānavasena pana tassa vinodetabbatāya tadaṅganissaraṇampi labbhatevāti sakkā viññātuṃ. Ālokasaññā upacārappattā, appanāppattā vā, yo koci kasiṇajjhānādibhedo samatho. Dhammavavatthānaṃ upacārappanāppattavasena gahetabbaṃ.

In passages such as ‘By observing impermanence, one abandons the perception of permanence,’ since ill-will and so forth are not mentioned, escape by substitution is not included in the section on non-ill-will. Although it is not included, it can be understood that escape by substitution is indeed obtainable through reflection for the purpose of dispelling it. The perception of light, whether attained in access concentration or absorption, is any kind of serenity meditation, such as the jhānas based on kasiṇas. The defining of phenomena should be understood as being attained by way of access or absorption concentration.

Kuthitoti tatto. Usmudakajātoti tasseva kuthitabhāvassa usmudakataṃ accuṇhataṃ patto. Tenāha “usumajāto”ti. Tilabījakādibhedenāti tilabījakakaṇṇikakesarādibhedena. Sevālena…pe… paṇakenāti udakapicchilena. Appasanno ākulatāya. Asannisinno [M 2.0448] kalaluppattiyā. Anālokaṭṭhāneti ālokarahite ṭhāne.

‘Kuthito’ means ‘heated.’ ‘Usmudakajāto’ means that its heated state has attained extreme heat, the state of hot water. Hence it is said, ‘usumajāto.’ ‘Tilabījakādibhedena’ means by the distinction of sesame seeds, seed-pods, filaments, and so on. ‘Sevālena…pe… paṇakena’ means with water-slime. ‘Appasanno’ means ‘unclear’ due to turbidity. ‘Asannisinno’ means ‘not settled’ due to the arising of mud. ‘Anālokaṭṭhāne’ means ‘in a place devoid of light.’

Saṅgāravasuttavaṇṇanā niṭṭhitā.

The Commentary on the Saṅgārava Sutta is concluded.

6. Abhayasuttavaṇṇanā

6. Commentary on the Abhaya Sutta

237. Ekaṃsena bhagavā nīvaraṇāti ekaṃsato eva bhagavā ete dhammā nīvaraṇā citte kusalappavattiyā [V 2.0154] nīvaraṇato. Kāyakilamathoti kāyaparissamo, so pana aṭṭhuppattiyā paccayattā “daratho”ti vutto. Cittakilamatho tappaccayajāto daṭṭhabbo. Tenāha – “tassa kirā”tiādi. Cittadarathopi paṭippassambhīti ānetvā sambandho. Maggenevāti yathādhigatena ariyamaggeneva. Assāti abhayassa rājakumārassa. Etaṃ kāyacittadarathadvayaṃ.
