397-407. Yā [V 2.0186][M 2.0484] vedanā sammasitvāti yā tebhūmakavedanā sammasitvā. Tāvassāti tā tebhūmakavedanā eva assa bhikkhuno. Sadisavasena cetaṃ vuttaṃ, aniccādisammasanavasena viditā pubbabhāge sammasanakāle upaṭṭhahanti. Pariggahitesūti parijānanavasena paricchijja gahitesu. “Vedanā taṇhāpapañcassa, vitakko mānapapañcassa, saññā diṭṭhipapañcassa mūlavasena sammasanaṃ vuttā”ti vadanti. “Vedanāvitakkasaññā taṇhāmānadiṭṭhipapañcānaṃ mūladassanavasenā”ti apare.

397–407. “Yā vedanā sammasitvā” means having contemplated the feeling of the three planes. “Tāvassa” means those very feelings of the three planes would be for that bhikkhu. This is said by way of similarity, for they appear during the time of contemplation in the preliminary stage, known through contemplation of impermanence, etc. “Pariggahitesu” means when they are distinctly grasped through thorough understanding. Some say: “Feeling is the root of craving’s proliferation, thought is the root of conceit’s proliferation, perception is the root of view’s proliferation—thus, the contemplation is stated in terms of their being the root.” Others say: “It is by way of seeing feeling, thought, and perception as the root of the proliferations of craving, conceit, and views.”

Ananussutavaggavaṇṇanā niṭṭhitā.

The commentary on the Ananussuta Vagga is concluded.

5. Amatavaggo

5. The Deathless Chapter

2. Samudayasuttavaṇṇanā

2. Commentary on the Samudaya Sutta

408. Sārammaṇasatipaṭṭhānāti ārammaṇalakkhitā satipaṭṭhānā kathitā, na satilakkhaṇā.

408. “Sārammaṇasatipaṭṭhānā” means the establishments of mindfulness are described as having an object, not as having mindfulness as their characteristic.

4. Satisuttavaṇṇanā

4. Commentary on the Sati Sutta

410. Suddhikaṃ katvā visuṃ visuṃ katvā. Tathā ca vuttaṃ pāḷiyaṃ “kāye vā bhikkhū”ti.

410. Having made it simple, having made it distinct. And thus it is said in the Pāli: “Monks, either in the body…”

6. Pātimokkhasaṃvarasuttavaṇṇanā

6. Commentary on the Pātimokkha Saṃvara Sutta

412. Jeṭṭhakasīlanti padhānakasīlaṃ. Sīlaggahaṇañhi pāḷiyaṃ pātimokkhasaṃvaravaseneva āgataṃ. Tenāha “tipiṭakacūḷanāgatthero panā”tiādi. Tattha pātimokkhasaṃvarova sīlanti avadhāraṇaṃ itaresaṃ tiṇṇaṃ ekadesena pātimokkhantogadhabhāvaṃ dīpeti. Tathā hi anolokiyolokane ājīvahetu ca chasikkhāpadavītikkamane gilānapaccayassa appaccavekkhitaparibhoge [V 2.0187] ca āpatti vihitāti. Tīṇīti indriyasaṃvarādīni. Sīlanti vuttaṭṭhānaṃ nāma natthīti sīlapariyāyena tesaṃ katthaci sutte gahitaṭṭhānaṃ nāma natthīti nippariyāyasīlataṃ tesaṃ [M 2.0485] paṭikkhipati. Chadvārarakkhaṇamattamevāti tassa sallahukatamāha cittādhiṭṭhānamattena paṭipākatikabhāvappattito. Itaradvayepi eseva nayo. Paccayuppattimattakanti phalena hetuṃ dasseti. Uppādanahetukā hi paccayānaṃ uppatti. Idamatthanti idaṃ payojanaṃ imassa paccayassa paribhuñjaneti adhippāyo. Nippariyāyenāti iminā indriyasaṃvarādīni tīṇi padhānasīlassa paripālanaparisodhanavasena pavattiyā pariyāyasīlāni nāmāti dasseti. Idāni pātimokkhasīlasseva padhānabhāvaṃ byatirekato anvayato ca upamāya vibhāvetuṃ “yassā”tiādimāha. Tattha soti pātimokkhasaṃvaro. Sesāni indriyasaṃvarādīni. Pātimokkhasaddassa attho pana visuddhimaggasaṃvaṇṇanādīsu vitthārito, tasmā tattha vuttanayeneva veditabbo.

412. ‘The chief virtue’ refers to the principal virtue. Indeed, in the Pāli Canon, the taking up of ‘virtue’ is found only in the sense of the Pātimokkha restraint. Hence, it is said, ‘But the elder Tipiṭaka Cūḷanāga,’ and so on. Here, the specification that ‘virtue is only the Pātimokkha restraint’ indicates that the other three are partially included within the Pātimokkha. For an offense is laid down for looking where one should not, for transgressing the six training rules for the sake of livelihood, and for using requisites meant for the sick without reflection. The ‘three’ refers to the restraint of the senses, and so on. The statement that ‘there is no place where they are called “virtue”’ means that there is no place in any sutta where these are taken up by way of the term ‘virtue’; thus, it rejects their being virtue in the primary sense. ‘Merely guarding the six sense-doors’ indicates its lightness because it is achieved merely by the mind’s resolve, thereby attaining a natural state. The same principle applies to the other two. ‘Merely the arising of requisites’ shows the cause through the effect. Indeed, the arising of requisites is due to a productive cause. ‘This is for this purpose’ means the intention is, ‘This is the benefit for which this requisite is used.’ By this it is shown that these three—restraint of the senses, and so on—are called ‘virtues in a figurative sense’ because they function to maintain and purify the principal virtue. Now, to clarify the preeminence of Pātimokkha virtue both by way of difference and agreement, as well as through simile, it is said, ‘Of which,’ and so on. Here, ‘that’ refers to the Pātimokkha restraint, while the remaining ones are the restraint of the senses, and so on. The meaning of the term ‘Pātimokkha,’ however, is elaborated in the commentary on the Visuddhimagga and other texts. Therefore, it should be understood according to the method explained there.

Ācārena ca gocarena ca sampannoti kāyikacetasikaavītikkamasaṅkhātena ācārena ca navesiyādigocaratādisaṅkhātena gocarena ca sampanno, sampannaācāragocaroti attho. Appamattakesūti atiparittakesu anāpattigamanīyesu. “Dukkaṭadubbhāsitamattesū”ti apare. Vajjesūti akaraṇīyesu gārayhesu. Te pana ekantato akusalā hontīti āha – “akusaladhammesū”ti. Bhayadassāvīti bhayato dassanasīlo, paramāṇumattampi vajjaṃ sineruppamāṇaṃ viya katvā dassanasīlo. Sammā ādiyitvāti sammadeva sakkaccaṃ sabbaso ca ādiyitvā. Sikkhāpadesūti niddhāraṇe bhummanti samudāyato avayavaniddhāraṇaṃ dassento “sikkhāpadesu taṃ taṃ sikkhāpada”ntiādimāha. Sikkhāpadaṃ samādātabbaṃ sikkhitabbañcāti adhippāyo. Sikkhāti adhisīlasikkhā. Pubbe pada-saddo adhiṭṭhānaṭṭho, idha bhāgatthoti daṭṭhabbanti āha – “sikkhākoṭṭhāsesū”ti. Mūlapaññattianupaññattiādibhedaṃ yaṃkiñci sikkhitabbaṃ pūretabbaṃ sīlaṃ, taṃ pana dvāravasena duvidhamevāti āha – “kāyikaṃ vā vācasikaṃ vā”ti. Imasmiṃ atthavikappe sikkhāpadesūti ādhāre bhummaṃ sikkhābhāgesu kassaci visuṃ aggahaṇato. Tenāha – “taṃ taṃ sabba”nti. “Tato tvaṃ bhikkhu sīlaṃ nissāyā”ti vacanato anabhijjhā abyāpādasammādiṭṭhiyopi sīlanti vuttā, tasmā imasmiṃ sutte “pātimokkhasaṃvarasīlameva kathita”nti vuttaṃ.

‘Ācārena ca gocarena ca sampanno’ means endowed with conduct and resort, where conduct refers to the absence of transgression in bodily and mental actions, and resort refers to the sphere of activity such as seeking out new supporters, and so on. The meaning is ‘endowed with conduct and resort.’ ‘Appamattakesu’ means in very minor matters that do not lead to offenses. Some say, ‘in mere matters of wrongdoing and wrong speech.’ ‘Vajjesu’ means in blameworthy actions that should not be done. But those are entirely unwholesome, hence it is said, ‘in unwholesome states.’ ‘Bhayadassāvī’ means having the habit of seeing danger, perceiving even a fault the size of an atom as if it were the size of Mount Sineru. ‘Sammā ādiyitvā’ means undertaking properly and carefully in every way. ‘Sikkhāpadesu’ is the locative of specification. To show the specification of a part from the whole, it is said, ‘from among the training rules, this or that training rule,’ etc. The meaning is that the training rule should be undertaken and practiced. ‘Sikkhā’ refers to the higher training in virtue. Previously, the word ‘pada’ had the meaning of foundation; here, it should be seen as having the meaning of a part. Hence it is said, ‘in the divisions of training.’ Primary regulations, secondary regulations, and so on—whatever virtue is to be practiced and fulfilled—are of two kinds by way of the doors of action. Hence it is said, ‘bodily or verbal.’ In this alternative meaning, ‘sikkhāpadesu’ is the locative of location, as no particular division of the training is taken up separately. Therefore, it is said, ‘each and every one.’ From the statement, ‘Therefore, monk, relying on virtue,’ non-covetousness, non-ill will, and right view are also called virtue. Therefore, in this sutta, it is said that ‘only the virtue of Pātimokkha restraint is spoken of.’

7. Duccaritasuttavaṇṇanā

7. Commentary on the Discourse on Misconduct

413. Etthāpi [M 2.0486] manosucaritaṃ sīlaṃ nāmāti dassetīti katvā vuttaṃ “pacchimāpi tayo”ti. Anabhijjhāabyāpādasammādiṭṭhidhammā sīlaṃ hotīti veditabbā kāyavacīsucaritehi saddhiṃ [V 2.0188] manosucaritampi vatvā “tato tvaṃ bhikkhu sīlaṃ nissāya sīle patiṭṭhāyā”ti vuttattā. Sesaṃ vuttanayameva. Chaṭṭhasattamesūti chaṭṭhasattamavaggesu apubbaṃ natthi. Tena vuttaṃ “heṭṭhā vuttanayeneva attho veditabbo”ti. Vaggapeyyālato pana imasmiṃ satipaṭṭhānasaṃyutte katipayavaggā saṅgahaṃ ārūḷhā, tathāpi tesaṃ atthavisesābhāvato ekaccesu potthakesu mukhamattaṃ dassetvā saṃkhittā, ekaccesu atisaṃkhittāva, te saṅkhepavasena dve katvā “chaṭṭhasattamesū”ti vuttaṃ.

413. Here too, because it shows that ‘mental good conduct is called virtue,’ the phrase ‘the last three’ is stated. It should be understood that the states of non-covetousness, non-ill will, and right view are virtue, because after stating mental good conduct along with bodily and verbal good conduct, it is said, ‘Therefore, bhikkhu, relying on virtue, established in virtue.’ The rest is as previously explained. ‘In the sixth and seventh’ refers to the sixth and seventh chapters, where there is nothing new. Hence, it is said, ‘The meaning should be understood as explained previously.’ However, from the Vaggapeyyāla, several chapters in this Satipaṭṭhāna Saṃyutta are included. Yet, due to the absence of any special meaning, in some books they are abbreviated, showing only the heading, while in others they are very abbreviated. These are made into two by way of summary, hence the phrase ‘in the sixth and seventh.’

Amatavaggavaṇṇanā niṭṭhitā.

The Commentary on the Chapter on the Deathless is concluded.

Satipaṭṭhānasaṃyuttavaṇṇanā niṭṭhitā.

The Commentary on the Satipaṭṭhāna Saṃyutta is concluded.

4. Indriyasaṃyuttaṃ

4. The Connected Discourses on the Faculties

1. Suddhikavaggo

1. The Chapter on Purity

1. Suddhikasuttavaṇṇanā

1. Commentary on the Suddhika Sutta

471. Catubhūmaka [V 2.0189][M 2.0487] …pe… labbhanti kusalābyākatabhāvato tesaṃ tiṇṇaṃ indriyānaṃ. Vīriyindriyasamādhindriyāni…pe… sabbattha labbhanti kusalattikasādhāraṇattā. Catubhūmaka …pe… vasenāti catubhūmakadhammaparicchedavasena ceva kusalādīhi sabbasaṅgāhakadhammaparicchedavasena ca vuttanti attho.

471. In the four planes... these three faculties are obtained because of their nature of being wholesome and indeterminate. The faculties of energy and concentration... are obtained everywhere because they are common to the triad of wholesome, unwholesome, and indeterminate. ‘In the four planes... by means of’ means it is said both by means of the delineation of phenomena according to the four planes and by means of the delineation of all-inclusive phenomena according to the wholesome, and so on. This is the meaning.

7. Dutiyasamaṇabrāhmaṇasuttavaṇṇanā

The Commentary on the Second Discourse on Ascetics and Brahmins

477. Dukkhasaccavasenāti dukkhasaccabhāvena na pajānanti. Tañhi pariññeyyatāya dukkhasaccasaṅgahaṃ. Samudayasaccavasenāti taṇhāvijjādiṃ saddhindriyassa samudayasaccabhāvena na pajānanti. Nirodhanti saddhindriyassa anupādāya nirodhanimittaṃ nibbānaṃ. Paṭipadanti saddhindriyanirodhagāminiṃ paṭipadaṃ ariyamaggaṃ. Sesesūti vīriyindriyādīsu.

477. ‘By means of the truth of suffering’ means they do not understand its nature as the truth of suffering. For that, being something to be fully understood, is included in the truth of suffering. ‘By means of the truth of origin’ means they do not understand craving, ignorance, etc., as the nature of the truth of origin with regard to the faculty of faith. ‘Cessation’: With regard to the faculty of faith, this means Nibbāna, the sign of cessation without clinging. ‘The path’ means the Noble Path, the way leading to the cessation of the faculty of faith. ‘The rest’ means in the remaining faculties, namely, the faculty of energy, and so on.

Sukkapakkheti “saddhindriyaṃ pajānantī”tiādinayappavatte anavajjapakkhe. Adhimokkhavasena āvajjanasamudayāti “atthi dinna”ntiādinayappavattapubbabhāgabhūtasaddhādhimokkhavasena āvajjanuppattiyā. Tasmā paṭhamuppannā saddhā eva hettha “āvajjana”nti vuttā, na manodvārāvajjanaṃ. Esa nayo sesesupi. Tasmā paṭhamuppannā āvajjanā paggahuppattiṭṭhānānaṃ tikkhānaṃ vīriyindriyādīnaṃ paṭhamuppattiyā āvajjanapariyāyena vuttāti daṭṭhabbaṃ. Dubbalā hi paṭhamuppannā paggahābhāvato vīriyindriyādīnaṃ samudayoti balavabhāvappattavīriyindriyādikassa āvajjanaṭṭhāniyāni hontīti tesaṃ samudayoti vuttā, pubbe adhimuccanādivasena pavattassa āvajjanassa samudayāti attho. Puna chandavasenāti kattukāmatākusalacchandavasena saddhādīnaṃ uppādetukāmatākārappavattassa [V 2.0190] chandassa vasena. Manasikāravasena āvajjanasamudayāti saddhindriyādivasena pavattassa dubbalassa tassa nibbattakayonisomanasikāravasena āvajjanassa uppattiyā. Evampīti “adhimokkhavasenā”tiādinā vuttākārenapi. Chasu suttesūti [M 2.0488] dutiyato paṭṭhāya chasu suttesu. Catusaccameva kathitaṃ. Assādaggahaṇena hi samudayasaccaṃ, ādīnavaggahaṇena dukkhasaccaṃ, nissaraṇaggahaṇena nirodhamaggasaccāni gahitānīti. Paṭhamasutte pana indriyānaṃ sarūpadassanamevāti.

In the bright side: in the faultless side that proceeds according to the method beginning with, 'understanding the faculty of faith.' The arising of attention by means of resolution: this means the arising of attention by means of the resolution of faith, which is the preliminary stage that proceeds according to the method beginning with, 'there is what is given.' Therefore, the initially arisen faith is here called 'attention,' not the mind-door attention. This method applies to the rest as well. Therefore, it should be understood that the initially arisen attention is spoken of by the term 'attention' because of the initial arising of the sharp faculties of energy and so on, which are the basis for the arising of exertion. For, being initially arisen, they are weak due to the absence of exertion. The arising of the faculties of energy and so on is spoken of because they become the basis for the attention of the faculties of energy, etc., that have attained a strong state. The meaning is the arising of the attention that proceeded previously by way of resolution and so on. Again, by means of desire: by means of wholesome desire to act, the desire that proceeds in the form of wishing to generate faith and so on. The arising of attention by means of mental attention: the arising of attention by means of the appropriate wise mental attention that generates that weak faculty which proceeds by way of the faculty of faith and so on. Even in this way: also by the method stated beginning with 'by means of resolution.' In the six discourses: in the six discourses starting from the second. Only the Four Noble Truths are discussed. For by grasping gratification, the truth of the origin is grasped; by grasping the danger, the truth of suffering is grasped; and by grasping the escape, the truths of cessation and the path are grasped. In the first discourse, however, there is only the seeing of the faculties' own nature.

8. Dabbasuttavaṇṇanā

8. Commentary on the Dabba Sutta

478. Soto āpajjīyati etenāti sotāpatti, anāgataṃ pati paṭhamamaggo. Sototi ariyamaggasoto daṭṭhabbo. Āpajjīyatītiādito paṭipajjīyati. Paṭhamamaggapaṭilābhanimittāni sotāpannassa aṅgāni idha “sotāpattiyaṅgānī”ti vuttāni. Tāni pana tīsu ṭhānesu saddhā ariyakantasīlañcāti veditabbāni. Savisayeti sakavisaye. Jeṭṭhakabhāvadassanatthanti padhānabhāvadassanatthaṃ. Yattha saddhādiindriyānaṃ sātisayakiccaṃ, tesaṃ kiccātirekataṃ dassetunti attho. Idāni tamatthaṃ upamāhi vibhāvetuṃ “yathā hī”tiādi vuttaṃ. Patvāti attano kiccātirekaṭṭhānaṃ paṭilabhitvā. Pubbaṅgamanti saddahanakiccesu purecāraṃ dhorayhaṃ. Sesāni vīriyindriyādīni. Tadanvayānīti tadanugatāni tassa saddhindriyādikassa pakkhikāni. Esa nayo sesesupi. Jhānavimokkheti jhānasaṅkhāte vimokkhe samādhipadhānatāya jhānānaṃ. Evañca katvā “sotāpattiyaṅgāni patvā”ti idañca vacanaṃ samatthitaṃ hoti. Saddhūpanisañhi sīlanti. Ariyasaccāni patvāti cattāri saccāni abhisametabbāni pāpuṇitvā.

478. One enters the stream by means of this, thus it is stream-entry; the first path with respect to the future. The stream should be understood as the noble path. `Āpajjīyati` means one practices from the beginning. The characteristics of attaining the first path, the qualities of a stream-enterer, are here called 'the factors of stream-entry.' These, in three instances, should be understood as faith and noble-beloved virtue. `With its object` means within its own sphere. `To show the state of being foremost` means to show the state of being principal. The meaning is: where the faculties of faith and so on have a superior function, their functional superiority is shown. Now, to clarify that meaning with similes, the passage beginning 'just as' is stated. `Having attained` means having obtained one's own state of functional superiority. `Forerunner` means being foremost, the one who bears the yoke in the functions of faith. The rest are the faculties of energy and so on. `Following it` means following that faculty of faith and so on, being its auxiliaries. This is the method in the rest as well. `In the jhāna-liberation` means: in the liberation designated as jhāna, because of the predominance of concentration for the jhānas. And thus, this statement, 'having attained the factors of stream-entry,' is established. For virtue has faith as its decisive support. `Having attained the noble truths` means having reached the four truths that are to be fully comprehended.

9-10. Paṭhamavibhaṅgasuttādivaṇṇanā

9-10. Commentary on the First Analysis Discourse and others

479-480. Nepakkaṃ vuccati paññāti āha – “paññāyetaṃ nāma”nti. Nipāyati saṃkilesadhamme visoseti nikkhāmetīti nipako, thiratikkhasatipuggalo, tassa bhāvo nepakkanti satiyāpi nepakkabhāvo yujjateva. Evañhi “satinepakkenā”ti idaṃ vacanaṃ samatthitaṃ hoti, satiyā ca nepakkenāti evaṃ vuccamānena satiniddeso nāma kato hotiyeva. Asukaṃ nāma suttaṃ vā kammaṭṭhānaṃ vā me bhāsitanti. Vossajjīyati saṅkhāragataṃ etasmiṃ adhigateti vossaggo, nibbānaṃ. Taṃ ārammaṇaṃ [M 2.0489] karitvāti āha – “nibbānārammaṇaṃ katvā”ti. Gacchantiyāti saṅkhārānaṃ udayañca vayañca udayabbayaṃ gacchantiyā bujjhantiyā. Tenāha [V 2.0191] “udayabbayapariggāhikāyā”ti. Saddhāsatipaññindriyāni pubbabhāgāni “itipi so bhagavā arahaṃ, cirakatampi cirabhāsitampi saritā anussaritā, udayatthagāminiyā paññāyā”ti ca vuttattā. “Āraddhavīriyo viharati, so anuppannāna”ntiādinā ca vuttattā vīriyindriyaṃ missakaṃ. “Vossaggārammaṇaṃ karitvā”ti vuttattā samādhindriyaṃ nibbattitalokuttarameva. Ayamevāti yvāyaṃ navame vutto. Ayameva pubbabhāgamissakalokuttarattadhammaparicchedo.

479-480. Sagacity is called wisdom; he said: 'this is a name for wisdom.' One who dries up, exhausts, and expels defiling states is 'nipaka,' a person of firm and keen mindfulness. His state is 'nepakka' (sagacity); thus, the state of sagacity is also suitable for mindfulness. For in this way the expression 'with mindfulness and sagacity' is established, and when it is said 'with mindfulness and sagacity,' a designation of mindfulness is indeed made. 'Such and such a discourse or meditation subject was spoken by me.' The conditioned is relinquished when this is attained; hence, relinquishment is Nibbāna. Taking that as the object, he said: 'making Nibbāna the object.' By 'discerning' is meant discerning and understanding the arising and passing away, the rise and fall, of formations. Therefore, he said, 'by that which grasps the arising and passing away.' The faculties of faith, mindfulness, and wisdom are preliminary, because it is said: 'Thus is the Blessed One an Arahant...'; and 'one remembers and recollects what was done and said long ago'; and 'with wisdom that discerns arising and passing away.' And because it is said, 'one dwells with aroused energy, for the non-arising of... ,' and so on, the faculty of energy is mixed. Because it is said, 'making relinquishment the object,' the faculty of concentration is exclusively that which has produced the supramundane. This very one: that which was stated in the ninth. This very is the delineation of phenomena as preliminary, mixed, and supramundane.

Suddhikavaggavaṇṇanā niṭṭhitā.

The Commentary on the Suddhika Chapter is completed.

2. Mudutaravaggo

2. The Chapter on the Softer Ones

1. Paṭilābhasuttavaṇṇanā

1. Commentary on the Discourse on Gains

481. Sammappadhāne ārabbhāti sammappadhāne bhāvanāvasena ārabbha. Tenāha “bhāvento”ti. Yathā vīriyindriyaniddese “cattāro sammappadhāne ārabbha vīriyaṃ paṭilabhatī”ti desanā āgatā, evaṃ satindriyaniddese “cattāro satipaṭṭhāne ārabbha satiṃ paṭilabhatī”ti, tasmā “satindriyepi eseva nayo”ti vuttaṃ. Saddhindriyādiniddesesu pana na tathā deseti.

481. Concerning right effort means based on right effort by way of cultivation. Therefore, he said, 'cultivating.' Just as in the exposition on the faculty of energy the teaching is given, 'based on the four right efforts, one obtains energy,' so too in the exposition on the faculty of mindfulness, 'based on the four foundations of mindfulness, one obtains mindfulness'; therefore it is said, 'in the case of the faculty of mindfulness, too, this is the method.' However, in the expositions on the faculty of faith and the others, he does not teach in that way.

2. Paṭhamasaṃkhittasuttavaṇṇanā

2. Commentary on the First Concise Discourse

482. Indriyānaṃ tikkhādibhāvo vipassanāvasena vā maggavasena vā phalavasena vā gahetabboti vuttaṃ “tatoti…pe… veditabba”nti. Nanu cettha mudubhāvo eva pāḷiyaṃ gahitoti? Saccametaṃ, taṃ pana tikkhabhāve asati na hoti tikkhādibhāvoti vuttaṃ. Yato hi ayaṃ mudu, ito taṃ tikkhanti vattabbataṃ labhati apekkhāsiddhattā tikkhamudubhāvānaṃ pārāpāraṃ viya. Idāni “tato”tiādinā saṅkhepato vuttamatthaṃ vitthārato dassetuṃ “samattānī”tiādi vuttaṃ. Tattha samattānīti sampannāni. Itaraṃ [M 2.0490] tasseva vevacanaṃ. Samattānīti vā pariyattāni, samattānīti attho. “Tato mudutarāni dhammānusārīmaggassā”ti kasmā vuttaṃ? Tatoti hi sotāpattimaggavipassanindriyāni adhippetāniyeva, “tato mudutarānī”ti vuttapaṭhamamaggo dhammānusārī vā siyā saddhānusārī vāti byabhicarati? Nāyaṃ doso, sotāpattimaggekadesavaseneva [V 2.0192] laddhabbapaṭhamamaggāpekkhāya vipassanāya vibhāgassa adhippetattā. Yo hi sotāpanno hutvā iriyāpathaṃ akopetvā yathānisinnova sakadāgāmimaggaṃ pāpuṇāti, tassa vipassanindriyāni sandhāya avibhāgena vuttaṃ – “tato mudutarāni sotāpattimaggassa vipassanindriyāni nāmā”ti. Yo pana sotāpanno hutvā kālantarena sakadāgāmī hoti, tassa sotāpattimaggatthāya pavattāni vipassanindriyāni nāma honti. So ce dhammānusārīgotto, tassa yathāvuttavipassanindriyato mudutarānīti “tato mudutarāni dhammānusārīmaggassā”ti vuttaṃ. Vipassanindriyāni nāmāti ānetvā sambandho. Dhammānusārīvipassanindriyato saddhānusārīvipassanindriyānaṃ mudubhāvassa kāraṇaṃ sayameva vakkhati. Dhammena paññāya maggasotaṃ anussaratīti dhammānusārī, paññuttaro ariyo. Saddhāya maggasotaṃ anussaratīti saddhānusārī, saddhuttaro ariyo.

482. The state of sharpness and so on of the faculties should be understood by way of insight, or the path, or the fruit, as it is said: 'From that... it should be understood.' But is it not the case that here softness alone is mentioned in the Pāḷi text? This is true, but it is said that without the state of sharpness, there is no state of sharpness and so on. For when this is soft, then that is called sharp, because the states of sharpness and softness are established in relation to each other, like the near and far shores. Now, with 'from that,' etc., the meaning stated concisely is shown in detail with 'complete,' etc. There, 'complete' means accomplished. The other is a synonym for that. Or 'complete' can mean fulfilled; 'complete' is the meaning. Why is it said, 'Softer than those are the faculties for the path of a Dhamma-follower'? For by 'from that,' the faculties of insight for the stream-entry path are indeed intended. Does the first path mentioned as 'softer than those' deviate, being either that of a Dhamma-follower or a faith-follower? This is not a fault, because the distinction is intended with reference to the insight that anticipates the first path to be attained, by way of just one part of the stream-entry path. For one who, having become a stream-enterer, attains the once-returner’s path without disturbing his posture, just as he was seated, the faculties of insight are spoken of without distinction: 'Softer than those are what are called the faculties of insight for the stream-entry path.' But for one who, having become a stream-enterer, becomes a once-returner after some time, there are what are called the faculties of insight that have arisen for the sake of the stream-entry path. If he is of the Dhamma-follower lineage, his faculties are softer than the aforementioned faculties of insight, thus it is said, 'Softer than those are the faculties for the path of a Dhamma-follower.' The connection is made by supplying the words 'are what are called the faculties of insight.' He himself will state the reason for the softness of the faculties of insight of the faith-follower compared to those of the Dhamma-follower. One who follows along the stream of the path by means of the Dhamma, that is, by wisdom, is a Dhamma-follower, a noble one for whom wisdom is foremost. One who follows along the stream of the path by means of faith is a faith-follower, a noble one for whom faith is foremost.

Evaṃ vipassanāvasena dassetvā maggavasena dassetuṃ “tathā”tiādi āraddhaṃ. Sampayogato sabhāvato ca arahattamaggapariyāpannāni arahattamaggindriyāni. Arahattaphalindriyānīti etthāpi eseva nayo.

Having thus shown by way of insight, in order to show by way of the path, 'thus,' etc., is begun. The faculties of the Arahantship Path are those included in the Arahantship Path both by association and by their own nature. And this same method applies also to the faculties of the fruit of Arahantship.

Idāni phalavasena dassetuṃ “samattāni paripuṇṇānī”tiādi vuttaṃ. Sotāpattimaggaṭṭhapuggalavasena nānattaṃ jātaṃ, tasmā te dvepi idha tatiyavāre na labbhantīti adhippāyo. Dhammānusārīsaddhānusārīnaṃ nānattaṃ kathaṃ jātanti āha “āgamanenapi maggenapī”ti. Tadubhayaṃ dassento “saddhānusārīpuggalo”tiādimāha. Uddisāpentoti uddesaṃ gaṇhanto.

Now, to show by way of the fruit, 'complete and perfect,' etc., is said. Diversity has arisen by way of the person established on the stream-entry path; therefore, the meaning is that those two are not found here in the third instance. How has the diversity of the Dhamma-follower and the faith-follower arisen? He said, 'by approach and by path.' Showing both of those, he said, 'the person who is a faith-follower,' etc. 'Uddisāpento' means 'one who takes up the recitation'.

Maggo tikkho hoti upanissayindriyānaṃ tikkhavisadabhāvato. Tenāha “sūraṃ ñāṇaṃ vahatī”ti. Asaṅkhārenāti saraseneva. Appayogenāti tasseva vevacanaṃ. Dhammānusārīpuggalo hi āgamanamhi kilese vikkhambhento appadukkhena appakasirena akilamantova vikkhambhetuṃ sakkoti. Saddhānusārīpuggalo pana dukkhena kasirena kilamanto hutvā vikkhambhetuṃ sakkoti, tasmā dhammānusārissa pubbabhāgamaggakkhaṇe [M 2.0491] kilesacchedakañāṇaṃ adandhaṃ tikhiṇaṃ hutvā vahati, yathā nāma tikhiṇena asinā kadaliṃ chindantassa chinnaṭṭhānaṃ maṭṭhaṃ hoti, asi khippaṃ vahati, saddo na suyyati, balavavāyāmakiccaṃ na hoti, evarūpā dhammānusārino pubbabhāgabhāvanā hoti, saddhānusārino pana pubbabhāgakkhaṇe kilesacchedakañāṇaṃ dandhaṃ na tikhiṇaṃ asūraṃ hutvā vahati, yathā nāma nātitikhiṇena asinā kadaliṃ chindantassa chinnaṭṭhānaṃ na maṭṭhaṃ hoti, asi sīghaṃ na vahati, saddo suyyati, balavavāyāmakiccaṃ icchitabbaṃ [V 2.0193] hoti, evarūpā saddhānusārino pubbabhāgabhāvanā hoti. Evaṃ santepi kilesakkhaye nānattaṃ natthi. Tenāha “kilesakkhaye panā”tiādi. Avasesā ca kilesā khīyanti saṃyojanakkhayāya yogattā.

The path is sharp because the supporting faculties are sharp and clear. Hence, it is said, 'it carries swift knowledge.' 'Without preparation' means spontaneously. 'With little application' is another term for the same. The Dhamma-follower, suppressing defilements upon approach, can suppress them with little suffering, little difficulty, and without weariness. The faith-follower, however, can suppress them with suffering, difficulty, and weariness. Therefore, for the Dhamma-follower, at the moment of the preliminary-part path, the knowledge that cuts off defilements operates by being not slow but sharp, like cutting a banana plant with a sharp knife—the cut is smooth, the knife moves quickly, no sound is heard, and no strong effort is needed. Such is the preliminary development of the Dhamma-follower. For the faith-follower, at the moment of the preliminary-part, the knowledge that cuts off defilements operates by being slow, not sharp, and not swift, like cutting a banana plant with a not-so-sharp knife—the cut is not smooth, the knife does not move quickly, a sound is heard, and strong effort is required. Such is the preliminary development of the faith-follower. Even so, there is no difference in the destruction of defilements. Hence, it is said, 'but in the destruction of defilements,' etc. The remaining defilements are destroyed because of the application to the destruction of the fetters.

3. Dutiyasaṃkhittasuttavaṇṇanā

3. Commentary on the Second Concise Discourse

483. Tatoti phalato phalavemattatāya cariyamānattā. Indriyavemattatā saddhādīnaṃ indriyānaṃ nānattena. Phalanānattanti arahattaphalādinānattaṃ. Puggalanānattanti anāgāmiādipuggalanānattaṃ.

483. 'From that' (tato) means from the fruit, because it is practiced with a diversity of fruit. 'Diversity of faculties' (indriyavemattatā) means the diversity of the faculties such as faith and so forth. 'Diversity of fruits' (phalanānattā) means the diversity of fruits such as the fruit of Arahantship and so forth. 'Diversity of persons' (puggalanānattā) means the diversity of persons such as the non-returner and so forth.

4. Tatiyasaṃkhittasuttavaṇṇanā

4. Commentary on the Third Concise Discourse

484. Sīlakkhandhādīhi saddhindriyādīhi ca parito pūraṇena paripūraṃ arahattamaggaṃ karonto nipphattito arahattaphalaṃ ārādheti nipphādeti. Tayo padesamaggeti sīlakkhandhādīnaṃ apāripūriyā ekadesabhūte tayo heṭṭhimamagge. Padesaṃ heṭṭhimaphalattayaṃ. Catūsūti imasmiṃ vagge paṭhamādīsu catūsu suttesu. Kāmañcettha tatiye “tatoti phalavasena nissakka”nti vuttaṃ, catutthe “paripūraṃ paripūrakārī ārādheti, padesaṃ padesakārī”ti, “catūsupi suttesu missakāneva indriyāni kathitānī”ti pana vacanato vipassanāvasenapi yojanā labbhatevāti daṭṭhabbaṃ.

484. By completely fulfilling the aggregate of virtue and so forth, and the faculties of faith and so forth, one brings to completion the path to Arahantship and actualizes the fruit of Arahantship. ‘The three partial paths’ are the three lower paths, which are a part only due to the incompleteness of the aggregate of virtue and so forth. ‘Partial’ refers to the three lower fruits. ‘In the four’ refers to the first four discourses in this section. Although in the third discourse it is said that ‘from that’ (tato) means ‘relinquishment by way of fruit,’ and in the fourth, ‘one who fulfills completely actualizes completely, one who acts partially actualizes partially,’ yet because of the statement that ‘mixed faculties are discussed in all four discourses,’ it should be understood that a connection by way of insight can indeed be obtained.

5-7. Paṭhamavitthārasuttādivaṇṇanā

5-7. Commentary on the First Detailed Discourse, etc.

485-487. Vipassanāvasena nissakkaṃ veditabbaṃ, na maggaphalavasena, imasmiṃ sutte sabbasova vipassanindriyānaṃ eva adhippetattā. Idāni tamatthaṃ [M 2.0492] pākaṭaṃ kātuṃ “paripuṇṇāni hī”tiādi vuttaṃ. Avihādīsu pañcasu suddhāvāsesu tattha tattha āyuvemajjhaṃ anatikkamitvā antarā kilesaparinibbānena parinibbāyanato antarāparinibbāyī, asaṅkhārena appayogena sarasatova parinibbāyanato asaṅkhāraparinibbāyī, tabbipariyāyato sasaṅkhāraparinibbāyī, uddhaṃ vāhibhāvena uddhamassa taṇhāsotaṃ vaṭṭasotaṃ vāti uddhaṃsoto, uddhaṃ vā gantvā paṭilabhitabbato uddhamassa maggasotanti uddhaṃsoto, akaniṭṭhabhavaṃ gacchatīti akaniṭṭhagāmīti evamettha saddattho daṭṭhabbo.

485-487. The relinquishment (nissakka) should be understood as being by way of insight, not by way of the path and fruit, because in this discourse only the faculties of insight are intended in every way. Now, to make that meaning clear, ‘Indeed, complete’ (paripuṇṇāni hi) and so forth was said. Among the five pure abodes beginning with Avihā, the antarāparinibbāyī is one who attains final Nibbāna through the extinction of defilements in between, without exceeding the midpoint of their lifespan there in each case. The asaṅkhāraparinibbāyī is one who attains final Nibbāna without preparation, without application, spontaneously. The sasaṅkhāraparinibbāyī is the opposite of that. The uddhaṃsoto is one whose stream of craving, the stream of the round of existence, flows upwards by way of being carried upwards; or, because it is to be attained by going upwards, his stream of the path is an 'upward stream'. The akaniṭṭhagāmī is one who goes to the Akaniṭṭha realm. Thus, the meaning of the words should be seen here.

Imasmiṃ pana ṭhāneti “vipassanāvasena nissakka”nti vuttaṭṭhāne. Arahattamaggeyeva ṭhatvāti imasmiṃyeva [V 2.0194] bhave arahattamaggeyeva, na vipassanāya ca ṭhatvā. Pañca nissakkāni nīharitabbānīti “tato mudutarehi antarāparinibbāyī hotī”tiādīni pañca nissakkāni niddhāretvā kathetabbāni. Idāni tamatthaṃ vivaranto “arahattamaggassa hī”tiādimāha. Tattha avihādīsu uppajjitvā uppannasamanantarāya parinibbāyanato paṭhamaantarāparinibbāyī. Tattha āyuppamāṇavemajjhaṃ appatvāva parinibbāyanato dutiyaantarāparinibbāyī, āyuvemajjhaṃ patvā parinibbāyanato tato paraṃ tatiyaantarāparinibbāyī veditabbo. “Pañca nissakkānī”ti kasmā vuttaṃ? Nanu asaṅkhārasasaṅkhāraparinibbāyīti vattabbanti? Na vattabbanti dassento āha “asaṅkhāraparinibbāyissa sasaṅkhāraparinibbāyinopi eteva pañca janā”ti.

‘In this context’ (imasmiṃ pana ṭhāne) refers to the place where it is said, ‘relinquishment by way of insight’ (vipassanāvasena nissakka). ‘Standing only on the path to arahantship’ (arahattamaggeyeva ṭhatvā) means in this very existence, only on the path to arahantship, not also by standing on insight. ‘The five relinquishments are to be extracted’ (pañca nissakkāni nīharitabbāni) means that the five relinquishments, such as ‘then, with gentler faculties, he becomes one who attains final Nibbāna in the interval,’ and so forth, are to be determined and explained. Now, elaborating on that meaning, he says, ‘Indeed, of the path to arahantship’ (arahattamaggassa hi) and so forth. There, the first one who attains final Nibbāna in the interval is one who attains final Nibbāna immediately after arising in the Avihā realm and so forth. The second one who attains final Nibbāna in the interval is one who attains final Nibbāna without reaching the midpoint of the lifespan there; the third one who attains final Nibbāna in the interval is to be understood as one who attains final Nibbāna after reaching the midpoint of the lifespan. ‘Why are ‘five relinquishments’ (pañca nissakkāni) mentioned?’ one might ask. ‘Should not one who attains final Nibbāna without exertion and one who attains final Nibbāna with exertion be stated?’ To show that it should not be stated thus, he says: ‘For one who attains final Nibbāna without exertion and also for one who attains final Nibbāna with exertion, these same five persons exist.’

Tīṇi nissakkānīti “tato mudutarehi sotāpanno hotī”tiādīni tīṇi nissakkapadāni. Idha sakadāgāmī na gahito, so anāgāmimagge ṭhatvā nīharitabbo, anāgāmimaggassa vipassanindriyehi mudutarehi sakadāgāmī hoti. Sakadā…pe… mudutarānīti idaṃ sakadāgāmimaggassa vipassanindriyehi ekabījisaṅkhātasotāpannavipassanindriyāni mudutarāni hontīti tīṇi katvā vuttaṃ. Dhammānusārītiādidvayaṃ kolaṃkolādidvayena gahitaṃ hotīti. Sakadāgāmimaggassa hītiādi vuttasseva atthassa vivaraṇaṃ. Sesaṃ heṭṭhā vuttanayameva. Chaṭṭhasattamāni vuttanayānevāti chaṭṭhasattamāni suttāni dutiyatatiyesu vuttanayāneva. Tattha pana missakāni indriyāni kathitāni, idha pubbabhāgavipassanindriyāni kathitānīti ayameva viseso.

‘Three relinquishments’ (tīṇi nissakkāni) refers to the three statements of relinquishment, beginning with ‘then, with gentler faculties, one becomes a stream-enterer,’ and so forth. Here, the once-returner is not included; he is to be extracted while standing on the path of the non-returner, for one becomes a once-returner with faculties gentler than the insight faculties of the non-returner’s path. ‘Once-returner… and so forth… gentler’ (sakadā…pe… mudutarāni) means that the insight faculties of the stream-enterer reckoned as a single-seed are gentler than the insight faculties of the once-returner’s path; thus, it is stated as three. The pair beginning with ‘Dhamma-follower’ (dhammānusārī) is included in the pair beginning with ‘family-to-family’ (kolaṃkola). ‘Indeed, of the once-returner’s path’ (sakadāgāmimaggassa hi) and so forth is an explanation of the meaning already stated. The rest is according to the method stated below. ‘The sixth and seventh are according to the methods already stated’ (chaṭṭhasattamāni vuttanayāneva) means that the sixth and seventh discourses are according to the methods stated in the second and third. But there, mixed faculties are discussed; here, preliminary insight faculties are discussed—this is the only difference.

8. Paṭipannasuttavaṇṇanā

8. Commentary on the Paṭipanna Sutta

488. Tanti [M 2.0493] maggaphalavasena nissakkaṃ. Pāḷiyaṃ vuttameva “arahattaphalasacchikiriyāya paṭipanno hotī”tiādinā. Aṭṭhahīti catūhi phalehi catūhi ca maggehīti aṭṭhahi. Bahibhūto na antobhāvo. Lokuttarāneva indriyāni kathitāni maggaphalacittuppādapariyāpannattā.

488. ‘That’ (taṃ) means relinquishment by way of path and fruit. It is stated in the Pāli itself by ‘he is one who has entered upon the path for the realization of the fruit of arahantship’ and so forth. ‘By eight’ (aṭṭhahi) means by the four fruits and the four paths, thus by eight. It is external, not internal. Only the supramundane faculties are spoken of because they are included in the arising of path and fruit consciousness.

9-10. Sampannasuttādivaṇṇanā

9-10. Commentary on the Sampanna Sutta and so forth

489-490. Indriyasampannoti ettha sampannasaddo paripūriatthoti āha “paripuṇṇindriyo”ti. Missakāni indriyāni kathitāni sāmaññatova desitattā.

489-490. Here, in ‘endowed with faculties’ (indriyasampanno), the word ‘endowed’ (sampanna) means ‘complete,’ thus it is said ‘one whose faculties are complete’ (paripuṇṇindriyo). The mixed faculties have been mentioned, as they are taught only in a general way.

Mudutaravaggavaṇṇanā niṭṭhitā.

The Commentary on the Mudutara Vagga is complete.

3. Chaḷindriyavaggo

3. The Chapter on the Six Faculties

2. Jīvitindriyasuttavaṇṇanā

2. Commentary on the Jīvitindriya Sutta

492. Itthibhāveti [V 2.0195] itthitāya. Indaṭṭhaṃ karoti tathāsattajanasāmaññakāraṇabhāvato. Itthiyā eva indriyaṃ itthindriyaṃ. Esa nayo purisindriye. Jīviteti sahajātadhammānaṃ jīvane pālane pavattane. Vaṭṭindriyānīti vaṭṭahetubhūtāni indriyāni. Imesu hi upādinnaindriyesu sati vaṭṭaṃ vattati paññāyati.

492. ‘In the state of being a woman’ (itthibhāve) means womanhood (itthitāya). It performs the function of a lord (indaṭṭhaṃ karoti) due to being the common cause for beings of that kind. The faculty of a woman is the female faculty. This same principle applies to the male faculty. ‘Life’ (jīvita) means in the living, maintaining, and continuing of co-nascent phenomena. ‘Faculties of the round of existence’ (vaṭṭindriyāni) means the faculties that are the cause of the round of existence. For when these appropriated faculties exist, the round of existence turns and becomes evident.

3. Aññindriyasuttavaṇṇanā

3. Commentary on the Aññindriya Sutta

493. Ajānitapubbaṃ dhammanti catusaccadhammamāha, tathā tesaṃyeva ñātadhammānanti. Ayaṃ panettha attho – ājānātīti aññā, paṭhamamaggena ñātamanatikkamitvā jānātīti attho. Sotāpannādīnaṃ chaariyānaṃ etaṃ nāmaṃ. Aññassa indriyāni aññindriyāni. Aññātāvīsūti ājānitavantesu. Yasmā aggaphaladhammesu ñāṇakiccaṃ sātisayaṃ, tasmā taṃ kiccaṃ sesadhammesupi samāropetvā vuttaṃ “aññātāvīsu arahattaphaladhammesū”ti. Tattha tattha tesu tesu maggaphalesu. Tena tenākārenāti anaññātajānanādiākārena.

493. ‘The Dhamma not previously known’ (ajānitapubbaṃ dhammaṃ) refers to the Dhamma of the Four Noble Truths; similarly, ‘the dhammas that have been known’ (ñātadhammānaṃ) refers to those same truths. Here, this is the meaning: ‘One knows’ (ājānāti), thus it is ‘final knowledge’ (aññā); the meaning is that one knows without transgressing what was known by the first path. This is the designation for the six noble individuals, from the stream-enterer onward. The faculties of one with final knowledge (añña) are the faculties of final knowledge (aññindriyāni). ‘Among those with final knowledge’ (aññātāvīsu) means among those who have come to know. Because the function of knowledge in the states of the highest fruit is pre-eminent, that function is superimposed upon the other states as well; hence it is said: ‘among those with final knowledge, in the states of the fruit of arahantship.’ ‘Therein’ (tattha tattha) means in those various paths and fruits. ‘In that particular manner’ (tena tenākārena) means in the manner of knowing the unknown, and so forth.

4. Ekabījisuttavaṇṇanā

4. Commentary on the Ekabīji Sutta

494. Vipassanato [M 2.0494] nissakkanti vipassanindriyehi nissakkaṃ. Idāni tameva saṅkhepato vuttamatthaṃ vivaranto “samattānī”tiādimāha. Tīṇi nissakkāni evamidha pañca nissakkāni nīharitabbāni ekabījiādivibhāvanato. Tenāha “sakadāgāmimaggassa hī”tiādi. Sotāpattimaggassa vipassanindriyāni nāma tenattabhāvena sakadāgāmimaggaṃ pattuṃ gacchantassa sotāpannassa vipassanindriyāni. Pañcapi te sotāpattimaggassa bhedāyevāti “sakadāgāmimagge ṭhatvā nīharitabbānī”ti vuttaṃ.

494. ‘Relinquishment by insight’ (vipassanato nissakkaṃ) means relinquishment by the insight faculties. Now, explaining that very meaning which was briefly stated, he says ‘completed’ (samattāni) and so forth. Here, three relinquishments, and thus five relinquishments, are to be extracted based on the distinctions of the one-seeder and so forth. Hence, it is said: ‘Indeed, of the once-returner’s path,’ and so forth. The insight faculties of the stream-entry path are the insight faculties of a stream-enterer who, by that very existence, is going to attain the once-returner’s path. Because these five are indeed divisions of the stream-entry path, it is said: ‘they are to be extracted while standing on the once-returner’s path.’

Ekabījīti ettha khandhabījaṃ nāma kathitaṃ. Yassa hi sotāpannassa ekaṃ khandhabījaṃ atthi, ekaṃ attabhāvaggahaṇaṃ, so ekabīji nāma. Tenāha – “sotāpanno hutvā”tiādi. Mānusakaṃ [V 2.0196] bhavanti idaṃ panettha desanāmattaṃ, devabhavaṃ nibbattetītipi vattuṃ vaṭṭatiyeva. Bhagavatā gahitanāmānetāni. Ettakañhi pamāṇaṃ gato sattakkhattuparamo nāma hoti, ettakaṃ kolaṃkolo, ettakaṃ ekabījīti bhagavatā etesaṃ nāmaṃ gahitaṃ.

In ‘one-seeder’ (ekabījī), what is called the ‘aggregate-seed’ (khandhabīja) is spoken of. For a stream-enterer for whom there is one aggregate-seed, one grasping of an existence, he is called a ‘one-seeder.’ Hence it is said: ‘having become a stream-enterer,’ and so forth. ‘A human existence’ (mānusakaṃ bhavaṃ) here is merely a mode of teaching; it is also proper to say that he brings about a divine existence. These are names given by the Blessed One. For one who has gone to such an extent is called a ‘seven-times-at-most,’ to such an extent a ‘family-to-family,’ and to such an extent a ‘one-seeder’—these names were given to them by the Blessed One.

Dve tayo bhaveti devamanussesu eva dve tayo bhave. Sambodhicatusaccadhammo paraṃ ayanaṃ nissayo gati etassāti sambodhiparāyaṇo. Kulato kulaṃ gacchatīti kolaṃkolo. Sotāpattiphalasacchikiriyato paṭṭhāya hi nīcakule uppatti nāma natthi, mahābhogesu kulesu eva nibbattatīti attho. Kevalopi hi kula-saddo mahābhogakulameva vadati. Dve vā tīṇi vā kulānīti devamanussavasena dve vā tayo vā bhaveti ayampi missakabhavena kathito. Jātassa kumārassa viya ariyāya jātiyā jātassa nāmametaṃ, yadidaṃ niyatoti sattakkhattuparamādikoti ca samaññāti dassento āha “bhagavatā gahitanāmavasenevā”tiādi.

‘Two or three existences’ means just two or three existences among gods and humans. He is ‘one bound for full enlightenment’ (sambodhiparāyaṇo) because the Dhamma of the Four Truths of full enlightenment is his supreme course, support, and destination. He is a ‘family-to-family’ one (kolaṃkolo) because he goes from family to family. Indeed, from the realization of the fruit of stream-entry onward, there is no rebirth in lowly families; the meaning is that one is reborn only in families of great wealth. Indeed, the word ‘family’ by itself refers only to a family of great wealth. ‘Two or three families’ means two or three existences in terms of gods and humans; this too is spoken of as a mixed state. Just as a newborn child is given a name, so too is a name given to one born with the noble birth. To show that this designation is ‘fixed’ (niyata), ‘seven-times-at-most,’ and so forth, he says: ‘by the name given by the Blessed One,’ and so forth.

Yadi pubbahetuniyamato sotāpanno ca niyatoti sotāpattimaggato uddhaṃ tiṇṇaṃ maggānaṃ upanissayābhāvato pubbahetukiccaṃ natthīti sotāpattimaggassa upanissayabhāvo āpajjati. Yadi tassapi pubbahetuupanissayo siyā, tāva niyamato sotāpattimagguppattito pubbe eva niyamito, yāvañca akaniṭṭhaṃ tassa pubbahetu nāma[M 2.0495], ahetukatā āpannā, iccassa ahetu appaccayā nipphatti pāpuṇāti. Kiñca hetu ce? Niyamato sotāpanno ca niyatoti paṭhamamaggādhigameneva anukkamena upari tiṇṇaṃ maggānaṃ kiccāni nipphajjanti, evaṃ sattakkhattuparamatādiniyame sati sattamabhavādito uddhaṃ pavattatāya dukkhassa mūlabhūtā kilesā paṭhamamaggeneva khīṇāti upari tayo maggā akiccā siyuṃ. Tenāha “paṭhamamaggassa upanissayo kato nāmā”tiādi.

If the stream-enterer is fixed by the determination of a prior cause, then because there is no supporting condition from a prior cause for the three paths above the stream-entry path, the function of a prior cause does not exist, and the stream-entry path itself comes to be the supporting condition. If there were also a supporting condition from a prior cause for that path, then it would be determined even before the arising of the stream-entry path. And if its prior cause were to extend up to the Akaniṭṭha realm, it would fall into causelessness; thus its accomplishment would be without cause and without condition. And what if there is a cause? If the stream-enterer is fixed by determination, then by the very attainment of the first path, the functions of the three higher paths are accomplished in succession. Thus, if there is the determination of being at most a seven-times-returner, then since the defilements—the root of suffering that would lead to continuation beyond the seventh existence—are destroyed by the first path itself, the three higher paths would be without function. Hence, it is said: ‘What is called making the first path a supporting condition?’ and so forth.

Yadi upari tayo maggā sattakkhattuparamāditaṃ niyamenti, tato ca añño sotāpanno natthīti sotāpattimaggassa akiccakatā nippayojanatā āpajjeyya. Atha sakkāyadiṭṭhiādippahānaṃ tassa kiccaṃ, tesaṃ tesaṃ pahānena sattakkhattuparamādiniyamatāya. Bhavitabbaṃ, yāva uparimaggā eva hontīti sattabhavādito uddhamapavattanato tena vinānena sakkāyadiṭṭhiādippahānena ca tena vinā bhavitabbanti āha – “paṭhamamagge anuppanneva upari tayo maggā uppannāti āpajjatī”ti. Tiṇṇaṃ maggānanti upari tiṇṇaṃ maggānaṃ. Vipassanā niyametīti yujjatīti vuttamatthaṃ vivaranto “sace hī”tiādimāha.

If the three higher paths were to determine the limit of being at most a seven-times-returner, then since there would be no other stream-enterer, the stream-entry path would become ineffective and purposeless. But the task of abandoning identity view and so forth belongs to it, and through the abandonment of these, the limitation to being at most a seven-times-returner is established. Since one does not continue beyond seven existences, and this is not possible without that abandoning of identity view and so forth, it would follow that this abandoning must occur without the first path. Therefore, he says: 'It would follow that the three higher paths have arisen even before the first path has arisen.' 'Of the three paths' means of the three higher paths. Expounding the meaning of what was said—'it is fitting that insight determines'—he states: 'If indeed...' and so forth.

Sotāpanno [V 2.0197] vaṭṭajjhāsayo. Tatrekacce pākaṭe paññāte dassento “anāthapiṇḍiko”tiādimāha. Idhaṭṭhakavokiṇṇasukkhavipassakassāti yo imasmiṃ kāmabhave ṭhito manussadevavasena vokiṇṇabhavūpapattiko sukkhavipassako ca, tassa vasena. Nāmaṃ kathitanti sattakkhattuparamoti nāmaṃ kathitaṃ. Keci pana “kāmabhave sattakkhattuṃyeva uppajjati, na tato”ti vadanti, taṃ vīmaṃsitabbaṃ.

The stream-enterer has an underlying tendency towards the round of existence. In this regard, showing some who are evident and well-known, he says 'Anāthapiṇḍika' and so forth. Here, 'of one who is a dry-insight meditator intermingled in this world' means with reference to one who, established in this realm of sensual existence, has mixed rebirths as humans and gods, and is also a dry-insight meditator. The name 'at most seven times' is spoken of. Some, however, say, 'He is reborn only seven times in the realm of sensual existence, and no more.' This should be examined.

Sodhessāmīti jambudīpe kenaci tepiṭakena bhikkhunā saddhiṃ piṭakattayameva mayhaṃ uggahaparipucchāvasena sodheyyāmīti paratīraṃ jambudīpaṃ gato. Yo bhikkhu sakkotīti yojanā. Aniccānupassanādīsu ekamukhena abhiniviṭṭhenapi abhidhammapariyāyena tīhi eva vimokkhehi maggaṃ labhatīti abhinivesabhedena tayo puggalā suññatato vuṭṭhitā, tathā tayo appaṇihitato vuṭṭhitāti cha honti, teva saddhādhurapaññādhuravasena dvādasa sakadāgāmino. Tathā arahanto, tayo antarāparinibbāyino [M 2.0496] eko upahaccaparinibbāyī eko uddhaṃsoto akaniṭṭhagāmīti pañca, te asaṅkhārasasaṅkhāraparinibbāyibhedena dasāti avihādīsu catūsupi cattālīsa, akaniṭṭhe pana uddhaṃsoto natthīti aṭṭhacattālīsa anāgāmino. Vipassanā kathitā sammasanacārassa kathitattā.

"I will purify it"—meaning that he went to Jambudīpa across the sea, thinking, "In Jambudīpa, I will purify the three baskets through learning and questioning with any monk versed in the three baskets." 'The monk who is capable'—this is the connection. Even one who is firmly established in the contemplation of impermanence, etc., through a single focus, attains the path by means of the three liberations according to the Abhidhamma method. Thus, by the distinction of adherence, there are three persons who arise from emptiness, and likewise three who arise from the desireless—making six. These, by the distinction of being faith-inclined and wisdom-inclined, become twelve once-returners. Similarly, regarding the non-returners: there are three who attain parinibbāna in the interval, one who attains parinibbāna upon arising, and one who goes upstream to the Akaniṭṭha realm—making five. These, by the distinction of attaining parinibbāna with or without prompting, become ten. Thus, in the four Pure Abodes beginning with Aviha, there are forty. In the Akaniṭṭha realm, however, the upstream-goer does not exist, so there are eight. This makes forty-eight non-returners. Insight is spoken of because the practice of comprehension has been spoken of.

5-10. Suddhakasuttādivaṇṇanā

5-10. Commentary on the Suddhaka Sutta, etc.

495-500. Yathā cakkhussa sahajātatadindriyanissitadhammesu adhipateyyaṃ anuvattanīyattā, evaṃ taṃdvārikadhammesupi adhipateyyaṃ tehi anuvattanīyattāti vuttaṃ – “adhipateyyasaṅkhātena indaṭṭhenā”ti. Esa nayo sesindriyādīsupi. Catusaccavasena kathitāni sabhāvādivibhāvanassa kathitattā.

495-500. Just as the eye-faculty has dominance over its co-nascent and faculty-dependent phenomena because they follow it, so too it has dominance over phenomena connected to its door because they follow it—thus it is said, "by the meaning of 'indriya' as controlling." This is the method for the remaining faculties, and so forth. They are spoken of in terms of the four truths because the exposition of their intrinsic nature, etc., has been given.

Chaḷindriyavaggavaṇṇanā niṭṭhitā.

The Commentary on the Chapter of the Six Faculties is finished.

4. Sukhindriyavaggo

4. The Chapter on the Pleasure Faculty

1-5. Suddhikasuttādivaṇṇanā

1-5. Commentary on the Suddhika Sutta, etc.

501-505. Yathā cakkhu dassane adhipateyyaṭṭhena cakkhundriyaṃ, evaṃ sukhavedanā sukhane adhipateyyaṭṭhena [V 2.0198] sukhindriyaṃ. Esa nayo sesesupi. Sesaṃ tebhūmakanti sesaṃ bhūmittayavasena tebhūmakaṃ.

501-505. Just as the eye is the eye-faculty by its meaning of dominance in seeing, so too, pleasant feeling is the pleasure-faculty by its meaning of dominance in pleasure. This is the method for the rest. “The remainder pertains to the three planes” means the remainder is of the three planes by way of the threefold planes of existence.

6. Paṭhamavibhaṅgasuttavaṇṇanā

6. Commentary on the First Analysis Sutta

506. Kāyikanti ayamassa nissayavasena niddesoti āha – “kāyapasādavatthuka”nti. Sarūpaniddeso sukhaṃ sukhindriyassa sarūpanti. Sādanīyaṭṭhena dhammapadāni attani assādayatīti sātaṃ, madhuraṃ. Vedayitanti vediyanaṃ, anubhavananti attho. Aññadhammavisiṭṭhoti phassādīhi aññehi dhammehi visadiso. Kāyikanti pasādakāyasannissitaṃ. Cetasikanti cetosannissitaṃ, tena vuttaṃ “ettha panā”tiādi. Kāyapasāda…pe… natthi, tasmā “cattāro pasādakāye vatthuṃ katvā”ti vuttaṃ.

506. The term 'bodily' is a designation based on its dependence—thus it is said, "having the body's sensitivity as its basis." The exposition of its own nature is that pleasure is the intrinsic nature of the pleasure faculty. By the meaning of 'pleasing,' it is `sāta` (pleasant), `madhura` (sweet), as when one savors Dhamma terms in oneself. 'Felt' means feeling, experiencing—that is the meaning. 'Distinct from other phenomena' means unlike other phenomena such as contact, etc. 'Bodily' means dependent on the sensitive body. 'Mental' means dependent on the mind, hence it is said, "but here," etc. The body's sensitivity... does not exist, therefore it is stated, "the four sensitivities, having the physical body as their base."

9. Kaṭṭhopamasuttavaṇṇanā

9. Commentary on the Simile of the Log Sutta

509. Dvinnaṃ [M 2.0497] araṇīnanti adharuttarāraṇīnaṃ. Kiñci dvayaṃ saṅghaṭṭitamattaṃ hutvā na samodhānagataṃ hotīti taṃnivattanatthaṃ “saṅghaṭṭanasamodhānā”ti vuttaṃ. Punappunaṃ saṅghaṭṭanena hi tejopātubhāvo. Adharāraṇī viya vatthārammaṇaṃ asatipi vāyāme tajjasamphassapaccayato. Uttarāraṇī viya phasso vatthārammaṇādiphassena pavattanato. Saṅghaṭṭo viya phassasaṅghaṭṭanaṃ araṇidvayasaṅghaṭṭanā viya phassasseva vatthārammaṇesu saṅghaṭṭanākārena pavattito. Aggi viya vedanā anudahanaṭṭhena khaṇikāvāyañca. Vatthārammaṇaṃ vā uttarāraṇī viya indhanāpātagahaṇādīsu ussāhassa viya pavattisambhavato. Phasso adharāraṇī viya nirussāhanirīhatāvasena attasādhanato.

509. "Of two fire-sticks" refers to the lower and upper fire-sticks. "By striking and joining" is said to prevent a situation where the two are merely struck together but not properly brought into conjunction. For the manifestation of heat arises through repeated striking. The lower fire-stick is like the basis and object, because of being a condition for contact arising from it, even in the absence of effort. The upper fire-stick is like contact, because it operates through contact with the basis and object, and so on. The striking is like the striking of contact, because contact itself proceeds in the manner of striking against the basis and object, just like the striking of the two fire-sticks. The fire is like feeling, due to its nature of burning and its momentariness. Or, the basis and object are like the upper fire-stick, because the arising of exertion is possible, as in the seizing of fuel and so on. Contact is like the lower fire-stick, because it accomplishes itself by way of being without exertion and without striving.

10. Uppaṭipāṭikasuttavaṇṇanā

10. Commentary on the Uppaṭipāṭika Sutta

510. Rasanaṃ bhañjanaṃ nirujjhanaṃ raso. Yo yo dhammānaṃ raso yathādhammaraso, tena yathādhammarasena. Paṭipāṭiyāti kamena, ubhayenapi dhammānaṃ pahānakkamenāti vuttaṃ hoti. Imasmiṃ indriyavibhaṅgeti imasmiṃ indriyasaṃyuttasaññite indriyavibhaṅge. Adesitattāti sesasuttāni viya “sukhindriya”ntiādinā adesitattā idaṃ uppaṭipāṭikasuttaṃ nāma. Valiyā kharasamphassāya phuṭṭhassa. Tanti kaṇṭakavedanādiṃ. Etassa dukkhindriyassa.

510. Tasting, breaking, and ceasing is 'taste.' Whatever is the taste of phenomena, the taste according to Dhamma—by that taste according to Dhamma. 'In sequence' means gradually; it is said that by both, phenomena are abandoned in due order. 'In this analysis of faculties' means in this analysis of faculties designated 'Connected with the Faculties.' 'Because it was not taught'—because, unlike the other suttas, it was not taught beginning with 'the pleasure faculty,' etc., this is named the Uppaṭipāṭika Sutta, the Sutta on the Contrary Order. 'Of one touched by the harsh contact of a creeper.' 'That' refers to the painful feeling from thorns, etc. Of this faculty of pain.

Tesaṃ [V 2.0199] tesanti domanassindriyādīnaṃ. Kāraṇavasenevāti taṃtaṃasādhāraṇakāraṇavasena. Tesañhi visesakāraṇaṃ dassento “pattacīvarādīnaṃ vā”ti āha.

'Of those, of those' refers to the faculties of displeasure, etc. "By reason of cause" means by reason of a cause peculiar to each. For, showing the specific cause of these, he says, "or of bowl and robes, etc."

Ekatovāti punappunaṃ paduddhāraṇaṃ akatvā ekajjhameva. Dutiyajjhānādīnaṃ upacārakkhaṇe eva nirujjhantīti ānetvā sambandho. Tesaṃ dukkhindriyadomanassādīnaṃ. Atisayanirodhoti suṭṭhu pahānaṃ ujuppaṭipakkhena vūpasamo. Nirodhoyevāti nirodhamattameva. Nānāvajjaneti yena āvajjanena appanāvīthi hoti, tato bhijjāvajjane, anekāvajjane vā. Appanāvīthiyañhi upacāro ekāvajjano, itaro anekāvajjano anekakkhattuṃ pavattanato. Visamanisajjāya uppannakilamatho visamāsanupatāpo[M 2.0498]. Pītipharaṇenāti pītiyā pharaṇarasattā. Pītisamuṭṭhānānaṃ vā paṇītarūpānaṃ kāyassa byāpanato vuttaṃ. Tenāha “sabbo kāyo sukhokkanto hotī”ti. Vitakkavicārapaccayepīti pi-saddo aṭṭhānapayutto, so “pahīnassā”ti ettha ānetvā sambandhitabbo “pahīnassapi domanassindriyassā”ti. Etaṃ domanassindriyaṃ uppajjatīti sambandho. “Tassa mayhaṃ aticiraṃ vitakkayato vicārayato kāyopi kilami, cittampi vihaññī”ti ca vacanato kāyacittakhedānaṃ vitakkavicārapaccayatā veditabbā. Vitakkavicārabhāve uppajjati domanassindriyanti ānetvā sambandhitabbaṃ. Tatthassa siyā uppattīti tattha dutiyajjhānupacāre assa domanassassa uppatti bhaveyya. “Tatthassa siyā uppattī”ti idaṃ parikappavacanaṃ upacārakkhaṇe domanassassa suppahīnabhāvadassanatthaṃ. Tathā hi vuttaṃ “na tveva dutiyajjhāne pahīnapaccayattā”ti. Pahīnampi somanassindriyaṃ pīti viya na dūreti katvā “āsannattā”ti vuttaṃ. Nānāvajjanupacāre pahīnampi pahānaṅgaṃ paṭipakkhena avihatattā antarantarā uppajjeyyāti imamatthaṃ dassento “appanāppattāyā”tiādimāha. “Tādisāya āsevanāya icchitabbattā yathā maggavīthito pubbe dve tayo javanavārā sadisānupassanāva pavattanti, evamidhāpi appanāvārato pubbe dve tayo javanavārā upekkhāsahagatāva pavattantī”ti vadanti. Aparisesanti suvikkhambhitanti katvā vikkhambhanena anavasesaṃ.

“Ekatovāti” means without repeating the words, but all at once. The connection should be made by bringing it to mean that they cease only at the moment of access to the second jhāna, and so on. “Of those” refers to the faculties of pain and displeasure, and so on. “Atisayanirodhoti” means thorough abandonment, the pacification by the direct opposite. “Nirodhoyevāti” means cessation alone. “Nānāvajjaneti” refers to the advertence by which the absorption process occurs, then in a different advertence, or in multiple advertences. For in the absorption process, access has a single advertence, while the other has multiple advertences because it occurs repeatedly. Fatigue arising from uneven sitting is the distress from an uneven seat. By “pervading with joy” (pītipharaṇena) is meant because of the pervading nature of joy. Or it is said because of the pervading of the refined forms born from joy throughout the body. Hence it is said, “The entire body is suffused with bliss.” In “vitakkavicārapaccayepīti,” the particle “pi” is used in the wrong place; it should be brought here and connected with “pahīnassa” as, “pahīnassapi domanassindriyassā,” meaning “even of the faculty of displeasure that has been abandoned.” This faculty of displeasure arises—this is the connection. From the statement, “For me, thinking and pondering for too long, my body grew tired and my mind became distressed,” it should be understood that bodily and mental fatigue are conditioned by thought and examination. It should be connected thus: the faculty of displeasure arises when there is thought and examination. “Tatthassa siyā uppattīti” means: there, in the access to the second jhāna, the arising of this displeasure could occur. This phrase, “there, it might arise,” is a speculative statement, meant to show the thorough abandonment of displeasure at the moment of access. Thus it is said, “But not in the second jhāna, because its conditions have been abandoned.” Because the faculty of mental pleasure, though abandoned, is not far away, like rapture, it is said “due to proximity.” To show that in access with multiple advertences, even though the factor of abandoning has been abandoned, it might arise intermittently because it has not been struck down by its opposite, he said, “appanāppattāyā,” and so on. They say: “Because such a practice is desirable, just as before the path process two or three impulsion-moments of similar insight occur, so here too, before the absorption-moment, two or three impulsion-moments accompanied by equanimity occur.” “Aparisesan ti” means thoroughly suppressed; that is, without remainder by means of suppression.

Tathattāyāti tathabhāvāya paṭhamajjhānasamaṅgitāya. Sā panassa uppādanena vā uppannassa samāpajjanena vā hotīti vuttaṃ “uppādanatthāya samāpajjanatthāyā”ti. Dvīsūti navamadasamesu suttantesu.

“Tathattāyāti” means for the sake of suchness, that is, for the sake of the state of being so, endowed with the first jhāna. And it occurs either through its production or through entering into it once produced, as stated: “for the purpose of production, for the purpose of attainment.” “Dvīsūti” means in two places, that is, in the ninth and tenth discourses.

Sukhindriyavaggavaṇṇanā niṭṭhitā.

The Commentary on the Chapter on the Faculty of Pleasure is finished.

5. Jarāvaggo

5. The Chapter on Aging

1. Jarādhammasuttavaṇṇanā

1. Commentary on the Jarādhamma Sutta

511. Pamukhe[V 2.0200], pāsādassa ca pacchimabhāge ātapo pacchātapo, tasmiṃ pacchātape. So hi pāsādassa pamukhabhāvena kato. Kiṃ pana bhagavato vajirasāraṃ sarīraṃ otāpetabbaṃ hotīti āha – “sammāsambuddhassā”tiādi[M 2.0499]. Ayañca imaṃ suttaṃ desitasamaye. Na sakkoti byāmappabhāya kāyaci pabhāya anabhibhavanīyattā. Kiñcāpi buddhābhā sūriyābhāya anabhibhavanīyā, ghammasabhāvatāya pana rasmīnaṃ parito pharantī sūriyābhā tikhiṇā uṇhāti āha “rasmiteja”nti. Idāni tamatthaṃ upamāya vibhāvetuṃ “yatheva hī”tiādi vuttaṃ.

511. “Pamukhe” means at the front, and in the rear part of the palace, there was sunshine, afternoon sunshine; in that afternoon sunshine. For it was made as the foremost part of the palace. But why should the adamantine body of the Blessed One be heated? He said: “For the Perfectly Self-Enlightened One,” etc. And this was at the time when this sutta was taught. He could not be overcome by any radiance, not even by the fathom-wide radiance, due to his unovercomable nature. Although the Buddha’s radiance is unovercomable by the sun’s radiance, yet because of the nature of heat, the sun’s rays, spreading all around, are sharp and hot—thus it is called “the heat of the rays.” Now, to clarify that meaning by means of a simile, “yatheva hī,” etc., was said.

Suvaṇṇāvaṭṭaṃ viyāti acche suvaṇṇapatte vinivattaāvaṭṭaṃ viya. Garahaṇacchariyaṃ nāma kiretaṃ “acchariyametaṃ avisicchephalavada”ntiādīsu viya. Na evametarahīti attano pākaṭavasena vadati, na itaresampīti. Sirājālāti sirāsantānā. Evarūpaṃ na hotīti aññesaṃ pākatikasattānaṃ vuttākāraṃ viya na hoti puññasambhārassa uḷāratamattā vipaccanassa pariyantagatattā. Tenāha “aññesaṃ apākaṭa”nti. Valiyāvaṭṭakanti appakaṃ valiyāvaṭṭaṃ. Tenāha “kesaggappamāṇa”nti. Sabbānīti sirājālāni. Purato vaṅkoti thokaṃ purato natamattaṃ sandhāyāha. Tena vuttaṃ “svāyaṃ aññesaṃ apākaṭo”ti. Nayaggāhatoti anumānato. Dhī tanti dhī-saddayoge upayogavacanaṃ. Dhīti jigucchanatthe nipāto. Dhikkāroti jigucchāpayogo. Taṃ phusatūti tuyhaṃ pāpuṇātu.

“Suvaṇṇāvaṭṭaṃ viyāti” means like a golden curl—like a curl turned back on a flawless golden plate. It is said that this is a reproachful wonder, as in “acchariyametaṃ avisaṃvādakaphalaṃ,” etc. “Na evametarahīti” means “Not so now”—he speaks of his own manifest state, not of others’. “Sirājālāti” means a network of veins—a continuous line of veins. “Evarūpaṃ na hotīti” means “Such does not exist”—unlike the appearance of ordinary beings, due to the supreme greatness of his accumulation of merit and because its ripening has reached its limit. Therefore, it is said: “For others, it is unmanifest.” “Valiyāvaṭṭakanti” means a slight wrinkle—a small wrinkle. Hence it is said: “The size of a hair-tip.” “Sabbānīti” means all—the network of veins. “Purato vaṅkoti” means bent forward—he refers to a slight bending forward. Hence it is said: “This is unmanifest in others.” “Nayaggāhatoti” means by inference—through reasoning. “Dhī tanti”: this is a statement of application in conjunction with the word “dhī.” “Dhīti” is an indeclinable in the sense of disgust. “Dhikkāroti” means reproach—an expression of disgust. “Taṃ phusatūti” means “May it strike you”—may it reach you.

2. Uṇṇābhabrāhmaṇasuttavaṇṇanā

2. Commentary on the Uṇṇābhabrāhmaṇa Sutta

512. Aññamaññassāti añño aññassa. Na paccanubhoti attano avisayabhāvato. Idāni taṃ aññavisayataṃ anvayato byatirekato vibhāvetuṃ “sace hī”tiādi vuttaṃ.

512. “Aññamaññassāti” means one to another. “Na paccanubhoti” means “does not experience,” because it is beyond one’s own sphere. Now, to explain that state of being within another’s sphere by means of affirmation and negation, “sace hī,” etc., was said.

Visayāni paṭisaranti etthāti paṭisaraṇaṃ. Indriyaviññāṇāni hi asatipi tādise adhippāye attano ārammaṇassa yāthāvato sampajānanato pavedanavijānanāni karontāni viya pavattanti, tathā lokassa aññattha siddhito. Tenāha bhagavā – “mano paṭisaraṇaṃ, manova nesaṃ [V 2.0201] gocaravisayaṃ paccanubhotī”ti manodvārikajavanamano hi savisesaṃ manovisayaṃ paccanubhoti, pañcadvārikajavanamano mananamattameva paccanubhavati. Rajjanādiggahaṇañcettha nidassanamattaṃ, tasmā saddahanādipi gahitamevāti daṭṭhabbaṃ, pañcadvārappavattivasena tathā vuttaṃ. Ekasmiṃ pana dvāreti cakkhudvāre.

Objects resort here (visayāni paṭisaranti ettha); thus, it is a resort (paṭisaraṇaṃ). For even without such an intention, the sense consciousnesses appear to function by clearly knowing their objects as they truly are, proceeding as if making them known and understood; and thus it is established elsewhere in the world. Therefore, the Blessed One said: “The mind is the resort; it is the mind that experiences their range of objects.” For the mind-door impulsion-mind specifically experiences a mental object, while the five-door impulsion-mind experiences only mere cognizing. And here, the mention of lust and so on is merely illustrative. Therefore, it should be understood that faith and so on are also included; this is said according to the mode of functioning of the five doors. “Ekasmiṃ pana dvāreti” means but in one door, that is, the eye-door.

Dubbalabhojakāti [M 2.0500] appānubhāvā rājabhoggā. Āyanti bhoguppattiṭṭhānaṃ. Yottabandhādinimittaṃ laddhabbakahāpaṇo yottakahāpaṇo. Addubandhādinimittaṃ gahetabbakahāpaṇo addukahāpaṇo. Māghātaghosanāya katāya hiṃsānimittaṃ gahetabbakahāpaṇo māpahārakahāpaṇo. Tassa parimāṇaṃ dassetuṃ “aṭṭhakahāpaṇo”tiādi vuttaṃ. Satavatthukanti satakarīsavatthukaṃ.

“Dubbalabhojakāti” means kings of little power who enjoy royal resources. “Āyan ti” means income: the place where wealth arises. A “yottakahāpaṇa” is a kahāpaṇa to be obtained as a token for yoke-binding and so on. An “addukahāpaṇa” is a kahāpaṇa to be taken as a token for carrying-pole binding and so on. A “māpahārakahāpaṇa” is a kahāpaṇa to be taken as a token for violence when the proclamation “Do not kill” has been made. To indicate its measure, “eight kahāpaṇas,” and so on, was said. “Satavatthukan ti” means having a plot of land of one hundred karīsas.

Maggasatīti ariyamaggasati. Bhāvanamanuyuñjantassa hi javanamano ussakkitvāva maggasatiṃ paṭisarati tappariyosānattā. Tanti nibbānaṃ. Sāti phalavimutti. Paṭisarati aggamaggasatiyā. Phalavimutti nibbānanti ubhayaṃ maggassa siddhāyevāti. Ārammaṇavasena natthi etassa paṭisaraṇanti appaṭisaraṇaṃ asaṅkhatāmatassa santiniccasabhāvattā. Sayaṃ pana sabbesaṃyeva ariyānaṃ paṭisaraṇaṃ. Tenāha “nibbānaṃ arahato gatī”ti (pari. 339). Nibbānaṃ anupaviṭṭhaṃ nibbānanissayattā. Na tato paraṃ gacchati gatassa aññassa tādisassa abhāvā. Nibbānaṃ pari sabbaso osānanti nibbānapariyosānaṃ.

“Maggasatīti” means the mindfulness of the noble path. For one devoted to cultivation, the impulsion-mind strives and recurs to path-mindfulness, because that is its culmination. “Tan ti” means that, which refers to Nibbāna. “Sā ti” means this, which is the liberation of fruition. It recurs to the supreme path-mindfulness. The liberation of fruition and Nibbāna are both accomplishments of the path. In terms of object, there is no resort for it, thus it is without resort, because the unconditioned deathless has a nature of peace and permanence. But it is itself the refuge for all noble ones. Therefore it is said: “Nibbāna is the destiny of the arahant” (Pari. 339). It has entered Nibbāna, because Nibbāna is its support. One does not go beyond that, because there is no other similar destination to which one could go. Nibbāna is the complete end all around; hence, “culminating in Nibbāna” (nibbānapariyosānaṃ).

Mūlajātā jātamūlā. Tato eva patiṭṭhitā. Kā panassāti āha “maggena āgatasaddhā”ti. Maggo daḷhāya asaṃhāriyasaddhāya mūlaṃ diṭṭhisampayuttāni ceva vicikicchācittañcāti pañca akusalacittāni samucchedavasena pahīnāni. Pañca nīvaraṇānīti ettha apāyagamanīyāni paṭhamamaggeneva pahīnāni, itarāni vikkhambhanavasena jhānena pahīnānīti pañcasu orambhāgiyakilesasaṃyojanesu ekadesavigameneva bahiddhāsaṃyojano viya jātoti vuttaṃ “jhānaanāgāmiṭṭhāne ṭhito”ti. Tenāha “so aparihīna…pe… nibbāyeyyā”ti.

‘Root-born’ means having a root that has arisen. They are established precisely from that. To the question, ‘But what is it?,’ it is said: ‘faith that has come by way of the path.’ The path is the root of firm, unshakable faith. The five unwholesome consciousnesses—namely, those associated with wrong view and the one associated with doubt—are abandoned by way of eradication. Here, regarding the five hindrances, those leading to the lower realms are abandoned by the first path itself, while the others are abandoned by suppression through jhāna. Thus, concerning the five lower fetters, through the removal of only a part of them, he is born as if with an external fetter; hence it is said, ‘standing in the state of a jhāna-non-returner.’ Therefore, it is said: ‘he, not having fallen away… might attain Nibbāna.’

5. Paṭhamapubbārāmasuttavaṇṇanā

5. Exposition of the First Pubbārāma Sutta

515. Pubbakoṭṭhake evaṃ āgatasuttaṃ ādiṃ katvā phalindriyāneva kathitāni aggaphalavasena desanāya āgatattā.

515. Beginning with the discourse that came to be at the eastern gatehouse, only the fruit-faculties were discussed, because they were presented in the teaching by way of the supreme fruit.

10. Āpaṇasuttavaṇṇanā

10. Exposition of the Āpaṇa Sutta

520. Upari [V 2.0202] saha vipassanāya tayo maggāti vipassanāya saha sotāpattiphalato upari tayo maggā. Maggādhigamena idāni paccakkhabhūtattā [M 2.0501] “ime kho te dhammā”ti vuttā. Tattha yaṃ aggabhūtaṃ, tassa vasena dassetuṃ “arahattaphalindriyaṃ nāmā”ti vuttaṃ indriyabhāvasāmaññena ekajjhaṃ katvā. Ativijjhitvā passāmīti sacchikatvā yāthāvato passāmi. Catūhi indriyehīti vīriyindriyādīhi catūhi indriyehi. Sā vipassanāmaggaphalasahagatā siyāti missakā vuttā.

520. “Above, along with insight, the three paths” means the three paths above the fruit of stream-entry, together with insight. Because they are now directly realized through the attainment of the path, it is said, “These indeed are those qualities.” Here, to indicate what is supreme, it is said, “It is called the faculty of the fruit of arahantship,” having combined them into one under the commonality of being a faculty. “I see by thoroughly penetrating” means I realize and see as it truly is. “By four faculties” refers to the four faculties beginning with the faculty of energy. That insight which is accompanied by path and fruit is said to be mixed.

Jarāvaggavaṇṇanā niṭṭhitā.

The Exposition on the Jarāvagga is concluded.

6. Sūkarakhatavaggo

6. The Sūkarakhatā Chapter

1. Sālasuttavaṇṇanā

1. Exposition of the Sāla Sutta

521. Sūrabhāvenāti atisūrabhāvena. Bujjhanatthāyāti saccapaṭivedhāya.

521. “By courage” means by extreme courage. “For the purpose of awakening” means for the penetration of the truths.

2. Mallikasuttavaṇṇanā

2. Exposition of the Mallika Sutta

522. Cattāri indriyānīti paññindriyaṃ ṭhapetvā sesāni cattāri. “Ariyañāṇaṃ lokuttara”nti vuttaṃ maggañāṇaṃ katvā. Ariya-saddo pana yathā tathā visuddhepi hotīti tādisaṃ sandhāya vuttaṃ “tampi panā”tiādi. Yathā hi cattārindriyāni missakāni, evaṃ paññindriyampi missakanti vuccamāne na koci virodhoti adhippāyenāha “tampi pana…pe… vaṭṭatī”ti.

522. “Four faculties” means the remaining four, excluding the faculty of wisdom. “Noble knowledge is supramundane” is said, taking it as path-knowledge. However, the term “noble” can also apply to what is purified in any way whatsoever; referring to such, it is said, “But even that,” etc. Just as the four faculties are mixed, so too, when it is said that the faculty of wisdom is mixed, there is no contradiction; with this intention it is stated, “But even that... is suitable.”

3. Sekhasuttavaṇṇanā

3. Exposition of the Sekha Sutta

523. Na sakkoti indriyānaṃ aparipakkattā. Atthīti pajānāti nayaggāhena, na paccakkhato. Na hi ariyāpi anadhigataṃ maggaphalaṃ paccavekkhituṃ sakkonti.

523. “He cannot” because of the immaturity of the faculties. “He understands ‘there is’” by grasping the method, not directly. For even noble ones cannot review a path or fruit they have not attained.

6. Patiṭṭhitasuttavaṇṇanā

6. Exposition of the Patiṭṭhita Sutta

526. Sāsavesūti [V 2.0203] caturāsavavinimuttesu sesadhammesu ārammaṇesu. Tesupi uppajjanakaanatthato cittaṃ rakkhati nāma.

526. Regarding ‘in things with taints’: this refers to the remaining phenomena, which are objects, that are freed from the four taints. Even in regard to these, it is called ‘guarding the mind’ from the harm that might arise.

8. Sūkarakhatasuttavaṇṇanā

8. Exposition of the Sūkarakhatā Sutta

528. Taṃ [M 2.0502] sandhāyāti taṃ sūkarakhataleṇaṃ sandhāya. Etaṃ “sūkarakhatāya”nti vacanaṃ vuttaṃ. Bhāvanapuṃsakanti bhāvajotakaṃ napuṃsakavacanaṃ yathā “visamaṃ vātā vāyanti, ekamantaṃ nisīdī”ti. Kiccapaṭipatti tesaṃ saṃkāsanaṭṭhena sapatiso, sapatiso eva sappatisso, sajeṭṭhakoti āha “sappatissoti sajeṭṭhako”ti.

528. “With reference to that” means with reference to the Sūkarakhatā cave. For this, the word ‘sūkarakhatāya’ is used. ‘Bhāvanapuṃsaka’ is a neuter word indicating a state, as in ‘the winds blow unevenly (visamaṃ)’ and ‘he sat down to one side (ekamantaṃ).’ The proper conduct for the purpose of making things clear to them is to be respectful (sapatiso). Sapatiso is the same as sappatisso, which means with a senior (sajeṭṭhako). Thus it is said: ‘sappatisso means with a senior.’

Sūkarakhatavaggavaṇṇanā niṭṭhitā.

The Exposition on the Sūkarakhata Chapter is concluded.

7. Bodhipakkhiyavaggavaṇṇanā

7. Exposition of the Chapter on the Requisites of Enlightenment

531-650. Sattānaṃ phalānaṃ hetubhūtāni “imesaṃ kho, bhikkhave, pañcannaṃ indriyānaṃ bhāvitattā”tiādinā vuttāni, pañcindriyāniyeva phalūpacārena “satta phalānī”ti vuttāni. Tāni ca pubbabhāgāni “imesaṃ, bhikkhave, pañcannaṃ indriyānaṃ bhāvitattā…pe… sattānisaṃsā pāṭikaṅkhā”ti vacanato. Tesanti sattānaṃ phalānaṃ. “Dvinnaṃ phalānaṃ aññataraṃ phala”nti evaṃ atītasutte vuttāni heṭṭhā dve phalāni nāmāti vadanti. Yehi pana indriyehi aññatra pana antarāparinibbāyiṃ sesāni phalāni honti, tāni cattāri sapubbabhāgāni lokuttarānīti vuttaṃ siyā.

531-650. The causes of the seven fruits are the five faculties, as stated in the passage beginning, ‘Monks, because these five faculties have been developed...’ The five faculties themselves are called the ‘seven fruits’ by way of a metaphor based on the result. And those faculties, being in the preliminary stage, are mentioned in the statement: ‘Monks, because these five faculties have been developed... seven benefits are to be expected.’ ‘Of them’ (tesaṃ) means of the seven fruits. They say that by ‘one of two fruits,’ as stated in the previous sutta, the two lower fruits are meant. But as for the faculties by which the remaining fruits are attained—excluding that of the one who attains parinibbāna in the interval—it might be said that those four are supramundane, along with their preliminary stages.

Indriyasaṃyuttavaṇṇanā niṭṭhitā.

The Exposition on the Connected Discourses on the Faculties is concluded.

5. Sammappadhānasaṃyuttavaṇṇanā

5. Exposition of the Connected Discourses on Right Efforts

651-704. Sammappadhānasaṃyutte [V 2.0204] pubbabhāgavipassanāva kathitā “akusalānaṃ dhammānaṃ pahānāyā”tiādivacanato.

651-704. In the Connected Discourses on Right Efforts, only insight of the preliminary stage is discussed, based on the statement beginning: “For the abandoning of unwholesome qualities...”

Sammappadhānasaṃyuttavaṇṇanā niṭṭhitā.

The Exposition on the Connected Discourses on Right Efforts is concluded.

6. Balasaṃyuttavaṇṇanā

6. Exposition of the Connected Discourses on Strengths

705-812. Balasaṃyutte balāni missakāneva kathitāni “vivekanissita”nti vacanato.

705-812. In the Connected Discourses on Strengths, the strengths are discussed as mixed, as indicated by the phrase: “dependent on seclusion.”

Balasaṃyuttavaṇṇanā niṭṭhitā.

The Exposition on the Connected Discourses on Strengths is concluded.

7. Iddhipādasaṃyuttaṃ

7. The Connected Discourses on the Bases of Spiritual Power

1. Cāpālavaggo

1. The Cāpāla Chapter

1. Apārasuttavaṇṇanā

1. Exposition of the Apāra Sutta

813. Chandaṃ [V 2.0205][M 2.0503] nissāya pavatto samādhīti kattukamyatāchandaṃ adhipatiṃ katvā paṭiladdhasamādhi chandhasamādhi. Padhānasaṅkhārāti catukiccasādhakassa sammappadhānavīriyassetaṃ adhivacanaṃ. Tehīti chandasamādhipadhānasaṅkhārehi. Iddhiyā pādanti nipphattipariyāyena ijjhanaṭṭhena, ijjhanti etāya saddhā iddhā vuddhā ukkaṃsagatā hontīti iminā vā pariyāyena “iddhī”ti saṅkhātānaṃ upacārajjhānādikusalacittasampayuttānaṃ chandasamādhipadhānasaṅkhārānaṃ adhiṭṭhānaṭṭhena pādabhūtaṃ, sesacittacetasikarāsinti attho.
