Cetasikasukhapaṭilābhasaṃvattanaṃ tikacatukkajjhānavasena, upekkhāya vā santabhāvena sukhagatikattā sabbesampi jhānānaṃ vasena veditabbaṃ. Jhānasamuṭṭhānapaṇītarūpaphuṭṭhasarīratāvasena pana kāyikasukhapaṭilābhasaṃvattanaṃ daṭṭhabbaṃ, tañca kho jhānato vuṭṭhitakāle. Imasmiṃ pakkhe “appitappitakkhaṇe”ti idaṃ hetumhi bhummavacanaṃ daṭṭhabbaṃ.

There is nothing to sprinkle upon it to bring about a peaceful and refined state; hence, it is 'unsprinkled' (asecanaka). Because it is unsprinkled, it is 'unmoistened' (anāsittaka); and because it is unmoistened, it is truly 'unadulterated' (abbokiṇṇa), unmixed with preparatory work and the like. Therefore, it is individual, separate, and unique; special and uncommon. All this is said to show its peaceful nature as inherent, or that preparatory work is a sign of its peaceful nature. And by 'preparatory work' is meant that which culminates in the arising of a sign through making a kasiṇa and so on; the intention is that such a thing is not present here. For then, the meditation subject, being without savor, might be unpeaceful and unrefined. Or the construction is: in access concentration, there is no peacefulness here. Just as at the moment of access, for others there is peacefulness through the removal of the hindrances and the manifestation of the factors, it is not so for this. The construction, however, is: 'This, from the very beginning... is peaceful and refined.' 'Some' refers to the dwellers in the Northern Monastery. 'Unmoistened' (anāsittaka) means not moistened by sprinkling. Hence it is said, 'full of sap' (ojavanto), meaning 'like one full of sap.' 'Sweet' (madhuro) means desirable. Leading to the attainment of mental pleasure should be understood by way of the triad and tetrad of jhānas, or by way of all jhānas because equanimity, by its peaceful nature, has a happy destination. Leading to the attainment of bodily pleasure, however, should be seen by way of the body being pervaded by refined matter originating from jhāna, and that only at the time of emerging from jhāna. In this view, the phrase 'at the moment of absorption' (appitappitakkhaṇe) should be seen as a locative expression in the sense of cause.

Avikkhambhiteti jhānena sakasantānato anīhate appahīne. Akosallasambhūteti akosallaṃ vuccati avijjā, tato sambhūte. Avijjāpubbaṅgamā hi sabbe pāpadhammā. Khaṇenevāti attano pavattikkhaṇeneva. Antaradhāpetīti ettha antaradhāpanaṃ vināsanaṃ, taṃ pana jhānakattukaṃ [M 2.0522] idhādhippetanti pariyuṭṭhānappahānaṃ hotīti āha – “vikkhambhetī”ti. Vūpasametīti visesena upasameti. Visesena upasamanaṃ pana sammadeva upasamanaṃ hotīti āha “suṭṭhu upasametī”ti. Sāsanikassa jhānabhāvanā yebhuyyena nibbedhabhāgiyā hotīti āha “nibbedhabhāgiyattā”ti. Ariyamaggassa pādakabhūto ayaṃ samādhi anukkamena vaḍḍhitvā ariyamaggabhāvaṃ upagato viya hotīti āha “anupubbena ariyamaggavuḍḍhippatto”ti. Ayaṃ panattho virāganirodhapaṭinissaggānupassanānaṃ vasena sammadeva yujjati. Sesaṃ suviññeyyameva.

'Not suppressed' (avikkhambhita) means not removed or abandoned from one's own continuum by the jhāna. 'Born from lack of skill' (akosallasambhūta) refers to lack of skill, which is called ignorance (avijjā); arising from that. For all unwholesome states are preceded by ignorance. 'In a moment' (khaṇeneva) means in the very moment of its arising. 'Makes disappear' (antaradhāpeti) here means destruction; but here it is intended as done by the jhāna; thus, it refers to the abandoning of manifest defilements, so he says, 'suppresses' (vikkhambheti). 'Pacifies' (vūpasameti) means especially pacifies. And especially pacifying is truly pacifying, thus he says, 'well pacifies' (suṭṭhu upasameti). The meditative development of one in the Dispensation is for the most part of the nature of leading to penetration, thus he says, 'because it is of the nature of leading to penetration' (nibbedhabhāgiyattā). This concentration, being the foundation of the noble path, gradually develops and becomes as if it has attained the state of the noble path, thus he says, 'having progressively attained the growth of the noble path' (anupubbena ariyamaggavuḍḍhippatto). But this meaning is properly applied by way of the contemplation of dispassion, cessation, and relinquishment. The rest is easily understood.

10. Kimilasuttavaṇṇanā

10. The Explanation of the Kimila Sutta.

986. Theroti [V 2.0224] ānandatthero. Ayaṃ desanāti “kathaṃ vibhāvito nu kho kimilā”tiādinā pavattā desanā. Kāyaññataranti rūpakāye aññatarakoṭṭhāse. Evanti yathā ānāpānaṃ anussaranto bhikkhu kāye kāyānupassī jāto, evaṃ sabbattha vāresu vedanānupassītiādi attho veditabbo.

986. 'The elder' (thero) is the Venerable Ānanda. 'This teaching' (ayaṃ desanā) is the discourse that proceeds beginning with, 'O Kimila, how indeed is it explained...?' 'A certain part of the body' (kāyaññatara) means a certain portion in the material body. 'Thus' (evaṃ) means: just as a monk, recollecting the in-and-out breath, has become a contemplator of the body in the body, so too in all instances the meaning should be understood as 'a contemplator of feelings in feelings,' and so on.

Desanāsīsanti desanāpadesaṃ. Manasikārapadesena vedanā vuttāti taṃ sabbaṃ sarūpato aññāpadesato apadisati. Yatheva hītiādinā tattha nidassanaṃ dasseti. Cittasaṅkhārapadadvayeti “cittasaṅkhārapaṭisaṃvedī passambhayaṃ cittasaṅkhāra”nti etasmiṃ padadvaye.

'Heading of the teaching' (desanāsīsa) means a section of the teaching (desanāpadesa). Because feelings are spoken of by way of the section on attention, he indicates all that by another designation according to its nature. With the phrase beginning 'Just as indeed...' (yatheva hi...), he shows an illustration there. 'In the pair of terms concerning mental formation' (cittasaṅkhārapadadvaye) means in this pair of terms: 'experiencing the mental formation... calming the mental formation.'

Evaṃ santepīti pītimanasikāracittasaṅkhārapadesena yadi vedanā vuttā, evaṃ santepi. Esā yathāvuttā vedanā ārammaṇaṃ na hoti. Vedanārammaṇā ca anupassanā, tasmā vedanānupassanā na yujjati. Yadi evaṃ mahāsatipaṭṭhānādīsu “vedanā vediyatī”ti vuttaṃ, taṃ kathanti āha – “mahāsatipaṭṭhānādīsupī”tiādi. Tattha sukhādīnaṃ vatthunti sukhādīnaṃ uppattiyā vatthubhūtaṃ rūpasaddādiṃ ārammaṇaṃ katvā vedanā vediyati, na puggalo puggalasseva abhāvato. Vedanāppavattiṃ upādāya nissāya yathā “puggalo vedanaṃ vediyatī”ti vohāramattaṃ hoti. Evaṃ idhāpi vedanāya assāsapassāse ārabbha pavatti, tathā pavattamanasikārasīsena “vedanāsu vedanānupassī bhikkhu tasmiṃ samaye viharatī”ti vutto. Taṃ sandhāyāti taṃ vedanāya ārammaṇabhāvaṃ sandhāya[M 2.0523]. Ādīnaṃ padānaṃ. Etassa “vedanānupassanā na yujjatī”ti vuttaanuyogassa.

'Even so' (evaṃ santepi) means: if feeling is spoken of by way of the section on mental formation as attention to joy, even so, this aforementioned feeling does not become an object. And contemplation has feeling as its object; therefore, contemplation of feelings is not appropriate. If so, since it is said in the Mahāsatipaṭṭhāna and other suttas, 'feeling is felt,' how is that? He says, 'Even in the Mahāsatipaṭṭhāna, etc.' There, 'the basis of pleasure, etc.' means that having made an object of form, sound, etc., which is the basis for the arising of pleasure, etc., a feeling is felt, not a person, because a person simply does not exist. Just as, depending on and based on the occurrence of feeling, there is the mere convention, 'a person feels a feeling.' So here too, there is the occurrence of feeling in relation to the in-and-out breaths; and by the heading of attention that occurs in such a way, it is said, 'a monk dwells at that time contemplating feelings in feelings.' 'Referring to that' means referring to that state of feeling as an object. This is the meaning of the words beginning with 'ādi.' This is the response to this objection that was stated: 'contemplation of feelings is not appropriate.'

Sappītike dve jhāne samāpajjatīti pītisahagatāni paṭhamadutiyajjhānāni paṭipāṭiyā samāpajjati. Tassāti tena. Paṭisaṃviditasaddāpekkhāya hi kattuatthe etaṃ sāmivacanaṃ. Samāpattikkhaṇeti samāpajjanakkhaṇe. Jhānapaṭilābhenāti jhānena samaṅgībhāvena. Ārammaṇatoti ārammaṇamukhena tadārammaṇajhānapariyāpannā pīti paṭisaṃviditā hoti, ārammaṇassa paṭisaṃviditattāti vuttaṃ hoti. Yathā nāma sappapariyesanaṃ carantena tassa āsaye paṭisaṃvidite sopi paṭisaṃvidito hoti mantāgadabalena tassa gahaṇassa sukarattā, evaṃ pītiyā āsayabhūte ārammaṇe paṭisaṃvidite sā pīti paṭisaṃviditāva hoti salakkhaṇato sāmaññalakkhaṇato ca tassā gahaṇassa sukarattā. Vipassanakkhaṇeti vipassanāpaññāya tikkhavisadabhāvappattāya [V 2.0225] visayato dassanakkhaṇe. Lakkhaṇappaṭivedhenāti pītiyā salakkhaṇassa sāmaññalakkhaṇassa ca paṭivijjhanena. Yañhi pītiyā visesato sāmaññato ca lakkhaṇaṃ, tasmiṃ vidite sā yāthāvato viditā eva hoti. Tenāha “asammohato pīti paṭisaṃviditā hotī”ti.

'He attains the two jhānas accompanied by joy' (sappītike dve jhāne samāpajjati) means he successively attains the first and second jhānas, which are accompanied by joy. 'Of him' (tassa) means 'by him' (tena). Indeed, with reference to the word 'understood' (paṭisaṃvidita), this is a genitive case in the sense of an agent. 'At the moment of attainment' (samāpattikkhaṇe) means at the moment of entering. 'By the acquisition of jhāna' (jhānapaṭilābhena) means by being endowed with jhāna. 'Through the object' (ārammaṇato) means that by way of the object, the joy included in the jhāna that has that object is understood; the meaning is that because the object is understood, it is understood. Just as when one searching for a snake has understood its abode, the snake itself is also understood—since its capture is easy through the power of mantras and antidotes—so too, when the object that is the basis for joy is understood, that joy itself is understood, because grasping it in terms of its specific and general characteristics is easy. 'At the moment of insight' (vipassanakkhaṇe) means at the moment of seeing its domain by means of insight-wisdom that has reached a state of sharpness and clarity. 'By penetrating the characteristics' (lakkhaṇappaṭivedhena) means by the penetration of the specific characteristic and the general characteristic of joy. For when the characteristic of joy, both specific and general, is known, then it is known as it really is. Therefore, he said, 'Joy is understood without delusion' (asammohato pīti paṭisaṃviditā hoti).

Idāni tamatthaṃ pāḷiyā eva vibhāvetuṃ “vuttañheta”ntiādimāha. Tattha dīghaṃ assāsavasenāti dīghassa assāsassa ārammaṇabhūtassa vasena. Pajānato sā pīti paṭisaṃviditā hotīti sambandho. Cittassa ekaggataṃ avikkhepaṃ pajānatoti jhānapariyāpannaṃ “avikkhepo”ti laddhanāmaṃ cittassekaggataṃ taṃsampayuttāya paññāya pajānato. Yathā hi ārammaṇamukhena pīti paṭisaṃviditā hoti, evaṃ taṃsampayuttadhammāpi ārammaṇamukhena paṭisaṃviditā eva hontīti. Sati upaṭṭhitā hotīti dīghaṃ assāsavasena jhānasampayuttā sati tasmiṃ ārammaṇe upaṭṭhitā ārammaṇamukhena jhānepi upaṭṭhitā nāma hoti. Tāya satiyāti evaṃ upaṭṭhitāya tāya satiyā yathāvuttena tena ñāṇena suppaṭividitattā ārammaṇassa tassa vasena tadārammaṇā sā pīti paṭisaṃviditā hoti. Dīghaṃ passāsavasenātiādīsupi imināva nayena attho veditabbo. Tena vuttaṃ “eteneva nayena avasesapadānipi atthato veditabbānī”ti.

Now, to clarify that meaning by means of the Pāli text itself, he begins with 'for it has been said' (vuttañheta) and so on. Here, 'by way of the long in-breath' (dīghaṃ assāsavasena) means by way of the long in-breath, which has become the object. The connection is that 'joy is understood by one who discerns' (pajānato sā pīti paṭisaṃviditā hoti). 'One discerns the one-pointedness of mind, its undistractedness' (cittassa ekaggataṃ avikkhepaṃ pajānato) means one discerns, by means of the wisdom associated with it, the one-pointedness of mind that is included in jhāna and has received the name 'undistractedness' (avikkhepo). For just as joy is understood by way of the object, so too are the associated states understood by way of the object. 'Mindfulness is established' (sati upaṭṭhitā hoti) means that mindfulness, associated with jhāna by way of the long in-breath, is established on that object; by way of the object, it is also said to be established in jhāna. 'By that mindfulness' (tāya satiyā) means: by that mindfulness thus established, because the object is well penetrated by that aforementioned knowledge, that joy which has that as its object is understood by way of that. In the case of 'by way of the long out-breath' (dīghaṃ passāsavasena) and so on, the meaning should be understood in this same way. Therefore, it is said: 'In this manner, the remaining terms should also be understood in meaning' (eteneva nayena avasesapadānipi atthato veditabbānī).

Yathevātiādīsu [M 2.0524] ayaṃ saṅkhepattho – jhānapaṭilābhena yathā ārammaṇato pītiādayo paṭisaṃviditā honti tena vinā tesaṃ appavattanato. Evaṃ jhānasampayuttena taṃpariyāpannena vedanāsaṅkhātamanasikārapaṭilābhena ārammaṇato vedanā paṭisaṃviditā hoti aruṇuggamena viya sūriyassa tena vinā vedanāya appavattanato. Tasmā vuttanayena atisayappavatti vedanā suppaṭividitā, tasmā suvuttametanti pubbe vuttacodanaṃ nirākaroti.

In the phrases such as 'just as' (yatheva), this is the concise meaning: through the attainment of jhāna, factors such as joy are known from their object, because without it, they do not occur. Similarly, through the attainment of attention—designated as feeling, associated with jhāna, and included within it—feeling is known from its object, just as the sun is known by the rising of the dawn, because without it, feeling does not occur. Therefore, the exceedingly intense occurrence of feeling is well understood in the stated manner; hence, 'it is well said.' Thus, he refutes the earlier objection.

Ārammaṇeti assāsapassāsanimittaṃ vadati. Cittānupassīyeva nāmesa hoti asammohato cittassa paṭisaṃviditattā. Tenāha “tasmā”tiādi. Cittapaṭisaṃviditavasenāti ādi-saddena itarā tissopi cittānupassanā saṅgaṇhāti.

'The object' (ārammaṇa) refers to the sign of the in-breath and out-breath. This is indeed the contemplation of mind (cittānupassī), as the mind is understood without delusion. Therefore, it is said, 'thus' (tasmā), etc. 'By means of the understanding of the mind' (cittapaṭisaṃviditavasena)—with the word 'etc.' (ādi), the other three contemplations of mind are also included.

Soti dhammānupassanaṃ anuyutto bhikkhu. Yaṃ taṃ pahānaṃ pahāyakañāṇaṃ. Paññāyāti aparāya vipassanāpaññāya aniccavirāgādito disvā duvidhāyapi ajjhupekkhitā hoti. Idañhi catukkanti aniccānupassanādivasena vuttaṃ catukkaṃ. Tassāpi dhammānupassanāya. Pajahatīti [V 2.0226] pahānanti āha “niccasaññaṃ…pe… pahānakarañāṇaṃ adhippeta”nti. Vipassanāparamparanti paṭipāṭiyā vipassanamāha. Pathapaṭipannaṃ ajjhupekkhatīti majjhimāya paṭipattiyā sammadeva vīthipaṭipannaṃ bhāvanācittaṃ paggahaniggahānaṃ akaraṇena ajjhupekkhati. Ekato upaṭṭhānanti yasmā majjhimasamathavīthinātivattiyā tattha ca pakkhandanena indriyānaṃ ekarasabhāvena, tatramajjhattatāya bhāvato avisesaṃ ekaggabhāvūpagamanena ekantato upaṭṭhāti, tasmā na tattha kiñci kātabbanti ajjhupekkhati. Tatthāti evaṃ ajjhupekkhane. Sahajātānampi ajjhupekkhanā hoti tesaṃ pavattanākārassa ajjhupekkhanato. “Paññāya disvā”ti vuttattā ārammaṇaajjhupekkhanā adhippetā. Na kevalaṃ nīvaraṇādidhamme ajjhupekkhitā, atha kho abhijjhā …pe… paññāya disvā ajjhupekkhitā hotīti yojanā.

He is the bhikkhu devoted to the contemplation of phenomena. That which is abandonment is the knowledge that abandons. By wisdom—that is, by the subsequent wisdom of insight, having seen impermanence, dispassion, and so on—it is regarded with twofold equanimity. This tetrad is the one stated in terms of the contemplation of impermanence, etc., of that same contemplation of phenomena. 'He abandons' means abandonment, as it is said: 'The perception of permanence… the knowledge that brings about abandonment is intended.' 'The sequence of insight' refers to insight in succession. 'He regards with equanimity one who has entered the path' means that, by the middle practice, he regards with equanimity the meditation mind that has rightly entered the course, without applying exertion or restraint. 'The establishing as one' means that, because of not transgressing the middle course of tranquility, and because of the engagement therein and the unified function of the faculties, and due to the state of neutrality there, it is established completely by reaching a state of one-pointedness without distinction; therefore, since nothing needs to be done there, he regards it with equanimity. 'There' refers to such regarding with equanimity. There is also equanimity towards co-arisen states, because their mode of occurrence is regarded with equanimity. Since it is said, 'having seen with wisdom,' equanimity towards the object is intended. Not only are the hindrances and other phenomena regarded with equanimity, but also craving… having been seen by wisdom, are regarded with equanimity—this is the connection.

Paṃsupuñjaṭṭhāniyassa kilesassa uppattiṭṭhānadassanatthaṃ “catumahāpatho viya cha āyatanāni”icceva vuttaṃ. Kāyādayo cattāro ārammaṇasatipaṭṭhānā. Catūsu ārammaṇesūti kāyādīsu catūsu ārammaṇesu pavattā [M 2.0525] cattāro satipaṭṭhānā paṃsupuñjaṭṭhāniyassa kilesassa upahananato.

To show the place of origin for the defilement that is like a dust heap, it is said: 'The six sense bases are like a great crossroads.' The four foundations of mindfulness, beginning with the body, are the objects. 'In the four objects' means the four foundations of mindfulness that proceed with regard to the four objects, beginning with the body, because they destroy the defilement that is like a dust heap.

Ekadhammavaggavaṇṇanā niṭṭhitā.

The commentary on the Ekadhamma Chapter is concluded.

2. Dutiyavaggo

2. The Second Chapter

1-2. Icchānaṅgalasuttādivaṇṇanā

1–2. Commentary on the Icchānaṅgala Sutta, etc.

987-988. Kasmātiādi vihārasamāpattiācikkhaṇe kāraṇaṃ vibhāvetuṃ āraddhaṃ.

987–988. 'Why?' etc., is begun in order to explain the reason for stating the attainments of abiding.

Eva-vākāroti eva-kāro vā-kāro ca. Ekantasantattā ekantena santamanasikārabhāvato. Sekkhavacaneneva tesaṃ sikkhitabbassa atthibhāve siddhepi sikkhitabbarahitesu tesupi sekkhapariyāyassa vuccamānattā sikkhitabbasaddena sekkhe visesetvā vuttā. Āsavakkhayatthaṃ sikkhitabbassa abhāvepi diṭṭhadhammasukhavihāratthaṃ jhānādisikkhanena vinā sikkhitabbābhāvā “asekkhā nāmā”ti vuttā. Buddhānaṃ pana sabbaso sammadeva pariniṭṭhitasikkhattā “sikkhāmī”ti na vuttaṃ. Ānāpānajjhānaphalasamāpatti tathāgatavihāro.

‘Eva-vākāro’ means the particle ‘eva’ and the particle ‘vā.’ ‘Ekantasantattā’ means ‘because of being completely tranquil,’ due to the state of completely tranquil attention. Although the existence of something to be trained in is established by the very word ‘trainee,’ since the designation ‘trainee’ is also applied to those who are devoid of anything to be trained in, the trainees are specified by the word ‘what is to be trained in.’ Although for them there is nothing to be trained in for the destruction of the taints, they are called ‘those beyond training’ because there is also nothing to be trained in for the sake of a pleasant abiding in this very life, apart from the training in jhāna and so on. As for the Buddhas, however, because their training is completely and rightly perfected in all respects, it is not said, ‘I am training.’ The Tathāgata’s abiding is the attainment of fruition and the jhāna of mindfulness of breathing.

3-10. Paṭhamaānandasuttādivaṇṇanā

3–10. Commentary on the First Ānanda Sutta, etc.

989-996. Aniccādivasenāti [V 2.0227] aniccadukkhānattavasena. Pavicinati pakārehi vicinati. Nikkilesāti apagatakilesā vikkhambhitakilesā. Pītisambojjhaṅgo, passaddhisambojjhaṅgoti pāṭho.

989–996. 'By way of impermanence, etc.' means by way of impermanence, suffering, and not-self. 'Examines thoroughly' means investigates in various ways. 'Free from defilements' means defilements are absent or suppressed. 'Joy as an enlightenment factor' and 'tranquility as an enlightenment factor' is the reading.

Yāya anosakkanaṃ anativattanañca hoti, ayaṃ tatramajjhattupekkhā majjhattākāroti vuttā. Ekacittakkhaṇikāti ekacittuppādapariyāpannattā.

That by which there is no shrinking back and no overstepping is called 'equanimity of neutrality therein' or 'the state of neutrality.' 'Lasting for one mind-moment' means being included within a single arising of consciousness.

Cattunnaṃ catukkānaṃ vasena soḷasakkhattukā. Ānāpānasannissayena pavattattā ārammaṇavasena pavattā ānāpānārammaṇāpi aparabhāge sati ānāpānassatīti pariyāyena vattabbataṃ arahatīti “ānāpānassati missakā kathitā”ti vuttaṃ. Ānāpānamūlakāti ānāpānasannissayena [M 2.0526] pavattā satipaṭṭhānā. Tesaṃ mūlabhūtāti tesaṃ satipaṭṭhānānaṃ mūlakāraṇabhūtā. Bojjhaṅgamūlakāti bojjhaṅgapaccayabhūtā. Tepi bojjhaṅgāti ete vīsati satipaṭṭhānahetukā bojjhaṅgā. Vijjāvimuttipūrakāti tatiyavijjāya tassa phalassa ca paripūraṇavasena pavattā bojjhaṅgā. Phalasampayuttāti catutthaphalasampayuttā, catubbidhaphalasampayuttā vā.

By way of the four tetrads, it is sixteenfold. Because it proceeds dependent on mindfulness of breathing, and because it proceeds by way of the object, even when mindfulness is established on the breath as its object in the later stages, it is fitting to be called 'mindfulness of breathing' by extension—hence it is said, 'Mindfulness of breathing is spoken of as inclusive.' 'Rooted in mindfulness of breathing' means the foundations of mindfulness proceed dependent on mindfulness of breathing. 'Being their root' means being the fundamental cause of those foundations of mindfulness. 'Rooted in enlightenment factors' means being the conditions for the enlightenment factors. 'Those enlightenment factors too' refers to these enlightenment factors that are caused by the foundations of mindfulness. 'Fulfilling knowledge and liberation' means the enlightenment factors that operate to fulfill the third knowledge and its fruit. 'Associated with the fruit' means associated with the fourth fruit or associated with the four kinds of fruits.

Dutiyavaggavaṇṇanā niṭṭhitā.

The commentary on the Second Chapter is concluded.

Ānāpānasaṃyuttavaṇṇanā niṭṭhitā.

The commentary on the Ānāpānasaṃyutta is concluded.

11. Sotāpattisaṃyuttaṃ

11. The Connected Discourses on Stream-Entry

1. Veḷudvāravaggo

1. The Veḷudvāra Chapter

1. Cakkavattirājasuttavaṇṇanā

1. Commentary on the Cakkavatti-rāja Sutta

997. Anuggahagarahaṇesu [M 2.0527][V 2.0228] nipātoti anuggaṇhanagarahatthajotako nipāto. Kimettha anuggaṇhāti, kiṃ vā garahatīti āha “catunna”ntiādi. Tattha anuggaṇhanto anucchavikaṃ katvā gaṇhanto. Garahanto nindanto. Issarasīlo issaro, tassa bhāvo issariyaṃ, pabhutā. Adhīnaṃ pati adhipati, tassa bhāvo ādhipaccaṃ, sāmibhāvoti āha – “issariyādhipacca”ntiādi. Anantakānīti antarahitāni.

997. ‘A particle in the sense of support or blame’ means a particle indicating the meaning of support or blame. What does it support or what does it blame? He says, ‘of the four,’ etc. Herein, ‘supporting’ means accepting approvingly. ‘Blaming’ means censuring. One with the nature of a ruler is a ruler; his state is lordship, supremacy. A master of dependents is an overlord; his state is overlordship, the state of being a master—hence he says, ‘lordship and overlordship,’ etc. ‘Endless things’ means things without end.

Aveccappasādenāti vatthuttayassa guṇe yāthāvato avecca pavisitvā pasādo. So pana kenaci calanarahitoti āha “acalappasādenā”ti. Maggenāti ariyamaggena. Āgatappasādo tassa adhigamena laddhappasādo. Apubbaṃ acarimanti ekajjhaṃ. Ariyasāvakānañhi ariyamaggo uppajjantova tīsu vatthūsu aveccappasādaṃ āvahanto eva uppajjati. Tesanti visayabhūtānaṃ tiṇṇaṃ vatthūnaṃ vasena tidhā vutto. Yasmā ca atthato eko, tasmāva ninnānākaraṇo hoti pavattaṭṭhānabhede satipi. Ariyasāvakassa hītiādinā nayena tamatthaṃ vivarati. Pasādo okappanā. Pemaṃ bhatti. Gāravaṃ garukaraṇaṃ. Mahantaṃ uḷāraṃ. Etaṃ vibhāgena natthi sabbattha samānattā.

‘Through assured faith’ means faith that is assured, having understood and entered into the qualities of the Triple Gem according to reality. Since that faith is not liable to be shaken by anything, he says, ‘with unshakable faith.’ ‘By the Path’ means by the Noble Path. ‘Faith that has come’ is faith obtained through its attainment. ‘Without previous or subsequent’ means all at once. For noble disciples, the Noble Path, as it arises, arises bringing with it assured faith in the three objects. ‘In them’ is said in three ways with respect to the three objects that are its sphere. And since in meaning it is one, it is therefore not of different kinds, even when there is a difference in the place of its occurrence. He explains that meaning by the method beginning with ‘For the noble disciple.’ ‘Faith’ is conviction. ‘Affection’ is devotion. ‘Reverence’ is respect. ‘Great’ is magnificent. This is not distinct, because it is uniform everywhere.

Evanti bhavantarepi akopanīyatāya. Sadisavasenāti aññehi akhaṇḍādīhi sadisavasena. Tenāha “mukhavaṭṭi yañhi chinne”tiādi. Khaṇḍā etissā atthīti khaṇḍā. Esa nayo sesesupi. Pātimokkhe āgatānukkamena sīlassa ādimajjhavibhāgo veditabbo. Dvinnaṃ vā tiṇṇaṃ vāti sīlakoṭṭhāsānaṃ. Ekantaraṃ bhinnanti abhinnena ekantaraṃ hutvā bhinnaṃ. Tesaṃ khaṇḍādīnaṃ. “Bhujissehī”ti uttarapadalopena niddesoti āha “bhujissabhāvakarehī”ti. Idaṃ [V 2.0229] vītikkantanti idampi sīlaṃ vītikkantaṃ. “Ayaṃ sīlassa vītikkamo”ti evaṃ parāmasituṃ asakkuṇeyyehi.

“Thus” means due to its unshakeable nature even in other existences. “By similarity” means by way of similarity to other unbroken qualities, and so forth. Therefore, it is said, “For when the felly is broken,” and so forth. “Broken” means it has breaks. This method applies to the rest as well. In the Pātimokkha, the division of virtue into beginning, middle, and end should be understood according to the order in which they occur. “Of two or three” refers to sections of virtue. “Broken in between” means broken with unbroken parts in between. “Of them” refers to those that are broken, and so forth. “`Bhujissehī`” is a designation made by eliding the latter term; hence it is said, “by those who bring about a state of freedom.” “This is transgressed” means this virtue, too, is transgressed. By those who are unable to reflect thus: “This is a transgression of virtue.”

2. Brahmacariyogadhasuttavaṇṇanā

2. Commentary on the Brahmacariyogadha Sutta

998. Yesanti [M 2.0528] aniyamato saddhādīnamādhārabhūtapuggaladassanaṃ. Buddhe pasādo gahito. So hi itarehi paṭhamaṃ gahetabboti. Ariyakantāni sīlāni gahitāni sotāpannassa sīlānaṃ adhippetattā. Saṅghe pasādo gahito dhammappasādassa anantaraṃ vuccamānattā. Dhamme pasādo gahito aveccappasādabhāvato. Sotaṃ ariyamaggaṃ ādito patti sotāpatti, tassā aṅgāni sotāpattiyaṅgāni. Paccentīti pajāyanti adhigacchanti. Tenāha “pāpuṇantī”ti. Brahmacariyanti maggabrahmacariyaṃ. Katarapasādo vattamānoti adhippāyo. Maggappasādoti maggasampayutto pasādo. Āgatamaggassāti adhigatamaggassa. Missakappasādo esoti tasmā ubhopi therā paṇḍitā bahussutā.

998. “`Yesaṃ`” refers to the seeing of individuals who are the foundation of faith and so on, without restriction. Confidence in the Buddha is understood, for he should be understood first by others. The virtues dear to the noble ones are understood, as they are intended to be the virtues of a stream-enterer. Confidence in the Saṅgha is understood because it is mentioned immediately after confidence in the Dhamma. Confidence in the Dhamma is understood due to its nature as unwavering confidence. The stream is the noble path; its attainment from the beginning is stream-entry; its factors are the factors of stream-entry. “`Paccenti`” means they arise, they realize. Therefore, it is said, “they attain.” “The holy life” means the holy life of the path. The meaning is: “Which confidence is being spoken of?” “Confidence in the path” means confidence connected with the path. “`Āgatamaggassa`” means of one who has realized the path. This is mixed confidence; therefore, both elders are wise and very learned.

3. Dīghāvuupāsakasuttavaṇṇanā

3. Commentary on the Dīghāvu Upāsaka Sutta

999. Yadaggena tasmiṃ upāsake yathāvuttāni sotāpattiyaṅgāni saṃvijjanti, tadaggena so tesu vattissatīti āha “catūsu sotāpattiyaṅgesu sandissasī”ti. Vijjaṃ bhajantīti vijjābhāgiyā, tesu koṭṭhāsesu pariyāpannāti vuttaṃ “vijjābhāgiyeti vijjākoṭṭhāsike”ti.

999. To the extent that the factors of stream-entry, as described, are present in that lay follower, to that extent he will abide in them—thus it is said, “You are found to possess the four factors of stream-entry.” Those who partake of wisdom are called `vijjābhāgiyā`; because they are included in those sections, it is said: “`vijjābhāgiyā` means those who have a share in wisdom.”

4-5. Paṭhamasāriputtasuttādivaṇṇanā

4-5. Commentary on the First Sāriputta Sutta, etc.

1000-1001. “Sotāpattī”ti paṭhamamaggo adhippeto, tassa adhigamūpāyo sotāpattiyaṅgaṃ. Tenāha “sotāpattiyā pubbabhāgapaṭilābhaṅga”nti. Sotāpattiatthāyāti sotāpattimaggatthāya. Aṅganti kāraṇaṃ. Itare ratanattayappasādādayo. Pubbabhāgiyāya sotāpattiyā aṅgaṃ kāraṇanti.

1000-1001. “Stream-entry” (`sotāpatti`) refers to the first path; the means of its attainment is the factor of stream-entry. Hence it is said, “the factor of preliminary attainment to stream-entry.” “For the sake of stream-entry” (`sotāpattiatthāya`) means for the purpose of the path of stream-entry. “Factor” (`aṅga`) means cause. The others are confidence in the Triple Gem, and so on. The factor for the preliminary stage of stream-entry is a cause.

6. Thapatisuttavaṇṇanā

6. Commentary on the Thapati Sutta

1002. (Vitānaṃ kāyānaṃ kālanakāya, kāraṇassa upagatānaṃ sātapariyā sijjhati. Tattha [V 2.0230] kāyaparipajjhāyamukhena tantiṃ ṭhapesi bhagavā. Na hi passatha namatthehi karaṇe niratthako piti vattati. “Niyatattā”ti vuttaṃ. Tameva niyamaṃ “majjhimapadeseyevā”ti avadhāraṇena vibhāveti[M 2.0529]. Mahāmaṇḍalacārikaṃ carantopi majjhimapadesassa antanteneva carati. Tattha ca vineyyajanassa samosaraṇatā mattapanavācamahatthasupananti.) [Etthantare pāṭho asuddho dussodhanīyo ca, suddhapāṭho gavesitabbo.] Aruṇuṭṭhāpanampi tattheva hoti. Paccantapadese pana dūre vineyyajanā honti, tattha gantvā maggaphalesu patiṭṭhāpetvā tato paccāgantvā majjhimapadese eva vāsaṃ upagacchati, tattha manussehi katānaṃ kārānaṃ mahapphalabhāvaniyamanatthanti daṭṭhabbaṃ. “Āsanne no bhagavā”ti idaṃ nidassanamattanti dassento “na kevala”nti ādimāha.

1002. (A canopy is for bodies, a covering for those with decaying bodies; for those who have approached the cause, it is accomplished by constant wear. There, the Blessed One established the teaching by means of the contemplation of the body. Do you not see that there is meaningless joy in doing things without purpose? “Because of certainty,” it is said. That very certainty is clarified by the specification, “only in the middle region.” Even when wandering on a great circuit, one wanders only within the boundaries of the middle region. And there, the assembly of those to be trained is one of moderate speech and great meaning.) [In this section the text is corrupt and difficult to correct; the correct reading should be sought.] The rising of the dawn also occurs there. In the border regions, however, those to be trained are far away. Having gone there, established them in the fruits of the path, and returned, one resorts again to dwelling in the middle region. There, it should be understood that this is for the purpose of determining the great fruitfulness of actions done by people. To show that “The Blessed One is near us” is merely an example, he begins with “not only” and so on.

Sakiñcanasapalibodhanaṭṭhenāti ettha kiñcanaṃ palibodho asamāpitakiccatā, tadubhayassa atthibhāvenāti attho. Mahāvāseti mahāgehe.

“Because of having impediments and obstructions” (`sakiñcanasapalibodhanaṭṭhena`): here, an impediment (`kiñcana`) is an obstruction (`palibodha`), an unfinished task (`asamāpitakiccatā`). The meaning is: because of the existence of both. “In a great dwelling” (`mahāvāse`) means in a large house.

Dvepi janāti isidattapurāṇā. Sitaṃ nāma mandahasitaṃ. Hasitaṃ nāma vissaṭṭhahasitaṃ. Muttacāgotiādīsu yaṃ vattabbaṃ, taṃ visuddhimaggaṭīkāyaṃ (visuddhi. mahāṭī. 1.160) vuttanayena veditabbaṃ. Saṃvibhāgeti parassa dānavasena saṃvibhajane. Akatavibhāganti deyyadhammavasena na katavibhāgaṃ. Puggalavasena pana “sīlavantehī”ti vuttattā katavibhāgameva mahapphalatākaraṇena. Tenāha “sabbaṃ dātabbameva hutvā ṭhita”nti.

“The two people” (`dvepi janā`) refers to Isidatta and Purāṇa. “A smile” (`sita`) means a gentle smile. “Laughter” (`hasita`) means unrestrained laughter. Regarding “liberated giving” (`muttacāga`) and so on, what should be said is to be understood according to the method explained in the Great Subcommentary on the Visuddhimagga (Visuddhi. Mahāṭī. 1.160). “In sharing” (`saṃvibhāge`) means in distributing by way of giving to others. “Not having shared” (`akatavibhāga`) means not having made a distribution in the manner of a gift. But regarding individuals, since it is said, “by those possessing virtue,” sharing is indeed done, due to its making great fruitfulness. Hence it is said, “everything should stand having become suitable for giving.”

(Etthantare pāṭho asuddho dussodhanīyo ca, suddhapāṭho gavesitabbo.)

[In this section the text is corrupt and difficult to correct; the correct reading should be sought.]

7. Veḷudvāreyyasuttavaṇṇanā

7. Commentary on the Veḷudvāreyya Sutta

1003. Paveṇiāgatassāti anekapurisayugavasena paramparāgatassa. Attani upanetabbanti attani netvā parasmiṃ upanetabbaṃ. Tenāha “yo kho myāyaṃ dhammo appiyo amanāpo, parassapeso dhammo appiyo amanāpo”tiādi. Amantabhāsenāti samphassa samphappalāpassa amantāya mantārahitabhāsanena.

1003. “Of that which has come down through tradition” (`paveṇiāgatassa`) means of that which has come down in succession through many generations of people. “To be applied to oneself” (`attani upanetabbaṃ`) means having applied it to oneself, it should be applied to another. Therefore, it is said, “Whatever principle is displeasing and disagreeable to me, that principle is also displeasing and disagreeable to another,” and so on. “By speech without counsel” (`amantabhāsena`) means speech without counsel, speech devoid of wise consideration, consisting of frivolous talk and idle chatter.

8-9. Paṭhamagiñjakāvasathasuttādivaṇṇanā

8-9. Commentary on the First Giñjakāvasatha Sutta, etc.

1004-5. Dve gāmā dvinnaṃ ñātīnaṃ gāmāti katvā. Ñātiketi evaṃladdhanāme ekasmiṃ [V 2.0231] gāmake. Giñjakāvasatheti giñjakā vuccanti iṭṭhakā, giñjakāhi eva kato āvasatho, tasmiṃ. So kira āvāso yathā [M 2.0530] sudhāhi parikammena payojanaṃ natthi, evaṃ iṭṭhakāhi eva cinitvā kato. Tena vuttaṃ “iṭṭhakāmaye āvasathe”ti. Tulāthambhadvārabandhakavāṭaphalakāni pana dārumayā eva. Oraṃ vuccati kāmadhātu, paccayabhāvena taṃ oraṃ bhajantīti orambhāgiyāni, orambhāgassa vā hitāni orambhāgiyāni. Tenāha “heṭṭhābhāgiyāna”ntiādi. Tīhi maggehīti heṭṭhimehi tīhi maggehi. Tehi pahātabbatāya hi tesaṃ saññojanānaṃ orambhāgiyatā, oraṃ bhañjiyāni vā orambhāgiyāni vuttāni niruttinayena. Idāni byatirekamukhena nesaṃ orambhāgiyabhāvaṃ vibhāvetuṃ “tatthā”tiādi vuttaṃ. Vikkhambhitāni samatthatāvighātena puthujjanānaṃ, samucchinnāni sabbaso abhāvena ariyānaṃ rūpārūpabhavūpapattiyā vibandhanāya na hontīti vuttaṃ “avikkhambhitāni maggena vā asamucchinnānī”ti. Nibbattivasenāti paṭisandhiggahaṇavasena gantuṃ na denti. Mahaggatabhavagāmikammāyūhanassa vibandhanato sakkāyadiṭṭhiādīni tīṇi saññojanāni kāmacchandabyāpādā viya mahaggatabhavūpapattiyā visesapaccayattā tattha mahaggatabhave nibbattampi tannibbattihetukammaparikkhaye kāmabhavūpapattipaccayatāya mahaggatabhavato ānetvā idheva kāmabhave eva nibbattāpenti. Tasmā sabbānipi pañcapi saṃyojanāni orambhāgiyāneva. Paṭisandhivasena anāgamanasabhāvoti paṭisandhiggahaṇavasena tasmā lokā idha na āgamanasabhāvo. Buddhadassana-theradassana-dhammassavanānaṃ pana atthāya assa āgamanaṃ anivāritaṃ.

1004-5. Two villages, because they were the villages of two sets of relatives. In a certain small village that had thus obtained the name 'Ñātika'. 'Giñjakāvasatha': 'giñjakā' are called bricks; the rest-house was made of those very bricks, in that place. That dwelling, it is said, was constructed by simply piling up bricks, such that there was no need for finishing work with stucco. Hence it was called 'a brick-made rest-house'. The beams, pillars, door-frames, and door-panels, however, were made of wood. 'Oraṃ' (the lower) is called the sense-sphere. Because they partake of that 'oraṃ' as their condition, they are called 'orambhāgiyāni' (belonging to the lower portion); or, 'orambhāgiyāni' are what is beneficial to the lower portion. Therefore, he said, 'belonging to the lower portion,' etc. 'By the three paths' means by the lower three paths. Indeed, the 'lower portion' nature of these fetters is due to their being abandoned by those paths; or, according to the etymological method, they are called 'orambhāgiyāni' because they are to be broken ('bhañjiyāni') in the lower portion ('oraṃ'). Now, to clarify their nature as 'belonging to the lower portion' by way of contrast, it is said, 'therein,' etc. For ordinary people, they are suppressed by the obstruction of their capability; for noble ones, they are eradicated by complete absence. It is said, 'not suppressed by the path or not eradicated,' because they are not an obstruction to rebirth in the form and formless existences. 'By way of rebirth' means that by way of grasping a rebirth-linking, they do not allow one to go. The three fetters—identity view, etc.—and sensual desire and ill-will obstruct the accumulation of kamma leading to an exalted existence. And although they are particular conditions regarding rebirth in the exalted realms, even for one who is reborn there in an exalted existence, when the kamma that caused that rebirth is exhausted, they bring one back from the exalted existence and cause rebirth right here in the sense-sphere, due to being a condition for rebirth in the sense-sphere existence. Therefore, all five fetters are indeed 'belonging to the lower portion'. 'Having the nature of not returning by way of rebirth' means that by way of grasping a rebirth-linking, there is no nature of returning from that world to this one. However, his coming for the purpose of seeing the Buddha, seeing the elders, or hearing the Dhamma is not prevented.

Kadāci uppattiyā viraḷākāratā, pariyuṭṭhānamandatāya abahalatāti dvedhāpi tanubhāvo. Abhiṇhanti bahuso. Bahalabahalāti tibbatibbā. Yattha uppajjanti, taṃ santānaṃ maddantā pharantā sādhentā andhakāraṃ karontā uppajjanti, dvīhi pana maggehi pahīnattā tanukatanukā mandamandā uppajjanti. Puttadhītaro hontīti idaṃ akāraṇaṃ. Tathā hi aṅgapaccaṅgaparāmasanamattenapi te honti. Idanti “rāgadosamohānaṃ tanuttā”ti idaṃ vacanaṃ. Bhavatanukavasenāti appakabhavavasena. Tanti mahāsīvattherassa vacanaṃ paṭikkhittanti sambandho. Ye bhavā ariyānaṃ labbhanti, te paripuṇṇalakkhaṇabhavā eva. Ye na labbhanti, tattha kīdisaṃ taṃ bhavatanukaṃ. Tasmā ubhayathāpi bhavatanukassa asambhavo evāti dassetuṃ “sotāpannassā”tiādi vuttaṃ. Aṭṭhame bhave bhavatanukaṃ natthi aṭṭhamasseva bhavassa sabbasseva abhāvato. Sesesupi eseva nayo.

Sometimes, their arising is rare, and due to the sluggishness of obsession, they are not abundant—thus, their attenuation is twofold. 'Abhiṇhaṃ' means frequently. 'Bahala-bahala' means very intense. Where they arise, they arise crushing, pervading, mastering, and darkening that mental continuum. However, having been abandoned by the two paths, they arise very attenuated and very sluggishly. 'They become sons or daughters'—this is not the reason. For they come into existence even by the mere touching of limbs and minor limbs. 'This' refers to the statement: 'the attenuation of lust, hatred, and delusion'. 'By way of attenuation of existence' means by way of few existences. By that, the statement of the Elder Mahāsīva is rejected—this is the connection. The existences that are obtained by the noble ones are only existences with fully perfected characteristics. For those that are not obtained, what kind of attenuated existence could there be? Therefore, to show that an attenuated existence is impossible in either case, it is said: 'For a stream-enterer,' etc. In an eighth existence, there is no attenuated existence, because the eighth existence is completely absent in its entirety. The same method applies to the rest.

Kāmāvacaralokaṃ [M 2.0531] sandhāya vuttaṃ itarassa lokassa vasena tathā vattuṃ asakkuṇeyyattā. Yo hi [V 2.0232] sakadāgāmī devamanussalokesu vomissakavasena nibbattati, sopi kāmabhavavaseneva paricchinditabbo. Bhagavatā ca kāmaloke ṭhatvā – “sakideva imaṃ lokaṃ āgantvā”ti vuttaṃ. Imaṃ lokaṃ āgantvāti ca iminā pañcasu sakadāgāmīsu cattāro vajjetvā ekova gahito. Ekacco hi idha sakadāgāmiphalaṃ patvā idheva parinibbāyati, ekacco idha patvā devaloke parinibbāyati, ekacco devaloke patvā tattheva parinibbāyati, ekacco devaloke patvā idhūpapajjitvā parinibbāyati, ime cattāro idha na labbhanti. Yo pana idha patvā devaloke yāvatāyukaṃ vasitvā puna idhūpapajjitvā parinibbāyati, ayamidha adhippeto. Aṭṭhakathāyaṃ pana “imaṃ lokanti kāmabhavo adhippeto”ti imamatthaṃ vibhāvetuṃ “sace hī”tiādinā aññaṃyeva catukkaṃ dassitaṃ.

This was said with reference to the sense-sphere world, because it is impossible to speak in such a way with respect to any other world. For a once-returner who is reborn in a mixed fashion in the worlds of devas and humans should also be defined solely by way of the sense-sphere existence. And the Blessed One, while abiding in the sense-sphere world, said: 'Having come to this world only once...'. And by this, 'having come to this world,' only one of the five kinds of once-returners is meant, excluding the other four. For one person, having attained the fruit of once-returning here, attains final Nibbāna right here. Another, having attained it here, attains final Nibbāna in the deva world. Another, having attained it in the deva world, attains final Nibbāna right there. Another, having attained it in the deva world, is reborn here and then attains final Nibbāna. These four are not included here. But the one who, having attained the fruit here, dwells in the deva world for their lifespan and then, having been reborn here again, attains final Nibbāna—this one is intended here. In the commentary, however, to clarify this meaning that 'this world' refers to the sense-sphere existence, another group of four is shown, beginning with 'if indeed,' etc.

Catūsu…pe… sabhāvoti attho apāyagamanīyānaṃ pāpadhammānaṃ sabbaso pahīnattā. Dhammaniyāmenāti maggadhammaniyāmena niyato uparimaggādhigamassa avassaṃbhāvibhāvato. Tenāha “sambodhiparāyaṇo”ti. Tesaṃ tesaṃ ñāṇagatinti tesaṃ tesaṃ sattānaṃ “asuko sotāpanno, asuko sakadāgāmī”tiādinā taṃtaṃñāṇādhigamanaṃ ñāṇūpapattiṃ. Ñāṇābhisamparāyanti tato parampi – “niyato sambodhiparāyaṇo sakideva imaṃ lokaṃ āgantvā dukkhassantaṃ karissatī”tiādinā ñāṇasahitaṃ upapattipaccayabhavaṃ. Olokentassa ñāṇacakkhunā apekkhantassa. Kevalaṃ kāyakilamathova, na tena kāci paresaṃ atthasiddhīti adhippāyo. Cittavihesā cittakhedo, sā kilesūpasaṃhitattā buddhānaṃ natthi.

In the four... etc. ... the meaning is 'by nature,' because the evil qualities that lead to the planes of misery have been completely abandoned. 'By the law of the Dhamma' means being fixed by the law of the path-Dhamma, due to the inevitability of attaining the higher paths. Hence he said, 'one destined for enlightenment'. 'The course of their knowledge' means the attainment of specific knowledge regarding those beings, the arising of knowledge, such as 'this one is a stream-enterer, that one is a once-returner.' 'The future state with knowledge' means even beyond that—the existence conditioned by rebirth accompanied by knowledge, as in 'being fixed and destined for enlightenment, having come to this world only once, he will make an end of suffering.' 'Of one looking' means of one considering with the eye of knowledge. The intention is that it is merely bodily fatigue, and no benefit for others is accomplished by it. 'Mental distress' is mental affliction; since it is connected with the defilements, it does not exist for the Buddhas.

Ādissati ālokīyati attā etenāti ādāsaṃ, dhammabhūtaṃ ādāsaṃ dhammādāsaṃ, ariyamaggañāṇassetaṃ adhivacanaṃ. Tena hi ariyasāvako catūsu ariyasaccesu viddhastasammohattā attānaṃ yāthāvato ñatvā yāthāvato byākareyya, tappakāsanato pana dhammapariyāyassa suttassa dhammādāsatā veditabbāti. Yena dhammādāsenāti idha pana maggadhammameva vadati. Sesaṃ uttānatthattā suviññeyyamevāti.

The self is seen and illuminated by it, thus it is a mirror (ādāsa). A mirror that has become Dhamma is a Dhamma-mirror; this is a designation for the knowledge of the Noble Path. By it, a noble disciple, having dispelled confusion regarding the Four Noble Truths, would know themself as they truly are and declare it as such. However, the sutta, the discourse on the Dhamma, should be understood as the Dhamma-mirror because it makes this known. 'By which Dhamma-mirror'—here, however, it refers specifically to the path-Dhamma. The rest, having an obvious meaning, is very easy to understand.

Veḷudvāravaggavaṇṇanā niṭṭhitā.

The commentary on the Veḷudvāra Chapter is concluded.

2. Rājakārāmavaggo

2. The Rājakārāma Chapter

1. Sahassabhikkhunisaṅghasuttavaṇṇanā

1. Commentary on the Sahassabhikkhunisaṅgha Sutta

1007. Dhammikattā [V 2.0233][M 2.0532] desavāsīhi anukampito rājāti rājako, tassa ārāmo rājakārāmo, tasmiṃ. Bhūmisīsaṃ seṭṭhappadeso, yattha vasanto lābhaggayasaggappatto hotīti tesaṃ adhippāyo.

1007. 'Rājako' means 'the king, being righteous, was regarded with compassion by the local residents.' His monastery is the Rājakārāma, in that place. 'Bhūmisīsaṃ' means 'the head of the land,' the most excellent region, where one residing attains the foremost gains and repute—this was their intention.

Vārāpehīti paṭisedhehi. Yujjhāpetunti kalahaṃ kārāpetuṃ. Puna āgacchantāti aparasmiṃ saṃvacchare āgacchantā. Ubbaṭṭetvāti mahatī vīciyo ubbaṭṭetvā. Yathā tassa sakalameva raṭṭhaṃ ekodakībhūtaṃ hoti, evaṃ katvā samuddameva jātaṃ. Tena vuttaṃ “samuddameva akaṃsū”ti.

'Prevent them' means prohibit them. 'Make them fight' means to cause a quarrel. 'Coming again' means coming in another year. 'Having surged up' means having raised up great waves. Having done so in such a way that the entire country became a single mass of water, it became just like the ocean. Hence it is said, 'they made it into an ocean.'

Isīnamantaraṃ katvāti isīnaṃ kāraṇaṃ katvā, isīnaṃ hetūti attho, isīnaṃ vā antarabhedaṃ katvā. Tathā loke kolāhalassa patthaṭataṃ vibhāvento bhagavā “me suta”nti āha paccakkhato jānampi. Ucchinnoti kulacchedena ucchinno. Na kevalaṃ sayameva, atha kho saha raṭṭhehi. Vibhavanti vināsaṃ upagato.

'Having made an interval between the seers' means having acted on account of the seers, due to the seers—this is the meaning, or 'having made a division among the seers.' Thus, the Blessed One, revealing the spread of uproar in the world, said, 'as I have heard,' even though knowing directly. 'Uprooted' means uprooted by the destruction of the lineage. Not only himself, but together with the kingdoms. 'Perished' means having undergone destruction.

Taṃ sandhāyetaṃ vuttanti taṃ yathāvuttaṃ pasenadinā kosalarājena kāritaṃ vihāraṃ sandhāya etaṃ “rājakārāme”ti vuttaṃ.

"That was said with reference to that"—this was said with reference to the monastery built by King Pasenadi of Kosala, as previously stated; hence it is called "Rājakārāma."

2-3. Brāhmaṇasuttādivaṇṇanā

2-3. Commentary on the Brāhmaṇa Sutta, and so on.

1008-9. Udayagāmininti ārambhato paṭṭhāya sampattiāvahaṃ.

1008-9. "Udayagāminī" means bringing about accomplishment from the very beginning.

4. Duggatibhayasuttavaṇṇanā

4. Commentary on the Duggatibhaya Sutta

1010. Duṭṭhā gati nipphatti duggati, duggatabhāvo dāliddiyaṃ, tadeva bhayaṃ, taṃ sabbaṃ anavasesaṃ vā duggatibhayaṃ daliddabhayaṃ. Sammadeva atikkantoti samatikkanto.

1010. A woeful state is the outcome of a corrupt destination; the state of being in a woeful state is poverty, and that itself is fear. Or, all that without remainder is the fear of a woeful state, the fear of poverty. "Rightly transcended" means fully overcome.

6. Paṭhamamittāmaccasuttavaṇṇanā

6. Commentary on the First Mittāmacca Sutta

1012. Vohāramittāti [V 2.0234] taṃtaṃdānaggahaṇavasena vohārakā mittā. Āmantanapaṭimantanairiyāpathādīsupīti ālāpasallāpagamananisajjādiatthasaṃvidhānādīsu[M 2.0533]. Ekato pavattakiccāti saha pavattakattabbā. Amā saha bhavantīti amaccā. “Amhākaṃ ime”ti ñāyantīti ñātī, āvāhavivāhasambaddhā. Tenāha “sassusasurapakkhikā”ti. Yonisambandhā vā sālohitā. Tenāha “bhātibhaginimātulādayo”ti.

1012. "Friends by interaction" refers to friends who interact by way of giving and receiving various things. "In greetings, invitations, deportment, and so on" means in arranging for things such as conversation, discussion, going, sitting, and so on. "Duties to be performed together" means things to be done together. "Amaccā" are so called because they are together with one. "Ñātī" are so called because they are known as "these are ours," being connected through marriage. Hence, it is said, "the party of the mother-in-law and father-in-law." Or, they are blood relations, that is, "sālohitā." Therefore, it is said, "brothers, sisters, maternal uncles, and the like."

7. Dutiyamittāmaccasuttavaṇṇanā

7. Commentary on the Second Mittāmacca Sutta

1013. Kismiñci kassaci ca tathā tathā uppannassa pasādassa aññathābhāvo pasādaññathattaṃ. Bhūtasaṅghātassa ghanaādikassa aññathābhāvo bhāvaññathattaṃ. Nirayādigatiantaraupapatti gatiaññathattaṃ. Sabhāvadhammānaṃ kakkhaḷaphusanādilakkhaṇassa aññathābhāvo lakkhaṇaññathattaṃ. “Ṭhitassa aññathattaṃ paññāyatī”ti evaṃ vuttaṃ aññathattaṃ vipariṇāmaññathattaṃ. Lakkhaṇaññathattaṃ na labbhati. Tenāha “lakkhaṇaṃ pana na vigacchatī”ti, sesaṃ labbhatīti. Pathavīdhātuyāti sasambhārapathavīdhātuyā. Āpodhātuyāti etthāpi eseva nayo. Purimabhāvoti ghanakaṭhinabhāvo. Bhāvaññathattaṃ rasaññathattasabhāvo. Gatiaññathattaṃ uggatūpapatti. Tenāha “tañhi ariyasāvakassa natthī”ti. Pasādaññathattampi natthiyeva ariyasāvakassa.

1013. "Change in faith" refers to the alteration of faith that arises in someone regarding something. "Change in state" refers to the alteration of solidity, and so on, in a mass of elements. "Change in destiny" refers to rebirth in another destination, such as hell. "Change in characteristic" refers to the alteration of characteristics like hardness, contact, and so on, of intrinsic phenomena. The "otherness" spoken of thus, "otherness is discerned in what is stable," is the otherness of transformation. "Change in characteristic" is not found. Hence, it is said, "but the characteristic does not disappear," while the rest is found. "Of the earth element" refers to the earth element with its constituents. The same method applies to the "water element." "Former state" refers to the state of solidity and hardness. "Change in state" has the nature of change in taste. "Change in destiny" refers to rebirth in a high or low state. Therefore, it is said, "for that does not exist for the noble disciple." "Change in faith" also does not exist for the noble disciple.

Rājakārāmavaggavaṇṇanā niṭṭhitā.

The Commentary on the Rājakārāma Chapter is concluded.

3. Saraṇānivaggo

3. The Saraṇāni Chapter

1-2. Paṭhamamahānāmasuttādivaṇṇanā

1-2. Commentary on the First Mahānāma Sutta, and so on.

1017-18. Samiddhanti sampuṇṇaṃ. Supupphitanti upasobhitatāya supupphitasadisattā. Byūhā nāma yehi eva pavisanti, tehi eva nikkhamanti. Tenāha “byūhā vuccanti avinibbiddharacchāyo”ti. Uddhatacārināti yaṭṭhantarā.

1017-18. "Flourishing" means complete. "Well-flowered" means, because of being adorned, it is like a well-flowered state. "Byūhā" are those through which one enters, and through which one exits. Therefore it is said, "'Byūhā' are called avenues with unbroken shade." "Uddhatacārin" means an interval between staves.

3. Godhasakkasuttavaṇṇanā

3. Commentary on the Godhasakka Sutta

1019. Tīhīti [V 2.0235] ratanattaye uppannehi tīhi pasādadhammehi. Catūhīti tehi eva saddhiṃ sīlena. Kocidevātiādi parikappavasena vuttaṃ bhagavati [M 2.0534] attano saddhāya uḷāratamabhāvadassanatthaṃ. Tenāha “bhagavato sabbaññutāyā”tiādi. Dhammo samuppādoti vivādadhammauppattihetu. Tadeva hi sandhāyāha “kiñcideva kāraṇa”nti. Kāraṇanti nānākāraṇaṃ. Kalyāṇakusalavimuttanti akalyāṇaṃ akusalaṃ, tadidaṃ yathāvuttaappasādanena apanītaatthadassanatthaṃ. Assāti mahānāmasakkassa. Anavajjanadoso esoti catūsu dhammesu ekenapi samannāgato sotāpanno hotīti anujānitvā catūhipi samannāgatena ācikkhitabbanti yāthāvato anavajjanadosoti attho.

1019. "By three" refers to the three qualities of faith that have arisen concerning the Triple Gem. "By four" means those very qualities together with virtue. "Even someone..." and so on, is said by way of conjecture to show the supreme state of one's own faith in the Blessed One. Therefore, it is said: "Because of the Blessed One's omniscience," and so on. "The Dhamma, origination" refers to the cause for the origination of matters of dispute. Precisely with that in mind, it is said: "some cause or other." "Cause" means various causes. "Wholesome, skillful, and liberated" means not unwholesome, not unskillful; this is for the purpose of showing the meaning of what is removed by the aforementioned lack of faith. "Of him" refers to Mahānāma the Sakyan. "This is a faultless, blameless matter" means this: having permitted that one endowed with even one of these four qualities is a stream-enterer, the declaration should be made by one endowed with all four; in this way, the meaning is 'truly faultless and blameless.'

4. Paṭhamasaraṇānisakkasuttavaṇṇanā

4. Commentary on the First Sutta about Saraṇāni the Sakyan

1020. Pamāṇenāti ekena pamāṇena, na sabbaso. Olokanaṃ khamantīti dassanamaggena catusaccadhammā paccattaṃ passitabbā paṭivijjhitabbā. Paṭhamamaggakkhaṇe hi catusaccadhammā ekadesatova diṭṭhā nāma honti. “Parimuccatī”ti pana vattuṃ vaṭṭati paṭivijjhanakiriyāya vattamānattā. Agantvā apāyesu anuppattirahattā. Tenāha “na gacchatī”ti, na uppajjatīti attho. Mahāsārarukkhe dassento āha – “yo koci viññujātiko mama ce gocaraṃ gacchati, ekassa āgamanaṃ avañjhaṃ amogha”nti dassetuṃ.

1020. "By measure" means by one measure, not entirely. "They endure the vision" means the Four Noble Truths should be personally seen and penetrated through the path of vision. For at the moment of the first path, the Four Noble Truths are only partially seen. However, it is appropriate to say "is liberated" because the act of penetration is occurring, and because of not going to the woeful states, due to the absence of arising there. Therefore, it is said: "does not go," meaning "does not arise." Illustrating with the great core-wood tree, he said: "Whatever intelligent person comes within my range, their coming is not in vain, not fruitless"—this is to show.

5. Dutiyasaraṇānisakkasuttavaṇṇanā

5. Commentary on the Second Sutta about Saraṇāni the Sakyan

1021. Dukkhettaṃ nirojakaṃ, tāya eva dukkhettatāya visamaṃ hotīti āha “visamakhetta”nti. Loṇūpahatanti jātasabhāvena loṇena ūsarena upahataṃ. Khaṇḍānīti khaṇḍitāni. Temetvāti temitattā. Vātātapahatānīti cirakālaṃ vātena ceva ātapena ca upahatāni ābādhitāni.

1021. "A barren field" means one that is unproductive. Because of that very barrenness, it is uneven—hence it is called "an uneven field." "Afflicted by salt" means damaged by naturally occurring salt, by saline soil. "Broken" means fragmented. "Having moistened" means because they are moistened. "Afflicted by wind and heat" means long damaged by wind and heat, thus injured.

6. Paṭhamaanāthapiṇḍikasuttavaṇṇanā

6. Commentary on the First Anāthapiṇḍika Sutta

1022. Ṭhānanti ṭhānaso. Tenāha “khaṇenā”ti. Niyyānikanti pavattaṃ ñāṇapaṭirūpakaṃ [V 2.0236] pakatipurisantarajānanādimicchāñāṇaṃ. Taṃ pana aniyyānikaṃ “niyyānika”nti paccavekkhaṇavasena pavatteyyāti āha “micchāpaccavekkhaṇenā”ti. Guṇaviyuttassa attano sakattani avaṭṭhānasaṅkhātā vimutti micchāvimutti.

1022. "Place" means in the proper case. Hence it is said: "by a moment." "Leading out" refers to the counterfeit of knowledge, the false knowledge of knowing the minds of other ordinary people, and so on. But that which does not lead out might be regarded as "leading out" by way of reflection—hence it is said: "by wrong reflection." The liberation of one devoid of qualities, which is defined as the establishment in one's own self, is wrong liberation.

7. Dutiyaanāthapiṇḍikasuttavaṇṇanā

7. Commentary on the Second Anāthapiṇḍika Sutta

1023. Yathākammaṃ [M 2.0535] samparetabbato samparāyo, peccabhavo, samparāyahetukaṃ samparāyikaṃ, maraṇabhayaṃ.

1023. "Samparāya" (the next life) is so called because one proceeds according to one's deeds; it is the afterlife. "Samparāyika" (pertaining to the next life) means having the next life as its cause, such as the fear of death.

Saraṇānivaggavaṇṇanā niṭṭhitā.

The Commentary on the Saraṇāni Chapter is concluded.

4. Puññābhisandavaggo

4. The Puññābhisanda Chapter

1. Paṭhamapuññābhisandasuttavaṇṇanā

1. Commentary on the First Puññābhisanda Sutta

1027. Avicchedena niccappavattiyamānāni puññāni abhisandanaṭṭhena “puññābhisandā”ti vuttā, tena puññanadiyoti attho vutto. Sukhassa āharaṇato ānayanato sukhassāhāro.

1027. Merits that flow continuously without interruption are called "outpourings of merit" in the sense of flowing onward. Hence, the meaning "a river of merit" is stated. As they bring and lead to happiness, they are a bringer of happiness.

4. Paṭhamadevapadasuttavaṇṇanā

4. Commentary on the First Devapada Sutta

1030. Devānanti visuddhidevānaṃ. Devapadānīti tesaṃ padāni devapadāni, devoti vā sammāsambuddho. Devassa ñāṇena akkantapadānīti paṭivedhañāṇena ceva desanāñāṇena ca akkantapadāni. Devā nāma jātidevā. Tesampi devaṭṭhena devoti devadevo, sambuddho.

1030. "Of devas" means of purified devas. "Divine abodes" are their abodes; or "Deva" refers to the Perfectly Enlightened One. "Abodes trodden by the knowledge of the Deva" means abodes trodden by the knowledge of penetration and the knowledge of teaching. "Devas" are deities by birth. With respect to them also, he is a deva in the sense of being a deva, the Deva of Devas, the Enlightened One.

8. Vassasuttavaṇṇanā

8. Commentary on the Vassa Sutta

1034. Pāraṃ vuccati nibbānaṃ saṃsāramahoghassa paratīrabhāvato. Tenāha – “tiṇṇo pāraṅgato, thale tiṭṭhati brāhmaṇo (saṃ. ni. 4.238; itivu. 69; pu. pa. 188), ye [V 2.0237] janā pāragāmino”ti (dha. pa. 85) ca. Atha vā pāti rakkhatīti pāraṃ, nibbānaṃ. Yo paṭivijjhati, taṃ vaṭṭadukkhato pāti rakkhati, accantahitena ca vimuttisukhena ca rameti, tasmā pāranti vuccati. Gacchamānā evāti pāraṃ nibbānaṃ gacchamānā eva. Te dhammā āsavānaṃ khayāya saṃvattanti sacchikiriyāpahānapaṭivedhānaṃ samakālattā.

1034. `The far shore` (pāraṃ) is said to be Nibbāna, as it is the further shore beyond the great flood of saṃsāra. Hence it is said: `The brahmin has crossed over, gone to the far shore, stands on dry ground` (Saṃyutta Nikāya 4.238; Itivuttaka 69; Puggalapaññatti 188), and `those people who go to the far shore` (Dhammapada 85). Alternatively, `pāraṃ` is that which protects (pāti rakkhati)—Nibbāna. It protects one who penetrates it from the suffering of the round, and delights them with supreme welfare and the bliss of liberation; therefore, it is called `the far shore`. `Just going` (gacchamānā eva) means just going to the far shore, Nibbāna. Those teachings conduce to the destruction of the taints because of the simultaneity of realization, abandonment, and penetration.

10. Nandiyasakkasuttavaṇṇanā

10. Commentary on the Nandiya-Sakka Discourse

1036. Pavivekatthāyāti [M 2.0536] pavivekasukhatthāya. Paṭisallānatthāyāti bahiddhā nānārammaṇato cittaṃ paṭinivattetvā kammaṭṭhāne sammadeva līnatthāya.

1036. `For the sake of seclusion` (pavivekatthāya) means for the sake of the happiness of seclusion. `For the sake of retreat` (paṭisallānatthāya) means for the sake of withdrawing the mind from various external objects and properly immersing it in the meditation subject.

Puññābhisandavaggavaṇṇanā niṭṭhitā.

The Commentary on the Chapter on the Outpouring of Merit is concluded.

5. Sagāthakapuññābhisandavaggo

5. The Chapter on the Outpouring of Merit with Verses

1. Paṭhamaabhisandasuttavaṇṇanā

1. Commentary on the First Discourse on the Outpouring

1037. Saṅkhyā atthi heṭṭhā mahāpathaviyā, upari ākāsena, parito cakkavāḷapabbatena, majjhe tattha tattha ṭhitehi dīpapabbatapariyantehi paricchinnattā. Jānantena yojanato saṅkhātuṃ sakkāti adhippāyo. Mahāsarīramaccha-kumbhīla-yakkha-rakkhasa-mahānāgadānavādīnaṃ saviññāṇakānaṃ, baḷavāmukhapātālādīnaṃ aviññāṇakānaṃ bheravārammaṇānaṃ vasena bahubheravaṃ.

1037. There is a reckoning below by the great earth, above by the sky, and around by the Cakkavāḷa mountain range, and in between, it is bounded by island-mountains situated here and there. The meaning is that one who knows can reckon it by calculation. It is very terrible because of terrifying objects such as great-bodied fish, crocodiles, yakkhas, rakshasas, great nāgas, and dānavas, all of whom are conscious, as well as unconscious terrifying objects like Baḷavāmukha and the netherworld (pātāla).

2. Dutiyaabhisandasuttavaṇṇanā

2. Commentary on the Second Discourse on the Outpouring

1038. Sambhedeti sambhedaṃ samodhānaṃ gataṭṭhāne. Yatthimā mahānadiyo saṃsandanti samentīti parikappavacanametaṃ. Tādisāsu hi mahānadīsu kāci puratthimasamuddaṃ paviṭṭhā, kāci pacchimaṃ.

1038. `Confluence` (sambheda) means a merging, a coming together at a place. This is a figurative expression indicating where these great rivers flow together and meet. For among such great rivers, some enter the eastern ocean, and others the western.

3. Tatiyaabhisandasuttavaṇṇanā

3. Commentary on the Third Discourse on the Outpouring

1039. “Kusale [V 2.0238] patiṭṭhito”ti ettha yaṃ accantikaṃ kusale patiṭṭhānaṃ. Taṃ dassento “maggakusale patiṭṭhito”ti āha, heṭṭhimamaggakusaleti adhippāyo. Tenāha “bhāveti magga”nti. Ariyaphalaṃyeva dhammasāro. Kilesā khīyanti etthāti kilesakkhayo, nibbānaṃ, tasmiṃ kilesakkhaye rato.

1039. “Established in the wholesome”—here, what is the ultimate establishment in the wholesome? Showing this, it is said, “established in the wholesome of the path,” the meaning being the wholesome of the lower path. Therefore, it is said, “develops the path.” The essence of the Dhamma is indeed the noble fruit. Defilements are destroyed herein—thus, the destruction of defilements, Nibbāna; one delights in that destruction of defilements.

4. Paṭhamamahaddhanasuttavaṇṇanā

4. Commentary on the First Discourse on Great Wealth

1040. Ariyānaṃ [M 2.0537] buddhānaṃ dhanantipi ariyadhanaṃ, nibbānanti keci. Anayatopi visuddhaṭṭhena ariyañca taṃ dhanañca dhanāyitaṭṭhenāti ariyadhanaṃ, tena ariyadhanena. Teneva bhogenāti ariyadhanabhogena.

1040. The wealth of the noble ones, the Buddhas, is also noble wealth—Nibbāna, according to some. Alternatively, because it is pure, it is noble, and it is wealth in the sense of being treasured—thus, noble wealth. By that noble wealth. `With that very enjoyment` means with the enjoyment of noble wealth.

Sagāthakapuññābhisandavaggavaṇṇanā niṭṭhitā.

The Commentary on the Chapter on the Outpouring of Merit with Verses is concluded.

6. Sappaññavaggo

6. The Chapter on One with Wisdom

2. Vassaṃvutthasuttavaṇṇanā

2. Commentary on the Vassaṃvuttha Discourse

1048. Samodhānetvāti yehi indriyādīhi bhāviyamānehi sotāpattimaggo anuppatto, tāneva. Dhammasamānatāya cetaṃ vuttaṃ. Aññāneva hi atthato taṃ taṃ maggaṃ sādhakāni indriyādīni. Tanti paveṇī kathitāti yaṃ kañci vineyyapuggalaṃ anapekkhitvā kevalaṃ tantivasena ṭhiti kathitā.

1048. “Having united” means those very faculties, etc., by which, when developed, the path of stream-entry is attained. This is said because of the similarity of the Dhamma. For in meaning, the faculties, etc., that accomplish each respective path are indeed different. `The thread` means the tradition is spoken of. The establishment is said to be spoken of solely by way of the thread, without regard for any particular individual to be trained.

3. Dhammadinnasuttavaṇṇanā

3. Commentary on the Dhammadinna Discourse

1049. Sattasu janesūti sattasu kittiyamānesu upāsakajanesu. Gambhīrātiādīsu dhammagambhīrāti pāḷigatiyā gambhīrā, tathā ca sallasuttaṃ heṭṭhā pakāsitameva. “Cetanāhaṃ, bhikkhave, kammaṃ vadāmī”tiādinā (a. ni. 6.63; kathā. 539) āgataṃ cetanāsuttaṃ[V 2.0239]. Tattha “cetanāsahajātaṃ nānākkhaṇika”ntiādinā paṭṭhāne āgatanayena, suttesu (a. ni. 3.101) ca “diṭṭhadhammavedanīya”ntiādinā āgatanayena gambhīrabhāvo veditabbo, nibbānassa ceva ariyamaggassa ca pakāsanato asaṅkhatasaṃyuttassa lokuttaratthadīpakatā. “Atītaṃpāhaṃ rūpena khajjiṃ, etarahi khajjāmī”tiādinā pañcannaṃ khandhānaṃ khādakabhāvassa, puggalassa khāditabbatāya vibhāvanena khajjanīyapariyāye (saṃ. ni. 3.79) visesato nissattanijjīvatā dīpitāti vuttaṃ “sattasuññatādīpakā khajjanikasuttantādayo”ti. Upasampajja viharissāmāti ye tesu suttesu vuttapaṭipadaṃ sammadeva paripūrenti, te tesu upasampajja viharanti nāma[M 2.0538]. Etthāti “na kho neta”nti ettha na-kāro “aññamañña”nti ettha ma-kāro viya byañjanasandhimattameva, nāssa koci attho.

1049. “Among seven people” means among seven praised lay followers. Regarding “deep,” etc., it means deep in the Dhamma according to the Pāli usage; and the Sallasutta has also been explained below. The Cetanāsutta is that which comes in such passages as “Intention, monks, I declare to be kamma,” etc. (AN 6.63; Kathā 539). Therein, its profundity should be understood by the method given in the Paṭṭhāna, such as “co-nascent with intention, with different moments,” and by the method given in the suttas (AN 3.101), such as “to be experienced in this very life,” and from its exposition of Nibbāna and the noble path, illuminating the supramundane meaning connected with the unconditioned. Because the Khajjanīyapariyāya (SN 3.79), with such phrases as “In the past, I was consumed by form; now I am being consumed,” reveals the nature of the five aggregates as consumers and the person as what is to be consumed, the state of being without a self or a soul is especially shown. Hence it is said, “the Khajjanika Suttas and others illuminate the emptiness of a being.” “Having attained, I will dwell” means that those who rightly fulfill the practice described in those discourses are said to attain and dwell in them. Here, the `na` in `na kho netaṃ` and the `ma` in `aññamañña` are merely for euphonic conjunction; they have no particular meaning.

4. Gilānasuttavaṇṇanā

4. Commentary on the Discourse to the Sick Person

1050. Na kho panetanti na kho etaṃ, noti ca amhehīti atthoti āha “na kho amhehī”tiādi. Assasantīti assāsanīyāti āha “assāsakarehī”ti. Marissatīti māriso, ekantabhāvimaraṇo, so pana maraṇādhīnavuttikoti vuttaṃ “maraṇapaṭibaddho”ti. Adhimuccehīti adhimuttiṃ uppādehi. Taṃ pana tathā cittassa paṇidhānaṃ ṭhapananti āha “ṭhapehī”ti. Āgamanīyaguṇesūti pubbabhāgaguṇesu. Pamāṇaṃ nāma natthi anantāparimāṇattā. Nānākaraṇaṃ natthi vimuttiyā ninnānattā.

1050. `Na kho panetaṃ` means `na kho etaṃ` (this is not so), and the meaning is `no ti ca amhehi` (and not by us); hence it is said, `na kho amhehi` (not by us), and so on. `Assasanti` means they are to be comforted; hence it is said `assāsakarehi` (by comforters). `Marissati` (he will die) means `māriso` (one who is to die), one whose death is certain. And since his existence is dependent on death, it is said `maraṇapaṭibaddho` (bound to death). `Adhimuccehi` means generate resolution. And that is the placing of the mind’s aspiration; hence it is said, `ṭhapehi` (establish it). `Āgamanīyaguṇesu` means in the preliminary qualities. There is no measure, because it is infinite and immeasurable. There is no differentiation, because of the non-difference of liberation.

9. Paññāpaṭilābhasuttavaṇṇanā

9. Commentary on the Discourse on the Attainment of Wisdom

1055. Paññāpaṭilābhāyāti maggaphalapaññāya paṭilābhatthaṃ. Tenāha “satta sekkhā”tiādi.

1055. “For the attainment of wisdom” means for the purpose of attaining the wisdom of the path and fruit. Hence it is said, “seven trainees,” and so on.

Sappaññavaggavaṇṇanā niṭṭhitā.

The Commentary on the Chapter on One with Wisdom is concluded.

7. Mahāpaññavaggo

7. The Chapter on Great Wisdom

1. Mahāpaññasuttavaṇṇanā

1. Commentary on the Discourse on Great Wisdom

1058. Mahante [V 2.0240] atthe pariggaṇhātīti saccapaṭiccasamuppādādike mahāvitthāre atthe paricchijja asesetvā muṭṭhigate viya katvā gaṇhāti. Sesaṃ heṭṭhā vuttanayameva.

1058. “It grasps great meanings” means it comprehends vast meanings such as the truths, dependent origination, and so on, delimiting them without remainder and making them as if held in the fist. The rest is as explained before.

Mahāpaññavaggavaṇṇanā niṭṭhitā.

The Commentary on the Chapter on Great Wisdom is concluded.

Sotāpattisaṃyuttavaṇṇanā niṭṭhitā.

The Commentary on the Connected Discourses on Stream-Entry is concluded.

12. Saccasaṃyuttaṃ

12. The Connected Discourses on the Truths

1. Samādhivaggo

1. The Chapter on Concentration

1. Samādhisuttavaṇṇanā

1. Commentary on the Concentration Discourse

1071. Cittekaggatāyāti [M 2.0539][V 2.0241] nissakkavacanaṃ “parihāyantī”ti padaṃ apekkhitvā. Yathābhūtādivasenāti yathāgatādivasena. Yathābhūtaṃ nāma imasmiṃ sutte “samāhito, bhikkhave, bhikkhu yathābhūtaṃ pajānātī”tiādi. Ādi-saddena “tathā yasmā”tiādisaṅgaho daṭṭhabbo. Tathā hi yathābhūtavasena kāraṇacchedo kato “tathā yasmā”tiādivacanehi. Vaṇṇāti akkharā, “guṇā”ti keci. Padabyañjanānīti nāmādipadāni ceva taṃsamudāyabhūtabyañjanāni ca.

1071. “From one-pointedness of mind”—this is an ablative expression, referring to the word “declining.” “In accordance with reality, etc.” means in accordance with what has been stated, etc. “In accordance with reality” in this discourse refers to “a concentrated monk, monks, understands in accordance with reality,” etc. By the word “etc.” the inclusion of “thus because,” etc., should be understood. For the cutting off of the cause is done in accordance with reality by statements such as “thus because.” “Vaṇṇā” means letters; some say “qualities.” “Words and phrases” means nouns and other words, and the phrases that are their aggregate.

3. Paṭhamakulaputtasuttādivaṇṇanā

3. Commentary on the First Discourse on the Son of Good Family, and so on

1073-75. Sāsanāvacarā adhippetā bāhirakānaṃ saccābhisamayassa abhāvato. Tathāti iminā catutthapañcamesu atthavisesābhāvaṃ dasseti. Yadi evaṃ kasmā visuṃ visuṃ desanāti āha “tena tena abhilāpenā”tiādi.

1073-75. Those who practice in the Dispensation are intended, because outsiders lack the realization of the truths. By “thus” he shows the absence of a special meaning in the fourth and fifth cases. If so, why the separate teachings? He said, “by this and that mode of expression,” and so on.

10. Tiracchānakathāsuttavaṇṇanā

10. Commentary on the Discourse on Animal-like Talk

1080. Duggatito saṃsārato ca niyyāti etenāti niyyānaṃ, saggamaggo mokkhamaggo ca. Tasmiṃ niyyāne niyuttā, taṃ ettha atthīti niyyānikā. Vacīduccaritasaṃkilesato vā niyyātīti ī-kārassa rassattaṃ ya-kārassa ka-kāraṃ katvā niyyānikā. Cetanāya saddhiṃ samphappalāpā veramaṇi. Tappaṭipakkhato aniyyānikā, tassa bhāvo aniyyānikattaṃ. Tiracchānabhūtanti tirokaraṇabhūtaṃ. Kammaṭṭhānabhāveti aniccatāpaṭisaṃyuttacatusaccakammaṭṭhānabhāve. Sātthakanti dānasīlādinissitattā hitapaṭisaṃyuttaṃ.

1080. Because one is led out (niyyāti) from a bad destination and from saṃsāra by means of it, it is an escape (niyyāna); it is the path to heaven and the path to liberation. Those who are devoted to this escape, or in whom it exists, are called ‘leading to escape’ (niyyānikā). Or, because it leads one out from the defilement of misconduct in speech, by shortening the vowel ‘ī’ and changing ‘y’ to ‘k’, it becomes ‘niyyānikā.’ It is abstinence from frivolous talk together with the intention. Its opposite is ‘not leading to escape’ (aniyyānikā), and the state of that is ‘the quality of not leading to escape’ (aniyyānikattaṃ). ‘Having become animal-like’ (tiracchānabhūtaṃ) means having become an obstruction. ‘In the cultivation of the meditation subject’ (kammaṭṭhānabhāve) means in the cultivation of the meditation subject of the four truths connected with impermanence. ‘Meaningful’ (sātthakaṃ) means connected with benefit because it is based on generosity, virtue, and so on.

Visikhāti [V 2.0242] gharasanniveso. Visikhāgahaṇena ca gāmādigahaṇe viya tannivāsino visesato gahitā “āgato gāmo”tiādīsu viya[M 2.0540]. Tenāha “sūrā samatthā”ti. Kumbhaṭṭhānāpadesena kumbhadāsiyo vuttāti āha – “kumbhadāsikathā”ti. Ayāthāvato uppattiṭṭhitisaṃhārādivasena loko akkhāyati etenāti lokakkhāyikā. Iti iminā pakārena bhavo, iminā abhavoti evaṃ pavattāya itibhavābhavakathāya saddhiṃ.

‘Street’ (visikhā) means a settlement of houses. And by the taking up of ‘street,’ its residents are specifically taken, just as in the taking up of ‘village,’ etc., its inhabitants are implied, as in such phrases as ‘the village has come.’ Therefore he said, ‘heroes, capable ones.’ He said that slave women are referred to by the designation of the potter’s place, hence ‘talk of the potter’s slave women’ (kumbhadāsikathā). Because the world is narrated (akkhāyati) by it in an unreal way, in terms of arising, persisting, dissolving, and so on, it is a ‘worldly narrative’ (lokakkhāyikā). Along with talk of existence and non-existence that proceeds thus: ‘in this way there is becoming, in this way there is non-becoming.’

Samādhivaggavaṇṇanā niṭṭhitā.

The commentary on the Chapter on Concentration is concluded.

2. Dhammacakkappavattanavaggo

2. The Chapter on the Setting in Motion of the Wheel of the Dhamma

1. Dhammacakkappavattanasuttavaṇṇanā

1. Commentary on the Discourse on the Setting in Motion of the Wheel of the Dhamma

1081. “Isīnaṃ patanuppatanavasena osīdanauppatanaṭṭhānavasena evaṃ ‘isipatana’nti ‘laddhanāme’ti saṅkhepato vuttamatthaṃ vivarituṃ ‘ettha hī’”tiādi vuttaṃ.

1081. To explain the meaning stated briefly as ‘Isipatana,’ which ‘gained its name’ from being the place of the alighting and ascending, that is, the descending and ascending of the seers, ‘for here,’ etc., was said.

Āmantesīti ettha yasmā dhammacakkappavattanatthaṃ ayaṃ āmantanā, tasmā samudāgamato paṭṭhāya satthu pubbacaritaṃ saṅkhepeneva pakāsetuṃ vaṭṭatīti “dīpaṅkarapādamūle katābhinīhārato paṭṭhāyā”tiādi āraddhaṃ. Tattha mārabalaṃ bhinditvāti mārañca mārabalañca bhañjitvā. Atha vā mārassa abbhantaraṃ bāhirañcāti duvidhaṃ balaṃ bhañjitvā. “Dveme, bhikkhave, antā”ti ettha anta-saddo “pubbante ñāṇaṃ aparante ñāṇa”ntiādīsu (dha. sa. 1063) viya bhāgapariyāyoti āha “dve ime, bhikkhave, koṭṭhāsā”ti. Saha samudāhārenāti uccāraṇasamakālaṃ. Pattharitvā aṭṭhāsi buddhānubhāvena. Brahmāno samāgacchiṃsu paripakkakusalamūlā saccābhisambodhāya katādhikārā.

Here, regarding ‘addressed them’ (āmantesīti), since this address is for the purpose of setting in motion the Wheel of the Dhamma, it is fitting to briefly explain the Teacher’s previous conduct starting from his aspiration; therefore, ‘starting from the aspiration made at the feet of Dīpaṅkara…’ etc., was begun. There, ‘having shattered Māra’s power’ (mārabalaṃ bhinditvā) means having crushed both Māra and Māra’s power. Alternatively, it means having destroyed Māra’s twofold power, both internal and external. Here, in ‘These two extremes, monks’ (dveme, bhikkhave, antā), the word ‘anta’ (extreme) is a synonym for ‘part,’ as in ‘knowledge of the past extreme, knowledge of the future extreme,’ etc. (Dhs. 1063); hence he said, ‘these two divisions, monks’ (dve ime, bhikkhave, koṭṭhāsā). ‘Simultaneously with the utterance’ (saha samudāhārenā) means at the same time as the utterance. He stood, having spread it forth by the Buddha’s power. Brahmās gathered, their wholesome roots being fully mature and they having made an aspiration for the full awakening to the truths.

Gihisaññojananti gihibandhanaṃ. Chinditvāti haritvā. Na vaḷañjetabbāti nānuyuñjetabbā. Kilesakāmasukhassāti kilesakāmayuttassa sukhassa. Anuyogoti anubhavo. Gāmavāsīhi sevitabbattā gāmavāsīnaṃ santako. Attanoti attabhāvassa. Āhito ahaṃmāno etthāti attā, attabhāvo. Dukkhakaraṇanti dukkhuppādanaṃ. Attamāraṇehīti [M 2.0541] attabādhanehi. Upasamāyāti [V 2.0243] kilesavūpasamo adhippeto, tadatthasampadānavacananti āha “kilesūpasamatthāyā”ti. Esa nayo sesesupi.

‘Householder’s fetter’ (gihisaññojanaṃ) means the bond of a householder. ‘Having cut off’ (chinditvā) means having taken away. ‘Should not be practiced’ (na vaḷañjetabbā) means should not be engaged in. ‘Of the pleasure of sensual desire connected with defilements’ (kilesakāmasukhassa) means of the pleasure associated with defilements and sensual desire. ‘Engagement’ (anuyogo) means experience. ‘Belonging to village-dwellers’ because it is to be practiced by village-dwellers. Because the conceit ‘I am’ is placed (āhito) in it, it is the self (attā), one’s own being. ‘Causing suffering’ (dukkhakaraṇaṃ) means the production of suffering. ‘By self-mortifications’ (attamāraṇehi) means by self-torments. ‘For peace’ (upasamāya)—the pacification of defilements is intended; this is a dative expression for that purpose, hence he says ‘for the purpose of pacifying defilements’ (kilesūpasamatthāya). This is the method in the remaining cases as well.

Saccañāṇādivasena tayo parivaṭṭā etassāti tiparivaṭṭaṃ, ñāṇadassanaṃ. Tenāha “saccañāṇā”tiādi. Yathābhūtaṃ ñāṇanti paṭivedhañāṇaṃ āha. Tesuyeva saccesu. Ñāṇena kattabbassa ca pariññāpaṭivedhādikiccassa ca jānanañāṇaṃ, “tañca kho paṭivedhato pagevā”ti keci. Pacchāti apare. Tathā katañāṇaṃ. Dvādasākāranti dvādasavidhaākārabhedaṃ. Aññatthāti aññesu suttesu.

Because it has three turnings by way of the knowledge of the truth, etc., it is the ‘threefold turning’ (tiparivaṭṭaṃ), that is, knowledge and vision. Therefore he said, ‘knowledge of the truth,’ etc. ‘Knowledge of things as they are’ (yathābhūtaṃ ñāṇaṃ) refers to the knowledge of penetration. In those very truths. The knowledge of knowing the task to be done by knowledge and the task of full understanding, penetration, etc.—some say, ‘and that is indeed before penetration.’ Others say, ‘after.’ Likewise, the knowledge of what has been done. ‘Twelvefold aspect’ (dvādasākāraṃ) means the division into twelve kinds of aspects. ‘Elsewhere’ (aññatthā) means in other suttas.

Paṭivedhañāṇampi desanāñāṇampi dhammacakkanti idaṃ tattha ñāṇakiccaṃ padhānanti katvā vuttaṃ. Saddhindriyādidhammasamudāyo pana pavattanaṭṭhena cakkanti dhammacakkaṃ. Atha vā cakkanti āṇā, dhammato anapetattā dhammañca taṃ cakkañca, dhammena ñāyena cakkantipi dhammacakkaṃ. Yathāha “dhammañca pavatteti cakkañcāti dhammacakkaṃ, cakkañca pavatteti dhammañcāti dhammacakkaṃ, dhammena pavattatīti dhammacakkaṃ, dhammacariyāya pavattatīti dhammacakka”ntiādi (paṭi. ma. 2.40-41). Ubhayampīti paṭivedhañāṇaṃ desanāñāṇanti ubhayampi. Etanti tadubhayaṃ. Imāya desanāyāti iminā suttena pakāsentena bhagavatā yathāvuttañāṇadvayasaṅkhātaṃ dhammacakkaṃ pavattitaṃ nāma pavattanakiccassa aniṭṭhitattā. Patiṭṭhiteti aññāsi koṇḍaññattherena sotāpattiphale patiṭṭhite. Pavattitaṃ nāma kassapasammāsambuddhassa sāsanantaradhānato paṭṭhāya yāva buddhuppādo, ettakaṃ kālaṃ appavattapubbassa pavattitattā, uparimaggādhigamo panassa atthaṅgato evāti.

Both the knowledge of penetration and the knowledge of teaching are the Dhamma Wheel—this was said taking the function of knowledge there as primary.
