The collection of phenomena such as the faculty of faith, however, is the Dhamma Wheel in the sense of ‘setting in motion.’ Or, ‘wheel’ means command; because it is not separate from the Dhamma, it is both the Dhamma and that wheel. It is also the Dhamma Wheel because it is a wheel by way of the Dhamma, by the right method. As was said: ‘He sets in motion the Dhamma and the wheel, thus it is the Dhamma Wheel; he sets in motion the wheel and the Dhamma, thus it is the Dhamma Wheel; it is set in motion by the Dhamma, thus it is the Dhamma Wheel; it is set in motion by the practice of the Dhamma, thus it is the Dhamma Wheel,’ etc. (Paṭis. II.40–41). ‘Both’ (ubhayampi) means both the knowledge of penetration and the knowledge of teaching. ‘This’ (etaṃ) means those two. ‘By this teaching’ (imāya desanāya) means that by this discourse, the Blessed One, while explaining it, is said to have set in motion the Dhamma Wheel, designated as the twofold knowledge mentioned, because the task of setting it in motion was not yet finished. ‘Established’ (patiṭṭhite) means when the Elder Aññāsi Koṇḍañña was established in the fruit of stream-entry. It is called ‘set in motion’ because from the disappearance of the dispensation of the Perfectly Enlightened Kassapa until the arising of the Buddha, for such a long time, what had not been set in motion before was set in motion. The attainment of the higher paths, however, had ceased for him.

Ekappahārenāti ekeneva pahārasaññitena kālena. Divasassa hi tatiyo bhāgo pahāro nāma. Pāḷiyaṃ pana “tena khaṇena tena layena tena muhuttenā”ti vuttaṃ. Taṃ pahārakkhaṇasallakkhaṇameva. Sabbaññutaññāṇobhāsoti sabbaññutaññāṇānubhāvena pavatto obhāso cittaṃ paṭicca utusamuṭṭhāno veditabbo. Yasmā bhagavato dhammacakkappavattanassa ārambhe viya parisamāpane ativiya uḷāratamaṃ pītisomanassaṃ udapādi, tasmā “imassapi udānassā”tiādi vuttaṃ.

‘In a single stroke’ (ekappahārena) means in a single period of time designated as a ‘stroke.’ For a third part of the day is called a ‘stroke.’ In the canonical text, however, it is said, ‘at that instant, at that moment, at that brief time.’ That is precisely the marking of the moment of a stroke. ‘The radiance of omniscient knowledge’ (sabbaññutaññāṇobhāso) means the radiance that arose through the power of omniscient knowledge; it should be understood as arising from temperature, dependent on the mind. Because at the conclusion of the Blessed One’s setting in motion of the Dhamma Wheel, just as at its beginning, an exceedingly sublime joy and happiness arose, therefore, ‘of this inspired utterance too,’ etc., was said.

9. Saṅkāsanasuttavaṇṇanā

9. Commentary on the Saṅkāsana Sutta

1089. Atthasaṃvaṇṇane [M 2.0542] vaṇṇīyanteti vaṇṇā. Teyeva pariyāyena akkharaṇato akkharāni[V 2.0244]. Atthaṃ byañjentīti byañjanāni. Yasmā pana akārādike sarasamaññā, kakārādike byañjanasamaññā, ubhayattha vaṇṇasamaññā, tasmā vuttaṃ “vaṇṇānaṃ vā ekadesā yadidaṃ byañjanā nāmā”ti. Nettiyaṃ pana vākye byañjanasamaññā. Byañjanaggahaṇeneva cettha ākāraniruttiniddesā gahitā evāti daṭṭhabbaṃ. Saṅkāsanāti atthassa ñāpanā bhāgaso. Tenāha “vibhattiyo”ti. Saṅkāsanaggahaṇeneva cettha pakāsanā vuttā hoti. Vibhattiyo hi atthavacaneneva vivaranti, tāhi kāraṇapaññattiyo vuttāyevāti, tāhipi atthapadāni gahitāneva honti. Ayamettha saṅkhepo, vitthāro pana visuddhimaggavaṇṇanāyaṃ nettiaṭṭhakathāyañca vuttanayena veditabbo. Sabbākārenāti sabhāgādivibhāvanākārena. Vaṇṇādīnanti tasmiṃ pana vitthāre pavattavaṇṇādīnaṃ. Tasmāti vaṇṇādīnaṃ antaabhāvato. Evamāhāti “aparimāṇā vaṇṇā byañjanā saṅkāsanā”ti evamāha.

1089. "Vaṇṇā" are so called because they are described in the explanation of meaning. By the same reasoning, "akkharāni" (letters) are so called because they are imperishable. "Byañjanāni" (consonants) are so called because they reveal meaning. Since vowels such as 'a' are termed "sara," consonants such as 'ka' are termed "byañjana," and both are called "vaṇṇa," therefore it is said: "One part of the vaṇṇas is what is called byañjana." In the Netti, however, the term "byañjana" signifies a sentence. Herein, it should be understood that the indications of the derivation of the word 'ākāra' are included by the very term 'byañjana'. "Saṅkāsanā" means the making known of meaning in parts. Hence it is said: "vibhattiyo" (inflections). By the very term 'saṅkāsanā', "pakāsanā" (expression) is also stated. For inflections clarify by the very expression of meaning; and by them, causal designations are also stated—thus, meaningful words are also included by them. This is the summary here. The detailed explanation, however, should be understood according to the method stated in the commentary on the Visuddhimagga and the Netti-aṭṭhakathā. "Sabbākārena" means in every way, such as the demonstration of division into parts, etc. "Vaṇṇādīnaṃ" refers to the letters, etc., that occur in that detailed explanation. "Tasmā" means because there is no end to letters, etc. Thus it is said: "The letters, consonants, and clarifications are immeasurable."

10. Tathasuttavaṇṇanā

10. Explanation of the Tathā Sutta.

1090. Sabhāvāvijahanaṭṭhenāti attano dukkhasabhāvassa kadācipi apariccajanena tathasabhāvaṃ. Tenāha “dukkhañhi dukkhameva vutta”nti. Sabhāvassāti dukkhasabhāvassa. Amoghatāyāti avañjhatāya. Avitathanti na vitathaṃ. Tenāha “na hi dukkhaṃ adukkhaṃ nāma hotī”ti. Aññabhāvānupagamenāti samudayādisabhāvānupagamanena musā na hotīti añño aññathā na hotīti anaññathaṃ. Tenāha “na hī”tiādi.

1090. "By the characteristic of not abandoning its own nature" means that by never abandoning its own nature of suffering, it is of such a nature. Therefore, it is said, "For suffering is indeed called suffering." "Of its own nature" refers to the nature of suffering. "By being non-deceptive" means by being not vain or fruitless. "It is not false" means it is not untrue. Therefore, it is said, "For suffering is not called non-suffering." "By not assuming another nature" means that by not taking on the nature of arising and so on, it is not false; it does not become otherwise, hence it is unchangeable. Therefore, it is said, "For it is not..." and so on.

Dhammacakkappavattanavaggavaṇṇanā niṭṭhitā.

The commentary on the chapter on Setting the Wheel of Dhamma in Motion is concluded.

3. Koṭigāmavaggo

3. The Koṭigāma Chapter.

1. Koṭigāmasuttavaṇṇanā

1. Explanation of the Koṭigāma Sutta.

1091. Ananubodhāti [M 2.0543] paṭivedhassa anurūpabodhābhāvena. Appaṭivedhāti saccānaṃ paṭimukhaṃ vedhābhāvena.

1091. "Ananubodha" (not understanding) means due to the lack of understanding that accords with penetration. "Appaṭivedha" (not penetrating) means due to the lack of piercing the truths face-to-face.

2. Dutiyakoṭigāmasuttavaṇṇanā

2. Explanation of the Second Koṭigāma Sutta.

1092. Phalasamādhiphalapaññānanti [V 2.0245] aggaphalasamādhiaggaphalapaññānaṃ.

1092. "Phalasamādhiphalapaññānaṃ" (of fruition-concentration and fruition-wisdom) refers to the concentration of the highest fruition and the wisdom of the highest fruition.

7. Tathasuttavaṇṇanā

7. Explanation of the Tathā Sutta.

1097. Ariyānanti buddhānaṃ ariyānaṃ. Tenāha “na hī”tiādi.

1097. "Ariyānaṃ" (of the noble ones) means of the noble Buddhas. Therefore, it is said, "For it is not..." and so on.

8. Lokasuttavaṇṇanā

8. Explanation of the Loka Sutta.

1098. Paṭividdhattā desitattā cāti iminā paṭivedhañāṇena desanāñāṇena ca pariggahitattā ariyasantakāni honti ariyassa bhagavato santakabhāvato.

1098. "Paṭividdhattā desitattā ca" (because they are penetrated and taught) means because they are encompassed by the knowledge of penetration and the knowledge of teaching, they belong to the noble ones, being the property of the noble Blessed One.

10. Gavampatisuttavaṇṇanā

10. Explanation of the Gavampati Sutta.

1100. Ekappaṭivedhoti ekeneva ñāṇena catunnaṃ ariyasaccānaṃ ekajjhaṃ paṭivedho.

1100. "Ekappaṭivedha" (single penetration) means the simultaneous penetration of the four noble truths by a single act of knowledge.

Koṭigāmavaggavaṇṇanā niṭṭhitā.

The commentary on the Koṭigāma Chapter is concluded.

4. Sīsapāvanavaggo

4. The Sīsapāvana Chapter.

3. Daṇḍasuttavaṇṇanā

3. Explanation of the Daṇḍa Sutta.

1101. Punappunaṃ vaṭṭasmiṃyeva nibbattanti adiṭṭhattā catunnaṃ ariyasaccānaṃ.

1101. They are born again and again in this very round because of not having seen the Four Noble Truths.

5. Sattisatasuttavaṇṇanā

5. Explanation of the Sattisata Sutta.

1105. Bhaveyya [M 2.0544] ceti dukkhadomanassāni ajjhupekkhitvā sahitehi tehi saccābhisamayo bhaveyyāti evaṃ parikappanā na kātabbāti.

1105. "Bhaveyya ca" (and it might be) means that the assumption should not be made that, having disregarded suffering and sorrow, the realization of the truths would occur together with them.

9. Indakhīlasuttavaṇṇanā

9. Explanation of the Indakhīla Sutta.

1109. Ajjhāsayanti [V 2.0246] sassatādibhedaṃ ajjhāsayaṃ. So hi “idameva saccaṃ moghamañña”nti gāhassa mukhabhūtattā mukhanti adhippeto. Tañca apare adiṭṭhasaccā olokenti, diṭṭhasaccā pana neva olokenti.

1109. "Ajjhāsaya" (inclination) refers to the inclination toward views such as eternalism. For it is intended as the "mouth" because it is the foremost aspect of grasping with the thought, "This alone is true, other views are vain." And others who have not seen the truths examine it, but those who have seen the truths do not examine it.

10. Vādatthikasuttavaṇṇanā

10. Explanation of the Vādatthika Sutta.

1110. Kukkuko pamāṇamajjhimassa purisassa hatthoti attho. Kukkūti tasseva nāmaṃ.

1110. "Kukkuko" means the hand of a man of medium size. "Kukku" is a name for that.

Sīsapāvanavaggavaṇṇanā niṭṭhitā.

The commentary on the Sīsapāvana Chapter is concluded.

5. Papātavaggo

5. The Papāta Chapter.

1. Lokacintāsuttavaṇṇanā

1. Explanation of the Lokacintā Sutta.

1111. Lokacintanti lokasannivesapaṭisaṃyuttavīmaṃsāva. “Lokacitta”ntipi pāṭho, taṃtaṃlokapariyāpannaṃ cittanti attho. Nāḷikerādayoti ādi-saddena avuttānaṃ osadhitiṇavanappatiādīnaṃ saṅgaho. Evarūpanti edisaṃ aññampi taṃtaṃlokacittaṃ.

1111. "Lokacintā" (worldly thought) refers to investigation connected with the arrangement of the world. There is also a reading "lokacitta" (worldly mind), meaning the mind included in this or that world. "Nāḷikerādayo" (coconuts and so on) means the inclusion, by the word "and so on," of unmentioned medicinal herbs, grasses, trees, and the like. "Evarūpa" (of such a kind) refers to other similar minds involved in this or that world.

Vigatacittoti attatthaparatthato apagatavitakko addasa evaṃ adhiṭṭhahiṃsūti sambandho. Sambarimāyanti sambarena asurindena uppāditaṃ asuramāyaṃ, yaṃ “indajāla”ntipi vuccati indassa mohanatthaṃ uppāditattā. Samparivattetvāti paridhāvetvā. Yathā neti ne asure yathā so puriso passati, evaṃ adhiṭṭhahiṃsu. Kasmā panete evaṃ adhiṭṭhahiṃsūti? Taṃ purisaṃ tattha tathānisinnaṃ disvā “ayañca devo”ti āsaṅkantā tathā adhiṭṭhahitvā bhisamuḷālachiddehi pavisitvā attano asurabhavanaṃ gatā. Tenāha bhagavā – “devānaṃyeva mohayamānā”ti.

"Vigatacitta" (free from thought) means devoid of applied thought concerning one's own welfare and that of others. The connection is: "He saw, and thus they resolved." "Sambarimāya" (the illusion of Sambara) refers to the asura-illusion created by Sambara, the lord of the asuras, which is also called "Indra's net" because it was created to delude Indra. "Samparivattetvā" (having circled around) means having run around. "Yathā ne" means they resolved in such a way that that man would not see them, the asuras. But why did they resolve thus? Seeing that man sitting there, and suspecting, "This is a god," they resolved thus, entered through the holes of the lotus stalks, and went to their asura abode. Therefore, the Blessed One said, "Deluding even the gods."

2-3. Papātasuttādivaṇṇanā

2-3. Commentary on the Papāta Sutta and Others.

1112-3. Mariyādapāsāṇoti [V 2.0247][M 2.0545] gijjhakūṭapabbatassa mariyādapākārasadiso mahanto pāsāṇo. Aniṭṭharūpanti ettha rūpa-saddo sabhāvattho “piyarūpe sātarūpe”tiādīsu (ma. ni. 1.408-409) viyāti āha – “aniṭṭhasabhāva”nti.

1112-3. The term 'mariyādapāsāṇa' (boundary stone) means a large boulder resembling the boundary wall of Vulture Peak Mountain. Regarding 'aniṭṭharūpa' (unpleasant form), here the word 'rūpa' has the meaning of 'nature' (sabhāva), as in 'piyarūpa' (pleasant form) and 'sātarūpa' (delightful form), etc. (Majjhima Nikāya 1.408-409). Thus it is said: 'aniṭṭhasabhāva' (unpleasant nature).

5. Vālasuttavaṇṇanā

5. Commentary on the Vāla Sutta.

1115. Upāsananti ācariyaupāsanaṃ, ācariyaṃ antevāsinā vā divase divase sikkhanavasena upāsitabbato upāsananti laddhanāmaṃ kaṇḍakhipanasippaṃ. Kaṇḍaṃ atikkamanteti saraṃ khipante. Poṅkhānupoṅkhanti poṅkhasaddatthaṃ pākaṭaṃ katvā dassetuṃ “ekaṃ kaṇḍaṃ khipitvā”tiādi vuttaṃ. Aparaṃ anupoṅkhanti ettha aparanti tatiyakaṇḍaṃ. Anupoṅkhaṃ nāma idanti dassetuṃ “anupoṅkhaṃ nāma dutiyassa poṅkha”nti vuttaṃ. Tañhi tatiyena sarena vijjhīyati. Puna aparaṃ tassa poṅkhanti idaṃ pana aparāparaṃ avirajjhanaṃ dassetuṃ vuttaṃ. Durabhisambhavataranti abhibhavituṃ asakkuṇeyyataraṃ. Vālanti kesaṃ. Sattadhā bhinditvāti sattakkhattuṃ viphāletvā. Tassa ekaṃ bhedanti tassa kesassa ekaṃ aṃsusaṅkhātaṃ bhedaṃ gahetvā. Vātiṅgaṇamajjhe bandhitvāti vātiṅgaṇaphalassa majjhaṭṭhāne bandhitvā. Aparaṃ bhedanti aparaṃ kesassa aṃsusaṅkhātaṃ bhedaṃ. Aggakoṭiyaṃ bandhitvāti yathā tassa vālabhedassa ūkāmattaṃ likhāmattaṃ vā kaṇḍassa aggakoṭiṃ adhikaṃ hutvā tiṭṭhati, evaṃ bandhitvā. Usabhamatteti vīsatiyaṭṭhimatte ṭhāne ṭhito. Kaṇḍabaddhāya koṭiyāti kaṇḍabaddhāya vālassa koṭiyā vātiṅgaṇabandhanavālassa koṭiṃ paṭivijjheyya.

1115. 'Upāsana' means attending on a teacher. The skill of shooting arrows (kaṇḍakhipanasippaṃ) is called 'upāsana' because it is to be practiced daily by a student through training under a teacher. 'Kaṇḍaṃ atikkamante' (surpassing the arrow) means shooting an arrow. 'Poṅkhānupoṅkha' (successive) is said, by stating 'having shot one arrow,' etc., to clarify the meaning of the word 'poṅkha.' Here, 'aparaṃ' (another) in 'aparaṃ anupoṅkhaṃ' (another following the poṅkha) refers to the third arrow. To show what 'anupoṅkha' means, it is said: 'Anupoṅkha is the poṅkha of the second (arrow),' for it is pierced by the third arrow. Furthermore, 'puna aparaṃ tassa poṅkhaṃ' (yet another of its poṅkha) is said to illustrate continuous non-interruption. 'Durabhisambhavataraṃ' (exceedingly difficult to achieve) means 'even more impossible to overcome.' 'Vāla' means hair. 'Sattadhā bhinditvā' (having split into seven parts) means 'having split it seven times.' 'Tassa ekaṃ bhedaṃ' (one split of it) means 'taking one division of that hair, which is counted as a strand.' 'Vātiṅgaṇamajjhe bandhitvā' (having tied in the middle of an eggplant) means 'having tied it in the center of an eggplant fruit.' 'Aparaṃ bhedaṃ' (another split) refers to 'another division of the hair, counted as a strand.' 'Aggakoṭiyaṃ bandhitvā' (having tied to the tip) means 'having tied it so that the split of the hair, like the size of a louse or a nit, stands beyond the tip of the arrow.' 'Usabhamatte' (at the measure of an usabha) means 'standing at a distance of twenty rods.' 'Kaṇḍabaddhāya koṭiyā' (with the arrow-tied tip) means 'with the tip of the hair tied to the arrow, one would pierce the tip of the hair that is tied to the eggplant'.

8. Dutiyachiggaḷayugasuttavaṇṇanā

8. Commentary on the Second Hole-in-the-Yoke Sutta.

1118. Adhiccuppattikanti yadicchāvasena uppajjanakaṃ. Chiggaḷenāti chiggaḷapadesena. Chiggaḷuparīti heṭṭhimayugassa chiggaḷapadesassa upari. Āruḷhassa chiggaḷenāti ubhinnampi chiddena. Gīvappavesanaṃ viyāti catunnaṃ yugānaṃ chiddapadeseneva uparūpari ṭhitānaṃ chiddantarena kāṇakacchapassa gīvappavesanaṃ [M 2.0546] adhiccatarasambhavaṃ. Tatopi adhiccatarasambhavo manussattalābho, tato adhiccatamasambhavo ariyamaggapaṭilābhoti dassento āha “catusaccapaṭivedho ativiya adhiccatarasambhavo”ti.

1118. 'Adhiccuppattika' (accidental) means 'arising by way of chance.' 'Chiggaḷena' (through a hole) means 'by means of the hole-area.' 'Chiggaḷupari' (above the hole) means 'above the hole-area of the lower yoke.' 'Āruḷhassa chiggaḷena' (by the hole of the one mounted) means 'by both holes.' 'Gīvappavesanaṃ viya' (like the insertion of a neck) means that the insertion of the neck of a one-eyed turtle through the holes of four yokes, placed one above the other, by means of their hole-areas, is a more chance occurrence. Even more of a chance occurrence than that is the attainment of human birth, and the most chance occurrence of all is the attainment of the noble path. Showing this, it is said: 'The penetration of the four truths is an exceedingly chance occurrence'.

Papātavaggavaṇṇanā niṭṭhitā.

The commentary on the Precipice Chapter is concluded.

6. Abhisamayavaggavaṇṇanā

6. Commentary on the Realization Chapter.

1121. Abhisamayasaṃyutte [V 2.0248] vitthāritova, tasmā tattha vuttanayeneva tassa attho veditabbo.

1121. It is explained in detail in the Abhisamaya Saṃyutta; therefore its meaning should be understood in the way stated there.

7. Paṭhamaāmakadhaññapeyyālavaggo

7. The First Chapter on Uncooked Grain (Repetitions).

3. Paññāsuttavaṇṇanā

3. Explanation of the Paññā Sutta.

1133. Lokiyampi visuddhatthena “ariya”nti vattabbataṃ labhatīti “lokiyalokuttarenā”ti vuttaṃ.

1133. Even the worldly attains the designation 'noble' (ariya) in the sense of purity; thus it is said 'by worldly and supramundane'.

4. Surāmerayasuttavaṇṇanā

4. Explanation of the Discourse on Intoxicating Drinks.

1134. Piṭṭhasurāti piṭṭhena kātabbasurā, tathā odanasurā pūvasurā, majjarasādibhūte kiṇṇe pakkhipitvā kattabbā surā kiṇṇapakkhittasurā. Sambhārasaṃyuttāti mūlabhesajjasambhārehi saṃyuttā. Pupphāsavoti nāḷikerapupphādito assavanakaāsavo. Muddikaphalādito assavanakaāsavo phalāsavo. Itītiādiattho. Tena madhvāsavaguḷāsavasambhārasaṃyutte saṅgaṇhāti. Surāsavavinimuttanti yathāvuttasurāsavavinimuttaṃ.

1134. 'Piṭṭhasurā' (flour liquor) is liquor made from flour. Similarly, 'odanasurā' (rice liquor) and 'pūvasurā' (cake liquor). 'Kiṇṇapakkhittasurā' (liquor with yeast added) is liquor made by adding yeast to substances such as fermented liquor (majja). 'Sambhārasaṃyuttā' (combined with ingredients) means 'combined with ingredients of roots and medicines'. 'Pupphāsava' (flower extract) refers to the extract that flows from flowers like coconut blossoms. 'Phalāsava' (fruit extract) is the extract that flows from fruits like grapes. 'Iti' has the meaning of 'and so on'. By this, it includes honey extract, molasses extract, and those combined with ingredients. 'Surāsavavinimuttaṃ' (free from liquor and extract) means 'free from liquor and extract as described'.

10. Pacāyikasuttavaṇṇanā

10. Explanation of the Discourse on Reverence.

1140. Nīcavuttinoti kule jeṭṭhānaṃ mahāpitucūḷapitujeṭṭhabhātikādīnaṃ abhivādanapaccuṭṭhānaañjalikammasāmīcikammādivasena nīcavuttino.

1140. 'Nīcavuttino' (of humble behavior) means being of humble behavior towards elders in the family—such as one's paternal grandfather, paternal uncles, elder brothers, etc.—through acts of reverence like bowing, rising to greet, offering the añjali, and performing proper duties.

8. Dutiyaāmakadhaññapeyyālavaggo

8. The Second Chapter on Uncooked Grain (Repetitions).

8. Bījagāmasuttavaṇṇanā

8. Explanation of the Discourse on Seeds and Plants.

1148. “Mūlabīja”ntiādīsu [V 2.0249][M 2.0547] mūlameva bījanti mūlabījaṃ, mūlabījaṃ etassātipi mūlabījaṃ. Tattha purimena bījagāmo vutto “bījānaṃ samūho”ti katvā, dutiyena bhūtagāmo. Duvidhopeso sāmaññaniddesena, “mūlabījañca mūlabījañca mūlabīja”nti ekasesanayena vā bījattho veditabbo. Esa nayo sesesupi. Phaḷubījanti pabbabījaṃ. Bāhirapaccayantarasamavāye sadisaphaluppattiyā visesakāraṇabhāvato viruhanasamatthe sāraphale niruḷho bīja-saddo. Tadatthasiddhiyā mūlādīsupi kesuci pavattatīti tato nivattanatthaṃ ekena bīja-saddena visesetvā vuttaṃ “bījabīja”nti “rūparūpaṃ, dukkhadukkha”nti (saṃ. ni. 4.327) ca yathā. Nīlatiṇarukkhādikassāti allatiṇassa ceva allarukkhādikassa ca. Ādi-saddena osadhigacchalatādīnaṃ gahaṇaṃ.

1148. In the case of 'mūlabīja' (root-seed), etc.: 'Mūlabīja' means the root itself is the seed. Or, 'mūlabīja' because it has the root as its seed. Here, by the former explanation, 'bījagāma' (seed-group) is stated, taking it as 'a collection of seeds'; by the latter, 'bhūtagāma' (plant-group). This twofold meaning is by general designation. Or, the meaning of 'seed' should be understood by way of ekasesa, as in 'mūlabījañca mūlabījañca mūlabīja' (root-seed and root-seed and root-seed). This method applies to the rest as well. 'Phaḷubīja' (joint-seed) means 'pabbabīja' (node-seed). The word 'seed' is conventionally established in the essential fruit that is capable of sprouting, because it is a special cause for the production of similar fruit through the combination of external and internal conditions. To accomplish that meaning, the word 'seed' is also applied to some roots and so on. To preclude this, it is specified with the word 'seed' itself, as 'bījabīja' (seed-seed), just as in 'rūparūpaṃ' (form-form) and 'dukkhadukkhaṃ' (suffering-suffering) (Saṃyutta Nikāya 4.327). 'Nīlatiṇarukkhādikassa' (of green grass, trees, etc.) means 'of fresh grass and fresh trees, etc.' By the word 'ādi' (etc.), medicinal herbs, shrubs, and vines, etc., are included.

9. Vikālabhojanasuttavaṇṇanā

9. Explanation of the Discourse on Untimely Eating.

1149. Aruṇuggamanato paṭṭhāya yāva majjhanhiko, ayaṃ buddhādiariyānaṃ āciṇṇasamāciṇṇo bhojanassa kālo, tadañño vikāloti āha – “vikālabhojanāti kālātikkantabhojanā”ti.

1149. From the break of dawn until midday, this is the time for eating that is customary and habitual for Buddhas and other noble ones. Any other time is the wrong time. Thus it is said: 'Eating at the wrong time' means eating beyond the proper time.

10. Gandhavilepanasuttavaṇṇanā

10. Explanation of the Discourse on Perfumes and Ointments.

1150. Yaṃ kiñci pupphanti ganthimaṃ aganthimaṃ vā yaṃ kiñci pupphajātaṃ, tathā pisitādibhedaṃ yaṃ kiñci gandhajātaṃ.

1150. 'Whatever flowers' refers to any kind of flowers, whether strung or unstrung, as well as any kind of scented substance, such as powdered fragrances and so forth.

9. Tatiyaāmakadhaññapeyyālavaggo

9. The Third Chapter on Uncooked Grain (Repetitions).

1. Naccagītasuttavaṇṇanā

1. Explanation of the Discourse on Dancing and Singing.

1151. Saṅkhepato [V 2.0250] “sabbapāpassa akaraṇa”ntiādinayappavattaṃ (dī.ni. 2.90; dha.pa. 183) bhagavato sāsanaṃ accantachandarāgapavattito naccādīnaṃ dassanaṃ na anulometīti āha “sāsanassa ananulomattā”ti. Attanā parehi ca payojiyamānaṃ [M 2.0548] payojāpiyamānañca eteneva nacca-saddena gahitaṃ, tathā gītavāditasaddehi cāti āha – “naccananaccāpanādivasenā”ti. Ādi-saddena gāyana-gāyāpana-vādana-vādāpanādīni saṅgaṇhāti. Dassanena cettha savanampi saṅgahitaṃ virūpekasesanayena. Yathāsakaṃ visayassa ālocanasabhāvatāya vā pañcannaṃ viññāṇānaṃ savanakiriyāyapi dassanasaṅkhepasabbhāvato “dassanā”icceva vuttaṃ. Avisūkabhūtassa gītassa savanaṃ kadāci vaṭṭatīti āha – “visūkabhūtā dassanā cā”ti. Tathā hi vuttaṃ paramatthajotikāya khuddakaaṭṭhakathāya (khu. pā. aṭṭha. 2.pacchimapañcasikkhāpadavaṇṇanā) – “dhammūpasaṃhitaṃ gītaṃ vaṭṭati, gītūpasaṃhito dhammo na vaṭṭatī”ti.

1151. In brief, the Blessed One's teaching, proceeding according to the principle 'the non-doing of all evil' (Dīgha Nikāya 2.90; Dhammapada 183), does not conform to the seeing of dances and the like, because they arise from intense desire and passion. Thus it is said: 'because of its non-conformity with the teaching.' What is performed by oneself and by others, and what is caused to be performed, is included by this word 'dance' (nacca), and similarly by the words 'song' and 'instrumental music.' Thus it is said: 'by way of dancing, causing to dance, and so on.' The term 'and so on' (ādi) includes singing, causing to sing, playing instruments, causing to play instruments, and the like. Here, listening is also included under 'seeing,' by way of the ellipsis of dissimilar words. Or, because of the nature of cognizing its own respective object, and because the act of hearing of the five consciousnesses is also included in the summary 'seeing' (dassana), it is simply stated as 'seeing.' Listening to a song that is not a spectacle is sometimes permissible. Thus it is said: 'and seeing that is a spectacle.' For it is stated in the Paramatthajotikā, the commentary on the Khuddakapāṭha: 'Singing connected with the Dhamma is allowable, but the Dhamma connected with singing is not allowable'.

2. Uccāsayanasuttavaṇṇanā

2. Explanation of the Uccāsayana Sutta.

1152. Uccāti ucca-saddena samānatthaṃ ekaṃ saddantaraṃ. Seti etthāti sayanaṃ, uccāsayanaṃ mahāsayanañca samaṇasārupparahitaṃ paṭikkhittanti āha – “pamāṇātikkantaṃ akappiyattharaṇa”nti. Āsandādiāsanañcettha sayaneneva saṅgahitaṃ. Yasmā pana ādhāre paṭikkhitte tadādhārakiriyā paṭikkhittāva hoti, tasmā “uccāsayanamahāsayanā”icceva vuttaṃ. Atthato pana tadupabhogabhūtanisajjānipajjanehi virati dassitāti daṭṭhabbaṃ. Atha vā uccāsayanamahāsayanañca uccāsayanamahāsayanañcāti uccāsayanamahāsayananti etasmiṃ atthe ekasesanayena ayaṃ niddeso kato yathā – “nāmarūpapaccayā saḷāyatana”nti (udā. 1). Āsanakiriyāpubbakattā vā sayanakiriyāya sayanaggahaṇeneva āsanampi gahitanti daṭṭhabbaṃ.

1152. 'Uccā' is another word synonymous with 'ucca.' That on which one lies is a 'sayana' (bed). Thus 'uccāsayana' is a high bed, and 'mahāsayana' is a large bed—both of which are rejected as unsuitable for an ascetic, as they are 'bedding that exceeds the proper measure and is unsuitable.' Here, seats such as chairs are also included under the term 'bed.' Since when the support is rejected, the action dependent on that support is also rejected, therefore, only 'high and large beds' are mentioned. In essence, however, it should be understood that what is indicated is abstinence from the acts of sitting and lying down that involve their use. Alternatively, 'uccāsayana' and 'mahāsayana' are combined into 'uccāsayanamahāsayanā' in this context through the method of ellipsis (ekasesa), just as it is said: 'With name-and-form as condition, the six sense bases' (Ud 1.1). Or, since the act of lying down is preceded by the act of sitting, by the mention of 'lying down' (sayana), 'sitting' is also included; this should be understood.

3. Jātarūpasuttavaṇṇanā

3. The Explanation of the Jātarūpa Sutta

1153. Aññepi uggahāpane upanikkhittasādiyane ca paṭiggahaṇattho labbhatīti āha [V 2.0251] – “na uggaṇhāpenti, na upanikkhittaṃ sādiyantī”ti. Atha vā tividhaṃ paṭiggahaṇaṃ kāyena vācāya manasāti. Tattha kāyena paṭiggahaṇaṃ uggahaṇaṃ, vācāya paṭiggahaṇaṃ uggahāpaṇaṃ, manasā paṭiggahaṇaṃ sādiyanaṃ. Tividhampi paṭiggahaṇaṃ sāmaññaniddesena, ekasesanayena vā gahetvā “paṭiggahaṇā”ti vuttanti āha – “neva naṃ uggaṇhantī”tiādi. Esa nayo “āmakadhaññapaṭiggahaṇā”tiādīsupi.

1153. Others say that the meaning of 'receiving' is found in 'causing to take' and 'accepting what is entrusted.' Thus it is said: 'They do not cause to take, nor do they accept what is entrusted.' Alternatively, receiving is threefold: through body, speech, or mind. Herein, receiving through the body is taking, receiving through speech is causing to take, and receiving through the mind is accepting. All three types of receiving are included by way of a general designation, or by way of the method of ellipsis (ekasesa), taking all three as 'receiving' (paṭiggahaṇa); thus it is said: 'They do not take it,' and so forth. This is the method in phrases like 'receiving raw grain' and others.

4. Āmakadhaññasuttavaṇṇanā

4. Explanation of the Discourse on Raw Grain

1154. Nīvārādiupadhaññassa [M 2.0549] sāliādimūladhaññantogadhattā vuttaṃ “sattavidhassā”ti.

1154. It is said 'of seven kinds' because secondary grains, such as wild rice, are included within the staple grains, such as cultivated rice.

5. Āmakamaṃsasuttavaṇṇanā

5. Explanation of the Discourse on Raw Flesh

1155. “Anujānāmi, bhikkhave, pañca vasāni bhesajjāni – acchavasaṃ, macchavasaṃ, susukāvasaṃ, sūkaravasaṃ, gadrabhavasa”nti (mahāva. 262; pārā. aṭṭha. 623) vuttattā idaṃ uddissa anuññātaṃ nāma. Tassa pana “kāle paṭiggahita”nti (mahāva. 262) vuttattā paṭiggahaṇaṃ vaṭṭatīti āha – “aññatra uddissa anuññātā”ti. Vinayavasena upaparikkhitabbo, tasmā samantapāsādikāya vinayaṭṭhakathāya vuttanayenettha vinicchayo veditabbo.

1155. 'I allow, monks, five kinds of fats as medicine: bear fat, fish fat, alligator fat, pig fat, and donkey fat' (Mahāvagga 262; Pārājika Aṭṭhakathā 623). Because it is said thus, this is called 'specifically permitted.' But since it is said, 'received at the proper time' (Mahāvagga 262), acceptance is allowable. Thus it is said: 'except for what is specifically permitted.' It should be examined according to the Vinaya; therefore, the decision here should be understood in the manner explained in the Samantapāsādikā, the commentary on the Vinaya.

10. Catutthaāmakadhaññapeyyālavaggo

10. The Fourth Section of Repetitions on Raw Grain

2-3. Kayavikkayasuttādivaṇṇanā

2–3. Commentary on the Kayavikkaya Sutta and so forth.

1162-63. Kassaci bhaṇḍassa gahaṇaṃ kayo, dānaṃ vikkayo. Tattha tatthāti gāmantare santike ca gamanaṃ dūtakammanti vuccatīti yojanā. Pahiṇagamanaṃ khuddakagamanaṃ.

1162–63. The taking of goods is buying; the giving is selling. The explanation is that ‘here and there’ (tattha tattha) means going to another village or nearby; this is called the work of a messenger. Going on an errand is a minor going.

4. Tulākūṭasuttavaṇṇanā

4. Commentary on the Tulākūṭa Sutta

1164. Rūpakūṭaṃ sarūpena sadisena chalavohāro. Aṅgakūṭaṃ attano hatthādinā aṅgānaṃ chalakaraṇaṃ[V 2.0252]. Gahaṇakūṭaṃ mānesu gahaṇavasena. Paṭicchannakūṭaṃ ayacuṇṇādinā paṭicchannena chalakaraṇaṃ. Mahatiyā tulāya. Pacchābhāgeti tulāya pacchimabhāge. Hatthenāti hatthapadesena. Akkamatīti uṭṭhātuṃ adento gaṇhāti. Dadanto pubbabhāgeti paresaṃ dadanto pubbabhāge hatthena tulaṃ akkamati. Tanti ayacuṇṇaṃ.

1164. ‘Deceit with forms’ (rūpakūṭaṃ) means deceitful trade with counterfeit or similar items. ‘Deceit with limbs’ (aṅgakūṭaṃ) means deceitful action with one’s own limbs, such as the hands. ‘Deceit in taking’ (gahaṇakūṭaṃ) means by way of taking with false measures. ‘Concealed deceit’ (paṭicchannakūṭaṃ) means deceitful action by means of something concealed, such as iron powder. ‘With a large scale’ (mahatiyā tulāya). ‘On the back part’ (pacchābhāge) means on the rear portion of the scale. ‘With the hand’ (hatthena) means with the region of the hand. ‘Presses down’ (akkamati) means one takes, not allowing it to rise. ‘Giving, on the front part’ (dadanto pubbabhāge) means when giving to others, one presses down the scale with the hand on the front part. ‘Tanti’ means iron powder.

Lohapātiyoti tambalohapātiyo. Suvaṇṇavaṇṇā karontīti asanikhādasuvaṇṇakanakalimpitā suvaṇṇavaṇṇā karonti. Mānabhājanassa [M 2.0550] hadayabhūtassa abbhantarassa bhinnaṃ hadayabhedo. Nimiyamānassa tilataṇḍulādikassa sikhāya aggakoṭiyā bhinnaṃ sikhābhedo. Khettādīnaṃ minanarajjuyā aññathākaraṇaṃ rajjubhedo. Rajjugahaṇeneva cettha daṇḍakassa gahaṇaṃ katamevāti daṭṭhabbaṃ.

‘Bowls of metal’ (lohapātiyo) means bowls made of copper. ‘They make them golden in color’ (suvaṇṇavaṇṇā karonti) means they make them golden in color by coating them with pure gold or plating them with gold. ‘Breaking the heart’ (hadayabhedo) means the breaking of the inner, essential part of the measuring vessel. ‘Breaking the peak’ (sikhābhedo) means the breaking of the topmost point of the heap of sesame, rice, and so forth, that is being measured. ‘Breaking the cord’ (rajjubhedo) means altering the measuring cord for fields and so forth. Here, by the mention of the cord, the mention of the measuring rod should be understood as included.

6-11. Chedanasuttādivaṇṇanā

6–11. Commentary on the Chedana Sutta and so forth.

1166-71. Vadhoti muṭṭhippahārakasātāḷanādīhi hiṃsanaṃ, viheṭhananti attho. Viheṭhanatthopi hi vadha-saddo dissati “atthānaṃ vadhitvā vadhitvā rodeyyā”tiādīsu. Aṭṭhakathāyaṃ pana “māraṇa”nti vuttaṃ, taṃ pana pothanaṃ sandhāyāti sakkā viññātuṃ māraṇasaddassa vihiṃsanepi dissanato. Sesaṃ suviññeyyameva.

1166–71. ‘Killing’ (vadha) means harming by means of blows of the fist, slaps, beatings, and so forth; the meaning is tormenting. Indeed, the word ‘vadha’ is also seen with the meaning of tormenting, as in such phrases as, “Having tormented and tormented, he would weep.” In the commentary, however, it is stated as ‘killing’ (māraṇa), but that can be understood as referring to striking, since the word ‘māraṇa’ is also seen used in the sense of harming. The rest is easily understood.

Āmakadhaññapeyyālavaggavaṇṇanā niṭṭhitā.

The Commentary on the Section of Repetitions on Raw Grain is completed.

Sāratthappakāsiniyā saṃyuttanikāya-aṭṭhakathāya

From the Sāratthappakāsinī, the Commentary on the Saṃyutta Nikāya

Mahāvaggavaṇṇanāya līnatthappakāsanā niṭṭhitā.

The Exposition of Hidden Meanings in the Commentary on the Mahāvagga is completed.

Nigamanakathāvaṇṇanā

Commentary on the Epilogue

Sakalarūpārūpasammasane [M 2.0551] saṇhasukhumavisayañāṇatāya vipassanācāranipuṇabuddhīnaṃ susaṃyatakāyavacīsamācāratāya samathavipassanāsu sammadeva yatanato ca yatīnaṃ bhikkhūnaṃ khandhāyatanadhātusaccindriyapaṭiccasamuppādabhede paramatthadhamme nānānayehi ñāṇavibhāgassa sannissayena bahukārassa saṃyuttāgamavarassa atthasaṃvaṇṇanaṃ kātuṃ sāratthappakāsanato eva nipuṇā yā mayā aṭṭhakathā āraddhāti sambandho. Savisesaṃ paññāvahaguṇattā eva hissa ganthārambhe āditopi “paññāpabhedajananassā”ti [V 2.0253] vuttaṃ. Mahāaṭṭhakathāya sāranti saṃyuttamahāaṭṭhakathāya sāraṃ. Ekūnasaṭṭhimattoti thokaṃ ūnabhāvato matta-saddaggahaṇaṃ.

The connection is this: This commentary, which is profound because it illuminates the essential meaning, was begun by me in order to explain the meaning of the excellent Saṃyutta Āgama. This Āgama is of great benefit to monks, who are ascetics, as a support for the division of knowledge by various methods regarding the ultimate truths—divided into aggregates, sense bases, elements, truths, faculties, and dependent origination—on account of their knowledge of subtle and refined objects in the comprehensive examination of all material and immaterial states, their profound wisdom in the practice of insight, their well-restrained bodily and verbal conduct, and their proper striving in serenity and insight. Indeed, because it possesses exceptional qualities that bring forth wisdom, it was said from the very beginning of composing this text, “It generates the divisions of wisdom.” ‘The essence of the Great Commentary’ (mahāaṭṭhakathāya sāraṃ) means the essence of the Saṃyutta Great Commentary. ‘About fifty-nine’ (ekūnasaṭṭhimattaṃ): the word ‘matta’ is used here to denote that it is slightly less.

Mūlaṭṭhakathāya sāranti pubbe vuttasaṃyuttamahāaṭṭhakathāya sārameva puna nigamanavasena vuttanti. Atha vā mūlaṭṭhakathāya sāranti porāṇaṭṭhakathāsu atthasāraṃ. Tena etaṃ dasseti “saṃyuttamahāaṭṭhakathāya atthasāraṃ ādāya imaṃ sāratthappakāsiniṃ karontena sesamahānikāyānampi mūlaṭṭhakathāsu idha viyogakkhamaṃ atthasāraṃ ādāya akāsi”nti. “Mahāvihārādhivāsīna”nti ca idaṃ purimapacchimapadehi saddhiṃ sambandhitabbaṃ “mahāvihārādhivāsīnaṃ samayaṃ pakāsayantiṃ mahāvihārādhivāsīnaṃ mūlaṭṭhakathāya sāraṃ ādāyā”ti ca. Tena puññena. Hotu sabbo sukhī lokoti kāmāvacarādivibhāgo sabbo sattaloko yathārahaṃ bodhittayādhigamavasena sampayuttena nibbānasukhena sukhito hotūti sadevakassa lokassa accantaṃ sukhādhigamāya attano puññaṃ pariṇāmeti.

‘The essence of the root commentary’ (mūlaṭṭhakathāya sāraṃ) means the essence of the Saṃyutta Great Commentary stated previously, repeated here by way of conclusion. Alternatively, ‘the essence of the root commentary’ means the essential meaning from the ancient commentaries. Thus, this shows: “In composing this Sāratthappakāsinī by taking the essential meaning from the Saṃyutta Great Commentary, he also extracted the essential meaning from the root commentaries of the remaining major Nikāyas, selecting what was suitable for inclusion here.” The phrase ‘of the residents of the Mahāvihāra’ (mahāvihārādhivāsīnaṃ) should be connected with the preceding and following words: “declaring the tradition of the residents of the Mahāvihāra” and “taking the essence from the root commentary of the residents of the Mahāvihāra.” ‘By that merit, may the whole world be happy’ (tena puññena hotu sabbo sukhī loko): by this, the author dedicates his merit for the complete attainment of happiness by the world together with its gods, so that the entire world of beings, with its division into the sense-sphere and other realms, may be happy, as is fitting, through attaining the threefold enlightenment and being united with the bliss of Nibbāna.

Ettāvatā sāratthappakāsiniyā

Thus far from the Sāratthappakāsinī.

Saṃyuttanikāya-aṭṭhakathāya līnatthappakāsanā niṭṭhitā.

The Exposition of Hidden Meanings in the Commentary on the Saṃyutta Nikāya is completed.

Saṃyuttaṭīkā samattā.

The Saṃyutta Subcommentary is completed.
